〔U0126 heading〕
第二章
> CHAPTER II.

〔U0127 heading〕
《奧德賽》的故事
> THE STORY OF THE ODYSSEY.

〔U0128 para〕
我將論證《奧德賽》出自女性之手、寫於西西里西岸的特拉帕尼，並追索這部詩在作者手中如何逐步成形。為了方便讀者跟上這些論證，我想先把自己已經譯完、但尚未出版的全譯本縮寫一份，擺在讀者面前。篇幅若容許，我本該把譯文全部印出來，但這顯然辦不到，因為我在這裡給出的只有全詩大約四分之一。所以我挑出最能說明上述問題的部分，中間只留下足以讓全篇讀得下去、看得明白的銜接文字。我很清楚，這樣一來全詩的美便被摧殘殆盡，因為最動人的段落，往往正是對我的論證最沒有用處的段落。因此，這裡的縮譯只能看作一幅索引草圖──就像一幅畫滿人物肖像的畫作，其版畫底下常附的那種標號略圖。它的用意不在成為藝術品，只是一張說明用的圖解。
> It will help the reader to follow the arguments by which I shall sustain the female authorship of the *Odyssey*, the fact of its being written at Trapani on the west coast of Sicily, and its development in the hands of the writer, if I lay before him an abridgement of the complete translation that I have made, but not yet published. If space permitted I should print my translation in full, but this is obviously impossible, for what I give here is only about a fourth of the whole poem. I have, therefore, selected those parts that throw most light upon the subjects above referred to, with just so much connecting matter as may serve to make the whole readable and intelligible. I am aware that the beauty of the poem is thus fatally marred, for it is often the loveliest passages that serve my purpose least. The abridgement, therefore, that I here give is not to be regarded otherwise than as the key-sketch which we so often see under an engraving of a picture that contains many portraits. It is intended not as a work of art, but as an elucidatory diagram.

〔U0129 para〕
至於縮譯與原文貼得多緊：每一段開頭所標的詩卷出處，會讓讀者看出哪些地方刪節得最多，也讓讀者可以拿希臘原文，或布徹與朗兩位先生的譯本，來查核我的譯法是否忠實。我敢有把握地說：讀者若肯費心這樣查核任何一段讓他覺得現代得不可能的文字，便會發現，在用自己的話講故事、又得加以刪節的限制之下，我已盡我所能，嚴格守住了原作的意圖。
> As regards its closeness to the text, the references to the poem which will be found at the beginning of each paragraph will show where the abridgement has been greatest, and will also enable the reader to verify the fidelity of the rendering either with the Greek or with Messrs. Butcher and Lang's translation. I affirm with confidence that if the reader is good enough to thus verify any passages that may strike him as impossibly modern, he will find that I have adhered as severely to the intention of the original as it was possible for me to do while telling the story in my own words and abridging it.

〔U0130 para〕
有一位評論者──而且是十分友善的一位──對我說，我「扭曲了《奧德賽》的質樸，好讓它顯得荒唐可笑」。我不這麼認為。我揭露了，但我沒有扭曲。要我相信自己曾經這樣做過，哪怕只有片刻，我也會大感震驚。不錯，我「毫無寬飾」，但我也「未嘗懷著惡意落筆」。作者想讓我們相信不可能的事時，我指出了她正在這麼做，也指出了她為什麼要我們相信；但是，除非有人能指出哪怕一段我改動了原作意圖的地方，否則我仍要堅持：我在後面篇幅中呈現給讀者的這部詩的面貌，比就我所知迄今公開過的任何見解都更公允；而且，比起其他任何見解，它都讓這部作品和它的作者有趣上百倍。
> One of my critics, a very friendly one, has told me that I have "distorted the simplicity of the *Odyssey* in order to put it in a ludicrous light." I do not think this. I have revealed, but I have not distorted. I should be shocked to believe for one moment that I had done so. True, I have nothing extenuated, but neither have I set down aught in malice. Where the writer is trying to make us believe impossibilities, I have shown that she is doing so, and have also shown why she wanted us to believe them; but until a single passage is pointed out to me in which I have altered the intention of the original, I shall continue to hold that the conception of the poem which I lay before the reader in the following pages is a juster one than any that, so far as I know, has been made public hitherto; and, moreover, that it makes both the work and the writer a hundred times more interesting than any other conception can do.

〔U0131 para〕
在縮譯之前，我先附上一張奧德修斯宅邸的平面圖，這是我盡力從詩中推敲出來的。讀者若肯稍加用心研究這裡所附的平面圖，會發現自己對故事的理解要好得多。
> I preface my abridgement with a plan of Ulysses' house, so far as I have been able to make it out from the poem. The reader will find that he understands the story much better if he will study the plan of the house here given with some attention.

〔U0132 para〕
傑布教授就這個題目所寫的文字[註1]，我讀過了；布徹與朗合譯的《奧德賽》書末，安德魯．朗先生的第 18 則附註，我也讀過了。我還讀了亞瑟．普拉特先生論求婚者遭殺戮的文章[註2]，結果發現，我與朗先生的看法，遠比與另外兩位接近。我與朗先生意見不同之處只有幾點。其一是內院。他把它看成有屋頂的大廳，我則認為它是露天的，只有四周帶頂的迴廊，也就是 μέγαρα σκιόεντα（有蔭的廳廊）除外──這種格局在西西里的房屋中至今仍很常見，特拉帕尼與巴勒莫尤其如此。其二，我看不出有什麼理由認為那片「石砌地面」是墊高的。其三，我認為 ὀρσοθύρα（暗門）位於忒勒馬科斯那座塔樓的頂上，之所以說它「在牆上」，是因為塔樓緊貼著牆。這些都只是細節。大體而言，我對殺戮求婚者時的情節與場景的看法，與朗先生一致。我不打算說明我為何不得不與傑布教授和普拉特先生意見相左，只把我的宅邸平面圖與縮譯，交由讀者評判。
> I have read what Prof. Jebb has written on this subject,[註1] as also Mr. Andrew Lang's Note 18 at the end of Messrs. Butcher and Lang's translation of the *Odyssey*. I have also read Mr. Arthur Platt's article on the slaying of the suitors,[註2] and find myself in far closer agreement with Mr. Lang than with either of the other writers whom I have named. The only points on which I differ from Mr. Lang are in respect of the inner court, which he sees as a roofed hall, but which I hold to have been open to the sky, except the covered cloister or μέγαρα σκιόεντα, an arrangement which is still very common in Sicilian houses, especially at Trapani and Palermo. I also differ from him in so far as I see no reason to think that the "stone pavement" was raised, and as believing the ὀρσοθύρα to have been at the top of Telemachus's tower, and called "in the wall" because the tower abutted on the wall. These are details: substantially my view of the action and scene during the killing of the suitors agrees with Mr. Lang's. I will not give the reasons which compel me to differ from Prof. Jebb and Mr. Platt, but will leave my plan of the house and the abridged translation to the judgement of the reader.

〔U0133 para〕
*A* 是宅邸主體，內有婦女的居室和其他房間。它有樓上一層，潘妮洛碧的房間就在那裡，俯瞰著求婚者大半時間都待在其中的那座庭院。
> *A* was the body of the house, containing the women's apartments and other rooms. It had an upper story, in which was Penelope's room overlooking the court where the suitors passed the greater part of their time.

〔U0134 para〕
宅邸主體內也有庫房，似乎設在房子的最裡頭，或許在地下一層。從門口 *A'* 有一條通道可以通到庫房，從側門 *A"* 也有幾條後面的通道可達；我猜想這道側門就是宅邸的後門。婦女的居室就開向那條從 *A'* 通往庫房的通道。
> It also contained the store-room, which seems to have been placed at the far end of the house, perhaps in a basement. The store-room could be reached by a passage from a doorway *A'*, and also by back-passages from a side-entrance *A"*, which I suppose to have been the back door of the house. The women's apartments opened on to the passage leading from *A'* to the store-room.

〔U0135 para〕
*B* 與 *B'* 就是大廳（Megaron，或作複數 Megara），也就是內院；其中 *B'* 是帶頂的迴廊，屋頂由立柱撐起，上有橫樑與椽木。庭院露天的部分沒有鋪地，只是天然的泥土。牲畜似乎就在這裡剝皮、整治，因為求婚者被殺時，墨東無疑就在內院，他藏身在一張剛剝下的牛皮（或小母牛皮）底下（《奧德賽》卷二十二 363 行）。
> *B* and *B'* were the Megaron or Megara, that is to say inner court, of which *B'* was a covered cloister with a roof supported by bearing-posts with cross-beams and rafters. The open part of the court had no flooring but the natural soil. Animals seem to have been flayed and dressed here, for Medon, who was certainly in the inner court while the suitors were being killed, concealed himself under a freshly-flayed ox (or heifer's) hide (xxii. 363).

〔U0136 para〕
*B'* 稱為 μέγαρα σκιόεντα，即庭院中「有蔭」的部分，以別於曬得到太陽的那部分。靠近宅邸主體的一端鋪著石板，靠近外院的那一端（大概另外兩側也一樣）則鋪著梣木地板。迴廊鋪石的部分，似乎並沒有比其餘部分墊高；石砌地面的一端有一扇門 *a*，通往一條窄巷。這扇門雖然緊接在 ὀρσοθύρα，亦即暗門（卷二十二 126 行）之後被提到──暗門我們待會兒就會談到──卻與暗門毫無關係。比試斧頭時，石砌地面約略正中處擺著一張座椅 *b*，奧德修斯就是坐在這裡一箭射穿斧頭，開始射殺求婚者時，也是從這裡一躍而起；撐著迴廊屋頂的一根立柱旁邊，有一座長矛架 *c*。
> *B'* was called the μέγαρα σκιόεντα or "shaded" part of the court, to distinguish it from that which was open to the sun. The end nearest the house was paved with stone, while that nearest the outer court (and probably the other two sides) were floored with ash. The part of the cloister that was paved with stone does not appear to have been raised above the level of the rest; at one end of the stone pavement there was a door *a*, opening on to a narrow passage; this door, though mentioned immediately after the ὀρσοθύρα or trap door (xxii. 126), which we shall come to presently, has no connection with it. About the middle of the pavement, during the trial of the axes, there was a seat *b*, from which Ulysses shot through the axes, and from which he sprang when he began to shoot the suitors; against one of the bearing-posts that supported the roof of the cloister, there was *c*, a spear-stand.

〔U0137 figure〕
〔圖版 3：奧德修斯的宅邸〕
> The House of Ulysses.

〔U0138 para〕
迴廊四邊擺滿了小桌，求婚者就在這些桌上用餐。一個人可以把這樣一張桌子舉在身前當盾牌（卷二十二 74、75 行）。
> All the four sides of the cloisters were filled with small tables at which the suitors dined. A man could hold one of these tables before him as a shield (xxii. 74, 75).

〔U0139 para〕
迴廊裡還有：
> In the cloisters there were also

〔U0140 para〕
*d*，一處開放式的爐灶或火塘，設在與宅邸主體相接的那面牆成直角的另一面牆上。至少，我是這樣解讀 τοίχου τοῦ ἑτέρου（另一面牆；卷二十三 90 行）的。
> *d*, an open hearth or fire-place in the wall at right angles to the one which abutted on the house. So, at least, I read τοίχου τοῦ ἑτέρου (xxiii. 90).

〔U0141 para〕
*e*，在調酒缸裡兌酒用的桌子──當然，上面說過的其他桌子也都在。
> *e*, the table at which the wine was mixed in the mixing-bowl—as well, of course, as the other tables above mentioned.

〔U0142 para〕
*f*，一扇門，通往 *g*，即忒勒馬科斯平常睡覺的塔樓〔我把 ἄγχι παρ' ὀρσοθύρην（卷二十二 333 行）譯作「朝著 ὀρσοθύρα 那邊靠近」，而不是「在 ὀρσοθύρα 附近」〕。
> *f*, a door leading into *g*, the tower in which Telemachus used to sleep [translating ἄγχι παρ' ὀρσοθύρην (xxii. 333) not "near the ὀρσοθύρα," but "near towards the ὀρσοθύρα"].

〔U0143 para〕
這座塔樓頂上有一扇暗門 *g'*（ὀρσοθύρα），人可以從那裡爬出去，到塔樓的屋頂上發出警報；但這扇門既不是進來的路，也不是出去的路。
> At the top of this tower there was a trap-door *g'* (ὀρσοθύρα), through which it was possible to get out on to the roof of the tower and raise an alarm, but which afforded neither ingress nor egress.

〔U0144 para〕
*C* 是外院，即 αὐλή，由 *C'* 進入；*C'* 是正門，即 πρῶται θύραι，一座上面蓋有房間的有頂門樓。這座有頂門樓，就是 αἰθούση ἐρίδουπος，也就是回聲響亮的門廊；我們在《奧德賽》裡別的人家也會碰到它，而在今天義大利與西西里的房屋中，更是再熟悉不過。外院四周圍著 *C"*，即有頂的棚舍或穀倉，用來存放車輛、農具，大概還有一些農產。外院之中有：
> *C* was the outer court or αὐλή, approached by *C'*, the main entrance, or πρῶται θύραι, a covered gateway with a room over it. This covered gateway was the αἰθούση ἐρίδουπος, or reverberating portico which we meet with in other Odyssean houses, and are so familiar with in Italian and Sicilian houses at the present day. It was surrounded by *C"*, covered sheds or barns in which carts, farm implements, and probably some farm produce would be stored. It contained

〔U0145 para〕
*h*，前廳（prodomus），也就是內院前方的門廳；訪客要穿過下面這道門才能進去──
> *h*, the prodomus, or vestibule in front of the inner court, into which the visitor would pass through

〔U0146 para〕
*i*，πρόθυρον，亦即內門（不過 πρόθυρον 這個字也用來指外門），以及
> *i*, the πρόθυρον or inner gateway (the word, πρόθυρον, however, is used also for the outer gateway), and

〔U0147 para〕
*k*，圓頂屋（tholus），也就是一間有拱頂的房間。關於它的確切位置，我們所知道的只是卷二十二 459、460 行說它緊貼著外院的牆。我懷疑──但無法證明──這就是奧德修斯親手造床的那間屋子（卷二十三 181–204 行）。
> *k*, the tholus or vaulted room, about the exact position of which all we know is that it is described in xxii. 459, 460, as close up against the wall of the outer court. I suspect, but cannot prove it, that this was the room in which Ulysses built his bed (xxiii. 181-204).

〔U0148 para〕
*D* 是 τυκτὸν δάπεδον，也就是奧德修斯宅邸前方那片平整的場地，求婚者常在這裡擲鐵餅、朝靶子投擲長矛來消遣（卷四 625、627 行）。
> *D* was the τυκτὸν δάπεδον or level ground in front of Ulysses' house, on which the suitors amused themselves playing at quoits and aiming a spear at a mark (iv. 625, 627).

〔U0149 para〕
上述平面圖與說明中，唯一勉強了原文的地方是正門的位置：我把它放得離 λαύρα（巷道）口太遠，一個人不可能守在那裡，把所有想來援救求婚者的人都擋在外面。不過，想想我們還得接受多少其他不可能的事，我以為這一點也不妨跟著其餘的一併放行。像我所設想的《奧德賽》作者那樣一位年輕女子，只要合她的意，把大門往 λαύρα 挪近一點這種小事，她是不會躊躇的。
> The only part of the foregoing plan and explanatory notes that forces the text is in respect of the main gateway, which I place too far from the mouth of the λαύρα for one man to be able to keep out all who would bring help to the suitors; but considering how much other impossibility we have to accept, I think this may be allowed to go with the rest. A young woman, such as I suppose the writer of the *Odyssey* to have been, would not stick at such a trifle as shifting the gates a little nearer the λαύρα if it suited her purpose.

〔U0150 para〕
順帶一提，阿伽門農遇害的地方（《奧德賽》卷四 530、531 行），看來就是一座與上面為奧德修斯宅邸所設想的頗為相似的迴廊庭院。宴席似乎擺在庭院一側的迴廊裡。對面一側的迴廊裡，則埋伏著人。
> In passing, I may say that Agamemnon appears to have been killed (*Od*. iv. 530, 531) in much such a cloistered court as above supposed for the house of Ulysses. A banquet seems to have been prepared in the cloister on one side the court, while men were ambuscaded in the one on the opposite side.

〔U0151 para〕
最後──姑且一提，價值如何，由讀者自行判斷──我想提醒讀者：在奧德修斯宅邸中求婚者經常出入的那部分，詩裡連一點有窗戶的暗示都沒有。
> Lastly, for what it may be worth. I would remind the reader that there is not a hint of windows in the part of Ulysses' house frequented by the suitors.

〔U0152 quote〕
*眾神的會議──忒勒馬科斯與求婚者在奧德修斯家中*
> *The council of the gods—Telemachus and the suitors in the house of Ulysses*.

〔U0153 quote〕
繆思啊，請告訴我那位足智多謀的英雄，特洛伊城陷落之後，他一心要把手下帶回家鄉，途中歷經了許多奇遇。他沒能做到，因為那些人吃了赫利俄斯的牛，全因自己愚不可及而喪命；他本人雖然渴望回到妻子身邊，此刻卻困在一座孤島上憔悴度日──那是仙女卡呂普索的居所。卡呂普索想要他娶她，把他留在身邊許多年，直到最後，眾神都憐憫起他來，只有波賽頓例外，因為奧德修斯弄瞎了他的兒子波呂斐摩斯。
> Tell me, oh Muse, of that ingenious hero who met with many adventures while trying to bring his men home after the Sack of Troy. He failed in this, for the men perished through their own sheer folly in eating the cattle of the Sun, and he himself, though he was longing to get back to his wife, was now languishing in a lonely island, the abode of the nymph Calypso. Calypso wanted him to marry her, and kept him with her for many years, till at last all the gods took pity upon him except Neptune, whose son Polyphemus he had blinded.

〔U0154 quote〕
卻說事有湊巧，波賽頓到埃塞俄比亞人那裡作客去了；埃塞俄比亞人分居兩半，一半面向大西洋，另一半面向印度洋。其餘的神於是召開會議，宙斯對他們發表一篇演說，講埃癸斯托斯追求克呂泰涅斯特拉、謀殺阿伽門農是何等愚蠢；最後，他向雅典娜讓步，同意讓奧德修斯回到伊薩卡。
> Now it so fell out that Neptune had gone to pay a visit to the Ethiopians, who lie in two halves, one half looking on to the Atlantic and the other on to the Indian Ocean. The other gods, therefore, held a council, and Jove made them a speech about the folly of Ægisthus in wooing Clytemnestra and murdering Agamemnon: finally, yielding to Minerva, he consented that Ulysses should return to Ithaca.

〔U0155 quote〕
「既然如此，」雅典娜說，「我們就該派赫耳墨斯去找卡呂普索，告訴她我們的決定。我也要到伊薩卡去，給奧德修斯的兒子忒勒馬科斯壯壯膽，讓他把他母親潘妮洛碧的求婚者打發走；這些人揮霍無度，正在把他吃垮。此外，我還要送他到斯巴達和皮洛斯去打聽父親的消息，這會為他贏得好名聲。」
> "In that case," said Minerva, "we should send Mercury to Calypso to tell her what, we have settled. I will also go to Ithaca and embolden Ulysses' son Telemachus to dismiss the suitors of his mother Penelope, who are ruining him by their extravagance. Furthermore, I will send him to Sparta and Pylos to seek news of his father, for this will get him a good name."

〔U0156 quote〕
女神於是振翅飛到奧德修斯家門口，扮成這家人的一位世交老友，看見求婚者在宅前下著跳棋，擺出好大的威風。忒勒馬科斯見她站在門口，便走上前去，把她領進屋內，將她的長矛放進靠在一根粗壯立柱旁的矛架上，給她搬來座椅，又擺上點心招待。
> The goddess then winged her way to the gates of Ulysses' house, disguised as an old family friend, and found the suitors playing draughts in front of the house and lording it in great style. Telemachus, seeing her standing at the gate, went up to her, led her within, placed her spear in the spear-stand against a strong bearing-post, brought her a seat, and set refreshments before her.

〔U0157 quote〕
這時，求婚者成群湧進環繞內院的那些有遮蔭的迴廊；他們照例在這裡大吃大喝，吃完了，又逼著著名的歌人費彌俄斯為他們唱歌。忒勒馬科斯趁機悄悄和雅典娜說起話來：他告訴她，父親的歸來看來已全然無望，最後又問起她的姓名與家鄉。
> Meanwhile the suitors came trooping into the sheltered cloisters that ran round the inner court; here, according to their wont, they feasted: and when they had done eating they compelled Phemius, a famous bard, to sing to them. On this Telemachus began talking quietly to Minerva: he told her how his father's return seemed now quite hopeless, and concluded by asking her name and country.

〔U0158 quote〕
雅典娜說她是塔福斯人的首領門特斯，正載著一船鐵要到忒墨塞[註3]去換銅。她告訴忒勒馬科斯，她的船停在城外涅里通山[註4]下一個名叫瑞特隆[註5]的港灣裡。她又說：「你去問問老拉厄爾特斯吧，我聽說他如今住在鄉下，日子過得很清苦，從來不進城；他會告訴你，我是你父親的老朋友。」
> Minerva said she was Mentes, chief of the Taphians, and was on her way to Temesa[註3] with a cargo of iron, which she should exchange for copper. She told Telemachus that her ship was lying outside the town, under Mt. Neritum,[註4] in the harbour that was called Rheithron.[註5] "Go," she added, "and ask old Laertes, who I hear is now living but poorly in the country and never comes into the town; he will tell you that I am an old friend of your father's."

〔U0159 quote〕
接著她說：「可是，我看見在你家裡胡作非為的這些人，到底是些什麼人？這一切是怎麼回事？他們的行徑，足以讓任何正派的人作嘔。」
> She then said, "But who are all these people whom I see behaving so atrociously about your house? What is it all about? Their conduct is enough to disgust any right-minded person."

〔U0160 quote〕
「他們是我母親的求婚者，」忒勒馬科斯答道，「從鄰近的杜利基昂島、薩墨、扎金索斯來，也有伊薩卡本地的。我母親既不說她不再嫁，又沒辦法讓這場求婚收場。所以他們就把我吃窮了。」
> "They are my mother's suitors," answered Telemachus, and come from the neighbouring islands of Dulichium, Same, and Zacynthus, as well as from Ithaca itself. My mother does not say she will not marry again and cannot bring her courtship to an end. So they are ruining me."

〔U0161 quote〕
雅典娜非常氣憤，勸他裝備一條船，到皮洛斯和斯巴達去打聽父親的消息。「如果，」她說，「你聽說他還活著，那就把這種揮霍再忍上十二個月。反之，如果你聽說他已經死了，就立刻回來，把你母親送回她父親家，然後不擇手段，殺掉那些求婚者。」
> Minerva was very indignant, and advised him to fit out a ship and go to Pylos and Sparta, seeking news of his father. "If," she said, "you hear of his being alive, you can put up with all this extravagance for yet another twelve months. If on the other hand you hear of his death, return at once, send your mother to her father's, and by fair means or foul kill the suitors."

〔U0162 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯謝過她的忠告，答應照辦，又力勸她多留些時候。她解釋說無論如何不能久留，隨即像一隻老鷹般飛上天去。
> Telemachus thanked her for her advice, promised to take it, and pressed her to prolong her visit. She explained that she could not possibly do so, and then flew off into the air, like an eagle.

〔U0163 quote〕
費彌俄斯還在唱；他選的題目，是亞該亞人從特洛伊歸來的悲慘遭遇。潘妮洛碧在樓上房裡聽得見，便下樓來到求婚者面前，用面紗遮著臉，兩名侍女一左一右陪在她身旁。她站在撐著迴廊屋頂的一根立柱旁邊，叫費彌俄斯換個題目，因為這歌讓她想起失去的丈夫，實在太痛苦。
> Phemius was still singing: he had chosen for his subject the disastrous return of the Achæans from Troy. Penelope could hear him from her room upstairs, and came down into the presence of the suitors holding a veil before her face, and waited upon by two of her handmaids, one of whom stood on either side of her. She stood by one of the bearing-posts that supported the roof of the cloisters, and bade Phemius change his theme, which she found too painful as reminding her of her lost husband.

〔U0164 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯跟她講道理，最後要她回樓上去。「回去吧，」他說，「回到屋裡，照料你每天的事──你的織機、你的紡錘，還有使喚你的僕婦；因為說話是男人的事，而首先是我的事，因為這裡是我當家。」
> Telemachus reasoned with her, and ended by desiring her to go upstairs again. "Go back," he said, "within the house and see to your daily duties, your loom, your distaff, and the ordering of your servants; for speech is man's matter, and mine above all others, for it is I who am master here."

〔U0165 quote〕
潘妮洛碧聽了，帶著她的侍女回到屋裡，心中驚異不已；她一走，忒勒馬科斯就向求婚者下戰帖，要他們第二天到全體大會上與他相見，好讓他正式、公開地警告他們離開他的家。
> On this Penelope went back, with her women, wondering into the house, and as soon as she was gone Telemachus challenged the suitors to meet him next day in full assembly, that he might formally and publicly warn them to leave his house.

〔U0166 quote〕
他們的兩名首領安提諾俄斯與歐律馬科斯都出言反駁；但不久夜幕降臨，求婚者全體離開宅邸，各自回家。他們走後，老奶媽歐律克蕾亞拿著火把，領忒勒馬科斯到他的臥房去；臥房在一座高高的塔樓裡，俯瞰外院，遠近都看得見。
> Antinous and Eurymachus, their two leaders, both rejoined; but presently night fell, and the whole body of suitors left the house for their own several abodes. When they were gone, his old nurse Euryclea conducted Telemachus by torch light to his bedroom, in a lofty tower, which overlooked the outer courtyard and could be seen from far and near.

〔U0167 quote〕
歐律克蕾亞年紀還很輕時，就被拉厄爾特斯買了下來；他為她付了值二十頭牛的價錢。她在家中所受的看重，與他自己的妻子不相上下，但他並沒有讓她上他的床，因為他顧忌妻子不高興。這位好心的老婦人領忒勒馬科斯進房，等他脫衣。她從他手上接過襯衣，仔細摺好，掛在床邊的木釘上。辦完這些，她便離開了，讓他整夜夢著即將啟程的航行。
> Euryclea had been bought by Laertes when she was quite young; he had given the worth of twenty oxen for her, and she was made as much of in the house as his own wife was, but he did not take her to his bed, for he respected his wife's displeasure. The good old woman showed Telemachus to his room, and waited while he undressed. She took his shirt from him, folded it carefully up, and hung it on a peg by his bed side. This done, she left him to dream all night of his intended voyage.

〔U0168 quote〕
*伊薩卡人民大會──忒勒馬科斯啟程前往皮洛斯*
> *Assembly of the people of Ithaca—Telemachus starts for Pylos*.

〔U0169 quote〕
第二天早上，忒勒馬科斯一起身穿好衣服，就派傳令官繞城一周，召集人民開會。眾人到齊後，他向他們訴說自己的不幸：父親已死，而更大的不幸，是求婚者正在糟蹋他的家產。他最後說：「如果非得有人把我吃得傾家蕩產，我寧可是你們自己來吃；因為你們都是有家底的人，我若挨家挨戶向你們討，還討得回來；可是告一群自己身無分文的年輕人，什麼也拿不到。」
> Next morning, as soon as he was up and dressed, Telemachus sent the criers round the town to call the people in assembly. When they came together he told them of his misfortune in the death of his father, and of the still greater one that the suitors were making havoc of his estate. "If anybody," he concluded, "is to eat me out of house and home I had rather you did it yourselves; for you are men of substance, so that if I sued you household by household I should recover from yon; whereas there is nothing to be got by suing a number of young men who have no means of their own." 85

〔U0170 quote〕
安提諾俄斯回答說，這是潘妮洛碧自己的錯。她一直在鼓勵求婚者，給他們每一個人都捎去討好的口信。他說明她如何在將近四年間，用那匹布騙了他們──她說那是要給拉厄爾特斯做壽衣的。「所以，」他說，「我們給你的答覆是：『把你母親送走，讓她嫁給她自己和她父親所挑選的人。』因為她一天不嫁給我們當中的某一個，我們就一天不走。」
> To this Antinous rejoined that it was Penelope's own fault. She had been encouraging the suitors all the time by sending flattering messages to every single one of them. He explained how for nearly four years she had tricked them about the web, which she said was to be a pall for Laertes. "The answer, therefore," said he, "that we make you is this: 'Send your mother away, and let her marry the man of her own and of her father's choice;' for we shall not go till she has married some one or other of us."

〔U0171 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯回答說，他不能違背母親的意願逼她離開。他若這麼做，就得把奧德修斯娶潘妮洛碧時收下的嫁妝退還給外祖父伊卡里俄斯，這對他會是沉重的負擔。況且，這也不是什麼光彩的事。
> Telemachus answered that he could not force his mother to leave against her will. If he did so he should have to refund to his grandfather Icarius the dowry that Ulysses had received on marrying Penelope, and this would bear hardly on him. Besides it would not be a creditable thing to do.

〔U0172 quote〕
於是宙斯從一座山頂[註6]放出兩隻老鷹，牠們以自己那種王者般的姿態飛呀飛，直飛到會場上空，在那裡尖叫、搏鬥，向底下眾人的臉上投以死亡的怒視。人們納悶這究竟是什麼兆頭，直到老預言家哈利瑟耳塞斯告訴他們，這預示奧德修斯即將歸來，向求婚者報仇。
> On this Jove sent two eagles from the top of a mountain,[註6] who flew and flew in their own lordly flight till they reached the assembly, over which they screamed and fought, glaring death into the faces of those who were below. The people wondered what it might all mean, till the old Soothsayer Halitherses told them that it foreshadowed the immediate return of Ulysses to take his revenge upon the suitors.

〔U0173 quote〕

忒勒馬科斯卻獨自走到海邊去禱告。他在灰色的浪裡洗了手，懇求雅典娜幫助他；女神於是化作門托爾的模樣來到他身邊。她就他的一般行止訓誡了他一番，末了說，她不但要替他找一條船，還要親自與他同行。所以他該回家去，把所需的糧食備好。

Meanwhile Minerva, still disguised as Mentor, borrowed a ship from a neighbour. Noëmon, and at nightfall, after the suitors had left as usual, she and Telemachus with his crew of twenty volunteers got the provisions on board and set sail, with a fair wind that whistled over the waters.

> When morning came Telemachus told Menelaus about the suitors, and asked for any information he could give him concerning the death of his father. Menelaus was greatly shocked, but could only tell him what he had heard from Proteus. He said that as he was coming from Egypt he had been detained some weeks through, the displeasure of the gods, in the island of Pharos, where he and his men would have been starved but for the assistance given him by a goddess Idothea, daughter to Proteus, who taught him how to ensnare her father, and compel him to say why heaven was detaining him.

〔U0199 quote〕

> "Idothea," said Menelaus, "disguised me and my three chosen comrades as seals; to this end she had brought four fresh-flayed seal-skins, under which she hid us. The strong smell of these skins was most distressing to us—Who would go to bed with a sea monster if he could help it? but Idothea put some ambrosia under each man's nostrils, and this afforded us great relief. Other seals (Halsoydne's chickens as they call them) now kept coming up by hundreds, and lay down to bask upon the beach.

〔U0200 quote〕
「近午時分，普洛透斯本人上岸了。他先把所有海豹點了一遍，看數目對不對，連我們也一起點了進去；點完之後，他就在牠們中間躺下，像牧人躺在羊群中一樣；他一睡著，我們就撲上去把他牢牢抓住。他一下子變成獅子，接著又變成流水，然後又變成一棵樹；但我們始終沒有鬆手，最後他累了，便把我們想知道的事告訴了我們。
> "Towards noon Proteus himself came up. First he counted all his seals to see that he had the right number, and he counted us in with the others; when he had so done he lay down in the midst of them, as a shepherd with his sheep, and as soon as he was asleep we pounced upon him and gripped him tight; at one moment he became a lion, the next he was running water, and then again he was a tree; but we never loosed hold, and in the end he grew weary, and told us what we would know.

> "He told me also of the fate of Ajax, son of Oïleus, and of my brother Agamemnon. Lastly he told me about Ulysses, who he said was in the island of the nymph Calypso, unable to get away inasmuch as he had neither ship nor crew.

"I could listen to you," said he, "for a whole twelve months, and never once think about my home and my parents; but my men, whom I have left at Pylos, are already impatient for me to return. As for any present you may make me, let it be a piece of plate. I cannot take horses to Ithaca; it contains no plains nor meadow lands, and is more fit for breeding goats than horses. None of our islands are suited for chariot races, and Ithaca least among them all."

〔U0204 quote〕
墨涅拉俄斯笑了，說他看得出忒勒馬科斯出身良好。他有一件極其貴重的器皿，正合適，就送給忒勒馬科斯。
> Menelaus smiled, and said he could see that Telemachus came of good family. He had a piece of plate, of very great value, which was just the thing, and Telemachus should have it.

〔U0205 quote〕
〔這時賓客不斷來到國王家中，帶來了酒和羊，他們的妻子則為他們備好了麵包。他們就這樣在庭院裡張羅起晚飯來。〕[註11]
> [Guests now kept coming to the king's house, bringing 621 both wine and sheep, and their wives had put them up a provision of bread. Thus, then, did they set about cooking their dinner in the courts.][註11]

〔U0206 quote〕
這時，在伊薩卡，求婚者正在奧德修斯宅前的平整場地上擲鐵餅、向靶子投矛，一如既往地蠻橫放肆。他們正玩著，諾埃蒙走過來問安提諾俄斯，知不知道忒勒馬科斯大概什麼時候會從皮洛斯回來，因為他要用他的船。這下一切都露了餡；求婚者壓根兒沒想到忒勒馬科斯真的走了（他們以為他只是到伊薩卡島上自家的某個農莊去了），於是大為光火。他們因此決定在他歸途中埋伏，並準備動身。
> Meanwhile, the suitors in Ithaca were playing at quoits, aiming spears at a mark, and behaving with all their old insolence on the level ground in front of Ulysses' house. While they were thus engaged Noëmon came up and asked Antinous if he could say when Telemachus was likely to be back from Pylos, for he wanted his ship. On this everything came out, and the suitors, who had no idea that Telemachus had really gone (for they thought he was only away on one of his farms in Ithaca), were very angry. They therefore determined to lie in wait for him on his return, and made ready to start.

〔U0207 quote〕
僕人墨東偷聽到他們的陰謀，全部告訴了潘妮洛碧；她和求婚者一樣，這才頭一次知道兒子已經離家到皮洛斯去了。她狠狠責罵侍女們，怪她們在忒勒馬科斯動身時沒把她從床上叫起來，因為她說她確信她們全都知情。不過，經歐律克蕾亞一番安撫，她不久就上樓向雅典娜獻祭。過了一會兒，她沉沉睡去，睡夢中雅典娜把她的姊妹伊芙提墨的幻影送到她床邊，給了她安慰。
> Medon, a servant, overhead their plot, and told all to Penelope, who, like the suitors, learned for the first time that her son had left home and gone to Pylos. She bitterly upbraided her women for not having given her a call out of her bed when Telemachus was leaving, for she said she was sure they knew all about it. Presently, however, on being calmed by Euryclea, she went upstairs and offered sacrifice to Minerva. After a time she fell into a deep slumber, during which she was comforted by a vision of her sister Ipthime, which Minerva had sent to her bedside.

〔U0208 quote〕
入夜後，求婚者揚帆出海，打算在薩墨與伊薩卡之間的海峽伏擊忒勒馬科斯。
> When night fell the suitors set sail, intending to way-lay Telemachus in the Strait between Same and Ithaca.

〔U0209 quote〕
*奧德修斯在卡呂普索島上──他乘木筏離島，歷盡艱辛，抵達費埃克斯人的國度*
> *Ulysses in the island of Calypso—He leaves the island on a raft, and after great suffering reaches the land of the Phæacians*.

〔U0210 quote〕
眾神這時召開第二次會議，雅典娜和宙斯都發了言。
> The gods now held a second council, at which Minerva and Jove both spoke.

〔U0211 quote〕
宙斯說完，便派赫耳墨斯去告訴卡呂普索，奧德修斯要回家了，將在二十天內抵達費埃克斯人的國度。費埃克斯人會送他滿船禮物，再把他送回伊薩卡。
> When Jove had done speaking he sent Mercury to Calypso to tell that Ulysses was to return home, reaching the land of the Phæacians in twenty days. The Phæacians would load him with presents and send him on to Ithaca.

〔U0212 quote〕
於是赫耳墨斯飛越大海，像一隻搜遍深海每個角落捕魚的鸕鶿。過了些時候，他到了卡呂普索的洞府，說明來意。卡呂普索非常生氣，但見無可奈何，便答應遵命。赫耳墨斯一走，她就去找奧德修斯，見他照常在哭，愁苦地一直望著大海；她叫他給自己造一隻木筏，乘著它回家。但奧德修斯疑心很重，非要她發下極其鄭重的誓言，保證她無意加害於他，一片誠心為他打算，他才肯放心。
> Mercury, therefore, flew over the sea like a cormorant that fishes every hole and corner of the deep. In the course of time he reached Calypso's cave and told his story. Calypso was very angry, but seeing there was no help for it promised obedience. As soon as Mercury was gone she went to look for Ulysses, whom she found weeping as usual and looking out ever sadly upon the sea; she told him to build himself a raft and sail home upon it, but Ulysses was deeply suspicious and would not be reassured till she had sworn a very solemn oath that she meant him no harm, and was advising him in all good faith.

〔U0213 quote〕
兩人隨後回到卡呂普索的洞府。「我真不明白，」她說，「你為什麼不肯安安靜靜地跟我待在這裡，偏要一天到晚惦記著你那個妻子。我不相信我長得有哪一點比她差。你要是知道回家之前得吃多少苦，就會留在原地，讓我使你長生不死。」
> The pair then returned to Calypso's cave. "I cannot understand," she said, "why you will not stay quietly here with me, instead of all the time thinking about this wife of yours. I cannot believe that I am any worse looking than she is. If you only knew how much hardship you will have to undergo before you get back, you would stay where you are and let me make you immortal."

〔U0214 quote〕
「別生我的氣，」奧德修斯答道，「你比潘妮洛碧好看得多了。你是女神，她不過是凡間女子。根本沒得比。儘管如此，不管會遭遇什麼，我就是渴望回到自己的家。」
> "Do not be angry with me," answered Ulysses, "you are infinitely better looking than Penelope. You are a goddess, and she is but a mortal woman. There can be no comparison. Nevertheless, come what may, I have a craving to get back to my own home."

〔U0215 quote〕
接下來四天都花在造木筏上。卡呂普索借給他斧頭和鑽子，又指點他哪裡長著最乾燥、木質最老成的樹，奧德修斯便把它們砍倒。他把木筏造得大約和一般人造一艘上好大船時一樣寬，還給它裝了一支舵──好讓他能夠掌舵。
> The next four days were spent in making the raft. Calypso lent him her axe and auger and shewed him where the trees grew which would be driest and whose timber would be the best seasoned, and Ulysses cut them down. He made the raft about as broad in the beam as people generally make a good big ship, and he gave it a rudder—that he might be able to steer it.

〔U0216 quote〕
接著卡呂普索為他沐浴，給他乾淨的衣服，他便出發了，憑著那支舵巧妙地駕著他的船。他朝大熊星座駛去──人們也稱之為「大車」──讓它保持在左手邊，因為卡呂普索是這樣叮囑他的。
> Calypso then washed him, gave him clean clothes, and he set out, steering his ship skilfully by means of the rudder. He steered towards the Great Bear, which is also called the Wain, keeping it on his left hand, for so Calypso had advised him.

〔U0217 quote〕
一連十七天，他一路順利；第十八天，他望見費埃克斯海岸淡淡的輪廓，又長又低地橫在天邊。
> All went well with him for seventeen days, and on the eighteenth he caught sight of the faint outlines of the Phæacian coast lying long and low upon the horizon.

〔U0218 quote〕
然而就在這時，正從埃塞俄比亞人那裡回家的波賽頓瞥見了他，發覺其他眾神趁他不在時搶先下了一著。他於是用三叉戟攪動四周的海水，掀起一場可怕的颶風，奧德修斯什麼也看不見了，四下漆黑如夜；不久他被浪捲下木筏，但總算又爬了回去。
> Here, however, Neptune, who was on his way home from the Ethiopians, caught sight of him and saw the march that the other gods had stolen upon him during his absence. He therefore stirred the sea round with his trident, and raised a frightful hurricane, so that Ulysses could see nothing more, everything being dark as night; presently he was washed overboard, but managed to regain his raft.

〔U0219 quote〕
他正要認定自己必死無疑時，伊諾──又名琉科忒亞──起了憐憫之心，像一隻海鷗般飛落在他的木筏上；她安慰他，把自己的面紗交給他，同時囑咐他一到岸上，就把面紗扔回海裡，扔的時候還要把臉轉開，背向大海。
> He was giving himself up for lost when Ino, also named Leucothea, took pity on him and flew on to his raft like a sea gull; she reassured him and gave him her veil, at the same time telling him to throw it back into the sea as soon as he reached land, and to turn his face away from the sea as he did so.

〔U0220 quote〕
風暴仍在肆虐，木筏在狂風巨浪下解體了，奧德修斯便把伊諾的面紗繫在腋下，開始游泳。波賽頓看到這情景，心滿意足地走了。
> The storm still raged, and the raft went to pieces under its fury, whereon Ulysses bound Ino's veil under his arms and began to swim. Neptune on seeing this was satisfied and went away.

〔U0221 quote〕
他一走，雅典娜就平息了所有的風，只留下北風；北風猛吹了兩天兩夜，於是奧德修斯又被吹回南方。第三天早上，他看見陸地就在近處，但想上岸時，差一點在礁石上撞得粉碎。最後，他找到一條河的河口，河神應了奧德修斯的禱告，止住水流，奧德修斯才得以游進內陸，爬上岸去。
> As soon as he was gone Minerva calmed all the winds except the North, which blew strong for two days and two nights, so that Ulysses was carried to the South again. On the morning of the third day he saw land quite close, but was nearly dashed to pieces against the rocks on trying to leave the water. At last he found the mouth of a river, who, in answer to Ulysses's prayer, stayed his flow, so that Ulysses was able to swim inland and get on shore.

〔U0222 quote〕
他累得半死，不知如何是好，先把伊諾的面紗扔進河中帶鹹味的水裡，然後到內陸的高地上找地方棲身。他在那裡給自己蓋上厚厚一層落葉，便沉沉睡去。
> Nearly dead with exhaustion and in great doubt what to do, he first threw Ino's veil into the salt waters of the river, and then took shelter on the rising ground, inland. Here he covered himself with a thick bed of leaves and fell fast asleep.

〔U0223 quote〕
*奧德修斯與瑙西卡相遇*
> *The meeting between Ulysses and Nausicaa*.

〔U0224 quote〕
奧德修斯這樣睡著的時候，雅典娜去了費埃克斯人的國度──奧德修斯被沖上岸的正是這片土地。
> While Ulysses was thus slumbering, Minerva went to the land of the Phæacians, on which Ulysses had been cast.

〔U0225 quote〕
費埃克斯人從前住在許珀瑞亞，靠近無法無天的庫克羅普斯人；庫克羅普斯人比他們強，常來劫掠他們，所以他們的國王瑙西托俄斯把他們遷到斯刻里亞[註12]，在那裡才得安全。瑙西托俄斯如今已經去世，由他的兒子阿爾基諾俄斯在位。
> Now the Phæacians used to live in Hypereia near the lawless Cyclopes, who were stronger than they were and plundered them; so their king Nausithous removed them to Scheria,[註12] where they were secure. Nausithous was now dead, and his son Alcinous was reigning.

〔U0226 quote〕
阿爾基諾俄斯有個獨生女瑙西卡，她正在臥房裡熟睡。雅典娜來到她床邊，在夢中向她顯現，化身為一位狄馬斯船長的女兒──那是瑙西卡的閨中密友。她提醒瑙西卡婚期將近（不過新郎是誰還沒定），又責備她沒有把她自己和家裡的衣物洗乾淨，做好應有的準備。所以她提議，瑙西卡第二天早上把所有沒洗的衣服帶到洗衣池去，並說她會來幫忙；洗衣池離城有一段路，她勸瑙西卡向父親要一輛車和幾匹騾子。
> Alcinous had an only daughter, Nausicaa, who was in her bedroom fast asleep. Minerva went to her bedside and appeared to her in a dream, having assumed the form of one Captain Dymas's daughter, who was a bosom friend of Nausicaa's. She reminded her of her approaching marriage (for which, however, the bridegroom had not yet been decided upon), and upbraided her for not making due preparation by the washing of her own and of the family linen. She proposed, therefore, that on the following morning Nausicaa should take all the unwashed clothes to the washing cisterns, and said that she would come and help her: the cisterns being some distance from the town, she advised Nausicaa to ask her father to let her have a waggon and mules.

〔U0227 quote〕
瑙西卡醒來，把夢告訴了父親和母親。「爸爸，親愛的，」[註13]她說，「你能不能給我一輛好大的車？我想把我們的髒衣服全拿到河邊去洗。你是這裡的頭號人物，所以你出席議會的時候，穿件乾淨的襯衣才像樣。何況你有五個兒子，兩個已經成家，另外三個是相貌堂堂的年輕單身漢；你知道，他們出門跳舞，總愛穿乾淨的衣裳。」
> Nausicaa, on waking, told her father and mother about her dream, "Papa, dear,"[註13] said she, "could you manage to let me have a good big waggon? I want to take all our dirty clothes to the river and wash them. You are the chief man here, so it is only proper that you should have a clean shirt when you attend meetings of the council. Moreover you have five sons, two of them married, while the other three are good looking young bachelors; you know they always like to have clean linen when they go out to a dance."

〔U0228 quote〕
她父親答應給她一切所需。車子備好了，母親給她裝了一籃吃食，瑙西卡便駕車載著侍女們來到河岸邊的洗衣池；池裡流過的清水十分充足，衣服再髒也洗得乾淨。她們在池中把衣服踩洗乾淨，攤在海灘上晾乾，趁衣服晾著的時候吃了午飯，然後瑙西卡唱起歌，她們便開始玩球。
> Her father promised her all she wanted. The waggon was made ready, her mother put her up a basket of provisions, and Nausicaa drove her maids to the bank of the river, where were the cisterns, through which there flowed enough clear water to wash clothes however dirty they might be. They washed their clothes in the pits by treading upon them, laid them out to dry upon the sea-beach, had their dinner as the clothes were drying, and then began to play at ball while Nausicaa sang to them.

〔U0229 quote〕
過了一陣子，她們正想著要回家時，雅典娜叫醒了奧德修斯；他就在她們上方的樹林裡。他坐起身，聽見女子們的說話聲和笑聲，不知道自己身在何處。
> In the course of time, when they were thinking about starting home, Minerva woke Ulysses, who was in the wood just above them. He sat up, heard the voices and laughter of the women, and wondered where he was.

〔U0230 quote〕
他決定去看看，但想起自己身上一絲不掛，就拿一根橄欖枝擋在身前，然後，就這樣一副猙獰、赤裸、蓬頭垢面的模樣，走出來朝那些女子靠近；她們全都沿著海灘和伸入海中的岬角四散奔逃。然而瑙西卡站穩了沒動，奧德修斯便盤算起來：是大膽走上前去抱住她的膝頭，還是隔著一段恭敬的距離跟她說話。
> He resolved on going to see, but remembering that he had no clothes on, he held a bough of olive before him, and then, all grim, naked, and unkempt as he was, he came out and drew near to the women, who all of them ran away along the beach and the points that jutted into the sea. Nausicaa, however, stood firm, and Ulysses set himself to consider whether he should go boldly up to her and embrace her knees, or speak to her from a respectful distance.

〔U0231 quote〕
他權衡再三，斷定後一種做法最為穩妥；打定主意之後，他開口先請瑙西卡告訴他，她到底是不是女神。如果她是女神，那麼從她的美貌看來，顯然只可能是阿蒂蜜絲。反之，如果她是凡人，那麼哪一位求婚者在聘禮條件上最教人中意，能把她娶回自己家中，那人該是何等幸福啊。最後，他請她行個好，把她帶來包衣服用的舊布隨便給他一塊，再指點他進城的路。
> On the whole he concluded that this would be the most prudent course; and having adopted it, he began by asking Nausicaa to inform him whether she was a goddess or no. If she was a goddess, it was obvious from her beauty that she could only be Diana. If on the other hand she was a mortal, how happy would he be whose proposals in the way of settlements had seemed most advantageous, and who should take her to his own home. Finally he asked her to be kind enough to give him any old wrapper which she might have brought with her to wrap the clothes in, and to show him the way to the town.

〔U0232 quote〕
瑙西卡答道，他看來確實是個很明理的人，但是人不論碰上什麼運氣，都只能忍受。接著她說明他來到的是費埃克斯人的國度，並答應帶他到他們的城裡去。
> Nausicaa replied that he seemed really to be a very sensible person, but that people must put up with their luck whatever it might happen to be. She then explained that he had come to the land of the Phæacians, and promised to conduct him to their city.

〔U0233 quote〕
說完這話，她叫侍女們別那麼膽小。「這個人，」她說，「一點也不危險；我們住在天涯海角，遠離所有鄰邦，兩邊都是怒吼的大海，沒有人能傷害我們。所以，照顧一下這個可憐人，給他點東西吃吧。」
> Having so said, she told her maids not to be such cowards. "The man," she said, "is quite harmless; we live away from all neighbours on a land's end, with the sea roaring on either side of us, and no one can hurt us. See to this poor fellow, therefore, and give him something to eat."

〔U0234 quote〕
侍女們聽了，便走回來，給奧德修斯一件襯衣和一件斗篷；她們還給他一瓶油，叫他到河裡去洗澡。但他說：「你們一直站在這裡，我是不會洗的。要我在一群漂亮的年輕姑娘面前脫光衣服，我實在做不到。」於是她們走開，去告訴她們的女主人。
> When they heard this the maids came back and gave Ulysses a shirt and cloak; they also gave him a bottle of oil and told him to go and wash in the river, but he said, "I will not wash myself while you keep standing there. I cannot bring myself to strip before a number of good-looking young women." So they went and told their mistress.

〔U0235 quote〕
奧德修斯洗完後，雅典娜讓他顯得高大威嚴得多，又給了他一頭濃密的頭髮，如風信子般捲曲，垂到肩上。瑙西卡對他外貌的變化大為驚訝。「起先，」她說，「我覺得他長得很普通，現在卻俊美如神。真希望我能有這樣一個人做丈夫，只要他肯留在這裡。不過這且不管；姑娘們，給他點吃的喝的吧。」
> When Ulysses had done washing, Minerva made him look much grander and more imposing, and gave him a thick head of hair which flowed down in hyacinthine curls about his shoulders, Nausicaa was very much struck with the change in his appearance. "At first," she said, "I thought him quite plain, but now he is of godlike beauty. I wish I might have such a man as that for my husband, if he would only stay here. But never mind this; girls, give him something to eat and drink."

〔U0236 quote〕
侍女們於是在奧德修斯面前擺上肉和酒；他餓壞了。他吃的時候，瑙西卡把衣服摺好放上車，然後向他交代該怎麼做。「跟在車子後面，」她說，「和侍女們一起走，直到靠近房舍為止。至於城，你會發現它坐落在兩座良港之間，要經過一道狹窄的地峽才能到達；地峽兩邊，你會看見船都拖上了岸──因為每個人都有一處可以停放自己船隻的地方。你還會看見城牆，以及立在石砌市集正中央的波賽頓神廟，四周環繞著船商的店鋪。
> The maids then set meat and drink before Ulysses, who was ravenously hungry. While he was eating, Nausicaa got the clothes folded up and put on to the cart; after which she gave him his instructions. "Follow after the cart," she said, "along with the maids, till you get near the houses. As for the town, you will find it lying between two good harbours, and approached by a narrow neck of land, on either side of which you will see the ships drawn up—for every man has a place where he can let his boat lie. You will also see the walls, and the temple of Neptune standing in the middle of the paved market-place, with the ship-brokers' shops all round it.

〔U0237 quote〕
「快到城邊的時候，你就落在後面，因為這裡的人心眼很壞，會在背後說我閒話。他們會說：『跟著瑙西卡到處走的這個英俊外鄉人是誰？她是在哪裡找到他的？我看她是要嫁給他了。他是她從哪條外國船上撿來的水手嗎？還是哪位神應了她的禱告，從天而降，要娶她為妻？她要是到別處去找丈夫倒也好，反正眾多愛慕她的優秀費埃克斯人，她一個也不理。』人們會這樣說，我也不能怪他們，因為我自己要是看到哪個姑娘，父母都還健在，就跟一個外鄉人到處走，又沒有當著全世界的面正式跟他成婚，我也會覺得看不下去。
> "When you get near the town drop behind, for the people here are very ill-natured, and they would talk about me. They would say, 'Who is this fine looking stranger that is going about with Nausicaa? Where did she find him? I suppose she is going to marry him. Is he a sailor whom she has picked up from some foreign vessel, or has a god come down from heaven in answer to her prayers and he is going to marry her? It would be a good thing if she would go and find a husband somewhere else, for she will have nothing to say to any of the many excellent Phæacians who are in love with her.' This is what people would say, and I could not blame them, for I should be scandalised myself if I saw any girl going about with a stranger, while her father and mother were yet alive, without being married to him in the face of all the world.

〔U0238 quote〕
「所以照我說的做吧。你走到城外不遠處雅典娜的聖林時，就在那裡等，等到你估計我和侍女們應該已經到家了。然後跟過來，打聽哪一棟是阿爾基諾俄斯的家；到了以後，直接穿過外院和內院，一直走到我母親面前。你會看見她背靠著一根立柱坐著，在爐火旁紡她的紫色紗線。我父親會坐在她旁邊；別管他，只管去抱住我母親的膝頭，因為她若是對你的請求有好感，你多半就能如願以償。」
> "Do then as I say. When you come to the grove of Minerva a little outside the town, wait till you think I and the maids must have got home. Then come after us, ask which is Alcinous's house, and when you reach it go straight through the outer and inner courts till you come to my mother. You will see her sitting with her back to a bearing-post, and spinning her purple yarn by the fire. My father will be sitting close by her; never mind about him, but go and embrace my mother's knees for if she looks favourably on your suit, you will probably get what you want."

〔U0239 quote〕

「要大膽，」她說，「不論一個人從哪裡來，大膽總是管用的。先去找這家的女主人。她和她丈夫是同一家族的人，她的家世是這樣的：歐律墨東是巨人族的國王，但他和他的族人都被消滅了，他自己也送了命。他最小的女兒佩里波亞是位絕色美人，她為波賽頓生下了費埃克斯人的國王瑙西托俄斯。瑙西托俄斯有兩個兒子，瑞克塞諾爾和阿爾基諾俄斯；瑞克塞諾爾早逝，留下一個獨生女阿瑞忒，她的叔父阿爾基諾俄斯娶了她，而且敬重她，普天之下沒有哪個女人受丈夫如此敬重。她所有的家人和鄰人都愛戴她，把她當作朋友與和事佬，因為她是個徹頭徹尾的好女人。你若能得到她的幫忙，一切都會順利。」
> "Be bold," she said; "boldness always tells, no matter where a man comes from. First find the mistress of the house. She is of the same family as her husband, and her descent is in this wise. Eurymedon was king of the giants, but he and his people were overthrown, and he lost his own life. His youngest daughter was Peribœa, a woman of surpassing beauty, who gave birth by Neptune to Nausithous, king of the Phæacians. He had two sons, Rhexenor and Alcinous; Rhexenor died young, leaving an only daughter, Arete, whom her uncle Alcinous married, and whom he honours as no other woman in the whole world is honoured by her husband. All her family and all her neighbours adore her as a friend and peacemaker, for she is a thoroughly good woman. If you can gain her good offices all will go well with you."

雅典娜於是離開他，前往馬拉松和雅典，造訪了厄瑞克透斯的家；奧德修斯則繼續朝阿爾基諾俄斯的家走去。來到青銅門檻之前，他停下腳步，駐足片刻，心中思緒萬千，因為這座宮殿的輝煌有如太陽與月亮。兩側的牆從這頭到那頭全是青銅，簷口飾著藍色的琺瑯。門扇是黃金的，掛在從青銅地面升起的白銀門柱上；門楣是白銀的，門上的鉤環則是黃金的。
> Minerva then left him and went to Marathon and Athens, where she visited the house of Erechtheus, but Ulysses went on to the house of Alcinous, and he pondered much as he paused awhile before he reached the threshold of bronze, for the splendour of the palace was like that of the sun and moon. The walls on either side were of bronze from end to end, and the cornice was of blue enamel. The doors were of gold and hung on pillars of silver that rose from a floor of bronze, while the lintel was of silver and the hook of the door was of gold.

〔U0246 quote〕
兩旁有金獒和銀獒，是赫淮斯托斯以他絕妙的手藝，特地打造來看守阿爾基諾俄斯王宮的，所以牠們不死，永遠不會老。沿著牆邊從這頭到那頭，處處擺著座椅，上面鋪著織工精細的墊布，是家中女眷織成的。費埃克斯人的首要人物常坐在這裡吃喝，因為一年四季都豐足有餘；還有一些年輕男子的金像，手持點燃的火把，立在座台上，為坐席用餐的人照明。
> On either side there were gold and silver mastiffs which Vulcan with his consummate skill had fashioned expressly to keep watch over the palace of King Alcinous, so they were immortal and could never grow old. Seats were ranged here and there all along the wall, from one end to the other, with coverings of fine woven work, which the women of the house had made. Here the chief persons of the Phæacians used to sit and eat and drink, for there was abundance at all seasons; and there were golden figures of young men with lighted torches in their hands, raised on pedestals to give light to them that sat at meat.

〔U0247 quote〕
宅中有五十名女僕，有的總在磨坊裡磨著飽滿金黃的穀粒，有的在織機前工作，坐著紡紗，她們的梭子來回穿梭，像白楊葉片簌簌顫動；布織得那麼密，連油都滲不透。費埃克斯人是世上最好的水手，同樣地，他們的女人在紡織上也勝過其他所有人，因為雅典娜教會了她們各式各樣有用的技藝，而且她們都非常聰慧。

As the Phæacians are the best sailors in the world, so their women excel all others in weaving, for Minerva has taught them all manner of useful arts, and they are very intelligent.

〔U0248 quote〕
外院大門外有一座大果園[註14]，約四英畝，四周圍著牆。園中滿是美麗的果樹──梨、石榴，還有最可口的蘋果。也有甜美多汁的無花果，和長得正茂盛的橄欖。這些果子一年到頭不會爛也不會斷，不分冬夏，因為空氣如此和煦，舊的還沒落，新的一茬又熟了。梨接著梨長，蘋果接著蘋果，無花果接著無花果，葡萄也是如此，因為那裡有一片極好的葡萄園；在其中一塊平地上，葡萄正被曬成葡萄乾；另一塊上正在採收；有些在酒桶裡被踩踏；再往前一些，有的已經落了花，開始結果；還有一些正在轉色。園地的最遠處，有整齊美麗的花圃，四季花開不斷。兩道溪水流經園中，一道引入水渠，灌溉全園，另一道從外院地下引到宅邸本身，城裡的人也從那裡汲水。上天賜給阿爾基諾俄斯王宮的輝煌，就是這樣。
> Outside the gate of the outer court there is a large garden[註14] of about four acres, with a wall all round it. It is full of beautiful trees—pears, pomegranates, and the most delicious apples. There are luscious figs also, and olives in full growth. The fruits never rot nor fail all the year round, neither winter nor summer, for the air is so soft that a new crop ripens before the old has dropped. Pear grows on pear, apple on apple, and fig on fig, and so also with the grapes, for there is an excellent vineyard; on the level ground of a part of this, the grapes are being made into raisins; on another part they are being gathered; some are being trodden in the wine-tubs; others, further on, have shed their blossom and are beginning to show fruit; others, again, are just changing colour. In the furthest part of the ground there are beautifully arranged beds of flowers that are in bloom all the year round. Two streams go through it, the one turned in ducts throughout the whole garden, while the other is carried under the ground of the outer court to the house itself, and the townspeople drew water from it. Such, then, were the splendours with which heaven had endowed the house of King Alcinous.

於是奧德修斯在那裡站了一會兒，四下觀望，看夠了之後，才跨過門檻，進入宅邸的範圍。他穿過每晚都來阿爾基諾俄斯王席上作客的人群，這些人此時正照例向赫耳墨斯行奠酒之禮，準備就寢。雅典娜給他披上的隱身霧仍罩著他；他走到阿瑞忒面前，抱住她的膝頭，這時他忽然現了形。人人都很驚訝，竟看見那裡有一個人，但奧德修斯毫不理會，立刻懇求王后相助；接著他便坐到爐邊的灰燼中。
> So here Ulysses stood for a while and looked about him, but when he had looked long enough he crossed the threshold and went within the precincts of the house. He passed through the crowd of guests who were nightly visitors at the table of King Alcinous, and who were then making their usual drink offering to Mercury before going for the night. He was still shrouded in the mist of invisibility with which Minerva had invested him, and going up to Arete he embraced her knees, whereon he suddenly became visible. Every one was greatly surprised at seeing a man there, but Ulysses paid no attention to this, and at once implored the queen's assistance; he then sat down among the ashes on the hearth.

> When morning came Alcinous called an assembly of the Phæacian, and Minerva went about urging every one to come and see the wonderful stranger. She also gave Ulysses a more imposing presence that he might impress the people favourably. When the Phæacians were assembled Alcinous said:—

〔U0257 quote〕
「我不知道這位外鄉人是誰，也不知道他從哪裡來；但他希望我們送他回家，而我的客人從來沒有誰能抱怨我送他回家不夠快。所以我們來裝備一條新船，配上五十二名水手，送他回去。水手們到我家來，我會供給他們食材，讓他們自己煮。市議員和參事們就在宅中設宴款待。我不容推辭；我們還要請德摩多科斯來為我們唱歌。」
> "I do not know who this stranger is, nor where he comes from; but he wants us to send him to his own home, and no guest of mine was ever yet able to complain that I did not send him home quickly enough. The aldermen and councillors shall be feasted inside the house. I can take no denial, and we will have Demodocus to sing to us."

This so affected Ulysses that he kept on weeping as long as the bard sang, and though he was able to conceal his tears from the company generally, Alcinous perceived his distress, and proposed that they should all now adjourn to the athletic sports—which were to consist mainly of boxing, wrestling, jumping, and foot racing.

於是德摩多科斯把豎琴掛回木釘上，由人領到舉行競技的場地。全城的人都湧去觀看。克呂托紐斯贏了賽跑，歐律阿羅斯奪得摔角獎，安菲阿羅斯跳得最遠，阿爾基諾俄斯的兒子拉俄達馬斯則是拳擊第一。
> Demodocus, therefore, hung the lyre on its peg and was led out to the place where the sports were to be held. The whole town flocked to see them. Clytoneüs won the foot race, Euryalus took the prize for wrestling, Amphialus was the best jumper, and Alcinous' son Laodamas the best boxer.

〔U0262 quote〕

> Euryalus on this insulted Ulysses, and said that he supposed he was some grasping merchant who thought of nothing but his freights. "You have none of the look," said he, "of an athlete about you."

〔U0264 quote〕
奧德修斯勃然大怒，罵歐律阿羅斯是個空有一副好皮囊的年輕傻瓜。接著他拿起一塊鐵餅，比費埃克斯人平常擲的重得多。[註15]鐵餅呼嘯著劃過空中，輕輕鬆鬆就超越了此前所有人的成績。他受此鼓舞，又發表了一篇又長又極為憤怒的演說，說無論費埃克斯人要比什麼，他都奉陪到底，唯獨賽跑除外，因為他身子還虛得很，這一項恐怕會輸給他們。「還有，」他說，「我絕不跟拉俄達馬斯比任何項目。他是我東道主的兒子；客人向東道主家裡的任何人挑戰，實在是再愚蠢不過的事。會動這種念頭的人，必定是個白痴。」
> Ulysses was furious, and told Euryalus that he was a goodlooking young fool. He then took up a disc far heavier than those which the Phæacians were in the habit of throwing.[註15] The disc made a hurtling sound as it passed through the air, and easily surpassed any throw that had been made yet. Thus encouraged he made another long and very angry speech, in which he said he would compete with any Phæacian in any contest they chose to name, except in running, for he was still so much pulled down that he thought they might beat him here. "Also," he said, "I will not compete in anything with Laodamas. He is my host's son, and it is a most unwise thing for a guest to challenge any member of his host's family. A man must be an idiot to think of such a thing."

〔U0265 quote〕
「先生，」阿爾基諾俄斯說，「我明白，我們有位運動員說了些話，惹您不快；他懷疑您的本事，用的是有教養的人絕不會用的方式。我也聽說，您還向我們全體下了戰書。我得解釋一下：我們的拳擊和摔角並不出名，但我們跑得出奇地快，又是大膽的航海家。我們也非常愛好歌舞，喜歡洗熱水澡，常常換穿乾淨的衣裳。那麼，你們當中舞跳得最輕快的，現在就上前來，讓這位客人瞧瞧，在一切優雅的技藝上，我們比別的民族高明多少。也找個人去我家，把德摩多科斯留在那裡的豎琴拿來。」
> "Sir," said Alcinous, "I understand that you are displeased at some remarks that have fallen from one of our athletes, who has thrown doubt upon your prowess in a way that no gentleman would do. I hear that you have also given us a general challenge. I should explain that we are not famous for our skill in boxing or wrestling, but are singularly fleet runners and bold mariners. We are also much given to song and dance, and we like warm baths and frequent changes of linen. So now come forward some of you who are the nimblest dancers, and show the stranger how much we surpass other nations in all graceful accomplishments. Let some one also bring Demodocus's lyre from my house where he has left it."

於是國王發表了一篇演說，說這位客人應當把名字告訴大家。他一定有名字，因為人們總是孩子一生下來就替他取名。他不必為送行的事掛心。他只要說出想去哪裡就行了；費埃克斯人的船聰明得很，自己就會把他載到。話雖如此，國王記得曾聽父親瑙西托俄斯說過，有朝一日，波賽頓會因為費埃克斯人太輕易替人送行而發怒；波賽頓還說過，要在他們的某艘船返航時把它擊毀，並且要用一座高山把他們的城市埋掉。
> The king, therefore, made a speech in which he said that the stranger ought to tell them his name. He must have one, for people always gave their children names as soon as they were born. He need not be uneasy about his escort. All he had to do was to say where he wanted to go, and the Phæacian ships were so clever that they would take him there of their own accord. Nevertheless he remembered hearing his father Nausithous say, that one day Neptune would be angry with the Phæacians for giving people escorts so readily, and had said he would wreck one of their ships as it was returning, and would also bury their city under a high mountain.

〔U0276 quote〕

〔U0277 quote〕
於是奧德修斯起身。「阿爾基諾俄斯王，」他說，「您問我的名字，我這就告訴您。我是奧德修斯，住在伊薩卡；島上有座高山，名叫涅里通。附近還有幾座彼此相鄰的島嶼：杜利基昂島、薩墨和扎金索斯。伊薩卡橫臥在天際線上，在海中位置最高、最靠西方，其他島嶼則遠在它的東邊。這就是我想回去的島；因為一個人在沒有父母的異鄉，房子就算再體面，對他來說也比不上自己的家鄉和親生父母那樣甜美。
> Then Ulysses rose. "King Alcinous," said he, "you ask my name and I will tell you. I am Ulysses, and dwell in Ithaca, an island which contains a high mountain called Neritum. In its neighbourhood there are other islands near to one another, Dulichium, Same, and Zacynthus. It lies on the horizon all highest up in the sea towards the West, while the other islands lie away from it to the East. This is the island which I would reach, for however fine a house a man may have in a land where his parents are not, there will still be nothing sweeter to him than his home and his own father and mother.

〔U0278 quote〕
「現在我來講講我的遭遇。離開特洛伊之後，我們先登陸基科涅斯人的土地，洗劫了他們的城市，但最後還是被打退了，不過戰利品倒是一併帶走了。
> "I will now tell you of my adventures. On leaving Troy we first made a descent on the land of the Cicons, and sacked their city but were eventually beaten off, though we took our booty with us.

〔U0279 quote〕
「我們從那裡往南航行，背後是強勁的北風，一直到了基西拉島；北風持續不停，把我們吹離了航道，使我無法繞過馬勒亞岬。
> "Thence we sailed South with a strong North wind behind us, till we reached the island of Cythera, where we were driven off our course by a continuance of North wind which prevented my doubling Cape Malea.

〔U0280 quote〕
「一連九天，我被惡劣的逆風吹得四處漂流，到了第十天，我們抵達食蓮人的土地。那裡的人待我的部下很和善，卻拿蓮花給他們吃；吃了蓮花的人便完全失去了回家的念頭，我費了好大一番功夫，才把那些嘗過蓮花的人重新弄回船上。
> "Nine days was I driven by foul winds, and on the tenth we reached the land of the Lotus eaters, where the people were good to my men but gave them to eat of the lotus, which made them lose all desire to return home, so that I had a great work to get those who had tasted it on board again.

〔U0281 quote〕
「我們從那裡又被帶往更遠處，來到了野蠻的庫克羅普斯人的土地。在他們的海岸外，不算太遠的地方，有一座長滿樹林的小島，島上到處是野山羊。那裡從來沒有人跡；就連獵人──他們通常在林中或山頂上什麼苦都吃得了──也從不上島；島上既無人耕種，也無人放牧，年復一年荒無人煙，只住著山羊。因為庫克羅普斯人沒有船，所以不能像有船的人那樣四處往來。假如他們有船，早就去那座島上殖民了，因為那座島一點也不差，四季萬物都能應時生長。島上有草地，水源充足，土質優良，一直延伸到水邊。葡萄在那裡一定長得極好；島上有好耕地，土壤肥沃，必能豐收；此外還有一處方便的港口──想必是哪位神把我們引進去的，因為那夜黑得伸手不見五指。船隻四周一片濃黑，天上也沒有月亮，因為雲層遮蔽了天空。誰也看不見那座島，連拍岸的浪花也看不見，直到我們發現自己已經進了港。於是我們就在那裡繫好船，在海灘上紮營。
> "Thence we were carried further, till we came to the land of the savage Cyclopes. Off their coast, but not very far, there is a wooded island abounding with wild goats. It is untrodden by the foot of man; even the huntsmen, who as a general rule will suffer any hardship in forest or on mountain top, never go there; it is neither tilled nor fed down, but remains year after year uninhabited save by goats only. For the Cyclopes have no ships, and cannot therefore go from place to place as those who have ships can do. If they had ships they would have colonised the island, for it is not at all a bad one and would bring forth all things in their season. There is meadow land, well watered and of good quality, that stretches down to the water's edge. Grapes would do wonderfully well there; it contains good arable land, which would yield heavy crops, for the soil is rich; moreover it has a convenient port—into which some god must have taken us, for the night was so dark that we could see nothing. There was a thick darkness all round the ships, neither was there any moon, for the sky was covered with clouds. No one could see the island, nor yet waves breaking upon the shore till we found ourselves in the harbour. Here, then, we moored our ships and camped down upon the beach.

〔U0283 figure〕

〔U0284 quote〕
「天亮後我們去獵野山羊，殺了一百多頭，[註17]一整天直到日落，我們都吃著羊肉，喝著從基科涅斯人那裡搶來的存酒。我們也不時眺望對面庫克羅普斯人的土地；那地方近得很，我們看得見他們焚燒麥茬的煙，幾乎覺得聽到了他們的綿羊和山羊在咩咩叫。
> "When morning came we hunted the wild goats, of which we killed over a hundred,[註17] and all day long to the going down of the sun we feasted on them and the store of wine we had taken from the Cicons. We kept looking also on the land of the Cyclopes over against us, which was so near that we could see the smoke of their stubble fires, and almost fancy we heard the bleating of their sheep and goats.

〔U0285 quote〕
「我們在海灘上又紮營過了第二夜。破曉時分，我召集眾人開會商議，說我要親自帶著自己的船去偵察那片土地，其他船隻則留在島上等候。我隨即出發；快到大陸時，我們看見懸崖上有個大洞穴，離海不遠，洞前還有幾座很大的羊圈。登岸後，我挑了十二個人往內陸走，隨身帶了一皮囊非常奇妙的酒，那是阿波羅的祭司馬容送我的──當初我們洗劫基科涅斯人的城市時，我饒了他和他一家人的性命。其餘船員則留在海邊等我回來。
> "We camped a second night upon the beach, and at day break, having called a council, I said I would take my own ship and reconnoitre the country, but would leave the other ships at the island. Thereon I started, but when we got near the main land we saw a great cave in the cliff, not far from the sea, and there were large sheep yards in front of it. On landing I chose twelve men and went inland, taking with me a goat skin full of a very wondrous wine that Maron, priest of Apollo, had given me when I spared his life and that of his family at the time that we were sacking the city of the Cicons. The rest of my crew were to wait my return by the sea side.

〔U0286 quote〕
「我們很快就到了洞穴，發現主人不在家，便把洞裡的東西察看了一番；只見一桶桶乳清滿到了桶緣，架子上堆滿了乳酪，羊圈裡也擠滿了小綿羊和小山羊。部下們懇求我讓他們偷些乳酪，趕走幾頭小羊，然後揚帆離去，但我不肯，因為我指望主人會送我點東西。
> "We soon reached the cave, and finding that the owner was not at home we examined all that it contained; we saw vessels brimful of whey, and racks loaded with cheeses: the yards also were full of lambs and kids. My men implored me to let them steal some cheeses, drive off some of the lambs and kids, and sail away, but I would not, for I hoped the owner might give me something.

〔U0287 quote〕
「我們在洞裡生了火，拿一些乳酪祭了神，其餘的自己吃了，等著主人回來。他回來時，我們才發現他是個巨大的怪物，與其說像人，不如說像高山上一座聳入天際的孤峰。他扛回一大捆柴火，往地上一扔，聲響之大，嚇得我們躲了起來。他把所有母山羊和母綿羊趕進洞裡，公羊則留在外頭；然後用一塊巨石堵住洞口，那石頭就是二十二輛大車也拉不動。他有條不紊地擠完山羊和綿羊的奶，讓每頭母羊去餵自己的小羊［因為小羊整天都留在圈裡］；接著他喝了一些奶，另外留一部分當晚飯。不久他生起火來，瞧見了我們，便問我們是什麼人。
> "We lit a fire in the cave, sacrificed some of the cheeses to the gods, and ate others ourselves, waiting till the owner should return. When he came we found him to be a huge monster, more like a peak standing out against the sky on some high mountain than a human being. He brought in with him a great bundle of firewood, which he flung down upon the floor with such a noise that we were scared and hid ourselves. He drove all his female goats and ewes into the cave, but left the males outside; and then he closed the door with a huge stone which not even two and twenty waggons could carry. He milked his goats and ewes all orderly, and gave each one her own young [for these had been left in the yards all day]; then he drank some of the milk, and put part by for his supper. Presently he lit his fire and caught sight of us, whereon he asked us who we were.

〔U0288 quote〕
「我告訴他，我們是從特洛伊回家的人，求他看在上天的分上別傷害我們；可我一答完他的問話，他就一把抓起我的兩個部下，往地上一摔，生吞下肚，血肉、骨頭、內臟一樣不剩，活像荒野裡一頭兇殘的獅子。然後他躺在洞底的地上睡著了；我本該悄悄爬到他身邊，趁他熟睡一劍刺進他的心臟，只是我知道，真這麼做，我們就永遠推不開那塊巨石了。於是我們只能等待天亮。

Then he lay down on the ground of the cave and went to sleep: on which I should have crept up to him and plunged my sword into his heart while he was sleeping had I not known that if I did we should never be able to shift the stone. 〔U0289 quote〕
「天一亮，怪物又生起火，有條不紊地擠了母羊的奶，讓每頭母羊去餵自己的小羊。接著他又抓起我的兩個部下，一吃完，就把巨石從洞口滾開，把羊群趕出去，再把石頭放回原處。不過他在洞裡留下了一根又長又大的橄欖木；他走後，我和部下動手把木頭的一端削尖，藏進洞裡堆得厚厚的羊糞底下。傍晚他回來，擠了母羊的奶，又吃了兩個人；於是我帶著馬容送我的那皮囊奇妙的酒走上前去，給他斟了滿滿一碗。他又要了一碗，接著再要一碗，我都一一給了他；他喜出望外，便問我叫什麼名字，我說我叫『無人』。
> "When day broke the monster again lit his fire, milked his ewes all orderly, and gave each one her own young. He had, however, left a large and long piece of olive wood in the cave, and when he had gone I and my men sharpened this at one end, and hid it in the sheep dung of which there was much in the cave. In the evening he returned, milked his ewes, and ate two more men; whereon I went up to him with the skin of wondrous wine that Maron had given me and gave him a bowl full of it. He asked for another, and then another, so I gave them to him, and he was so much delighted that he enquired my name and I said it was Noman.

〔U0290 quote〕
「酒力開始發作，不一會兒他就爛醉如泥，倒在地上。於是我和部下把那根橄欖木的尖端放進火裡，燒得通紅，然後戳進這惡棍的眼睛，像轉鑽子似的轉個不停。過了一會兒，他把木頭拔出來，甩到一旁，開始大聲呼喊鄰居來幫忙。他們來了之後說：『你怎麼啦？誰在害你？』他回答：『無人在害我。』他們便說他一定是病了，最好向他的父親波賽頓祈禱；說完就走了，而我則為自己的計謀得逞暗自發笑。
> "The wine now began to take effect, and in a short time he fell dead drunk upon the ground. After a while he plucked it out, flung it from him, and began crying to his neighbours for help. When they came, they said, 'What ails you? Who is harming you?' and he answered, 'No man is harming me.' If you leave these unharmed, after much trouble you will yet reach Ithaca; but if you harm them, you will lose your men, and though you may get home after all, it will be late, [on another man's ship,[註18] and you will find your house full of riotous men who are wasting your substance and wooing your wife.

> "'When you have got back you will indeed kill these men either by treachery or in fair fight, and you must then take an oar, which you must carry till you have reached a people who know nothing about the sea and do not mix salt with their bread. These people have never heard of ships, nor of oars that are the wings with which ships fly; I will tell you how you may know them; you will meet a man by the way who will ask you whether it is a winnowing shovel that you have got upon your shoulder; when you hear this you must fix your oar in the ground, and offer sacrifice to Neptune, a ram, a bull and a boar; then go home again, and offer hecatombs to the gods that dwell in heaven.[註19] As for your own end, death shall come to you very gently from the sea, and shall take you when you are full of years and peace of mind, and your people shall bless you.']

〔U0337 quote〕

> "'You will then arrive at the Thrinacian island, where you will see the cattle of the Sun (and also his sheep) in charge of the two nymphs Lampetie and Phaëthusa. If you leave these flocks unharmed, after much trouble you will yet reach Ithaca; but if you harm them, you will lose your men, and though you may get home after all, it will be late.'

〔U0338 quote〕
「她說到這裡為止；天一破曉，我們便乘著喀耳刻送來的順風出發了。我把兩位塞壬的事告訴了部下，可話才剛說完，我們就已到了那座島，海面隨即一片死寂，風平浪靜。我揉軟了蠟，塞住部下的耳朵；他們把我綁在桅杆的橫木上；我聽見了塞壬的歌聲，我掙扎著想脫身時，他們又把我綁得更緊。就這樣，我們駛過了那座島。
> "Here she ended, and at break of day we set out, with a fair wind which Circe sent us. I then told my men about the two Sirens, but had hardly done so before we were at the island itself, whereon it fell a dead calm. I kneaded wax and stopped the men's ears; they bound me to a cross-plank on the mast; I heard the Sirens sing, and when I struggled to free myself they bound me still tighter. So we passed the island by.

〔U0339 quote〕
「此後不久，我看見前方煙霧瀰漫、巨浪翻騰，又聽見一陣沉悶的轟隆聲。海面一片騷亂，部下們嚇得鬆開了槳，直到我給他們打氣，叫他們使出全力划，並吩咐舵手緊貼斯庫拉那一側行駛。但我對斯庫拉的事隻字未提，只是不停地睜大眼睛，把她的岩石從上到下掃視一遍又一遍，看能不能瞥見她。
> "Shortly after this I saw smoke and a great wave ahead, and heard a dull thumping sound. The sea was in an uproar, and my men were so frightened that they loosed hold of their oars, till I put heart into them, bade them row their hardest, and told the steersman to hug the Scylla side. But I said nothing about Scylla, though I kept straining my eyes all over her rock to see if I could espy her.

〔U0340 quote〕
「於是我們就這樣夾在中間：一邊是斯庫拉，另一邊是可怕的卡律布狄斯。我們看見海水像鍋裡的水一樣翻滾沸騰，海底的黑泥也露了出來，周圍一堵漩渦水牆急速奔旋。部下們看到這可怕的景象，正嚇得臉色發白時，斯庫拉伸出她的長脖子，猛撲下來，抓走了其中六個人。我看見他們可憐的手腳在她把他們高高舉起時在空中掙扎，聽見他們最後一次絕望地喊著我的名字。這是我在所有航程中見過最恐怖的景象。
> "So there we were, with Scylla on the one hand and dread Charybdis on the other. We could see the sea seething as in a cauldron, and the black ooze at the bottom with a wall of whirling waters careering round it. While my men were pale with fear at this awful sight, Scylla shot out her long necks and swooped down on six of them. I could see their poor hands and feet struggling in the air as she bore them aloft, and hear them call out my name in one last despairing cry. This was the most horrid sight that I saw in all my voyages.

〔U0341 quote〕
「通過了那些懸崖，[註22]也通過了斯庫拉和卡律布狄斯之後，我們來到特里納基亞島；我在船上就聽得見牛的哞叫和羊的咩叫。這時我想起忒瑞西阿斯和喀耳刻給我的警告，便命令部下遠遠避開那座島。但歐律洛科斯出言不遜，在眾人之間煽動不滿，我只得讓步，讓他們上岸過夜，但先要他們鄭重發誓，絕不傷害那些牛。於是我們在海灘上一條溪流附近紮營。
> "Having passed the cliffs,[註22] and Scylla and Charybdis, we came to the Thrinacian island, and from my ship I could hear the cattle lowing, and the sheep bleating. Then, remembering the warning that Tiresias and Circe had given me, I bade my men give the island a wide berth. But Eurylochus was insolent, and sowed disaffection among them, so that I was forced to yield and let them land for the night, after making them swear most solemnly that they would do the cattle no harm. We camped, therefore, on the beach near a stream.

〔U0342 quote〕
「可是到了夜裡第三更，颳起了一場大風暴；到了早上，我們把船拖上岸，停放在一個大洞穴裡，那是海中仙女們聚會、跳舞的地方。接著我把部下召集起來，再一次警告他們。
> "But in the third watch of the night there came up a great gale, and in the morning we drew our ship ashore and left her in a large cave wherein the sea nymphs meet and hold their dances. I then called my men together, and again warned them.

〔U0343 quote〕
「整整一個月都颳著強勁的南風，只是偶爾轉成東風，除了南風與東風之外，再沒有別的風。只要喀耳刻給我們的穀物和酒還沒吃完，部下們就一直信守諾言；但過了一段時間，他們開始飽受飢餓之苦，我便獨自走到一旁，祈求上天憐憫我們。我洗淨雙手，向上天祈禱，禱告完畢，便沉沉睡去。
> "It blew a gale from the South for a whole month, except when the wind shifted to the East, and there was no other wind save only South and East. As long as the corn and wine which Circe had given us held out, my men kept their word, but after a time they began to feel the pangs of hunger, and I went apart to pray heaven to take compassion upon us. I washed my hands and prayed, and when I had done so, I fell asleep.

〔U0344 quote〕
「就在這時，歐律洛科斯唆使我的部下違抗我，他們趕來幾頭牛宰了。我醒來，快走回船邊時，開始聞到烤肉的味道，心裡完全明白發生了什麼事。
> "Meanwhile Eurylochus set my men on to disobey me, and they drove in some of the cattle and killed them. When I woke, and had got nearly back to the ship, I began to smell roast meat and knew full well what had happened.

〔U0345 quote〕
「仙女蘭佩提亞立刻去把我的部下幹的事告訴赫利俄斯。他大發雷霆，威脅宙斯說，若不替他報仇，他就再也不在天上發光，要下到冥界，把光照給死人看。『一整天，』他說，『無論我是升上天空還是落下，沒有什麼比看那些牛更讓我喜愛的了。』
> "The nymph Lampetie went immediately and told the Sun what my men had done. He was furious, and threatened Jove that if he was not revenged he would never shine in heaven again but would go down and give his light among the dead. 'All day long,' said he, 'whether I was going up heaven or down, there was nothing I so dearly loved to look upon as those cattle.'

〔U0346 quote〕
「宙斯告訴他，等我們的船一遠離陸地，他就把它擊毀，赫利俄斯便不再多說。我之所以知道這一切，是卡呂普索告訴我的，而她是從赫耳墨斯那裡聽來的。
> "Jove told him he would wreck our ship as soon as it was well away from land, and the Sun said no more. I know all this because Calypso told me, and she had it from Mercury.

〔U0347 quote〕
「部下們大吃了六天──期間出現了最可怕的凶兆，嚇壞了他們：牛皮不停地四處走動，烤著的肉塊竟發出哞叫聲。第七天，風停了，我們離開那座島；但一看不見陸地，就突然颳起一陣暴風，宙斯趁機用雷電擊中我們的船，把它打得粉碎。我的部下全都淹死了，我本來也會淹死，若不是我把桅杆（我發現它在水上漂著）和船的龍骨綁在一起，給自己做了一個木筏。
> "My men feasted six days—alarmed by the most awful prodigies; for the skins of the cattle kept walking about, and the joints of meat lowed while they were being roasted. On the seventh day the wind dropped and we got away from the island, but as soon as we were out of sight of land a sudden squall sprang up, during which Jove struck our ship with his thunderbolts and broke it up. All my men were drowned, and so too should I have been, had I not made myself a raft by lashing the mast (which I found floating about) and the ship's keel together.

〔U0348 quote〕
［「暴風來時颳的是西風，這時轉成了南風，颳了一整夜，到了早上，我又回到了斯庫拉和卡律布狄斯之間。我的木筏被捲進漩渦，但我緊緊抓住那棵無花果樹的枝幹，苦苦撐了好久好久，那段時間裡，我心焦如焚，就像一位地方法官想回家吃晚飯，卻被一樁樁麻煩的案子困在法庭上一樣。但過了一段時間，我的木筏又從漩渦裡冒了出來，等它來到我正下方時，我便跳下去落在上面，隨即被帶出了漩渦。幸好宙斯沒讓斯庫拉看見我。］[註23]
> ["The wind, which during the squall came from the West, now changed to the South, and blew all night, so that by morning I was back between Scylla and Charybdis again. My raft got carried down the whirlpool, but I clung on to the boughs of the fig tree, for a weary weary while, during which I felt as impatient as a magistrate who is detained in court by troublesome cases when he wants to get home to dinner. But in the course of time my raft worked its way out again, and when it was underneath me I dropped on to it and was carried out of the pool. Happily for me Jove did not let Scylla see me.][註23]

「此後我在海上漂流了九天，被帶到卡呂普索所在的俄古癸厄島。這件事我昨天已經跟你們講過，就不再重複了，因為我最討厭把同一件事講兩遍。」
