〔U0350 quote〕
*費埃克斯人送奧德修斯返回伊薩卡*。
> *Ulysses is taken back to Ithaca by the Phæacians*.

〔U0351 quote〕
奧德修斯如此說罷，阿爾基諾俄斯立即提議再多送他一些禮物。不過，他說，這些禮物的開銷應當向全體公眾攤派或課徵地方稅來支付。賓客們都表示贊同，然後各自回家睡覺。
> Thus did Ulysses speak, and Alcinous immediately proposed that they should make him still further presents. The expence, however, of these, he said, should be borne by a levy or rate upon the public at large. The guests assented, and then went home to bed.

〔U0352 quote〕
第二天早上，他們把送的五金器物搬到船上，阿爾基諾俄斯親自看著把東西放妥，免得礙著划槳的人。隨後阿爾基諾俄斯家裡又舉行了第二場宴會，但奧德修斯一直望著太陽，盼著它快快落下，好讓他動身上路。最後他站起來向費埃克斯人致詞；感謝他們之後，他在結尾說，希望自己回家時，會看到妻子在親友之間過著平安寧靜的日子，[註24]也希望費埃克斯人繼續讓他們的妻兒稱心如意。他也向阿瑞忒道別，祝她和她的兒女、她的人民以及阿爾基諾俄斯王一起幸福美滿。
> Next morning they brought their presents of hardware down to the ship, and Alcinous saw them so stowed that they should not incommode the rowers. There was then a second banquet at Alcinous's house, but Ulysses kept looking at the sun all the time, longing for it to set that he might start on his way. At last he rose and addressed the Phæacians; after thanking them, he concluded by saying that he hoped he should find his wife on his return living among her friends in peace and quietness,[註24] and that the Phæacians would continue to give satisfaction to their wives and children. He also bade farewell to Arete, and wished her all happiness with her children, her people, and with King Alcinous.

〔U0353 quote〕
奧德修斯到了船上，有人替他鋪好一張毯子和一面帆布，他躺下來，立刻沉沉睡去──睡得之沉，宛如死去一般。船一路疾駛，比獵鷹飛得還快，天一破曉，他們便抵達了伊薩卡。
> When Ulysses reached the ship, a rug and sail were spread for him, on which he lay down, and immediately fell into a deep sleep—so deep as to resemble death itself. The ship sped on her way faster than a falcon's flight and with the break of day they reached Ithaca.

〔U0354 quote〕
話說伊薩卡有一處避風的港灣，船停在裡面連纜繩都不必繫。港灣盡頭有一棵大橄欖樹，樹旁有一個洞穴，是那伊阿得斯仙女的聖地；在那裡你可以看到她們的石杯和石製雙耳瓶，還有她們用來織海紫色袍子的石頭織機──十分奇特。野蜂也在洞裡築巢。洞裡終年有水，洞有兩個入口，一個朝北，凡人可以由此下到洞裡；另一個朝南，但人不能從那裡進去──那是眾神走的路。
> Now in Ithaca there is a sheltered harbour in which a ship can ride without being even moored. At the head of this there is a large olive tree, near which there is a cave sacred to the Naiads, where you may find their cups and amphoræ of stone, and the stone looms whereon they weave their robes of sea-purple—very curious. The wild bees, too, build their nests in it. There is water in it all the year round, and it has two entrances, one looking North, by which mortals can go down into the cave, and the other towards the South, but men cannot enter by it—it is the way taken by gods.

〔U0355 quote〕
水手們熟悉這個港口，便把船駛了進去。他們划得太用力，船身有一半衝上了岸；他們下船後，把仍躺在毯子和帆布上熟睡的奧德修斯抬下船，放在地上。他們又把費埃克斯人送他的所有禮物放在他旁邊；他們把禮物擱在橄欖樹的樹根旁，稍稍偏離小路，免得過路人偷走，然後便返回斯刻里亞。
> The sailors knew this harbour, and took the ship into it. They were rowing so hard that they ran half her length on to the shore, and when they had got out of her they took Ulysses off, still fast asleep on his rug and sail, and laid him down on the ground. Hard by him they also laid all the presents the Phæacians had made him; they left them by the roots of the olive tree, a little out of the path, that no passer by might steal them, and then went back to Scheria.

〔U0356 quote〕
這時波賽頓看到了費埃克斯人的所作所為，便去找宙斯商量該如何報復。商量的結果是：他到斯刻里亞去，趁那艘船正要入港時把它變成石頭，讓它在海裡生根。他就這麼做了，費埃克斯人便說：「唉呀，是誰讓那艘船正要進港就在海裡生了根？我們一分鐘前還看得見整艘船呢。」
> Neptune now saw what the Phæacians had done, and went to consult Jove how he should be revenged. It was arranged that he should go to Scheria, turn the ship into stone just as it was coming into port, and root it in the sea. So he did this, and the Phæacians said, "Alack, who has rooted the ship in the sea just as it was coming in? We could see all of it a minute ago."

〔U0357 quote〕
這時阿爾基諾俄斯告訴他們，波賽頓很久以前就威脅過，要在某艘費埃克斯船送客返航時這樣對付它，還要用一座高山埋掉他們的城市，作為他們太隨便替人送行的懲罰。因此費埃克斯人準備了隆重的祭品獻給波賽頓，祈求他手下留情。
> Then Alcinous told them how Neptune had long ago threatened to do this to some Phæacian ship on its return from giving an escort, and also to bury their city under a high mountain as a punishment for giving escorts so freely. The Phæacians, therefore, made ready great sacrifices to Neptune, that he might have mercy upon them.

〔U0358 quote〕
他們正這樣圍著神的祭壇站著的時候，奧德修斯在自己的故鄉醒來，但他離家太久，竟認不出這是哪裡。而且雅典娜也在他四周灑下一片濃霧，好讓她向他說明情況時，他不被人看見；因為她不想讓他的妻子或任何人知道他回來了，要等他向求婚者們報了仇再說。因此她讓一切在他眼中都顯得陌生──那些筆直的長路、停滿船隻的港灣、陡峭的懸崖，還有樹木。
> While they were thus standing round the altar of the god, Ulysses woke in his own land, but he had been away so long that he did not know it. Minerva, too, had shed a thick mist round him so that he might remain unseen while she told him how things were going on; for she did not want his wife or anyone else to know of his return until he had taken his revenge upon the suitors. Therefore she made everything look strange to him—the long straight paths, the harbours with their shipping, the steep precipices, and the trees.

〔U0359 quote〕
奧德修斯這時站起身來，納悶自己身在何處。他不相信自己是在伊薩卡，狠狠埋怨費埃克斯人把他送錯了地方。接著他清點他們送他的所有三腳鼎、大鍋、黃金和衣物，看看有沒有被偷；結果樣樣都在，他卻不知該拿這些東西怎麼辦，一籌莫展。他正這樣猶疑不決時，雅典娜化身成一個年輕牧人朝他走來，於是他問她這是什麼地方，她回答說這裡是伊薩卡。
> Ulysses now stood up and wondered where he was. He did not believe he was in Ithaca and complained bitterly of the Phæacians for having brought him wrong. Then he counted all the tripods, cauldrons, gold, and raiment, that they had given him, to see if he had been robbed; but everything was there, and he was in dismay as to what he should do with them. As he was thus in doubt Minerva came up to him disguised as a young shepherd, so he asked her what country he was in, and she answered that he was in Ithaca.

〔U0360 quote〕
奧德修斯說，他聽說過有這麼一個地方；他對雅典娜編了一大套謊話，說自己怎麼會來到她看見他的這個地方；女神聽了，便變成一位美麗、端莊又聰慧的女子，笑他竟認不出她來。奧德修斯回答說，她可不是個容易認出來的人，因為她老是變換模樣。再說，她在特洛伊時雖然待他很好，此後卻一直對他撒手不管，直到把他帶進費埃克斯人的城市為止。「別再騙我了，」他說，「告訴我，這裡到底是不是伊薩卡。」
> Ulysses said he had heard that there was such a place; he told Minerva a long lying story as to how he had come to be where she saw him, and on this the goddess assumed the form of a woman, fair, stately, and wise, and laughed at him for not knowing her. Ulysses answered that she was not an easy person to recognise for she was continually changing her appearance. Moreover, though she had been very good to him at Troy, she had left him in the lurch ever since, until she had taken him into the city of the Phæacians. "Do not," he said, "deceive me any further, but tell me whether or no this is really Ithaca."

〔U0361 quote〕
「你總是這麼狡猾多疑，」女神回答，「正因如此，我才狠不下心丟下你不管。換了別人，遠航歸來，一定馬上回家去看妻子兒女；你卻似乎一點也不想知道他們的事，只想著試探妻子是否忠貞。至於說我對你撒手不管，我其實一直都知道你最後會平安到家，而且我也不想跟我的叔父波賽頓鬧翻。現在我就向你證明這裡是伊薩卡──這裡是海之老人福爾庫斯的港灣，港灣盡頭就是那棵大橄欖樹；旁邊就是那伊阿得斯仙女的聖洞；這裡，還有那座穹頂洞窟，你曾在裡面向仙女們獻過許多次百牲祭；而這就是林木蓊鬱的涅里通山。」
> "You are always cunning and suspicious," replied the goddess, "and that is why I cannot find it in my heart to leave you. Any one else on returning from a long voyage would at once have gone up to his house to see his wife and children, but you do not seem to care about knowing anything about them, and only think of testing your wife's fidelity. As for my having left you in the lurch, I knew all the time that you would get home safely in the end, and I did not want to quarrel with my uncle Neptune. I will now prove to you that you are in Ithaca—Here is the harbour of the old merman Phorcys, with the large olive tree at the head of it; near it is the cave which is sacred to the Naiads; here, again, is the overarching cavern in which you have sacrificed many a hecatomb to the nymphs, and this is the wooded mountain of Neritum."

〔U0362 quote〕
女神於是驅散濃霧，讓景物顯現出來。奧德修斯這才信服，雅典娜便幫他把費埃克斯人送的財寶藏進洞裡。辦完這件事，她要奧德修斯好好想想該怎樣殺掉那些邪惡的求婚者。「他們在你家裡作威作福，」她說，「已經三年了，[註25]一直追求你的妻子，送她求婚的禮物；而她呢，可憐的女人，雖然對他們虛與委蛇，還派人捎信給每一個人，讓人人都抱著希望，其實卻為了你沉浸在最深的悲痛之中，她說的話沒有一句是真心的。」
> The goddess then dispersed the mist and let the prospect be seen. Ulysses was thus convinced, and Minerva helped him to hide the treasure which the Phæacians had given him, by concealing it in the cave. Having done this she bade Ulysses consider how he should kill the wicked suitors. "They have been lording it," she said, "in your house this three years,[註25] paying court to your wife and making her gifts of wooing, while she, poor woman, though she flatters them, and holds out hopes to every man of them by sending him messages, is really plunged in the deepest grief on your account, and does not mean a word of what she says."

〔U0363 quote〕
「天哪，」奧德修斯回答，「我差一點就落得和阿伽門農一樣的下場，真是好險。女神啊，請站在我這邊，教我該怎樣報仇。」
> "Great heavens," replied Ulysses, "what a narrow escape I have had from meeting the fate of Agamemnon. Stand by me, goddess, and advise me how I shall be revenged."

〔U0364 quote〕
「我會把你扮成一個可憐的老乞丐，」雅典娜說，「讓誰也認不出你。等我扮好了，你就去找你的牧豬人，他一向對你和你一家忠心耿耿。你會在阿瑞圖薩泉旁、那塊名叫『渡鴉』的岩石（科拉科斯岩）附近找到他和他的豬。這段時間，我要去斯巴達接忒勒馬科斯回來，他到那裡去打聽你的消息了。」
> "I will disguise you," said Minerva, "as a miserable old beggar so that no one shall know you. "When I have done so, go to your swineherd, who has been always loyal to you and yours. You will find him with his pigs by the fountain Arethusa near the rock that is called Raven. Meantime I will go to Sparta and fetch Telemachus, who is gone thither to try and get news of you."

〔U0365 quote〕
「可是，」奧德修斯回答，「你既然什麼都知道，為什麼不告訴他呢？」
> "But why," Ulysses answered, "did you not tell him, for you knew all about it?"

〔U0366 quote〕
「別替他擔心，」她回答，「他在那裡過得極其富足。是我派他去的，好讓他因這趟遠行博得好名聲。」
> "Do not be uneasy about him," she answered, "he is in the midst of great abundance. I sent him, that he might get himself a good name by having gone."

〔U0367 quote〕
於是雅典娜把奧德修斯變得讓人怎麼也認不出來，兩人隨即分手──她前往斯巴達，奧德修斯則前往牧豬人的住處。
> Minerva then disguised Ulysses beyond all possible recognition, and the two separated—she going to Sparta, and Ulysses to the abode of his swineherd.

〔U0368 quote〕
*奧德修斯在歐邁俄斯的小屋*。
> *Ulysses in the hut of Eumæus*.

〔U0369 quote〕
奧德修斯沿著一條陡峭的小路，從港口穿過森林，翻過山頂，一直走到歐邁俄斯的小屋。歐邁俄斯是他最勤儉持家的僕人，主人不在的這些年，他蓋了許多座很好的院欄和豬圈。
> Ulysses followed a steep path that led from the harbour through the forest and over the top of the mountain, till he reached the hut of Eumæus, who was the most thrifty servant he had, and had built a number of fine yards and pigstyes during his master's absence.

〔U0370 quote〕
奧德修斯看見他坐在小屋門口；那小屋蓋在高處，從老遠就能看見。他身邊圍著四條兇猛的狗，他自己則正在裁切皮革，替自己做一雙涼鞋。
> Ulysses found him sitting at the door of his hut, which had been built high up in a place that could been seen from far; he had his four fierce dogs about him, and was cutting himself out a pair of sandal shoes.

〔U0371 quote〕
群狗朝奧德修斯撲去，歐邁俄斯費盡力氣才把牠們喝住。「牠們差點，」他說，「就要了你的命，那我可就惹上麻煩了；我已經夠傷心了，因為失去了世上最好的主人，我沒有一刻不為此哀嘆。不過請進來吧，吃點東西，然後跟我說說你的身世。」
> The dogs flew at Ulysses, and it was all Eumæus could do to check them; "They were like," said he, "to have made an end of you, which would have got me into a scrape, and I am in sorrow enough already through the loss, which I deplore without ceasing, of the best of masters. But come in, have something to eat, and then tell me your story."

〔U0372 quote〕
說罷，他把奧德修斯領進屋裡，在地上鋪了些柴枝，再在上面攤開一張山羊皮，讓奧德修斯躺臥。「我能為你做的實在不多，」他說，「僕人頭上若壓著年輕的主子，日子總是提心吊膽，我眼下正是這樣；因為我那位好心的老主人跟著阿伽門農去了特洛伊，我這輩子再也見不到他了。」
> On this he brought him inside, threw some brushwood on the floor, and spread a goat's skin over it for Ulysses to lie on. "I cannot do much for you," he said; "servants go in fear when they have young lords over them, as I now have, for my good old master went to Troy with Agamemnon and I shall never see him again."

〔U0373 quote〕
接著他出門宰了兩頭乳豬，燎去豬毛，切成小塊，把肉串在叉子上擱到炭火餘燼上烤，烤好了便連叉帶肉、熱騰騰地端給奧德修斯，奧德修斯就在肉上撒了麵粉。「吃吧，」牧豬人說，「這是僕人吃的豬肉；長足了的肥豬都得送下去給求婚者吃。」隨後他又細細說明奧德修斯有多麼富有。
> He then went out and killed two sucking pigs, singed them, cut them up, put the pieces of meat on skewers to roast on the embers, and brought them smoking hot, skewers and all, to Ulysses, who floured them. "Eat," said the swineherd, "a dish of servant's pork; the fuller grown meat has to go down to the suitors." He then explained how rich Ulysses was.

〔U0374 quote〕
「請問，」奧德修斯說，「你這位高貴的主人究竟是誰？你說他死在特洛伊了；果真如此，我或許能給你帶來一點他的消息。」
> "And who, pray," said Ulysses, "was this noble master of yours? You say that he fell at Troy, and in that case I might be able to give you news of him."

〔U0375 quote〕
「那可不成，」歐邁俄斯答道，「老是有人上門來奉承我那可憐的女主人，給她虛假的希望，可他們全都是騙子。我的主人奧德修斯早已死了，再也回不來，像他那樣的人，我再也見不到第二個。我連提起他的名字都受不了。」
> "That," answered Eumæus, "may not be: people are always coming and flattering my poor mistress with false hopes, but they are all liars. My master Ulysses is dead and gone, and I shall never see another like him. I cannot bear even to mention his name."

〔U0376 quote〕
「朋友，」奧德修斯回答，「別這麼不肯相信。我憑著我如今來到的這座爐灶起誓：不出這個月，奧德修斯就會回來。他若回來了，你就得給我一件襯衫和一件斗篷；在那之前，我什麼也不收。」
> "My friend," replied Ulysses, "do not be too hard of belief. I swear by this hearth to which I am now come, that Ulysses will return before the present month is over. If he comes you shall give me a shirt and cloak, but I will take nothing till then."

〔U0377 quote〕
「朋友，」歐邁俄斯說，「別再多說一個字了。你這件襯衫和斗篷是永遠拿不到的。而且如今我替他的兒子忒勒馬科斯擔心，就跟從前替奧德修斯本人擔心一樣；因為他到皮洛斯去了，求婚者正埋伏著，等他回程時下手。不過眼下我們先別談他了；倒是說說你自己吧──你是誰，又是怎麼到這裡來的。」
> "My friend," said Eumæus, "say not another word. You will never get your shirt and cloak. Now, moreover, I am as anxious about his son Telemachus as I have been about Ulysses himself; for he is gone to Pylos, and the suitors are lying in wait for him on his return. Let us, however, say no more about him now; tell me, rather, about yourself who you are, and how you came here."

〔U0378 quote〕
於是奧德修斯對他編了一篇關於自己在克里特冒險的長長謊話：說他如何被迫與伊多梅紐斯一同統率克里特的軍隊遠征特洛伊；如何襲擊埃及，被俘，發了財，後來又如何被人誘騙前往利比亞；又說在駛往那裡的途中，船離開克里特之後遇難，他被沖上忒斯普洛提亞的海岸。「就是在那裡，」他接著說，「我從費冬王那裡聽到了奧德修斯的消息。國王天天盼著他從多多納回來──他到那裡求問神諭去了；國王告訴我，奧德修斯馬上就要回伊薩卡，可是恰好有一艘船要開往杜利基昂島，國王便在奧德修斯從多多納回來之前把我送上了那艘船。船上的水手決意把我賣作奴隸，把我綁了起來；可他們在伊薩卡的海岸登陸，我便趁機溜掉，一路摸到了你的小屋。」
> Then Ulysses told him a long lying story about his adventures in Crete: how he was compelled to go to Troy in joint command with Idomeneus over the Cretan forces; how he made a descent on Egypt, got taken prisoner, acquired wealth, and afterwards was inveigled into going to Libya; how on the voyage thither, after leaving Crete, the ship was wrecked and he was cast on the coast of Thesprotia. "Here it was," he continued, "that I heard of Ulysses from King Pheidon, who was expecting him back daily from Dodona, where he had been to consult the oracle; he told me Ulysses was to return to Ithaca immediately, but there was a ship bound for Dulichium, and the king sent me on board it before Ulysses returned from Dodona. The sailors on this ship resolved to sell me as a slave, and bound me; but they landed on the coast of Ithaca, where I gave them the slip, and found my way to your hut."

〔U0379 quote〕
「可憐的人，」牧豬人答道，「可是關於奧德修斯的事，你永遠沒法讓我相信。你何必對我說這種謊話呢？這類故事我聽得太多了，再也不會相信了。」
> "Poor man," answered the swineherd, "but you will never get me to believe about Ulysses. Why should you tell me such lies? I have heard these stories too often, and will never believe them again."

〔U0380 quote〕
奧德修斯想再進一步說服他，可是毫無用處。不久，底下的牧豬奴們把出外覓食的豬群趕了回來，歐邁俄斯吩咐他們宰一頭最好的豬，準備晚飯，他們便照辦了。歐邁俄斯是個好人，不忘對眾神應盡的本分，所以豬一宰好，他就把一些豬鬃丟進火裡，向上天禱告，求奧德修斯歸來。隨後他們吃了晚飯，便上床睡覺。
> Ulysses tried still further to convince him, but it was no use, and presently the under swineherds came back with the pigs that had been out feeding, and Eumæus told them to kill the best pig they had, and get supper ready, which they accordingly did. He was a good man and mindful of his duties to the gods, so when the pig was killed he threw some of its bristles into the fire and prayed heaven for the return of Ulysses. Then they supped and went to bed.

〔U0381 quote〕
那一夜風雨交加，十分惡劣。大家躺下之後，奧德修斯怕自己會冷，又編了一個謊話，說他曾在特洛伊跟奧德修斯一起有過一番經歷，藉此哄得歐邁俄斯拿自己一件多餘的斗篷給他蓋上。然後牧豬人便出門去和豬群一起過夜──奧德修斯看見他雖然以為主人不在，仍把主人的家產照料得這樣周到，心裡十分高興。他先把劍斜挎在肩上，披上一件厚斗篷擋風；又拿了一張肥壯山羊的皮，還帶了一根標槍，以防有人或狗來襲擊。這樣裝備停當，他便到豬群歇宿的地方去睡；豬群就臥在一塊突出的岩石底下，那岩石替牠們擋住了北風。
> Now it was a wild rough night, and after they had lain down, Ulysses, fearing that he might be cold, told another lying story of an adventure he had had at Troy in company with Ulysses, by means of which Eumæus was induced to cover him over with a spare cloak of his own. Then the swineherd went out to pass the night with the pigs—and Ulysses was pleased at seeing how well he looked after his property, though he believed his master to be absent. First he slung his sword over his shoulders, and put on a thick cloak to keep out the wind; he also took the skin of a well-fed goat, and a javelin in case of attack from men or dogs. Thus equipped he went to his rest where the pigs were camped under an overhanging rock that sheltered them from the North Wind.

〔U0382 quote〕
*忒勒馬科斯從皮洛斯歸來，上岸後前往歐邁俄斯的小屋*。
> *Telemachus returns from Pylos, and on landing goes to the hut of Eumæus*.

〔U0383 quote〕
這時雅典娜前往拉刻代蒙，看見忒勒馬科斯和佩西斯特拉托斯正睡得香甜。她在夢中向忒勒馬科斯顯現，告訴他必須立刻返回伊薩卡，因為他母親就要嫁給歐律馬科斯了，說不定還會帶走他一部分家產。她還告訴他，求婚者正埋伏在伊薩卡與薩墨之間的海峽裡等他。她說，他一到伊薩卡，就要先下船，再讓船開往城裡，自己則到牧豬人的小屋去。「在那裡過夜，」她說，「到早上再派牧豬人去告訴潘妮洛碧，說你已經平安回來了。」
> Minerva now went to Lacedæmon and found Telemachus and Pisistratus fast asleep. She appeared to Telemachus in a dream, and told him that he was to return at once to Ithaca, for his mother was about to marry Eurymachus, and would probably go off with some of his property. She also told him how the suitors were lying in wait for him in the straits between Ithaca and Samos. She said that as soon as he reached Ithaca he was to leave the ship before sending it on to the town, and go to the swineherd's hut. "Sleep there," she said, "and in the morning send the swineherd to tell Penelope that you have returned safely."

〔U0384 quote〕
說完她便離去，忒勒馬科斯也醒了過來。他踢了佩西斯特拉托斯一腳，把他踢醒，說他們必須馬上動身。佩西斯特拉托斯回答說這辦不到：天還黑著，而且他們總得向墨涅拉俄斯辭行；只要忒勒馬科斯肯等一等，墨涅拉俄斯一定會送他們一份禮物。
> Then she went away and Telemachus woke up. He kicked Pisistratus to wake him, and said that they must start at once. Pisistratus answered that this was impossible; it was still dark, and they must say good bye to Menelaus, who, if Telemachus would only wait, would be sure to give them a present.

〔U0385 quote〕
天一亮，忒勒馬科斯看見墨涅拉俄斯已經起身走動，便匆匆套上襯衫、披上斗篷，對他說他們非走不可了。
> At break of day, seeing Menelaus up and about, Telemachus flung on his shirt and cloak, and told him that they must go.

〔U0386 quote〕
墨涅拉俄斯說他不會強留他們，不過無論是為了禮數還是為了節省，他們動身之前都得先吃點東西，還要收下那些正等著忒勒馬科斯笑納的禮物。「我會吩咐僕人，」他說，「把家裡現成的東西替你們備辦一些；你們若想到伯羅奔尼撒的幾座大城巡遊一番，我可以親自陪同。沒有人會讓我們空手而去，人人都會送我們一點東西。」
> Menelaus said he would not detain them, but on the score alike of propriety and economy, they must have something to eat before starting, and also receive the presents that were waiting Telemachus's acceptance. "I will tell the servants," said he, "to get something ready for you of what there may be in the house, and if you would like to make a tour of the principal cities of the Peloponese, I will conduct you. No one will send us away empty handed. Every one will give us something."

可是忒勒馬科斯說他必須立刻動身，因為他留在家裡的產業沒有人好好看守。

墨涅拉俄斯聽了這話，便吩咐妻子和僕人馬上準備午飯。住得不遠的埃特俄紐斯這時也來了，墨涅拉俄斯叫他生火做飯；他便照著吩咐去做。
> When Menelaus heard this he told his wife and servants to get dinner ready at once. Eteoneus, who lived at no great distance, now came up, and Menelaus told him to light the fire and begin cooking; and he did as he was told.

〔U0389 quote〕
接著墨涅拉俄斯和墨伽彭忒斯一同下到庫房，取來一只雙耳杯和一只銀調酒缸；海倫則拿來一件美得出奇的衣裳，那是她親手織成的。墨涅拉俄斯送上酒杯和調酒缸之後，海倫便說：「收下吧，我的孩子，就當是海倫親手送你的紀念品，讓你的新娘在婚禮那天穿上。在那之前，就讓你親愛的母親替你保管著。願你就此帶著輕快的心情上路。」
> Menelaus then went down into his store room together with Megapenthes, and brought up a double cup and a silver mixing bowl, while Helen fetched a dress of wondrous beauty, the work of her own hands. Menelaus presented the cup and mixing bowl, and then Helen said, "Take this, my son, as a keepsake from the hand of Helen, and let your bride wear it on her wedding day. Till then let your dear mother keep it for you. Thus may you go on your way with a light heart."

〔U0390 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯向她道謝；佩西斯特拉托斯把禮物收進馬車，眾人便坐下吃飯。埃特俄紐斯切肉，墨伽彭忒斯替大家斟酒。吃完之後，兩個年輕人便準備上路。
> Telemachus thanked her; Pisistratus stowed the presents in the chariot, and they all sat down to dinner. Eteoneus carved, and Megapenthes served round the wine. When they had done eating the two young men prepared to set out.

他們正要動身時，一隻老鷹從他們右手邊飛過，爪子裡抓著一隻從農家院子裡叼來的鵝。這個徵兆好得不能再好，人人看了都歡喜，佩西斯特拉托斯便說：「請問，墨涅拉俄斯王，這個徵兆是給我們的，還是給您自己的？」
> As they were on the point of starting, an eagle flew upon their right hand, with a goose in its talons which it had carried off from the farm yard. This omen was so good that every one was delighted to see it, and Pisistratus said, "Say, king Menelaus, is the omen for us or for yourself?"

〔U0392 quote〕
墨涅拉俄斯一時不知如何回答，海倫卻說，正如這隻老鷹從山上飛來抓走了鵝，奧德修斯也將歸來，向求婚者報仇。忒勒馬科斯說，但願真是如此；兩人隨即驅車而去。第二天他們到了皮洛斯，忒勒馬科斯央求佩西斯特拉托斯直接送他到船邊，因為怕他若到涅斯托爾家去，涅斯托爾會把他留住。
> Menelaus was in doubt how to answer, but Helen said that as the eagle had come from a mountain and seized the goose, so Ulysses should return and take vengeance on the suitors. Telemachus said he only hoped it might prove so, and the pair then drove on. They reached Pylos on the following day, and Telemachus urged Pisistratus to drive him straight to his ship, for fear Nestor should detain him if he went to his house.

〔U0393 quote〕
「我知道，」佩西斯特拉托斯說，「他有多固執。他要是曉得你在這裡，一定會親自跑到你的船上來，不帶你回去絕不罷休。不過他一定會大發脾氣。」說完他便驅車到船邊，忒勒馬科斯吩咐船員盡快開船。
> "I know," said Pisistratus, "how obstinate he is. He would come down to your ship, if he knew you were there, and would never go back without you. But he will be very angry." He then drove to the ship, and Telemachus told the crew to get her under way as fast as they could.

〔U0394 quote〕
他正一面張羅大小事務、一面向雅典娜獻祭時，來了一個墨蘭波斯族裔的人，因為殺了人，正從阿爾戈斯逃亡出來。他名叫忒俄克呂墨諾斯，出身一個古老而極受尊崇的家族，父親和祖父都是有名的先知與占卜者。他懇求忒勒馬科斯帶他到伊薩卡去，好讓他躲過正在追捕他的仇家。忒勒馬科斯答應了，把忒俄克呂墨諾斯帶上船，並把他的長矛放在甲板上。
> Now as he was attending to every thing and sacrificing to Minerva, there came to him a man of the race of Melampus who was flying from Argos because he had killed a man. His name was Theoclymenus and he came of an old and highly honourable family, his father and grandfather having been celebrated prophets and divines. He besought Telemachus to take him to Ithaca and thus save him from enemies who were in pursuit. Telemachus consented, took Theoclymenus on board, and laid his spear down on the deck.

〔U0395 quote〕
於是他們揚帆而去；第二天，他們駛進了那些「飛逝的群島」之間，[註26]忒勒馬科斯不禁揣想，自己究竟會被逮住，還是能逃脫。
> Then they sailed away, and next day they got among the 297 flying islands,[註26] whereon Telemachus wondered whether he should be taken or should escape.

〔U0396 quote〕
這段時間，奧德修斯一直和歐邁俄斯待在小屋裡。晚飯後，奧德修斯說他明天想到城裡去，看看求婚者肯不肯收他當差。歐邁俄斯立刻向他說明，這種念頭根本想都別想。「你不知道，」他說，「這些求婚者是什麼樣的人；他們的蠻橫無禮直衝上天；伺候他們的年輕人個個面目俊秀、頭髮梳得齊齊整整；桌子總是擦得乾乾淨淨，堆滿了吃不完的東西。那些求婚者會要了你的命的；所以就留在這裡吧，在這裡你礙不著誰，一直待到忒勒馬科斯從皮洛斯回來。」
> All this time Ulysses was in the hut with Eumæus, and after supper Ulysses said he should like to go down to the town next day, and see if the suitors would take him into their service. Eumæus at once explained to him that any such idea was out of the question. "You do not know," he said, "what men these suitors are; their insolence reaches heaven; the young men who wait on them have good looking faces and well kempt heads; the tables are always clean and loaded with abundance. The suitors would be the death of you; stay here, then, where you are in nobody's way, till Telemachus returns from Pylos."

〔U0397 quote〕
奧德修斯謝過歐邁俄斯的指點，接著便打聽他自己的父母是否還在世；他得知安提克蕾亞已經過世，[註27]拉厄爾特斯雖然還活著，卻巴不得跟她一起去。歐邁俄斯說，他是在他們家裡長大的，從前的日子好過得多；如今卻休想從女主人那裡聽到一句好話，因為求婚者把整個家攪得天翻地覆。「做僕人的，」他說，「喜歡和女主人說說話，聽她親口吩咐些事情；他們喜歡聽主人叫他們吃、叫他們喝，也喜歡回鄉下時准他們帶點東西走。這樣才能讓僕人高高興興、心滿意足。」
> Ulysses thanked Eumæus for his information, and then began to ask whether his father and mother were still living; he was told that Anticlea was dead,[註27] and that Laertes, though still alive, would be glad to follow her. Eumæus said he had been brought up in their service, and was better off formerly, for there was no getting a good word out of his mistress now, inasmuch as the suitors had turned the house upside down. "Servants," he said, "like to have a talk with their mistress and hear things from her own lips; they like being told to eat and drink, and being allowed to take something back with them into the country. This is what will keep servants cheerful and contented."

〔U0398 quote〕
經奧德修斯進一步追問，歐邁俄斯才說出自己小時候如何被一些腓尼基商人拐走：那些商人勾引了他的保姆（她也是腓尼基人），說動她跟他們一起走，還把他也帶上。
> On being further questioned by Ulysses, Eumæus told how he had been kidnapped as a child by some Phœnician traders who had seduced his nurse (also a Phœnician) and persuaded her to go away with them, and bring him with her.

〔U0399 quote〕
「我出生在，」他說，「敘里埃島，就在俄爾提基亞的對面，太陽轉向的地方。[註28]那島上人口不多，卻有兩座城，兩城之間瓜分了整片土地，我父親就是這兩城的國王。保姆拐走我之後沒幾天，我們還在海上航行，阿蒂蜜絲就把她射死了，她被拋進海裡，我卻被帶到伊薩卡，拉厄爾特斯把我買了下來。」
> "I was born," he said, "in the island of Syra over against Ortygia, where the sun turns.[註28] It is not populous, but contains two cities which occupy the whole land between them, and my father was king over them both. A few days after my nurse had kidnapped me, and while we were on our voyage, Diana killed her, and she was flung overboard, but I was taken to Ithaca where Laertes bought me."

〔U0400 quote〕
奧德修斯和歐邁俄斯大半夜都在彼此交談。天亮時，忒勒馬科斯的船員駛近陸地，收起船帆，划進港口。他們在那裡拋下錨石，把船繫牢，上了岸，就在海灘上吃午飯。吃完之後，忒勒馬科斯說：「現在你們把船開到城裡去；我要去照看我的農莊，傍晚再下城來。明天早上我會請大家飽餐一頓，酬謝你們的辛勞。」
> Ulysses and Eumæus spent the greater part of the night talking with one another, and at dawn Telemachus's crew drew near to land, furled their sails and rowed into the harbour. There they threw out their mooring stones, made their ship fast, landed, and ate their dinner on the shore. When they had done, Telemachus said, "Now take the ship on to the city; I will go to look after my farm and will come down in the evening. Tomorrow morning I will give you all a hearty meal to reward you for your trouble."

> "But what," said Theoclymenus, "is to become of me? To whose house am I to go?"

〔U0402 quote〕

> "At any other time," answered Telemachus, "I should take you to my own house, but you would not find it convenient now, for I shall not be there, and my mother will not see you. I shall therefore send you to the house of Eurymachus, who is one of the first men we have, and is most eager in his suit for my mother's hand."

〔U0410 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯一聽說奧德修斯是個船難落難的乞援人，心裡很不高興。「我年紀還太輕，」他說，「在家裡還撐不起局面；那我又能給這個人什麼像樣的照應呢？不過他既然投奔到你這裡，我就送他衣服和一切必需的食物；讓他留在你這裡吧。我不許他靠近那些求婚者，否則一定會惹出禍事來。」

"I am as yet too young," he said, "to be able to hold my own in the house; what sufficient support, then, can I give this man? Still, as he has come to you I will send him clothes and all necessary food; and let him stay with you; I will not have him go near the suitors, for harm would be sure to come of it."

> Ulysses expressed his surprise and indignation about the suitors, whereon Telemachus explained still further, and wound up by telling Eumæus to go at once and inform Penelope of his return. Eumæus asked if he should turn a little out of his way and tell Laertes, but Telemachus said he was not to do so. Penelope would send him word all in due course.

〔U0412 quote〕
歐邁俄斯一走，雅典娜便來到小屋。奧德修斯認出了她，狗也認出了她，因為牠們嗚嗚哀叫著躲到院子另一頭去了；忒勒馬科斯卻看不見她。她向奧德修斯示意要他出來；他出來之後，她告訴他現在該向兒子表明身分了──說著便用魔杖點了他一下，賜給他堂堂的儀表，又給他穿上華美的衣裳。
> As soon as Eumæus was gone Minerva came to the hut. Ulysses knew her, and so did the dogs, for they went whining away to the other end of the yards, but Telemachus did not see her. She made a sign to Ulysses that he was to come outside, and when he had done so she told him he was to reveal himself to his son—whereon she struck him with her wand, endowed him with a noble presence, and clothed him in goodly raiment.

〔U0413 quote〕
於是他回到小屋裡，告訴兒子自己是誰；可是忒勒馬科斯好半天都不肯相信。最後他終於信了，父子倆便互相摟住脖子痛哭，就像被人奪走了雛鳥的鷹或禿鷲。說真的，若不是忒勒馬科斯想起要問父親是搭什麼船來到伊薩卡、是哪裡的船員送他來的，他們恐怕會一直哭到日落。
> Then he went back into the hut and told his son who he was; but for a long while Telemachus would not believe. At last, however, when he was convinced, the pair flung their arms about each other's necks, and wept like eagles or vultures who had been robbed of their young. Indeed they would have wept till sundown had it not occurred to Telemachus to ask his father in what ship he had come to Ithaca, and whose crew it was that had brought him.

奧德修斯把費埃克斯人的事告訴他，又說自己如何把他們送的禮物藏了起來。「我現在來，」他說，「是聽從雅典娜的指點，要和你商量怎樣向求婚者報仇。所以我想知道他們一共有多少人，再盤算一下我們兩個能不能把他們殺光，還是必須從外面找幫手。」
> Ulysses told him about the Phæacians, and how he had hidden the presents they had given him. "I am now come," he said, "by Minerva's advice, to consult with you as to how we shall take vengeance on the suitors. I would therefore learn how many there are of them, and consider whether we two can kill them, or whether we must get help from outside."

〔U0415 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯說，不靠援手就想攻擊求婚者，那是毫無指望的。其中五十二人來自杜利基昂島，另帶六名隨從；二十四人來自薩墨；二十人來自扎金索斯；十二人來自伊薩卡。
> Telemachus said it was hopeless to think of attacking the suitors without assistance. There were fifty-two from Dulichium, with six followers, twenty-four from Same, twenty from Zacynthus, and twelve from Ithaca.

〔U0416 quote〕
奧德修斯說明他可以倚仗宙斯和雅典娜相助，認為這樣就夠了。「一旦真正打起來，」他說，「他們很快就會來助我們一臂之力。那麼，明天你就到城裡去，混在求婚者當中；讓牧豬人晚一點帶我去，把我扮成一個可憐兮兮的窮乞丐。不管你看見求婚者對我怎樣施暴，都別放在心上。你只管看著，什麼也別說，頂多客客氣氣地請他們別找我麻煩。還有，找個藉口把牆上的兵器盔甲撤走。就說煙燻壞了它們，又說人看見兵器有時會動手打起來，所以還是收起來為好──但要留下兩把劍、兩面盾、兩支長矛，好讓你我隨手抓起來用。」
> Ulysses explained that he could rely on help from Jove and from Minerva, and thought that this would be enough. "They will not be long in joining us," said he, "when the fight has begun in good earnest. Go, then, tomorrow to the town, and join the suitors; let the swineherd bring me later, disguised as a poor miserable beggar. Never mind how much violence you may see the suitors do me. Look on and say nothing, beyond asking them in a friendly way to leave me alone. Also, find some pretext for removing the armour from the walls. Say it is taking harm with the smoke, and that the sight of armour sometimes sets men fighting, so that it is better away—but leave two swords, shields and spears for you and me to snatch up."

〔U0417 quote〕
他們正這樣談著，那艘從皮洛斯載忒勒馬科斯回來的船已經抵達伊薩卡港，船員把墨涅拉俄斯送給他的禮物送到克呂提俄斯家。他們派一個人去告訴潘妮洛碧，說忒勒馬科斯在農莊上，先把船打發回城，好讓她放心。這個人和牧豬人在奧德修斯家裡碰了頭，那人當著女僕們的面說：「夫人，您的兒子從皮洛斯回來了。」歐邁俄斯卻站到她身邊，把她兒子吩咐的話全都告訴了她。然後他便回他的養豬場去了。
> As they were thus conversing, the ship that had brought Telemachus from Pylos reached the harbour of Ithaca, and the crew took the presents which Menelaus had given him to the house of Clytius. They sent a man to tell Penelope that Telemachus was at the farm, and had sent the ship on to allay her anxiety. This man and the swineherd met at the house of Ulysses, and the man said, in the presence of the maids, "Madam, your son is returned from Pylos;" but Eumæus stood by her, and told her all that her son had bidden him. Then he went back to his pig-farm.

〔U0418 quote〕
求婚者氣惱極了，正打算派一艘船去把埋伏等候忒勒馬科斯的人叫回來，求婚者之一安菲諾摩斯恰好轉過身，看見他們的船正駛進港來。他便笑著說：「不必派人了，他們已經回來了。」於是眾人都去迎船。安提諾俄斯說，既然他們防得那麼嚴，忒勒馬科斯還是逃過了，那就必須殺了他──要麼在農莊上下手，要麼趁他從那裡回來的路上。否則他會揭穿他們的陰謀，民眾就會起來反對他們。「如果，」他最後說，「你們不贊成這辦法，想留他一條命，那我們就不能再吃他的家產了，只能各自回家，從自己家裡去求婚，讓我們當中出價最高的、或是抽籤抽中的那一個娶潘妮洛碧。」
> The suitors were very angry, and were about sending a ship to fetch those who had been lying in wait for Telemachus, when Amphinomus, a suitor, happened to turn round and saw their ship coming into harbour. So he laughed and said, "We have no need to send, for the men are here." On this they all went to meet the ship, and Antinous said that as Telemachus had escaped them in spite of their great vigilance, they must kill him, either at the farm or as he was coming thence. Otherwise he would expose their plot, and they would have the people rise against them. "If," he concluded, "this does not please you, and you would let him live, we cannot eat up his estate any longer, but must go home, urge our suit each from his own house, and let the one among us take Penelope who will give most for her, or whose lot it may happen to be."

〔U0419 quote〕
這時，來自牧草豐美、盛產穀物的杜利基昂島的安菲諾摩斯開口了。他生性厚道，他的談吐比其他任何求婚者都更討潘妮洛碧喜歡。「要我同意殺忒勒馬科斯，」他說，「除非眾神表示贊許。殺害一個王族出身的人，可是一件嚴重的事。眾神若是准許，我就跟你們一起幹；否則我主張別碰這件事。」
> Amphinomus, who came from the well-grassed and grain-growing island of Dulichium, then spoke. He was a man of good natural disposition, and his conversation was more pleasing to Penelope than that of any of the other suitors; "I will only consent to kill Telemachus," said he, "if the gods give us their approval. It is a serious thing to kill a man who is of royal race. If they sanction it, I will be with you; otherwise I am for letting it alone."

〔U0420 quote〕
其餘的人都表示同意，便回到宅邸。可是墨東把這條新陰謀告訴了潘妮洛碧，她便帶著侍女們下樓，站在撐住迴廊屋頂的一根承重柱旁，嚴厲斥責安提諾俄斯忘恩負義，竟忘了當年奧德修斯曾救過他父親歐佩特斯的命。
> The rest assented, and they went back to the house. But Medon told Penelope of this new plot, so she went attended by her gentlewomen, stood by one of the bearing-posts that supported the roof of the cloister, and bitterly rebuked Antinous for his ingratitude in forgetting how Ulysses in old days had saved the life of his father Eupeithes.

〔U0421 quote〕
接著歐律馬科斯發表了一篇冠冕堂皇卻虛情假意的演說，發誓永遠與忒勒馬科斯為友；潘妮洛碧則回到自己房裡哀悼丈夫，直到雅典娜讓她合上雙眼沉沉睡去。
> Eurymachus then made a fair but false speech vowing eternal friendship to Telemachus, and Penelope returned to her own room to mourn her husband till Minerva closed her eyes in slumber.

〔U0422 quote〕
傍晚時分，歐邁俄斯回到小屋，其他人正好宰了一頭一歲的小豬，在準備晚飯。在這之前，雅典娜又把奧德修斯變回了一個老乞丐的模樣。
> In the evening Eumæus got back to his hut just as the others had killed a yearling pig and were getting supper ready. Meanwhile Minerva had again disguised Ulysses as an old beggar.

〔U0423 quote〕
「城裡有什麼消息，歐邁俄斯？」忒勒馬科斯問，「求婚者的船回來了嗎？」
> "What news from the town, Eumæus?" said Telemachus. "Have the suitors got back with their ship?"

〔U0424 quote〕
「我沒有打聽，」歐邁俄斯答道，「因為我把話傳到之後，就直接轉身回家了；不過我碰到了你那艘船上派來的信差，他搶在我前頭把你回來的消息告訴了你母親。我回來的路上，走到城上方赫耳墨斯山丘時，看見一艘船駛進港來，船上人很多，兵器也很多；所以我猜那是求婚者，但也說不準。」
> "I did not ask," answered Eumæus, "for when I had given my message I turned straight home; but I met the messenger from your own crew, who told your mother of your return before I could do so. As I was coming here, and was on the hill of Mercury above the town, I saw a ship with many men and much armour coming into port; so I suppose it was the suitors, but I cannot be sure."

〔U0425 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯朝父親使了個眼色，卻沒讓牧豬人看見。隨後他們一起吃了晚飯，便上床睡覺。
> Telemachus gave his father a look, but so that the swineherd could not see him. Then they all got their supper and went to bed.

〔U0426 quote〕
*忒勒馬科斯進城，歐邁俄斯與奧德修斯隨後跟去；奧德修斯遭求婚者虐待*。
> *Telemachus goes to the town, and is followed by Eumæus and Ulysses, who is maltreated by the suitors*.

〔U0427 quote〕
早晨一到，忒勒馬科斯就告訴歐邁俄斯，他現在要進城去讓母親見見他，因為她不親眼看見他，是怎麼也不會安心的。「至於這個可憐的陌生人，」他接著說，「你把他帶到城裡去，讓他在那裡討飯，能討多少算多少；他要是不樂意，那是他自己倒楣，我這個人就喜歡有話直說。」
> When morning came Telemachus told Eumæus that he would now go to the town and show himself to his mother, who would never be comforted till she saw him with her own eyes. "As for this miserable stranger," he continued, "take him to the town, that he may beg there and get what he can; if this does not please him, so much the worse for him, but I like to say what I mean."

〔U0428 quote〕
奧德修斯說他很樂意去，因為乞丐在城裡討生活比在鄉下好得多；不過他得先暖暖身子，等太陽曬得有點熱度再走；他身上的衣服破爛不堪，城又離這裡有段路，他會凍死的。
> Ulysses said he should be glad to go, for a beggar could do much better in town than country; but he must warm himself first, and wait till the sun had got some heat in it; his clothes were very bad, and he should perish with cold, for the town was some way off.

〔U0429 quote〕
於是忒勒馬科斯先走了。他到家之後，把長矛靠在一根結實的承重柱上，穿過石鋪的地面走進屋裡。他看見歐律克蕾亞正在座椅上鋪羊皮。她和其他女僕一看見他，全都跑過來，親吻他的頭和肩膀。接著潘妮洛碧也哭著從房裡出來，擁抱他，要他把所見所聞都講給她聽。
> Telemachus then left, and when he reached the house he set his spear against a strong bearing post, crossed the stone pavement and went inside. He found Euryclea putting the sheep skins on to the seats. She and all the other maids ran up to him as soon as they saw him, and kissed him on the head and shoulders. Then Penelope came weeping from her room, embraced him, and told him to tell her all that he had seen.

〔U0430 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯請她回房去，向雅典娜禱告，求讓他們向求婚者報仇雪恨。「我得到議事場去一趟，」他說，「去照應一位我帶回來的客人，我把他留在珀萊俄斯那裡了。」
> Telemachus bade her go back to her room and pray to Minerva that they might be revenged on the suitors. "I must go," said he, "to the place of assembly, to look after a guest whom I have brought with me, and whom I have left with Piræus."

〔U0431 quote〕
潘妮洛碧照兒子的話去做；忒勒馬科斯則前往議事場，他的兩條狗也跟著他。那些當天還沒到奧德修斯家去的求婚者圍到他身邊，對他說盡好話，可他知道他們虛情假意，便走去和他的老朋友門托爾、安提福斯和哈利瑟耳塞斯坐在一起。不久珀萊俄斯帶著忒俄克呂墨諾斯走過來，說：「希望你派幾個女僕到我家來，把墨涅拉俄斯送你的禮物拿走。」
> Penelope did as her son had said, while Telemachus went to the place of assembly, and his two dogs with him. The suitors, who had not yet gone to the house of Ulysses for the day, gathered round him, and made him fair speeches, but he knew their falsehood and went to sit with his old friends Mentor, Antiphus and Halitherses. Presently Piræus came up, bringing Theoclymenus with him, and said, "I wish you would send some of your women to my house to take away the presents that Menelaus gave you."

〔U0432 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯說，他不知道會發生什麼事；要是求婚者殺了他，他寧可讓珀萊俄斯留著這些禮物，也不願落到求婚者手裡。反過來，要是他殺了求婚者，那麼珀萊俄斯若肯把禮物交還給他，他會非常感激。
> Telemachus said he did not know what might happen; if the suitors killed him, he had rather Piræus kept the presents than that the suitors should have them. If, on the other hand, he killed the suitors he should be much obliged if Piræus would let him have the presents.

〔U0433 quote〕
然後他帶著忒俄克呂墨諾斯回到自己家裡，兩人洗了澡，有人把吃食擺到他們面前。他們用餐時，潘妮洛碧坐在近旁紡紗，隨後說她要上樓去，躺到那張床榻上──自從丈夫離開她的那天起，她就沒有一天不以淚水浸濕那張床榻。「可是，」她又說，「在求婚者進來之前，你竟沒有耐心告訴我，你究竟有沒有打聽到你父親的一點消息。」
> Then he took Theoclymenus to his own house, where they had a bath, and refreshments were set before them. Penelope sat near them, spinning, while they were at table, and then said she should go up stairs and lie down on that couch which she had never ceased to water with her tears from the day her husband left her. "But you had not the patience," she added, "to tell me, before the suitors came, whether you had been able to hear anything about your father."

〔U0434 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯告訴她，涅斯托爾待他多麼好，又如何送他去見墨涅拉俄斯；墨涅拉俄斯向他保證奧德修斯還活著，只是被卡呂普索留住了，因為沒有船，脫不了身。潘妮洛碧聽了心緒大亂，忒俄克呂墨諾斯便把忒勒馬科斯回到伊薩卡時迎面出現的那個徵兆告訴她，好讓她安心。
> Telemachus told her how good Nestor had been to him, and how he had sent him on to Menelaus, who had assured him that Ulysses was still alive, but was detained by Calypso, from whom he could not get away for want of a ship. Penelope was very much agitated, but Theoclymenus reassured her by telling her about the omen which had greeted Telemachus on his return to Ithaca.

〔U0435 quote〕
他們這樣交談的時候，求婚者正在奧德修斯家門前的平地上擲鐵餅、朝靶子投標槍取樂。可是快到午飯時候，羊群照常由牧人從四鄉趕回來［擠奶］，深得求婚者歡心的墨東便招呼他們進屋，著手準備午飯。他們於是進了屋，開始宰殺一些綿羊、山羊、豬和一頭小母牛。
> While they were thus conversing, the suitors were playing at quoits and aiming javelins at a mark on the level ground in front of Ulysses' house. But when it was near dinner time and the flocks were coming in from all the country round with their shepherds as usual [to be milked], Medon, who was a great favourite with the suitors, called them to come in and set about getting their dinner ready. They therefore came in and began to butcher some sheep, goats, pigs, and a heifer.

〔U0436 quote〕
這時歐邁俄斯對奧德修斯說，該動身了，因為天已經大亮，要是等到午後，他會覺得更冷。「無論如何，」奧德修斯說，「你若有手杖就給我一根吧，因為這條路很崎嶇。」歐邁俄斯給了他一根，兩人便沿著通往城裡的陡峭小路出發了。快到城邊時，他們來到伊薩科斯、涅里同斯和波呂克托爾所造的泉水旁，城裡的人都從這裡汲水；他們就在這裡撞見了多利俄斯之子墨蘭提俄斯（Melantheus），[註29]他正和兩個手下的牧人一起趕著山羊，要送去給求婚者做午飯。
> Meanwhile Eumæus told Ulysses that it was time to make a start, for the day was well up and if he waited till afternoon he would find the cold more severe. "At any rate," said Ulysses, "let me have a staff if you have one, for the path is rugged." Eumæus gave him one, and they set out along the steep path leading to the town. When they were nearly there they came to the fountain which Ithacus, Neritus, and Polyctor had made, and from which the people drew their water; here they fell in with Melantheus[註29] son of Dolius, who was bringing goats for the suitors' dinner, he and his two under shepherds.

〔U0437 quote〕
墨蘭提俄斯對奧德修斯和歐邁俄斯百般辱罵，還想一腳把奧德修斯踢下小路，卻沒能踢動他。奧德修斯忍住了，向寧芙們禱告；墨蘭提俄斯便說，他要把他押上船，賣到外國去。說完他就匆匆趕路，留下牧豬人和他的主人慢慢跟在後頭。
> Melanthius heaped all kinds of insult on Ulysses and Eumæus, and tried to kick Ulysses off the path, but could not do so. Ulysses restrained himself, and prayed to the nymphs, whereon Melanthius said he would put him on board ship and sell him in some foreign country. He then hurried on, leaving the swineherd and his master to follow at their own pace.

〔U0438 quote〕
他們走近宅邸時，聽得見費彌俄斯的豎琴聲，還有他為求婚者歌唱的嗓音。他們也聞得到烤肉的香味。[註30]歐邁俄斯說，他先進去，但奧德修斯最好隨即跟進來，因為他要是被人看見在外院裡站著閒晃，大家可能會拿東西扔他。
> When they got near the house they could hear the sound of Phemius's lyre, and his voice as he sang to the suitors. They could also smell the savour of roast meats.[註30] Eumæus said that he would go in first, but that Ulysses had better follow him soon, for if he was seen standing about in the outer court people might throw things at him.

〔U0439 quote〕
他們正這樣說著，躺在糞堆上、滿身跳蚤的老獵犬阿爾戈斯看見了奧德修斯，認出了他，搖著尾巴，想要到他跟前去，卻已經做不到了。奧德修斯見了，抹去眼中的一滴淚，問歐邁俄斯這條狗還有沒有用處，還是只因為長得好看才養著。歐邁俄斯說起阿爾戈斯當年是多麼出色的一條獵犬；可是那條狗見過了主人，就在歐邁俄斯走進屋裡的那一刻死去了。
> As they were thus talking the old hound Argus who was lying on the dunghill, very full of fleas, caught sight of Ulysses, recognised him, wagged his tail, and tried to come to him, but could not do so. Thereon Ulysses wiped a tear from his eyes, and asked Eumæus whether the dog was of any use, or whether he was kept only for his good looks. Eumæus said what a noble hound Argus had been, but the dog, having seen his master, died just as Eumæus went inside the house.

〔U0440 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯看見他進來，便招手請他到自己桌旁坐下。奧德修斯隨後也跟了進來，坐在門口內側的梣木地板上，背靠著一根削得方方正正的柏木承重柱。忒勒馬科斯注意到他，便對歐邁俄斯說：「把這一把麵包和肉拿給那位陌生人，再叫他到其他人那裡挨個去討，因為乞丐可不能臉皮薄。」歐邁俄斯把話和麵包、肉都交給了他。
> Telemachus saw him enter and beckoned him to a seat at his own table. Ulysses followed him shortly, and sat down on the floor of ash wood inside the door way, leaning against a bearing-post of well-squared Cyprus wood. Telemachus noted him and said to Eumæus, "Take the stranger this handful of bread and meat, tell him also to go round and beg from the others, for a beggar must not be shamefaced." Eumæus gave him both the message and the bread and meat.

〔U0441 quote〕
於是奧德修斯開始挨桌乞討，因為他想摸清求婚者的底細。他從左往右一路討過去，有些人可憐他，另一些人則開始打聽他是誰；墨蘭提俄斯便說他是跟著牧豬人來的。安提諾俄斯因此質問歐邁俄斯，帶這樣一個人來煩擾他們，究竟是什麼意思。
> Then Ulysses began to go round begging, for he wanted to exploit the suitors. He went from left to right, and some took compassion on him while others begun asking who he might be; Melanthius then said that he had come with the swineherd. Antinous, therefore, asked Eumæus what he meant by bringing such a man to plague them.

〔U0442 quote〕
「不是我請他來的，」歐邁俄斯答道，「誰會去請那種人呢？要請也是請占卜者、醫生、木匠或是吟遊詩人。所有求婚者當中，就數你對奧德修斯的僕人最苛刻，尤其是對我；不過只要潘妮洛碧和忒勒馬科斯站在我這一邊，我就不在乎。」
> "I did not ask him to come," answered Eumæus. "Who was likely to ask a man of that sort? One would ask a divine, a physician, a carpenter, or a bard. You are always hardest of all the suitors on Ulysses' servants, and especially upon me, but I do not care so long as I have Penelope and Telemachus on my side."

〔U0443 quote〕
「別說了，」忒勒馬科斯說，「安提諾俄斯的嘴是他們當中最刻薄的，還帶壞了其他人。」接著他轉向安提諾俄斯說：「給他一點東西吧，我並不心疼。用不著顧慮我母親或是哪個僕人──你才不會顧慮呢──只不過你愛吃勝過愛給。」
> "Hush," said Telemachus, "Antinous has the bitterest tongue of them all, and he makes the others worse." Then he turned towards Antinous and said, "Give him something: I do not grudge it. Never mind my mother or any of the servants—not you—but you are fonder of eating than of giving."

〔U0444 quote〕
安提諾俄斯說：「你這個趾高氣揚、不知天高地厚的小子；要是所有求婚者都給他我要給的這麼多，他三個月都不會再靠近這座宅子。」
> Antinous said, "You are a swaggering upstart; if all the suitors will give him as much as I will, he will not come near the house again this three months."

〔U0445 quote〕
他一邊說，一邊拿起桌下的腳凳朝奧德修斯比劃，作勢威嚇。其他求婚者都給了他一點東西；他正要離開，卻又決定再向安提諾俄斯討一次，還給他編了一套自己在埃及遭逢的種種不幸。
> As he spoke he menaced Ulysses with the footstool from under his table. The other suitors all gave him something; and he was about to leave, when he determined to again beg from Antinous and trumped him up a story of the misfortunes that had befallen him in Egypt.

〔U0446 quote〕
「滾開，」安提諾俄斯說，「滾到院子的露天處去，[註31]離我的桌子遠一點，不然我就再讓你嘗一回埃及的滋味。」
> "Get out," said Antinous, "into the open part of the court,[註31] and away from my table, or I will give you Egypt over again."

〔U0447 quote〕
奧德修斯退後幾步，說：「你的相貌比你的見識好。看得出來，你要是在自己家裡，連一撮鹽也不肯施捨給窮人。」
> Ulysses drew back, and said, "Your looks are better than your understanding. I can see that if you were in your own house you would not spare a poor man so much as a pinch of salt."

〔U0448 quote〕
安提諾俄斯怒目瞪著他。「接著，」他叫道，「然後給我滾出院子去。」說著便把一張腳凳朝他扔去，正打中他的右肩；可是奧德修斯像岩石一樣屹立不動，並禱告說：如果世上真有神明，或是真有替乞丐報仇的神祇，就讓安提諾俄斯還沒當上新郎就先成為一具屍體。
> Antinous scowled at him. "Take that," he cried, "and be off out of the court." As he spoke he threw a footstool at him which hit him on the right shoulder, but Ulysses stood firm as a rock, and prayed that if there was a god, or an avenger of beggars, Antinous might be a corpse before he was a bridegroom.

〔U0449 quote〕
「當心點，」安提諾俄斯回答，「給我閉嘴，否則我們就活剝你的皮。」
> "Have a care," replied Antinous, "and hold your peace, or we will flay you alive."

〔U0450 quote〕
其他人都責備安提諾俄斯。「你打這人可就錯了，」他們說，「誰知道他會不會是哪位神明，喬裝改扮在世間行走，好糾正冤屈、懲戒人類的蠻橫？」
> The others reproved Antinous. "You did ill," they said, "to strike the man. Who knows but he may be one of the gods who go about the world in disguise to redress wrong, and chastise the insolence of mankind?"

〔U0451 quote〕
潘妮洛碧在樓上的房間裡聽見了這一切，便和女僕們痛罵那些求婚者。女管家歐律諾墨答道，要是她的禱告得蒙應允，他們當中沒有一個能活到天亮。「奶媽，」潘妮洛碧回答，「他們我全都恨，但安提諾俄斯最壞。」接著她派人把歐邁俄斯叫來，說：「去告訴那位陌生人，我想見他；他看起來像個走過許多地方的人，說不定見過或聽過一點奧德修斯的消息。」
> Penelope from her room upstairs heard what had been going on, and spoke with her women bitterly about the suitors. The housekeeper Eurynome answered that if her prayers were heard, not a single one of them would live till morning. "Nurse," replied Penelope, "I hate them all, but Antinous is the worst." Then she sent for Eumæus and said, "Tell the stranger that I want to see him; he looks like a man who has travelled, and he may have seen or heard something of Ulysses."

〔U0452 quote〕
「他在我的小屋裡已經待了三天三夜，夫人，」歐邁俄斯回答，「就是最高明的吟遊詩人也不能讓我聽得更入迷了。他告訴我奧德修斯就在忒斯普洛提亞人那裡，不久就要回來，還帶著大批財寶。」
> "He has been three days and three nights at my hut, Madam," replied Eumæus, "and the most accomplished bard could not have given me better entertainment. He told me that Ulysses was among the Thesprotians and would return shortly, bringing much treasure with him."

〔U0453 quote〕
「那就叫他來見我，」潘妮洛碧說，「至於其他那些人，以後就讓他們自掏腰包吃飯，或者愛怎麼吃就怎麼吃，只要別再上這兒來就好。」
> "Then call him to me," said Penelope, "and as for the others, let them dine at their own expense for the future or how they best may, so long as they leave off coming here."

〔U0454 quote〕
她話音剛落，在樓下的忒勒馬科斯就打了一個大噴嚏，響聲傳遍了整座宅子。潘妮洛碧向歐邁俄斯解釋說，這是個大吉之兆；她又說，那陌生人告訴她的話她若能確信屬實，就送他一件襯衫和一件斗篷。
> Telemachus, who was down below, gave a great sneeze as she spoke, which echoed over the whole house. Penelope explained to Eumæus that this was a most favourable omen, and added that if she was satisfied of the truth of what the stranger told her she would give him a shirt and cloak.

〔U0455 quote〕
歐邁俄斯把潘妮洛碧的話轉告奧德修斯；可是奧德修斯害怕求婚者動粗，便叫他回話說，她必須等到天黑，求婚者都走了以後再說。「到那時，」他說，「請她讓我在火邊找個暖和的位子坐下，我再把她丈夫的事告訴她；因為我的衣服實在破得不成樣子──你是知道的，我頭一個投奔的就是你家。」
> Eumæus gave Penelope's message to Ulysses, but he feared the violence of the suitors, and told him to say that she must wait till nightfall, when the suitors would be gone. "Then," he said, "let her set me down in a warm seat by the fire, and I will tell her about her husband; for my clothes are in a very bad state; you know they are, for your's was the first house I came to."

〔U0456 quote〕
潘妮洛碧對他的推延很不高興，問歐邁俄斯，那人的顧慮是否有道理，還是只不過臉皮薄。歐邁俄斯解釋說他十分有道理，潘妮洛碧這才滿意了；歐邁俄斯隨後回到求婚者那邊，告訴忒勒馬科斯他要回去照看他的豬了。
> Penelope was displeased at his delay, and asked Eumæus whether his fears were reasonable, or whether it was only that he was shamefaced. Eumæus explained that he was quite reasonable, whereon Penelope was satisfied; he then went back to where the suitors were, and told Telemachus that he would return to his pigs.

〔U0457 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯說，他最好先吃點東西再走，並吩咐他明天早上再進城來，把要宰來給大家做午飯的那幾頭豬一起帶來。這時已經是下午了，求婚者都已轉而去唱歌跳舞。
> Telemachus said that he had better get something to eat first, and was to come back to the town on the following morning, bringing the pigs that were to be killed for dinner. It was now afternoon, and the suitors had turned to their singing and dancing.

〔U0458 quote〕
*奧德修斯與伊羅斯打鬥──求婚者向潘妮洛碧獻禮──並虐待奧德修斯*。
> *The fight between Ulysses and Irus—The suitors make presents to Penelope—and ill-treat Ulysses*.

〔U0459 quote〕
這時來了一個下等的流浪漢，到奧德修斯家討飯──一個高頭大馬卻毫無耐力的傢伙──他名叫阿爾奈俄斯；可是大家都叫他伊羅斯，因為誰肯差遣他，他就替誰跑腿。這人開始威嚇奧德修斯，說求婚者已經催他把奧德修斯趕出這座宅子。
> Now there came a common tramp to Ulysses' house, begging—a great hulking fellow with no stay in him—whose name was Arnæus; but people called him Irus, because he would run errands for any one who would send him on them. This man began to threaten Ulysses, and said the suitors had urged him to turn him away from the house.

〔U0460 quote〕
奧德修斯說，這裡的地方容得下他們兩個，彼此應該各過各的、互不相擾。「不過，」他接著說，「真要動起手來，我會讓你滿嘴滿胸都是血，這地方就歸我一個人了，因為你再也不會回來。」
> Ulysses said there was room enough for both of them, and that it should be a case of live-and-let-live between them. "If, however," he continued, "it comes to blows, I will deluge your mouth and chest with blood, and I shall have the place to myself, for you will not come back again."

〔U0461 quote〕
伊羅斯怒沖沖地回嘴；安提諾俄斯聽見他們吵嘴，便告訴其他求婚者，伊羅斯和那個陌生人要打架了。「這可是上天送進這座宅子來的最精彩的一場好戲，」他說，「我們晚飯要吃塞滿了血和肥油的山羊肚；這場架誰打贏了，那些羊肚就隨他挑。」
> Irus retorted angrily, and Antinous, hearing them wrangle, told the other suitors that Irus and the stranger were about to have a fight. "It is the finest piece of sport," he said, "that heaven ever sent into this house. We are to have goat's paunches stuffed with blood and fat for supper; whichever of the two beats in this fight shall have his pick of the lot of them."

〔U0462 quote〕
預備事項安排妥當，奧德修斯也講定了要公平比試，便開始脫衣。伊羅斯一看見他的肌肉，心就虛了；可是安提諾俄斯硬逼著他上場，打鬥於是開始。[註32]奧德修斯一出手就差點要了伊羅斯的命，抓著他的腳跟把他拖到外院，把手杖塞進他手裡，讓他靠著牆撐坐著，已是半死不活。安提諾俄斯隨即給了奧德修斯一個大山羊肚，安菲諾摩斯也舉杯祝他健康。
> The preliminaries being arranged, and fair play bargained for by Ulysses, he began to strip. When Irus saw his muscles his heart misgave him; but Antinous kept him up to it, and the fight began.[註32] Ulysses forthwith nearly killed Irus and dragged him by the heels into the outer court, where he put his staff in his hand and propped him up against the wall more dead than alive. Antinous then gave Ulysses a great goat's paunch, and Amphinomus drank his health.

〔U0463 quote〕
奧德修斯向安菲諾摩斯發表了一番極其莊重、懇切動人的話，警告他離開這座宅子，因為奧德修斯很快就要回來了。「你看來，」他說，「是個明白事理的人，看看你是誰的兒子，這也就不足為奇了。我聽人稱讚過你父親；他是杜利基昂島的尼索斯，既勇敢又富有。人家告訴我你是他的兒子，而你看起來也是個有身分的人；所以請聽好，把我的話記在心上。人是世上所有生存、活動的生物當中最虛妄的：只要上天賜他健康和力氣，他就以為往後不會遭到任何禍患；即便有福的眾神降下憂患給他，他也只能不得不忍受，盡量往好處想，因為全能的神日復一日地給人當天的心思。這一切我都清楚，因為我也曾經是個富人，仗著一股頑固的傲氣，又自恃有父親和兄弟們撐腰，做了許多錯事；所以人在任何事上都應時時敬畏神，上天願意賜給他什麼好處，就接受下來，不要虛榮自誇。」可是安菲諾摩斯雖然心裡隱隱覺得不祥，卻還是不肯聽勸。
> Ulysses made Amphinomus a very grave and impressive speech, warning him to leave the house, inasmuch as Ulysses would return shortly. "You seem," said he, "to be a man of good understanding, as indeed you may well be, seeing whose son you are. I have heard your father well spoken of; he is Nisus of Dulichium, a man both brave and wealthy. They tell me you are his son and you seem to be a considerable person; listen, therefore, and take heed to what I am saying. Man is the vainest of all creatures that live and move upon the earth: as long as heaven vouchsafes him health and strength he thinks that he shall come to no harm hereafter, and even when the blessed gods bring sorrow upon him, he bears it as he needs must and makes the best of it, for God Almighty gives men their daily minds day by day. I know all about it, for I was a rich man once, and did much wrong in the stubbornness of my pride and in the confidence that my father and my brothers would support me; therefore let a man fear God in all things always, and take the good that heaven may see fit to send him without vainglory." But Amphinomus, though his heart boded ill, would not be persuaded.

〔U0464 quote〕
這時雅典娜讓潘妮洛碧心生一念，要從求婚者身上弄些禮物來。「我恨他們，」她對歐律諾墨說，「不過我還是要破例見他們一次；我想提醒我兒子提防他們。」
> Minerva then put it in Penelope's mind to get some presents out of the suitors. "I hate them," said she to Eurynome, "but still for once in a way I will see them; I want to warn my son against them."

〔U0465 quote〕
「當然好，我親愛的孩子，」歐律諾墨答道，「可是你得先洗把臉。你不能帶著滿臉淚痕出去見人。」
> "Certainly, my dear child," answered Eurynome, "but you must wash your face first. You cannot be seen with the stain of tears upon your cheeks."

〔U0466 quote〕
「歐律諾墨，」她的女主人回答，「別來勸我了。自從我丈夫揚帆去特洛伊的那一天，上天就奪走了我所有的美貌；不過你去叫奧托諾厄和希波達彌亞來陪我吧，因為我不能想像沒有人陪著就去見那些求婚者。」老婦人便穿過宅子去叫那兩個女僕；她一走，雅典娜就讓潘妮洛碧沉沉睡去，在她睡著時賜給她光彩奪目的美貌：用阿芙蘿黛蒂與美惠三女神一同出去跳舞時所用的那種仙饌般的嬌豔替她洗臉，又讓她的儀態更加端莊、更加雍容。兩個女僕來了，她們走近的聲響把她吵醒。「好一場甜美的睡眠，」她驚嘆道，「竟這樣籠罩了我。但願那是死亡的長眠，就此結束我所有的悲傷。」
> "Eurynome," replied her mistress, "do not try to persuade me. Heaven robbed me of all my beauty on the day when my husband sailed for Troy; but send Autonoë and Hippodamia to attend me, for I cannot think of seeing the suitors unattended." The old woman then went through the house to fetch the women; and as soon as she was gone, Minerva sent Penelope into a deep sleep during which she endowed her with the most dazzling beauty, washing her face with the ambrosial loveliness which Venus wears when she goes out dancing with the Graces, and giving her a statelier and more imposing presence. When the two maids came, the noise of their coming woke her. "What a delicious sleep," she exclaimed, "has overshadowed me. Would that it had been the sleep of death, which had thus ended all my sorrows."

〔U0467 quote〕
接著她走下樓，求婚者都被她的美貌晃得目眩神迷。她一開口就責備忒勒馬科斯，不該讓那場打鬥發生。忒勒馬科斯承認自己有錯，但辯稱自己處境極為艱難，何況說到底，是奧德修斯把伊羅斯痛揍了一頓。
> She then went down stairs, and the suitors were dazzled with her beauty. She began by upbraiding Telemachus for having allowed the fight to take place. Telemachus admitted his fault, but pleaded the extreme difficulty of his situation and the fact that after all Ulysses had thrashed Irus.

〔U0468 quote〕
歐律馬科斯插嘴打斷他們的談話，對潘妮洛碧說她多麼美麗；潘妮洛碧回答說，自從她丈夫揚帆去特洛伊的那一天，上天就奪走了她所有的美貌。「還有，」她又說，「我還有另一樁大傷心事──你們這些求婚者求婚的方式不合常規。男人向一位他們認為會是好妻子的女子求親時，通常會帶來牛羊讓她的親屬宴飲，並送給女方本人貴重的禮物，而不是白吃別人的家產。」
> Enrymachus broke in upon their conversation by telling Penelope how very beautiful she was; and Penelope answered that heaven had robbed her of all her beauty on the day when her husband sailed for Troy. "Moreover," she added, "I have another great sorrow—you suitors are not wooing me in the usual way. When men are suing for the hand of one who they think will make them a good wife, they generally bring oxen and sheep for her relations to feast upon, and make rich presents to the lady herself, instead of sponging upon other people's property."

〔U0469 quote〕
奧德修斯聽見她這樣說，心裡十分高興，看著自己的妻子一邊設法從求婚者身上弄到禮物，一邊把他們耍得團團轉。
> When Ulysses heard her say this, he was delighted at seeing his wife trying to get presents out of the suitors, and hoodwinking them.

〔U0470 quote〕
這時安提諾俄斯說：「潘妮洛碧，能拿多少禮物就儘管拿吧，可是你不嫁給我們當中最好的那個人，我們就不走。」於是他們都送給潘妮洛碧華貴的禮物，她便回到自己房裡，女僕們跟在後頭，替她捧著那些禮物。
> Then Antinous said, "Penelope, take all the presents you can get, but we will not go till you have married the best man among us." On this they all made Penelope magnificent presents, and she went back to her own room, followed by the women, who carried the presents for her.

〔U0471 quote〕
求婚者這時轉而唱歌跳舞，照明的是擺在院子裡的幾座大火盆，[註33]還有女僕們輪流高舉的火把。過了一會兒，奧德修斯叫她們進屋去，說他自己來舉火把。女僕們聽了哈哈大笑，其中一個叫墨蘭索的開始挖苦他。她是多利俄斯的女兒，可是潘妮洛碧從小把她帶大，還常給她玩具；她卻一點也不體恤潘妮洛碧的傷心，反而和歐律馬科斯私通。「你喝醉了嗎？」她對奧德修斯說，「還是你一向就是這副德性？」
> The suitors now turned to singing and dancing, lighted by large braziers that were placed in the court,[註33] 307 and also by torches, which the maids held up by turns. Ulysses after a while told them to go inside, saying that he would hold the torches himself. The maids laughed at this, and Melantho, who was one of them, began to gibe at him. She was daughter to Dolius but Penelope had brought her up from childhood, and used to give her toys; she showed no consideration, however, for Penelope's sorrows, but misconducted herself with Eurymachus. "Are you drunk?" she said to Ulysses, "or are you always like this?"

〔U0472 quote〕
奧德修斯怒目瞪著她，說要去告訴忒勒馬科斯，讓他把她剁成肉醬。女人們因此嚇壞了，都走開了，於是只剩奧德修斯一個人舉著熊熊的火把──看著所有的求婚者，心裡盤算著他的復仇。
> Ulysses scowled at her, and said he would tell 337 Telemachus, who would have her cut up into mincemeat. The women, therefore, were frightened and went away, so Ulysses was left holding up the flaming torches—looking upon all the suitors and brooding over his revenge.

〔U0473 quote〕
不久，歐律馬科斯開始嘲弄他，譏笑他寧可乞討也不願幹活。奧德修斯回答說：「歐律馬科斯，要是你我在初夏白晝最長的時候比試一番──給我們每人一把好鐮刀，在牧草長成的時節從天亮割到天黑，看是你還是我割得更有力、餓得更久。或者讓我們在一塊四英畝的田裡，每人各配一對吃得飽飽的黃褐色公牛，看誰犁的溝更直。再說，要是今天就爆發戰爭──給我一副盔甲，你就會看見我在最前列作戰。你蠻橫又殘忍，自以為是個大人物，只因為你活在一個小小的世界裡，而且是個糟糕的世界。」
> Presently Eurymachus began to jeer at him, and taunt him by saying he preferred begging to working. Ulysses answered, "If you and I, Eurymachus, were matched one against the other in early summer, when the days are at their longest—give us each a good scythe, and see whether you or I will mow the stronger or fast the longer, from dawn till dark when the mowing grass is about. Or let us be in a four acre field with a couple of tawny full fed oxen each, and see which of us can drive the straighter furrow. Again, let war break out this day—give me armour and you will find me fighting among the foremost. You are insolent and cruel, and think yourself a great man because you live in a little world, and that a bad one."

〔U0474 quote〕
歐律馬科斯勃然大怒，抓起一張凳子；可是奧德修斯因為害怕，便坐到來自杜利基昂島的安菲諾摩斯膝邊。凳子打中了斟酒的侍者，把他打倒在地，於是全場一片大亂；亂哄哄之中，忒勒馬科斯說他不會勉強任何人，但他認為大家最好都回家睡覺。眾人表示同意，不久便離開了宅子。
> Eurymachus was furious, and seized a stool; but Ulysses sat down by the knees of Amphinomus of Dulichium, for he was afraid; the stool hit the cupbearer and knocked him down, whereon there was a general uproar, amid which Telemachus said that he would compel no man, but he thought it would be better if they would all go home to bed. To this they assented, and shortly afterwards left the house.

Telemachus was lost in wonder, but Ulysses said, "Hush, this is the manner of the gods. Get you to bed, and leave me to talk with your mother and the maids." 〔U0481 quote〕
「陌生人，」潘妮洛碧答道，「自從阿爾戈斯人出發去特洛伊、奧德修斯也跟著他們去了以後，上天就奪走了我所有的美貌。」接著她講起求婚者的事、她織的那匹布，說她如今已經山窮水盡了。她的父母一直催她再嫁，她兒子也是如此，因為她拖了這麼久的求婚期，讓他背上了沉重的開銷，他已經忍無可忍。「不過，儘管如此，」她接著說，「我還是想知道你是誰；因為你總不會是從岩石或橡樹裡生出來的吧。」
> "Stranger," answered Penelope, "heaven robbed me of all my beauty when the Argives set out for Troy and Ulysses with them." She then told about the suitors, and her web, and said that she was now at the very end of her resources. Her parents were urging her to marry again, and so also was her son, who chafed under the heavy burden of expense which her long courtship had caused him. "In spite of all this, however," she continued, "I want to know who you are; for you cannot be the son of a rock or of an oak."

〔U0482 quote〕
被她這樣追問，奧德修斯便說他名叫埃同，來自克里特；奧德修斯和他的部下前往特洛伊途中，他曾在那裡款待他們好些日子。潘妮洛碧聽著聽著就痛哭起來，奧德修斯費了好大的勁才忍住自己的眼淚──但他總算忍住了。「現在我要考考你，」她說，「告訴我我丈夫當時穿什麼衣服。也告訴我他是個什麼樣的人，還有跟他在一起的那些人。」
> Thus pressed, Ulysses said that his name was Æthon and that he came from Crete, where he had entertained Ulysses and his men for many days when they were on their way to Troy. Penelope wept bitterly as she listened, and it was all Ulysses could do to restrain his own tears—but he succeeded. "I will now prove you," said she; "tell me how my husband was dressed. Tell me also what manner of man he was, and about the men who were with him."

〔U0483 quote〕
「我就告訴你，」奧德修斯回答，「隔了這麼久，盡我所能記得的說。他穿著一件紫色羊毛的斗篷，是雙層的，用一枚金胸針扣著，胸針上有兩個扣針的鉤子。胸針正面有一幅圖案，刻著一條狗用兩隻前爪按住一頭帶斑點的小鹿，盯著那頭倒在地上喘息的小鹿。人人都驚嘆這些東西是怎麼用黃金做出來的──狗望著小鹿、正把牠勒死，小鹿則拚命抽搐掙扎，想要逃脫。至於他的襯衫，貼身得就像一層洋蔥皮，在陽光下閃閃發亮，凡是看見的女人無不讚嘆。他身邊有個僕人，年紀比他稍長一些，肩膀是駝的；皮膚黝黑，頭髮濃密捲曲。他的名字叫歐律巴特斯。」
> "I will tell you," replied Ulysses, "as nearly as I can remember after so long a time. He wore a mantle of purple wool, double lined, and it was fastened by a gold brooch with two catches for the pin. On the face of this there was a device that shewed a dog holding a spotted fawn between its fore paws, and watching it as it lay panting on the ground. Every one marvelled at the way in which these things had been done in gold—the dog looking at the fawn and strangling it, while the fawn was struggling convulsively to escape. As for his shirt, it fitted him like the skin of an onion, and glistened in the sunlight to the admiration of all the women who beheld it. He had a servant with him, a little older than himself, whose shoulders were hunched; he was dark, and had thick curly hair. His name was Eurybates."

〔U0484 quote〕
潘妮洛碧深受感動。「你什麼都不會缺，」她說，「你說的那些衣服和胸針，都是我給他的，可是我再也見不到他了。」
> Penelope was deeply moved. "You shall want for nothing," said she, "It was I who gave him the clothes and the brooch you speak of, but I shall never see him again."

〔U0485 quote〕
「別太灰心，夫人，」奧德修斯答道，「我在忒斯普洛提亞人那裡的時候，確確實實聽說他還活著，而且安然無恙。說真的，他早就該到家了，只是他認為最好先積攢大筆財富再回來。我最鄭重地起誓：不出這個月，他就會回到這裡。」
> "Be not too dejected, Madam," answered Ulysses; "when I was with the Thespotians I heard for certain that he was alive and well. Indeed he would have been here ere now, had he not deemed it better to amass great wealth before returning. Before this month is out I swear most solemnly that he will be here."

「你若說的是真話，」潘妮洛碧答道，「你一定會得到豐厚的報償，可是他不會回來的。不過，你們這些女人，帶這位陌生人去替他洗洗；給他鋪一張舒服的床，明天早上再替他洗一遍、抹上油，讓他和忒勒馬科斯同桌吃飯；求婚者當中誰要是欺負他，誰就會後悔，因為不管那人怎麼發火，他在這個家裡都別想再有什麼作為。說真的，先生，我若不讓你穿得體面、吃得飽足，你又怎能知道我在賢德與明慧上勝過其他所有女人多少呢？」
> "If you say truly," replied Penelope, "you shall indeed be rewarded richly, but he will not come. Still, you women, take the stranger and wash him; make him a comfortable bed, and in the morning wash him again and anoint him, that he may sit at the same table with Telemachus; if any of the suitors molests him, he shall rue it, for fume as he may, he shall have no more to do in this house. How indeed, Sir, can you know how much I surpass all other women in goodness and discretion unless I see that you are well clothed and fed?"

〔U0487 quote〕
「別替我鋪床了，夫人，」奧德修斯說，「我照往常那樣睡在光禿禿的地上就好。我也不喜歡讓人洗腳。我不許你的任何一個女僕碰我的腳；不過，你若有哪位正派的老婦人，和我一樣飽經風霜，我就讓她替我洗。」
> "Make me no bed, Madam, said Ulysses, "I will lie on the bare ground as I am wont to do. Nor do I like having my feet washed. I will not allow any of your serving women to touch my feet; but if you have any respectable old woman who has gone through as much as I have, I will let her wash them."

「陌生人，」潘妮洛碧答道，「到過這裡的外鄉人裡，沒有一個比你更懂得分寸。你說的那種人，我這裡正好有一位；她從奧德修斯出生那天起就是他的奶媽，如今已經年老體衰，不過她會替你洗腳。歐律克蕾亞，過來替這位陌生人洗腳。他和你的主人如今該有的年紀差不多。」
> "Stranger," answered Penelope, "your sense of propriety exceeds that of any foreigner who has ever come here. I have exactly the kind of person you describe; she was Ulysses' nurse from the day of his birth, and is now very old and feeble, but she shall wash your feet. Euryclea, come and wash the stranger's feet. He is about the same age as your master would be."

歐律克蕾亞滿懷憐憫地對奧德修斯說了一番話，最後說他長得很像她的主人。奧德修斯回答說，很多別的人也都注意到了這點相像。

Parnassus with his mother's father Autolycus, whom Mercury had endowed with the gift of being the most accomplished thief and perjurer in the whole world, for he was very fond of him. She immediately dropped the leg, which made a loud noise against the side of the bath and upset all the water. Her eyes filled with tears, and she caught Ulysses by the beard and told him that she knew him.

Ulysses gripped Euryclea's throat, and swore he would kill her, nurse to him though she had been, unless she kept his return secret—which she promised to do. 接著潘妮洛碧向奧德修斯細訴她的愁苦。她說，別人都睡得著，她卻不行，白天黑夜都睡不著。她一心想著自己該盡什麼本分，怎麼也不得安寧。她該留在原地，守住兒子的家產呢，還是該嫁給求婚者當中的一個，到別處去？她兒子還小的時候，根本不肯聽她這麼做，可是如今他已經長大，明白求婚者把他的家產糟蹋成了什麼樣子，便一再催她離開。此外，她還做了一個奇怪的夢：夢見一隻老鷹從山上飛來，向她心愛的那群鵝猛撲下去──那時牠們正在吃木盆裡的糊食[註35]──把牠們全都咬死了。接著那老鷹又飛回來，告訴她牠就是奧德修斯，而那群鵝就是求婚者；可是她醒來時，那群鵝還在糊食盆邊啄食。那麼，這一切究竟是什麼意思呢？
> Then Penelope detailed her sorrows to Ulysses. Others, she said, could sleep, but she could not do so, neither night nor day. She could not rest for thinking what her duty might be. Ought she to stay where she was and stand guard over her son's estate, or ought she to marry one of the suitors and go elsewhere? Her son, while he was a boy, would not hear of her doing this, but now that he was grown up and realised the havoc that the suitors were making of his property, he was continually urging her to go. Besides, she had had a strange dream about an eagle that had come from a mountain and swooped down on her favourite geese as they were eating mash out of a tub,[註35] and had killed them all. Then the eagle came back and told her he was Ulysses, while the geese were the suitors; but when she woke the geese were still feeding at the mash tub. Now, what did all this mean?

可是潘妮洛碧不肯相信他。「夢這種東西，」她說，「是非常古怪的。夢從兩道門裡來，一道是角門，另一道是象牙門。從象牙門來的夢毫無意義。唯有另外那些才是真的，而我的夢是從象牙門來的。所以明天我要把奧德修斯的弓擺到求婚者面前，誰能最輕鬆地拉開這張弓，一箭射穿那十二個孔──十二把斧頭就是靠這些孔裝上斧柄的──我就跟他離開這個家。」
> But Penelope would not believe him. "Dreams," she said, "are very curious things. They come through two gates, one of horn, and the other of ivory. Those that come through the gate of ivory have no significance. It is the others that alone are true, and my dream came through the gate of ivory. Tomorrow, therefore, I shall set Ulysses' bow before the suitors, and I will leave this house with him who can draw it most easily and send an arrow through the twelve holes whereby twelve axeheads are fitted into their handles."

> "Stranger," replied Penelope, "I could stay talking with you the whole night through, but there is a time for everything, and I will now go to lie down upon that couch which I have never ceased to water with my tears from the day my husband set out for the city with an ill-omened name. He saw the women who misbehaved themselves with the suitors go giggling out of the house, and was sorely tempted to kill them then and there, but he restrained himself. Put the cloths on the seats, sponge down the tables; wash the cups and mixing bowls, and go at once, some of you, to fetch water from the fountain.
