〔U0511 quote〕
接著男僕們進來劈柴。女僕們從泉邊回來了，歐邁俄斯也跟著來，趕來三頭肥豬，任牠們在院子裡四處覓食。他看見奧德修斯，問他近來可好；奧德修斯則祈求上天替他向求婚者報仇。
> The men servants then came and chopped wood. The women came back from the fountain, and Eumæus with them, bringing three fine pigs, which he let feed about the yards. When he saw Ulysses he asked him how he was getting on, and Ulysses prayed that heaven might avenge him upon the suitors.

〔U0512 quote〕
接著墨蘭提俄斯帶著他最好的幾頭山羊來了，把牠們拴在門樓裡。拴好之後，他又挖苦了奧德修斯一番，奧德修斯卻沒有理他。
> Then Melanthius came with the best goats he had, and made them fast in the gate-house. When he had done this he gibed at Ulysses, but Ulysses made him no answer.

〔U0513 quote〕
第三個來的是菲羅提俄斯，帶來一頭不曾生育的小母牛和幾頭肥山羊給求婚者。這些牲口是那些專門載送往來客人的船夫替他運過海來的。他一看見奧德修斯，便問歐邁俄斯這人是誰，又說他長得很像他們那位失蹤的主人。接著他告訴奧德修斯，老主人待他有多好，他又是如何盡心服侍老主人。唉！可惜主人已不在人世了。「我們落到了倒楣的年頭，」他說，「我常常想：忒勒馬科斯還在世，我要是把牛群趕走，連人帶牛投靠別的主人，固然於理不合；可就算這樣，也比眼下這種日子強。說真的，要不是我還死守著奧德修斯或許會回來的指望，我早就帶著牛群走了。」
> Thirdly came Philœtius with a barren heifer and some fat goats for the suitors. These had been brought over for him by the boatmen who plied for all comers. When he saw Ulysses, he asked Eumæus who he was, and said he was very like his lost master. Then he told Ulysses how well his old master had treated him, and how well also he had served his old master. Alas! that he was no longer living. "We are fallen," said he, "on evil times, and I often think that though it would not be right of me to drive my cattle off, and put both myself and them under some other master while Telemachus is still alive, yet even this would be better than leading the life I have to lead at present. Indeed I should have gone off with them long ago, if I did not cling to the hope that Ulysses may still return."

〔U0514 quote〕
「我看得出來，」奧德修斯說，「你是個非常誠實、明理的人。所以我要鄭重向你發誓：奧德修斯馬上就會回來；你願意的話，還可以親眼看著他殺死求婚者。」
> "I can see," said Ulysses, "that you are a very honest and sensible person. Therefore I will swear you a solemn oath that Ulysses will be here immediately, and if you like you shall see him with your own eyes kill the suitors."

〔U0515 quote〕
他們這樣談著的時候，求婚者又在密謀殺害忒勒馬科斯；可是天上出現了不祥的徵兆，於是安菲諾摩斯說，他們最好還是回宅邸去準備午餐，他們也就照辦了。入席之後，歐邁俄斯給他們分杯子，菲羅提俄斯傳遞麵包，墨蘭提俄斯替他們斟酒。忒勒馬科斯故意讓奧德修斯坐在迴廊鋪石那一段的一張小桌旁，並告誡求婚者，誰要是欺負他，誰就要倒大楣。「這裡，」他說，「不是什麼酒館，而是我的家；這份家業是從奧德修斯傳到我手上的。」
> While they were thus conversing the suitors were again plotting the murder of Telemachus, but there appeared an unfavourable omen, so Amphinomus said they had better go to the house and get dinner ready, which they accordingly did. When they were at table, Eumæus gave them their cups, Philœtius handed round the bread and Melantheus poured them out their wine. Telemachus purposely set Ulysses at a little table on the part of the cloister that was paved with stone, and told the suitors that it should be worse for any of them who molested him. "This," he said, "is not a public house, but it is mine, for it has come to me from Ulysses."

〔U0516 quote〕
求婚者十分惱怒，安提諾俄斯卻勸住了他們。「我們就忍一忍吧，」他說，「要是宙斯准許，我們早就要了他的命了。」這時正逢阿波羅的節日，城裡的人正抬著獻給這位神的神聖百牲祭品，在街上巡行。
> The suitors were very angry but Antinous checked them. "Let us put up with it," said he; "if Jove had permitted, we should have been the death of him ere now." Meanwhile, it being the festival of Apollo, the people of the town were bearing his holy hecatomb about the streets.

〔U0517 quote〕
僕人分給奧德修斯的份，和分給別人的一樣多，因為忒勒馬科斯這樣吩咐過。不久，一個名叫克忒西波斯的求婚者注意到了，便說：「我看這位客人分到的，跟誰都一樣好。我要再送他一份更好的，好讓他有東西打賞給澡堂的女僕，或是府裡別的什麼僕人。」──說著便抓起一隻牛蹄，朝奧德修斯的頭扔過去。
> The servants gave Ulysses an equal portion with what they gave the others, for Telemachus had so bidden them. Presently one of the suitors named Ctesippus observed this and said, "I see the stranger has as good a portion as any one else. I will give him a better, that he may have something to give the bath-woman or some other of the servants in the house"—and with this he flung a cow's heel at Ulysses' head.

〔U0518 quote〕
奧德修斯露出一抹陰沉的、薩丁尼亞式的[註36]冷笑，把頭一低，牛蹄從他頭頂飛過，砸在牆上。忒勒馬科斯聲色俱厲地斥責了克忒西波斯，眾人一時鴉雀無聲，直到阿革拉俄斯出來打圓場，說：「忒勒馬科斯說得公道，我們就別回嘴了。不過我還是要勸他，好好跟他母親談一談，告訴她：只要奧德修斯還有一絲回來的可能，她遲遲不再嫁，也沒什麼不近情理；可是如今他已絕無歸來之望，你若想安安穩穩享有自己的家業，就叫她嫁給我們當中最優秀、出價也最豐厚的那一位吧。」
> Ulysses smiled with a grim Sardinian[註36] smile, and bowed his head so that the heel passed over it and hit the wall. Telemachus rebuked Ctesippus very fiercely, and all were silent till Agelaus tried to calm them saying, "What Telemachus has said is just: let us not answer. Nevertheless I would urge him to talk quietly with his mother and tell her that as long as there was any chance of Ulysses coming back there was nothing unreasonable in her deferring a second marriage; but there is now no hope of his return, and if you would enjoy your own in peace, tell her to marry the best man among us and the one who will make her the most advantageous offer."

〔U0519 quote〕
「不，」忒勒馬科斯答道，「耽擱她婚事的不是我。我催她再嫁，可是我不能、也不願逼她。」
> "Nay," answered Telemachus, "it is not I that delay her marriage. I urge her to it, but I cannot and will not force her."

〔U0520 quote〕
於是雅典娜使求婚者爆出一陣勉強而歇斯底里的狂笑，他們正吃著的肉全都沾滿了血污。他們眼裡噙滿淚水，心頭壓著可怕的不祥預感。忒俄克呂墨諾斯看出大事不妙，便說：「不幸的人哪，你們這是怎麼了？一層黑暗的屍布從頭到腳罩住了你們，你們臉頰上淚水漣漣；空中滿是哀號的聲音；四壁和屋樑都在滴血；迴廊的大門和門外的院子裡擠滿了鬼魂，成群結隊奔下地府的長夜；太陽從天上給抹去了，一片令萬物枯萎的陰霾籠罩著整片大地。」
> Then Minerva made the suitors break out into a forced hysterical laughter, and the meats which they were eating became all smirched with blood. Their eyes were filled with tears and their hearts were oppressed with terrible forebodings. Theoclymenus saw that all was wrong, and said, "Unhappy men, what is it that ails you? There is a shroud of darkness drawn over you from head to foot, your cheeks are wet with tears; the air is alive with wailing voices; the walls and roof beams drip blood; the gate of the cloisters, and the yard beyond them are full of ghosts trooping down into the night of hell; the sun is blotted out from heaven, and a blighting gloom is over all the land."

〔U0521 quote〕
求婚者都笑他，歐律馬科斯說：「你要是覺得這裡這麼黑，我們最好派個人送你到外頭空曠的地方去。」
> The suitors laughed at him, and Eurymachus said, "If you find it so dark here, we had better send a man with you to take you out into the open."

〔U0522 quote〕
「我有眼睛，」他答道，「能替我引路；也有雙腳，能帶我躲開我所看見的、懸在你們每一個人頭上的厄運。」說罷他便離開他們，回珀萊俄斯家去了。
> "I have eyes," he answered, "that can guide, and feet that can take me from the doom that I see overhanging every single one of you." On this he left them and went back to the house of Piræus.

〔U0523 quote〕
這時一個求婚者說：「忒勒馬科斯，你招待客人的運氣真是差透了。你最好把那個外鄉人和這個傢伙一起裝上船，運到西克洛伊人那裡賣掉。」忒勒馬科斯沒有答話，只是盯著他父親，等著父親向他打什麼暗號。
> Then one of the suitors said, "Telemachus, you are very unfortunate in your guests. You had better ship both the stranger and this man off to the Sicels and sell them." Telemachus made no answer, but kept his eye on his father for any signal that he might make him.

〔U0524 quote〕
潘妮洛碧叫人把她的座椅擺在可以俯瞰迴廊的地方，所有的經過都聽在耳裡。這頓午餐既豐盛又可口，席間笑聲不斷，因為他們宰了許多牲口；可是他們萬萬料想不到，女神和一位壯士正在為他們準備一頓多麼可怕的晚餐。
> Penelope had had a seat placed for her overlooking the cloister, and heard all that had passed. The dinner had been good and plentiful and there had been much laughter, for they had slaughtered many victims, but little did they guess the terrible supper which the goddess and a strong man were preparing for them.

〔U0525 quote〕
*比弓與穿斧*
> *The trial of the bow and of the axes*.

〔U0526 quote〕
於是雅典娜讓潘妮洛碧心中生出一個念頭：讓求婚者比試那張弓，爭奪鐵製的獎品。她便上樓取來庫房的鑰匙；奧德修斯的金、銅、鐵等財寶都收在那間庫房裡，那張大弓也在那裡──那是歐律托斯之子伊菲托斯送給他的，歐律托斯在世時一直常用它。她在侍女陪同下來到庫房，打開門，從木釘上取下那張弓，連同裝滿致命箭矢的箭囊一起拿去給求婚者，侍女們則抬來那口箱子，裡面裝著奧德修斯贏來的許多鐵製獎品。接著，她仍由兩名侍女陪著，站在撐著迴廊屋頂的一根承重柱旁，對求婚者說：他們當中誰能最輕鬆地為奧德修斯的弓上弦，並一箭射穿十二個孔──十二個斧頭就是靠這些孔裝在斧柄上的──她就嫁給誰。
> Then Minerva put it in Penelope's mind to let the suitors compete for the bow and for a prize of iron. So she went upstairs and got the key of the store room, where Ulysses' treasures of gold, copper, and iron were kept, as also the mighty bow which Iphitus son of Eurytus had given him, and which had been in common use by Eurytus as long as he was alive. Hither she went attended by her women, and when she had unlocked the door she took the bow down from its peg and carried it, with its quiverfull of deadly arrows, to the suitors, while her maids brought the chest in which were the many prizes of iron that Ulysses had won. Then, still attended by her two maidens, she stood by one of the bearing-posts that supported the roof of the cloister, and told the suitors she would marry the man among them who could string Ulysses' bow most easily, and send an arrow through the twelve holes by which twelve axe-heads were fastened on to their handles.

〔U0527 quote〕
說罷，她把弓交到歐邁俄斯手裡，吩咐他照她說的讓求婚者比試。歐邁俄斯接弓時哭了，在一旁看著的菲羅提俄斯也哭了；安提諾俄斯便罵他們是一對鄉巴佬。
> So saying she gave the bow into the hands of Eumæus and bade him let the suitors compete as she had said. Eumæus wept as he took it, and so did Philœtius who was looking on, whereon Antinous scolded them for a couple of country bumpkins.

〔U0528 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯說他也要參加比試；要是他成功了，他絕不讓母親跟著第二個丈夫離開這個家，把他一個人丟下。說著，他挖了一道筆直的長溝，把斧頭在溝裡排成一列，再把周圍的土踩實，讓斧頭立穩；大家看他把斧頭立得如此精準，都很驚訝，因為他以前從沒見過這種東西。[註37]斧頭擺好之後，他站在石砌地面上試著為弓上弦，一連三次都失敗了。不過第四次他本來是會成功的，要不是奧德修斯向他示意，叫他別再試了。於是他把弓和箭都放下，回到座位上。
> Telemachus said that he too should compete, and that if he was successful he should certainly not allow his mother to leave her home with a second husband, while he remained alone. So saying he dug a long trench quite straight, set the axes in a line within it, and stamped the earth about them to keep them steady; every one was surprised to see how accurately he fixed them, considering that he had never seen anything of the kind before.[註37] Having set the axes duly, he stood on the stone pavement, and tried to string the bow, but failed three times. He would, however, have succeeded the fourth time, if Ulysses had not made him a sign that he was not to try any more. So he laid both bow and arrow down and took his seat.

〔U0529 quote〕
「那麼，」安提諾俄斯說，「就從司酒人開始斟酒的地方起，大家依次輪流，由左往右。」於是雷俄得斯第一個上前。他是他們的獻祭祭司，坐在調酒缸旁邊的牆角裡；不過他向來看不慣求婚者的惡行。他沒能為弓上弦，便說這張弓實在太難上弦，會奪走他們當中許多人的性命和心魂──為了這句話，安提諾俄斯狠狠斥責了他。
> "Then," said Antinous, "begin at the place where the cupbearer begins, and let each take his turn, going from left to right." On this Leiodes came forward. He was their sacrificial priest, and sat in the angle of the wall hard by the mixing bowl; but he had always set his face against the wicked conduct of the suitors. When he had failed to string the bow he said it was so hard to string that it would rob many a man among them of life and heart—for which saying Antinous rebuked him bitterly.

〔U0530 quote〕
「墨蘭提俄斯（Melantheus），拿火來，再從屋裡拿一大塊油脂來，」他對墨蘭提俄斯（Melanthius）說［*sic*，原文如此］，「我們好把弓烤熱，抹上油。」他們照辦了，可是儘管許多人都試過，誰也拉不上弦。只剩下他們的兩個首領，安提諾俄斯和歐律馬科斯。
> "Bring some fire, Melantheus, and a wheel of fat from inside the house," said he to Melanthius, [*sic*] "that we may warm the bow and grease it." So they did this, but though many tried they could none of them string it. There remained only Antinous and Eurymachus who were their ring leaders.

〔U0531 quote〕
牧豬奴和牧牛人菲羅提俄斯這時走出前院，奧德修斯跟在他們後面；走過外院之後，奧德修斯試探了他們一番，確信他們忠心耿耿，便表明了身分，還把腿上的傷疤給他們看。兩人欣喜若狂；奧德修斯說：「你們跟在我後面，一個一個回去，照我的吩咐辦。其他求婚者是不會讓我拿到那張弓的；可是歐邁俄斯，弓一到你手上，你就把它拿來給我，再叫女人們把自己關在房裡。她們待在裡面，要是聽見有人呻吟或喧鬧，叫她們只管做自己的活兒，不要出來。菲羅提俄斯，把外院大門牢牢閂上的事，就交給你了。」他隨即走進去，回到剛才離開的座位上。
> The swineherd and the stockman Philœtius then went outside the forecourt, and Ulysses followed them; when they had got beyond the outer yard Ulysses sounded them, and having satisfied himself that they were loyal he revealed himself and shewed them the scar on his leg. They were overjoyed, and Ulysses said, "Go back one by one after me, and follow these instructions. The other suitors will not be for letting me have the bow, but do you, Eumæus, when you have got it in your hands, bring it to me, and tell the women to shut themselves into their room. If the sound of groaning or uproar reaches any of them when they are inside, tell them to stick to their work and not come out. I leave it to you. Philœtius, to fasten the gate of the outer court securely." He then went inside, and resumed the seat that he had left.

〔U0532 quote〕
歐律馬科斯接著試著上弦，也失敗了。「娶不到潘妮洛碧，」他說，「我倒不怎麼在乎，伊薩卡和別處多的是女人。叫我難過的是，比起我們的祖先，我們竟是這樣一群軟弱無能的人。」
> Eurymachus now tried to string the bow but failed. "I do not so much mind," he said, "about not marrying Penelope, for there are plenty of other women in Ithaca and elsewhere. What grieves me is the fact of our being such a feeble folk as compared with our forefathers."

〔U0533 quote〕
安提諾俄斯提醒他，今天是阿波羅的節日。「在這樣的日子裡，」他說，「誰還能射箭？斧頭就留在原地吧──不會有人拿走的；我們也把墨蘭提俄斯能帶來的最好的山羊獻祭給阿波羅，明天再接著比。」
> Antinous reminded him that it was the festival of Apollo. "Who," said he, "can shoot on such a day as this? Let us leave the axes where they are—no one will take them; let us also sacrifice to Apollo the best goats Melanthius can bring us, and resume the contest tomorrow."

〔U0534 quote〕
這時奧德修斯狡猾地要求，讓他也試一試，看自己是否還像從前那樣強壯，為此請他們把弓交到他手上。求婚者大為光火，潘妮洛碧卻堅持要讓奧德修斯拿弓；她說，就算他真把弦上好了，以為她會嫁給他，也是荒唐可笑的；不過她會送他一件襯衣和一件斗篷、一支標槍、一把劍和一雙涼鞋，他想去哪裡，她都會派人送他去。
> Ulysses then cunningly urged that he might be allowed to try whether he was as strong a man as he used to be, and that the bow should be placed in his hands for this purpose. The suitors were very angry, but Penelope insisted that Ulysses should have the bow; if he succeeded in stringing it she said it was absurd to suppose that she would marry him; but she would give him a shirt and cloak, a javelin, sword, and a pair of sandals, and she would send him wherever he might want to go.

〔U0535 quote〕
「母親，弓是我的，」忒勒馬科斯說，「我要是高興把它送給這個人，送了就是送了。您回屋裡去，管好您自己分內的事吧。」
> "The bow, mother, is mine," said Telemachus, "and if I choose to give it this man out and out I shall give it him. Go within the house and mind your own proper duties."

〔U0536 quote〕
潘妮洛碧滿心驚異，帶著侍女回到屋裡，上樓進了房間，為她親愛的丈夫哭泣，直到雅典娜把甜美的睡意灑在她眼上。
> Penelope went back, with her women, wondering into the house, and going upstairs into her room she wept for her dear husband till Minerva shed sweet sleep over her eyes.

〔U0537 quote〕
歐邁俄斯正要把弓拿給奧德修斯，求婚者卻把他嚇住了，他便想把弓放下；直到忒勒馬科斯威脅說，他要是不立刻拿過去，就用石頭把他砸回農莊去，他這才把弓交給了奧德修斯。接著他把歐律克蕾亞叫到一旁，吩咐她把女人們關起來，就算聽到呻吟或喧鬧，也不要放她們出來。她便把她們關了起來。
> Eumæus was about to take the bow to Ulysses, but the suitors frightened him and he was for putting it down, till Telemachus threatened to stone him back to his farm if he did not bring it on at once; he therefore gave the bow to Ulysses. Then he called Euryclea aside and told her to shut the women up, and not to let them out if they heard any groans or uproar. She therefore shut them up.

〔U0538 quote〕
這時菲羅提俄斯溜了出去，用一條碰巧放在門邊、紙莎草纖維搓成的船纜，把外院的正門牢牢繫住。辦完之後，他回到座位上，目不轉睛地盯著奧德修斯；奧德修斯正仔仔細細地察看那張弓，看它每個部分是否都完好無損。
> At this point Philœtius slipped out and secured the main gate of the outer court with a ship's cable of Byblus fibre that happened to be lying beside it. This done, he returned to his seat and kept his eye on Ulysses, who was examining the bow with great care to see whether it was sound in all its parts.

〔U0539 quote〕
「這傢伙，」求婚者說，「是個玩弓的老行家；說不定他家裡也有一把一樣的，要不就是想照樣做一把──瞧這老無賴擺弄得多麼在行。」
> "This man," said the suitors, "is some old bow-fancier; perhaps he has got one like it at home, or wants to make one, so cunningly does the old rascal handle it."

〔U0540 quote〕
奧德修斯察看完畢，便為弓上了弦，輕鬆得就像歌者給豎琴換上一根新弦。他試了試弓弦，弦在他手下鳴響，宛如燕子的叫聲。他拿起一支放在桌旁、沒有收進箭囊的箭，把箭尾的凹口扣上弓弦，就坐在座位上，一箭射穿了所有斧頭的柄孔，直飛到外面的院子裡。
> Ulysses, having finished his scrutiny, strung the bow as easily as a bard puts a new string on to his lyre. He tried the string and it sang under his hand like the cry of a swallow. He took an arrow that was lying out of its quiver by his table, placed the notch on the string, and from his seat sent the arrow through the handle-holes of all the axes and outside into the yard.

〔U0541 quote〕
「忒勒馬科斯，」他說，「你的客人沒有給你丟臉。現在是求婚者吃晚飯的時候了，飯後再唱唱歌、彈彈豎琴，好好樂一樂。」說著，他向忒勒馬科斯使了個眼色；忒勒馬科斯便佩上劍，握緊長矛，全副武裝地站在父親的座位旁。
> "Telemachus," said he, "your guest has not disgraced you. It is now time for the suitors to have their supper, and to take their pleasure afterwards with song and playing on the lyre." So saying he made a sign to Telemachus, who girded on his sword, grasped his spear, and stood armed beside his father's seat.

〔U0542 quote〕
*殺戮求婚者*
> *The killing of the suitors.*

〔U0543 quote〕
奧德修斯扯掉身上的破衣，帶著弓和裝滿箭的箭囊，一躍跳上寬闊的石砌地面，[註38]把箭倒在腳前的地上，說道：「比試結束了。現在我要看看，阿波羅肯不肯讓我射中另一個還沒有人瞄準過的靶子。」
> Ulysses tore off his rags, and sprang on the broad pavement,[註38] with his bow and his quiver full of arrows. He shed the arrows on to the ground at his feet and said, "The contest is at an end. I will now see whether Apollo will vouchsafe me to hit another mark which no man has yet aimed at."

〔U0544 quote〕
他一邊說，一邊瞄準了安提諾俄斯。箭射中了他的咽喉，他往後一倒，一股濃稠的血從鼻孔噴湧而出。他一腳把桌子踢開，打翻了桌上的東西，麵包和肉都掉到地上，沾滿了污穢。求婚者頓時鬧成一團，朝牆上張望著要找盔甲，卻一件也沒有。「外鄉人，」他們叫道，「你這樣射殺人，要付出慘重代價的。你死定了。」可是他們還不明白，奧德修斯是故意殺死安提諾俄斯的。
> He took aim at Antinous as he spoke. The arrow struck 8 him in the throat, so that he fell over and a thick stream of blood gushed from his nostrils. He kicked his table from him and upset the things on it, whereby the bread and meats were all soiled as they fell over on to the ground. The suitors were instantly in an uproar, and looked towards the walls for armour, but there was none. "Stranger," they cried, "you shall pay dearly for shooting people down in this way. You are a doomed man." But they did not yet understand that Ulysses had killed Antinous on purpose.

〔U0545 quote〕
奧德修斯怒目瞪著他們，說：「狗東西，你們以為我不會從特洛伊回來了嗎？你們揮霍我的家產，玷污我家中的女人，我還活著，你們就向我的妻子求婚；你們既不怕神，也不怕人，現在你們都得死。」
> Ulysses glared at them and said, "Dogs, did you think that I should not return from Troy? You have wasted my substance, you have violated the women of my house, you have wooed my wife while I was still alive, you have feared neither god nor man, and now you shall die."

〔U0546 quote〕
只有歐律馬科斯開口回答。「如果你真是奧德修斯，」他說，「那我們確實大大虧待了你。這全是安提諾俄斯幹的。他根本不是真想娶潘妮洛碧：他想殺掉你的兒子，自己當伊薩卡的頭號人物。如今他已經死了；那就饒了你這些子民的性命吧，我們會全數賠償你。」
> Eurymachus alone answered. "If you are Ulysses," said he, "we have done you great wrong. It was all Antinous's doing. He never really wanted to marry Penelope: he wanted to kill your son and to be chief man in Ithaca. He is no more; then spare the lives of your people and we will pay you all."

〔U0547 quote〕
奧德修斯又怒目瞪著他，說：「不把你們一個不剩全部殺光，我絕不住手。你們要嘛就打，要嘛就盡力逃，要嘛就死──而逃，你們既逃不掉，也休想逃。」
> Ulysses again glared at him and said, "I will not stay my hand till I have slain one and all of you. You must fight, or fly as you can, or die—and fly you neither can nor shall."

〔U0548 quote〕
歐律馬科斯便說：「朋友們，這人是不會饒過我們的。我們跟他拚了吧。拔出劍來，把桌子舉在身前當盾牌。一齊向他衝過去，把他從石砌地面和門口趕走。這樣我們就能衝進城裡去求救。」
> Eurymachus then said, "My friends, this man will give us no quarter. Let us show fight. Draw your swords and hold the tables up in front of you as shields. Have at him with a rush, and drive him from the pavement and from the door. We could then get through into the town and call for help."

〔U0549 quote〕
他一邊說，一邊向前撲去，奧德修斯一箭射進他的心臟，他便蜷起身子，整個人倒在自己的桌上。他在臨死的痛苦中以額頭猛撞地面，杯子和桌上的肉食也全都跟著翻落在地上。
> While he spoke and was springing forward, Ulysses sent an arrow into his heart and he fell doubled up over his table. The cup and all the meats went over on to the ground as he smote the earth with his forehead in the agonies of death.

接著安菲諾摩斯向奧德修斯衝去，想把他從門口逼開，但忒勒馬科斯繞到他背後，一矛刺穿了他。他把長矛留在屍體裡，飛奔回父親身邊。「父親，」他說，「讓我去拿盔甲來，給您和我，也給歐邁俄斯和菲羅提俄斯。」「快去拿，」奧德修斯答道，「趁我的箭還夠用；動作要快，不然我單槍匹馬，他們可能會把我從門口逼開。」
> Amphinomus then made for Ulysses to try and dislodge him from the door, but Telemachus got behind him, and struck him through. He left his spear in the body and flew back to his father's side; "Father," said he, "let me bring armour for you and me, as well as for Eumæus and Philœtius." "Run and fetch it," answered Ulysses, "while my arrows hold out; be quick, or they may get me away from the door when I am single-handed."

〔U0551 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯到庫房拿來四面盾牌、八支長矛和四頂頭盔。他自己披掛起來，歐邁俄斯和菲羅提俄斯也一樣，然後站到奧德修斯身旁。只要箭還夠，奧德修斯就把求婚者一個接一個射倒，屍首層層疊疊；箭用完了，他便把弓靠在宅邸盡頭、門口旁邊的牆上，自己披掛起來。

As long as his arrows held out Ulysses shot the suitors down thick and threefold, but when they failed him he stood the bow against the end wall of the house hard by the door way, and armed himself.

> Now there was a trap-door (see plan, and *f* on p. 17) on the wall, while at one end of the pavement there was an exit, closed by a good strong door and leading out into a narrow passage; Ulysses told Philœtius to stand by this door and keep it, for only one person could attack it at a time. Then Agelaus shouted out, "Go up, somebody, to the trap-door and tell the people what is going on; they would come in and help us."

〔U0553 quote〕
「這可不行，」墨蘭提俄斯答道，「窄巷的出口離街上通往外院的入口近得危險。一個勇士就能擋住任何數目的人進來。不過我可以從庫房給你們拿武器來，我敢說他們一定是把武器放在那裡了。」說著，他便從後面的通道進了庫房，給求婚者拿來十二面盾牌和同樣數目的頭盔；奧德修斯看見求婚者在披掛，心裡開始發虛，對忒勒馬科斯說：「屋裡有女人在幫求婚者──要不然就是墨蘭提俄斯。」
> "This may not be," answered Melanthius, "the mouth of the narrow passage is dangerously near the entrance from the street into the outer court. One brave man could prevent any number from getting in, but I will bring you arms from the store-room, for I am sure it is there that they have put them." As he spoke he went by back passages to the store-room, and brought the suitors twelve shields and the same number of helmets; when Ulysses saw the suitors arming his heart began to fail him, and he said to Telemachus, "Some of the women inside are helping the suitors—or else it is Melanthius."

〔U0554 quote〕
忒勒馬科斯說這是他的錯，因為他沒有關上庫房的門。「歐邁俄斯，」他又說，「去把門關上；看看是哪個女人，還是多利俄斯的兒子墨蘭提俄斯。」
> Telemachus said that it was his fault, for he had left the store-room door open. "Go, Eumæus," he added, "and close it; see whether it is one of the women, or Melanthius, son of Dolius."

〔U0555 quote〕
墨蘭提俄斯正要再回去拿盔甲，被歐邁俄斯看見了，便告訴奧德修斯；奧德修斯說：「你和菲羅提俄斯跟著他；把他的手腳反綁起來，丟進庫房；再把他吊在一根承重柱上，一直吊到貼近椽木，讓他慢慢受罪，拖著死不了。」
> Melanthius was now going back for more armour when Eumæus saw him and told Ulysses, who said, "Follow him, you and Philœtius; bind his hands and feet behind him, and throw him into the store-room; then string him up to a bearing-post till he is close to the rafters, that he may linger on in agony."

〔U0556 quote〕
兩人到庫房抓住了墨蘭提俄斯。他們用一條叫人吃盡苦頭的繩子捆住他，照奧德修斯的吩咐把他吊了起來。歐邁俄斯還祝他晚安，然後兩人回到奧德修斯身邊。雅典娜也化作門托爾的模樣來到他們當中；但奧德修斯斷定那就是雅典娜。求婚者看見她，大為惱怒。「門托爾，」他們叫道，「你要為此賠上性命，我們還要沒收你在世上的全部家產。」
> The men went to the store-room and caught Melanthius. They bound him in a painful bond and strung him up as Ulysses had told them. Eumæus wished him a good night and the two men returned to the side of Ulysses. Minerva also joined them, having assumed the form of Mentor; but Ulysses felt sure it was Minerva. The suitors were very angry when they saw her; "Mentor," they cried, "you shall pay for this with your life, and we will confiscate all you have in the world."

〔U0557 quote〕
這話惹得雅典娜大怒，她把奧德修斯狠狠數落了一頓。「你的本事，」她說，「已經不如在特洛伊時了。怎麼到了自己的地盤上，反倒沒那麼勇猛了？來吧，好傢伙，看看門托爾怎樣替你作戰，報答你的種種恩惠。」但她還不打算這麼快就讓他得勝，便飛上一根椽木，化作一隻燕子停在那裡。[註39]
> This made Minerva furious, and she rated Ulysses roundly. "Your prowess," said she, "is no longer what it was at Troy. How comes it that you are less valiant now that you are on your own ground? Come on, my good fellow, and see how Mentor will fight for you and requite you for your many kindnesses." But she did not mean to give him the victory just yet, so she flew up to one of the rafters and sat there in the form of a swallow.[註39]

〔U0558 quote〕
搏鬥仍在繼續。「朋友們，」阿革拉俄斯說，「他很快就撐不住了。瞧，門托爾除了說大話，什麼也沒幫他做，就丟下他走了。別一齊向他擲矛，先讓六個人擲。」
> The struggle still continued. "My friends," said Agelaus, "he will soon have to leave off. See how Mentor has left him after doing nothing for him except brag. Do not aim at him all at once, but six of you throw your spears first."

〔U0559 quote〕
他們照辦了，但雅典娜使他們的長矛全都落空。接著奧德修斯和另外三人擲出長矛，每人各殺一人。求婚者驚恐地退到一個角落裡，四人趁機衝上前去，拔回了自己的武器。求婚者再次擲矛，這一回安菲墨東當真從忒勒馬科斯的手腕上削下一小塊表皮，克忒西波斯的矛也擦過了歐邁俄斯盾牌上方的肩膀。接著輪到奧德修斯和他的人，他們每一支矛都殺了一個人。

The suitors drew back in fear into a corner, whereon the four sprang forward and regained their weapons. The suitors again threw, and this time Amphimedon really did take a piece of the top skin from Telemachus's wrist, and Ctesippus just grazed Eumæus's shoulder above his shield. It was now the turn of Ulysses and his men, and each of their spears killed a man.

> Then Minerva from high on the roof held up her deadly ægis, and struck the suitors with panic, whereon Ulysses and his men fell upon them and smote them on every side. They made a horrible groaning as their brains were being battered in, and the ground seethed with their blood. Leiodes implored Ulysses to spare his life, but Ulysses would give him no quarter.

這時歌人費彌俄斯也來求饒。他站在靠近暗門的那一邊，決意去抱奧德修斯的膝頭，便把豎琴放在調酒缸和那張鑲銀釘的高椅之間的地上。「饒了我吧，」他叫道，「您要是殺了我這樣的歌人，日後會後悔的。我是個自出機杼的創作者，上天把種種靈感都賜給了我。別急著砍我的頭。忒勒馬科斯會告訴您，我給求婚者唱歌，只是因為他們逼我。」

He was standing near towards the trap-door, and resolving to embrace Ulysses' knees, he laid his lyre on the ground between the mixing-bowl and the high silver-studded seat. "Spare me," he cried, "you will be sorry for it afterwards if you kill such a bard as I am. I am an original composer, and heaven visits me with every kind of inspiration. Do not be in such a hurry to cut my head off. Telemachus will tell you that I only sang to the suitors because they forced me."

> "Hold," cried Telemachus to his father, "do him no hurt, he is guiltless; and we will spare Medon, too, who was always good to me when I was a boy, unless Eumæus or Philœtius has already killed him, or you happened to fall in with him yourself."

> "Here I am, my dear Sir," said Medon, coming out from 361 under a freshly flayed heifer's hide[註40] which had concealed him; "tell your father, or he will kill me in his rage against the suitors for having wasted his substance and been so disrespectful to yourself." Ulysses smiled, and told them to go outside into the outer court till the killing should be over. So they went, but they were still very much frightened. Ulysses then went all over the court to see if there were any who had concealed themselves, or were not yet killed, but there was no one; they were all as dead as fish lying in a hot sun upon the beach.

When she saw the corpses she was beginning to raise a shout of triumph, but Ulysses checked her: "Old woman," said he, "rejoice in silence; it is an unholy thing to vaunt over dead men. When you have thoroughly cleansed the cloisters take the women outside and run them through with your swords."

女人們下樓來，哭哭啼啼，哀號不已。她們先把屍體抬出去，在外院的門樓裡一具靠著一具地堆起來。奧德修斯指揮她們，盯著她們不許拖延。屍體抬完了，她們又用海綿和水把所有的桌子、座椅洗乾淨；忒勒馬科斯和另外兩人則把地上的血和污泥鏟起來，由女人們全部運到外面。等整座庭院都徹底清理乾淨，他們便把那些女人帶出去，圍堵在圓頂屋和外院牆壁之間的狹窄空間裡。忒勒馬科斯在這裡決定把她們吊死，因為這比用劍刺死更為可恥。於是他把一條船纜牢牢繫在撐著圓頂屋屋頂的一根粗壯承重柱上，繞了一圈，叫女人們一個接一個把頭伸進繩套裡。然後他把繩子高高拉起，讓她們的腳都碰不到地。她們的腳痙攣地踢蹬了一陣，但為時不久。
> The women came down weeping and wailing bitterly. First they carried the dead bodies out, and propped them against one another in the gatehouse of the outer court. Ulysses ordered them about and saw that they lost no time. When they had carried the bodies out they cleaned all the tables and seats with sponges and water, while Telemachus and the two others shovelled up the blood and dirt from the ground and the women carried it all outside. When they had thus thoroughly cleaned the whole court, they took the women out and hemmed them up in the narrow space between the vaulted room and the wall of the outer yard. Here Telemachus determined to hang them, as a more dishonourable death than stabbing. He therefore made a ship's rope fast to a strong bearing-post supporting the roof of the vaulted room, and threw it round, making the women put their heads in the nooses one after another. He then drew the rope high up, so that none of their feet might touch the ground. They kicked convulsively for a while, but not for very long.

〔U0569 quote〕
至於墨蘭提俄斯，他們把他押過迴廊，帶到外院。他們在那裡割下他的鼻子和耳朵；掏出他的內臟，生著丟給狗吃；接著又砍下他的手腳。完事之後，他們洗了手腳，回到屋裡。「去，」奧德修斯對歐律克蕾亞說，「給我拿硫磺來，我要燒硫磺淨化迴廊。再去叫潘妮洛碧帶著她的侍女和府裡的女人們到這裡來。」
> As for Melanthius they took him through the cloisters into the outer court. There they cut off his nose and ears; they drew out his vitals and gave them to the dogs, raw; then they cut off his hands and feet. When they had done this they washed their hands and feet, and went back into the house. "Go," said Ulysses, to Euryclea, "and bring me sulphur that I may burn it and purify the cloisters. Go, moreover, and bid Penelope come here with her gentlewomen and the women of the house."

〔U0570 quote〕
「讓我先給您拿一件乾淨的襯衣和斗篷來吧，」歐律克蕾亞說，「別再穿著這身破衣了，這樣不成體統。」
> "Let me first bring you a clean shirt and cloak," said Euryclea, "do not keep those rags on any longer, it is not right."

〔U0571 quote〕
「給我生火，」奧德修斯答道。她依言照辦，拿來硫磺，他便用它把內院、外院和迴廊都徹底淨化了一遍。接著歐律克蕾亞把女人們從她們的住處帶來，她們擁到奧德修斯身邊，親吻他的頭和肩膀，握住他的雙手。這讓他很想哭，因為她們每一個他都記得。
> "Light me a fire," answered Ulysses, and she obeyed and brought him sulphur, wherewith he thoroughly purified both the inner and outer court, as well as the cloisters. Then Euryclea brought the women from their apartment, and they pressed round Ulysses, kissing his head and shoulders, and taking hold of his hands. It made him feel as if he should like to weep, for he remembered every one of them.

〔U0572 quote〕
*潘妮洛碧下樓來見奧德修斯，終於確信他就是她的丈夫，便與他一同回到他們從前的臥房──早晨，奧德修斯、忒勒馬科斯、菲羅提俄斯和歐邁俄斯前往拉厄爾特斯家*
> *Penelope comes down to see Ulysses, and being at last convinced that he is her husband, retires with him to their own old room—In the morning Ulysses, Telemachus, Philœtius, and Eumæus go to the house of Laertes.*

〔U0573 quote〕
歐律克蕾亞這時上樓，把發生的事告訴潘妮洛碧。「醒醒，我親愛的孩子，」她說，「奧德修斯終於回家了，還把那些在家裡搗亂、吃光他家產、欺負他兒子的求婚者都殺了。」
> Euryclea now went upstairs and told Penelope what had happened. "Wake up, my dear child," said she, "Ulysses is come home at last and has killed the suitors who were giving so much trouble in the house, eating up his estate and ill-treating his son."

〔U0574 quote〕
「我的好奶媽，」潘妮洛碧答道，「你一定是瘋了。神明有時會讓非常明理的人失去理智，又讓愚蠢的人變得明理。他們一定就是這樣對付你的。再說，你把我從丈夫離家以來睡得最沉的一覺中吵醒了。回女人們的房間去吧；換了是別人，我早就狠狠罵她一頓了。」
> "My good nurse," answered Penelope, "you must be mad. The gods sometimes send very sensible people out of their minds, and make foolish people sensible. This is what they must have been doing to you. Moreover, you have waked me from the soundest sleep that I have enjoyed since my husband left me. Go back into the women's room; if it had been any one but you, I should have given her a severe scolding."

〔U0575 quote〕
歐律克蕾亞仍然堅持她說的是真的；潘妮洛碧再三追問，她便把自己所知道的殺死求婚者的經過全說了。「我下樓的時候，」她說，「看見奧德修斯站在屍堆中間；您要是看見他渾身濺滿鮮血和污穢、活像一頭獅子的模樣，一定會很痛快。不過屍體現在都堆在門樓裡了，他叫我來帶您去見他。」
> Euryclea still maintained that what she had said was true, and in answer to Penelope's further questions told her as much as she knew about the killing of the suitors. "When I came down," she said, "I found Ulysses standing over the corpses; you would have enjoyed it, if you had seen him all bespattered with blood and filth, and looking just like a lion. But the corpses are now piled up in the gatehouse, and he has sent me to bring you to him."

> "My dear nurse," answered Penelope, "however wise you may be, you can hardly fathom the counsels of the gods. Still I will go and find my son that I may see the corpses of the suitors, and the man who has killed them."

> On this she came down into the cloister and took her seat opposite Ulysses, in the fire-light, by the wall at right angles to that by which she had entered, while her husband sat by one of the bearing-posts of the cloister, looking down and waiting to hear what she would say. For a long time she sat as one lost in amazement and said nothing, till Telemachus upbraided her for her coldness. "Your heart," he said, "was always hard as a stone."

Telemachus, Philœtius, and Eumæus now left off dancing, and made the women leave off also. Then they laid themselves down to sleep in the cloisters.

〔U0591 quote〕
兩人同床共枕之後，潘妮洛碧告訴奧德修斯，眼看家裡擠滿了邪惡的求婚者，為了她宰殺了那麼多牛羊，喝掉了那麼多桶酒，她吃了多少苦。奧德修斯也把自己的全部經歷說給她聽，每件事都簡要提到，不但細述自己所受的苦，也說了他加諸別人的苦。她聽得津津有味，一直沒有睡著，直到他講完故事，沉沉睡去。
> When they were in bed together, Penelope told Ulysses how much she had had to bear in seeing the house filled with wicked suitors who had killed so many oxen and sheep on her account, and had drunk so many casks of wine. Ulysses in his turn told her the whole story of his adventures, touching briefly upon every point, and detailing not only his own sufferings but those he had inflicted upon other people. She was delighted to listen, and never went to sleep till he ended his story and dropped off into a profound slumber.

〔U0592 quote〕
雅典娜認為奧德修斯睡夠了，便讓黎明女神厄俄斯從俄刻阿諾斯的水中升起，奧德修斯也起身了。「妻子，」他對潘妮洛碧說，「既然我們終於重聚，你要照管好我家裡的財物。至於那些邪惡的求婚者吃掉的綿羊和山羊，我會從別人那裡強取許多回來，其餘的就逼本地人賠償。現在我要到鄉下我父親家去。太陽一出來，我殺死求婚者的事就會傳開。所以你上樓去，和你的侍女待在那裡。誰也別見，什麼也別問。」
> When Minerva thought that Ulysses had slept long enough she permitted Dawn to rise from the waters of Oceanus, and Ulysses got up. "Wife," said he to Penelope, "Now that we have at last come together again, take care of the property that is in my house. As for the sheep and goats that the wicked suitors have eaten, I will take many by force from other people, and will compel the men of the place to make good the rest. I will now go out to my father's house in the country. At sunrise it will get noised about that I have been killing the suitors. Go upstairs, therefore, and stay there with your waiting women. See nobody, and ask no questions."

他一邊說，一邊披上盔甲；他也叫醒其他人，吩咐他們武裝起來。接著他打開大門，眾人一齊出發。這時天已大亮，但雅典娜用黑暗把他們罩住，領著他們迅速出了城。
> As he spoke he girded on his armour; he roused the others also and bade them arm. He then undid the gate, and they all sallied forth. It was now daylight, but Minerva enshrouded them in darkness, and led them quickly out of the town.

> *The Ghosts of the suitors in Hades—Ulysses sees his father—is attacked by the friends of the suitors—Laertes kills Eupeithes—Peace is made between him and the people of Ithaca.*

〔U0595 quote〕
這時赫耳墨斯拿起那根美麗的金杖──他可以隨心所欲地用它讓人闔眼入睡，或把人喚醒──領著求婚者的鬼魂前往黑帝斯的宅邸；鬼魂們跟在後面，一路嗚咽啾啁。就像一隻蝙蝠從倒掛的群裡跌落時，蝙蝠們在大洞窟深處吱吱尖叫、四處亂飛──他們也這樣嗚咽尖叫著，由解憂者赫耳墨斯領著，下到幽暗的死亡居所。他們經過俄刻阿諾斯的水流和琉卡斯岩石，來到赫利俄斯之門和夢之國，隨即抵達阿福花草地；那些再也不能勞作的人的靈魂與影子，就住在那裡。
> Then Mercury took the fair golden wand with which he seals men's eyes in sleep or wakes them just as he pleases, and led the ghosts of the suitors to the house of Hades whining and gibbering as they followed. As bats fly squealing about the hollow of a great cave when one of them has fallen from the cluster in which they hang—even so did they whine and squeal as Mercury the healer of sorrow led them down into the dark abode of death. When they had passed the waters of Oceanus and the rock Leucas, they came to the gates of the Sun and the land of dreams, whereon they reached the meadow of asphodel where dwell the souls and shadows of men that can labour no more.

〔U0596 quote〕
他們在這裡遇見了阿基里斯、帕特羅克洛斯、安提洛科斯和埃阿斯的鬼魂，阿伽門農的鬼魂也過來加入他們。他們正交談時，赫耳墨斯帶著求婚者的鬼魂來到，阿伽門農的鬼魂認出了安菲墨東的鬼魂──他在伊薩卡時，安菲墨東曾招待過他；於是他問安菲墨東，這麼一大群俊秀的年輕人突然到來──而且全都年紀相仿──究竟是怎麼回事；安菲墨東便把整件事從頭到尾告訴了他。
> Here they came upon the ghosts of Achilles, Patroclus, Antilochus, and Ajax, and that of Agamemnon joined them. As these were conversing, Mercury came up with the ghosts of the suitors, and Agamemnon's ghost recognised that of Amphimedon who had been his host when he was in Ithaca; so he asked him what this sudden arrival of fine young men—all of an age too—might mean, and Amphimedon told him the whole story from first to last.

他們就這樣在大地深處的黑帝斯宅邸裡交談。這時奧德修斯一行人已經出了城，很快就到了拉厄爾特斯的農莊；這片農莊是他費盡千辛萬苦開墾出來的。他的房子就在這裡，四周圍著一圈披屋，替他幹活的奴隸在披屋裡吃飯睡覺；屋裡則有一個西克洛伊老婦人，在這處鄉間農莊照料他。
> Thus did they converse in the house of Hades deep within the bowels of the earth. Meanwhile Ulysses and the others passed out of the city and soon reached the farm of Laertes, which he had reclaimed with infinite labour. Here was his house with a lean-to running all round it, where the slaves who worked for him ate and slept, while inside the house there was an old Sicel woman, who looked after him in this his country farm.

〔U0598 quote〕
「你們進屋去，」奧德修斯對其他人說，「把最好的一頭豬宰了做午餐；我想試探一下我父親，看他認不認得出我。」
> "Go," said Ulysses to the others, "to the house, and kill the best pig you have for dinner; I wish to make trial of my father and see whether he will know me."

〔U0599 quote〕
說罷，他把盔甲交給歐邁俄斯和菲羅提俄斯，轉身走進葡萄園，在那裡找到了獨自一人、正在給一株葡萄藤鋤地的父親。他穿著一件骯髒的舊襯衣，補丁累累，破爛不堪；腿上纏著牛皮條，以防荊棘刮傷，手臂上套著皮袖子，以擋尖刺。他頭戴一頂山羊皮帽，一副愁苦不堪的樣子。
> So saying he gave his armour to Eumæus and Philœtius, and turned off into the vineyard, where he found his father alone, hoeing a vine. He had on a dirty old shirt, patched and very shabby; his legs were bound round with thongs of oxhide to keep out the brambles, and he wore sleeves of leather against the thorns. He had a goatskin cap on his head and was looking very woebegone.

〔U0600 quote〕
奧德修斯看見他如此憔悴、衰老、滿懷哀傷，便站在一棵高大的梨樹下哭了起來。他猶豫著是該擁抱他、親吻他，把自己回家的事全告訴他，還是先盤問他一番，看他怎麼說。考慮再三，他決定對父親耍個心眼，於是走到正彎著腰、在一株植物周圍挖土的父親跟前。
> When Ulysses saw him so worn, so old and full of sorrow, he stood still under a tall pear tree and began to weep. He doubted whether to embrace him, kiss him, and tell him all about his having come home, or whether he should first question him and see what he would say. On the whole he decided that he would be crafty with him, so he went up to his father who was bending down and digging about a plant.

〔U0601 quote〕
「老先生，」奧德修斯說，「我看您是位出色的園丁──您對這座園子可真是費心啊。這裡沒有一株植物──無論是無花果樹、葡萄藤、橄欖樹、梨樹，還是花圃──不帶著您悉心照料的痕跡。不過，我說一句話，希望您別見怪：您照顧園子，比照顧自己還周到。您年紀老邁，邋遢不潔，穿得又十分寒酸。您的主人這樣虧待您，不可能是因為您懶惰；說真的，您的相貌身材沒有半點奴僕的樣子，反倒顯出您出身高貴。照您的樣子，我倒要說您是那種該好好洗澡、好好吃飯、夜裡睡得舒舒服服的人，老年人本來就有權這樣過日子。可是請告訴我，老實告訴我，您是誰家的奴僕？您在誰的園子裡做活？還有一件事也請告訴我──我來到的這個地方，真的是伊薩卡嗎？我剛才碰到一個人，他說是；可他是個遲鈍的傢伙，我向他打聽，我從前住在這裡的一位老朋友是否還活著，他卻沒耐心聽我把話說完。我那位朋友說，他是阿爾克西俄斯之子拉厄爾特斯的兒子；他離開我時，我送了他許多厚禮。」
> "I see, Sir," said Ulysses, "that you are an excellent gardener—what pains you take with it to be sure. There is not a single plant, not a fig-tree, vine, olive, pear, nor flower-bed, but bears the traces of your attention. I trust, however, that you will not be offended if I say that you take better care of your garden than of yourself. You are old, unsavoury, and very meanly clad. It cannot be because you are idle that your master takes such poor care of you; indeed, your face and figure have nothing of the slave about them, but proclaim you of noble birth. I should have said you were one of those who should wash well, eat well, and lie soft at night as old men have a right to do. But tell me, and tell me true, whose bondsman are you, and in whose garden are you working? Tell me also about another matter—is this place that I have come to really Ithaca? I met a man just now who said so, but he was a dull fellow, and had not the patience to hear my story out when I was asking whether an old friend of mine who used to live here was still alive. My friend said he was the son of Laertes son of Arceisius, and I made him large presents on his leaving me."

〔U0602 quote〕
拉厄爾特斯哭了，回答說既然如此，他是再也收不回那些禮物了；不過要是奧德修斯還活著，這位客人一定會得到豐厚的回報。「可是請告訴我，」他說，「你是誰？從哪裡來？你的船在哪裡？還是你搭別人的船來的？」
> Laertes wept and answered that in this case he would never see his presents back again, though he would have been amply requited if Ulysses had been alive. "But tell me," he said, "who and whence are you? Where is your ship? or did you come as passenger on some other man's vessel?"

〔U0603 quote〕
「我會把一切都告訴您，」奧德修斯答道，「句句實話。我從阿呂巴斯來，是阿菲丹斯王的兒子。我名叫埃佩里托斯；我離開西卡尼亞時，上天把我吹離了航道，身不由己地被帶到了這裡。至於我的船，就停在那邊城外的曠野海岸邊。奧德修斯離開我已經五年了──可憐的人！我們本來滿心希望能再相見，互贈禮物。」
> "I will tell you every thing," answered Ulysses, "quite truly. I come from Alybas, and am son to king Apheides. My name is Eperitus; heaven drove me off my course as I was leaving Sicania, and I have been carried here against my will. As for my ship, it is lying over yonder off the open country outside the town. It is five years since Ulysses left me—Poor fellow! we had every hope that we should meet again and exchange presents."

〔U0604 quote〕
拉厄爾特斯悲痛欲絕，奧德修斯深受感動，便表明了身分。父親要他拿出證據，他便把腿上的傷疤給父親看。「此外，」他又說，「我還可以把您送給我的那些葡萄園裡的樹一一指給您看；我跟著您在園子裡轉的時候，一棵棵都問過您。我們把每一棵都走了一遍，您告訴我它們的名字，各是什麼樹。您給了我十三棵梨樹、十棵蘋果樹、四十棵無花果樹，還說要給我五十行葡萄藤；每行之間都種著穀物，天上的烈日曬過之後，那些葡萄藤就結出各種各樣的葡萄。」他也告訴父親，他已殺死了那些求婚者。
> Laertes was overcome with grief, and Ulysses was so much touched that he revealed himself. When his father asked for proof, he shewed him the scar on his leg. "Furthermore," he added, "I will point out to you the trees in the vineyard which you gave me, and I asked you all about them as I followed you round the garden. We went over them all, and you told me their names and what they all were. You gave me thirteen pear trees, ten apple trees, and forty fig trees, and you also said you would give me fifty rows of vines; there was corn planted between each row, and the vines yield grapes of every kind when the heat of heaven has beaten upon them." He also told his father that he had killed the suitors.

〔U0605 quote〕
拉厄爾特斯這下信了，但說他擔心伊薩卡全城的人都會來攻打他們。奧德修斯回答說他不必為此操心，他們最好先進去吃午餐；等他們走到屋裡，飯也就準備好了。
> Laertes was now convinced, but said he feared he should have all the people of Ithaca coming to attack them. Ulysses answered that he need not trouble about this, and that they had better go and get their dinner, which would be ready by the time they got to the house.

〔U0606 quote〕
他們回到屋裡，那個西克洛伊老婦人把拉厄爾特斯帶進去，替他洗澡、抹油。雅典娜也讓他更顯威儀，看上去比先前更高大、更強壯。他回來時，奧德修斯說：「我親愛的父親，一定是哪位神明讓您變得高大俊朗了許多。」拉厄爾特斯回答說，要是他還像當年攻下岬角上的涅里科斯要塞時那樣年輕健壯，昨天他就能大大幫上兒子的忙，殺掉許多求婚者。
> When they reached the house the old Sicel woman took Laertes inside, washed him, and anointed him. Minerva also gave him a more imposing presence and made him look taller and stronger than before. "When he came back, Ulysses said, "My dear father, some god has been making you much taller and better looking." To which Laertes answered that if he was as young and hearty as when he took the stronghold Nericum on the foreland, he should have been a great help to him on the preceding day, and would have killed many suitors.

〔U0607 quote〕
多利俄斯和他的兒子們原本在附近幹活，這時也來了，因為那個西克洛伊老婦人──她是多利俄斯的妻子──去叫了他們。他們確信奧德修斯真的在這裡之後，欣喜若狂，一個接一個地擁抱他。「可是請告訴我，」多利俄斯說，「潘妮洛碧知道了嗎？還是我們該派人去告訴她？」「老人家，」奧德修斯答道，「她已經知道了。這干你什麼事？」接著大家便入席就座。
> Dolius and his sons, who had been working hard by, now came up, for the old Sicel woman, who was Dolius's wife, had been to fetch them. When they were satisfied that Ulysses was really there, they were overjoyed and embraced him one after the other. "But tell me," said Dolius, "does Penelope know, or shall we send and tell her?" "Old man," answered Ulysses, "she knows already. What business is that of yours?" Then they all took their seats at table.

〔U0608 quote〕
這時求婚者被殺的消息已經傳開，人們聚集在奧德修斯的宅邸前，叫罵哀號。他們領回自己的死者，各自埋葬自家的人；從外地來的，就把屍體放上漁船，讓漁夫各自送回他們的家鄉。接著他們召開大會，歐佩特斯力勸眾人，趁奧德修斯一行還沒逃到大陸上去，趕快追捕他們。
> Meanwhile the news of the slaughter of the suitors had got noised abroad, and the people gathered hooting and groaning before the house of Ulysses. They took their dead, buried every man his own, and put the bodies of those who came from elsewhere on board the fishing vessels, for the fishermen to take them every man to his own place. Then they met in assembly and Eupeithes urged them to pursue Ulysses and the others before they could escape over to the main land.

〔U0609 quote〕
然而墨東和費彌俄斯這時已經醒來，也來到了大會上。墨東勸阻人們不要照歐佩特斯的主意去做，因為他親眼看見一位神明在殺戮求婚者，違抗上天的旨意是很危險的。哈利瑟耳塞斯也說了同樣意思的話，一半的人被他說服了。另一半人則武裝起來，跟著歐佩特斯去追捕奧德修斯。
> Medon, however, and Phemius had now woke up, and came to the assembly. Medon dissuaded the people from doing as Eupeithes advised, inasmuch as he had seen a god going about killing the suitors, and it would be dangerous to oppose the will of heaven. Halitherses also spoke in the same sense, and half the people were pursuaded by him. The other half armed themselves and followed Eupeithes in pursuit of Ulysses.

〔U0610 quote〕
雅典娜於是去請教宙斯，問事情該如何發展。宙斯告訴她，到目前為止一切都照她的意思進行，她儘可以繼續隨心所欲。不過他建議，雙方如今都應當和解，由奧德修斯繼續統治。雅典娜贊同這個主意，便疾飛而下，到了伊薩卡。
> Minerva then consulted Jove as to the course events should take. Jove told her that she had had everything her own way so far, and might continue to do as she pleased. He should, however, advise that both sides should now be reconciled under the continued rule of Ulysses. Minerva approved of this and darted down to Ithaca.

〔U0611 quote〕
拉厄爾特斯一家這時已經吃完午餐，只見歐佩特斯帶著他那一夥人已經逼近。奧德修斯一行穿上盔甲，雅典娜也來到他們當中。「忒勒馬科斯，」奧德修斯說，「如今你就要參加一場決定性的戰鬥了，務必不要辱沒你的祖先；他們的勇力與英武，可是名揚天下的。」
> Laertes and his household had now done dinner, and Eupeithes with his band of men were seen to be near at hand. Ulysses and the others put on their armour, and Minerva joined them. "Telemachus," said Ulysses, "now that you are about to fight in a decisive engagement, see that you do no discredit to your ancestors, who were eminent all the world over for their strength and valour."

〔U0612 quote〕
「親愛的父親，」忒勒馬科斯答道，「您要是願意，儘管看著吧，我絕無意像您說的那樣，辱沒您的家門。」
> "You shall see, my dear father," replied Telemachus, "if you choose, that I am in no mind, as you say, to disgrace your family."

〔U0613 quote〕
「天哪，」拉厄爾特斯驚呼道，「今天我真是快活！我的兒子和孫子在比誰更勇敢。」這時雅典娜走到他跟前，叫他向她祈禱。她給他注入了新的力氣，他向她祈禱完，便瞄準歐佩特斯擲出長矛，把他殺死了。奧德修斯和他的人撲向其餘的人，把他們打得潰不成軍；要不是雅典娜高聲一喊，叫所有人都停下手來，他們早已把那些人一個不剩全殺光了。「伊薩卡人哪，」她喊道，「停止這場可怕的戰爭吧，不要再流血，把這件事了結了。」
> "Good heavens," exclaimed Laertes, "what a day I am enjoying. My son and grandson are vying with one another in the matter of valour." Minerva then came up to him, and bade him pray to her. She infused fresh vigour into him, and when he had prayed to her he aimed his spear at Eupeithes and killed him. Ulysses and his men fell upon the others, routed them, and would have killed one and all of them had not Minerva raised her voice and made every one pause. "Men of Ithaca," she cried, "cease this dreadful war, and settle the matter without further bloodshed."

〔U0614 quote〕
他們聽了，嚇得臉色發白，扔下兵器，各自往城裡逃去。奧德修斯像一隻老鷹般向他們猛撲過去，可是宙斯擲下一道火焰霹靂，正落在雅典娜面前。於是她說：「奧德修斯，住手，停止這場爭鬥，否則宙斯要對你發怒了。」
> On this they turned pale with fear, dropped their armour, and fled every man towards the city. Ulysses was swooping down upon them like an eagle, but Jove sent a thunderbolt of fire that fell just in front of Minerva. "Whereon she said, "Ulysses, stay this strife, or Jove will be angry with you."

〔U0615 quote〕
奧德修斯欣然從命。雅典娜接著化作門托爾的聲音和模樣，不久便在交戰雙方之間訂立了和平盟約。
> Ulysses obeyed her gladly. Minerva then assumed the voice and form of Mentor, and presently made a covenant of peace between the two contending parties.
