〔U0618 footnote〕
忒墨塞位於義大利「靴尖」的西側，一度以銅礦聞名，不過到了斯特拉波的時代，礦藏已經採盡。見史密斯《古典地理辭典》（*Smith's Dictionary of Ancient Geography*）。
> Temesa was on the West side of the toe of Italy and was once famous for its copper mines, which, however, were worked out in Strabo's time. See *Smith's Dictionary of Ancient Geography*.

〔U0619 footnote〕
讀作 Νηρίτῳ，而不讀 Νηίῳ；參見卷十三 96 行以下及 351 行，兩處所指顯然是同一個港口。
> Heading Νηρίτῳ instead of Νηίῳ, cf. Book xiii. 96, &c., and 351, where the same harbour is obviously intended.

〔U0620 footnote〕
即「流動的」，或者說其中有水流。
> *i.e.* "flowing," or with a current in it.

〔U0621 footnote〕
這座山用的是單數，彷彿作者心中想的是一座孤立的山，而不是一條山脈。在卷十五 175 行與卷十九 538 行這兩處類似的地方，用的也是單數，而非複數。
> The mountain is singular, as though it were an isolated mountain rather than a range that was in the mind of the writer. It is also singular, not plural, in the parallel cases of xv. 175 and xix. 538.

〔U0622 footnote〕
讀作 ὑπονηρίτου，而不讀 ὑπονηίου；參見卷一 186 行，亦見卷十三 351 行。
> Reading ὑπονηρίτου for ὑπονηίου, *cf*.i. 186 and also xiii. 351.

〔U0623 footnote〕
讀者會注意到，俄瑞斯忒斯連他母親也殺了這件事，這裡並沒有明說；另外三處提到俄瑞斯忒斯復仇的段落也都沒有明說──想必是因為此事太過駭人。另外那幾處是《奧德賽》卷一 40 行與 299 行（本縮譯未收），以及卷十一 408 行以下。
> The reader will note that the fact of Orestes having also killed his mother is not expressly stated here, nor in any of the three other passages in which the revenge taken by Orestes is referred to—doubtless as being too horrible. The other passages are *Od*. i. 40 and 299 (not given in this summary), and xi. 408, &c.

〔U0624 footnote〕
更完整的譯文，以及我為什麼把這段加上方括號，見第六章。
> For fuller translation and explanation why I have bracketed the passage, see Chapter VI.

〔U0625 footnote〕
說來奇怪，海倫為忒勒馬科斯與佩西斯特拉托斯所做的就寢安排，以及阿瑞忒王后為奧德修斯所做的安排（卷七 336 行以下），雖然幾乎是逐字照搬阿基里斯為普里阿摩斯與伊代俄斯所做的安排（《伊里亞德》卷二十四 643–647 行與 673–676 行），用在墨涅拉俄斯的宅邸卻同樣妥貼──而墨涅拉俄斯的宅邸，與阿基里斯在特洛伊城下的營房，性質想必大不相同。
> It is curious that the sleeping arrangements made by Helen for Telemachus and Pisistratus, as also those made for Ulysses by Queen Arete (vii. 336, &c.), though taken almost verbatim from those made by Achilles for Priam and Idicus (*Il*. xxiv. 643-47 and 673-76), should do so well for a building of such a different character as the house of Menelaus must have been from the quarters of Achilles before Troy.

〔U0626 footnote〕
我為什麼把這段加上方括號，說明見第六章。
> For explanation why I bracket this passage see Chapter VI.

〔U0627 footnote〕
斯刻里亞（Scheria）意為「日德蘭」（Jutland）──一塊伸入（jutting out）海中的陸地。
> Scheria means "Jutland"—a piece of land jutting out into the sea.

〔U0628 footnote〕
希臘原文作 πάππα φίλ'（親愛的爸爸），第 57 行。
> Gr. πάππα φίλ', line 57.

〔U0629 footnote〕
潘妮洛碧和卡呂普索也有花園，拉厄爾特斯也有（卷二十四 217 行）。我不記得《伊里亞德》裡提到過花園。
> Penelope and Calypso also had gardens: so had Laertes (xxiv. 217). I remember no allusion to them in the *Iliad*.

〔U0630 footnote〕
這塊鐵餅竟被拿了來，有點奇怪，因為費埃克斯人並不用這樣的鐵餅。我們只能假定，是雅典娜把它和其他鐵餅放在一起，然後在它上面罩了一層濃厚的黑暗，使侍從沒有注意到它。
> It is a little odd that this disc should have been brought, considering that none such were used by the Phæacians. We must suppose that Minerva put it in along with the others, and then shed a thick darkness over it, which prevented the attendants from noticing it.

〔U0631 footnote〕
阿爾基諾俄斯似乎始終只停留在說要送那只杯子；他從沒真把它送出去，那一塔蘭特也沒送──那句熟悉的 ῶς εἰπὼν ἐν χερσὶ τίθει κ.τ.λ.（他如此說罷，便放到他手中云云）在這裡明顯缺席。他找來了那口箱子，又費了好大功夫把禮物裝上那艘要送奧德修斯回伊薩卡的船（見卷十三 18 行以下），但他自己的貢獻，似乎就到此為止了。
> Alcinous never seems to have got beyond saying that he was going to give the cup; he never gives it, nor yet the talent—the familiar ῶς εἰπὼν ἐν χερσὶ τίθει κ.τ.λ. is noticeably absent. He found the chest, and he took a great deal of pains about stowing the presents in the ship that was to take Ulysses to Ithaca (see xiii. 18, &c.), but here his contributions seem to have ended.

〔U0632 footnote〕
住在西西里東岸的人相信，這裡所說的島就是阿奇特雷扎，位於阿奇雷亞萊與卡塔尼亞之間。我把那座島走遍了，不相信島上能有超過兩英畝可供任何山羊吃草的土地。說《奧德賽》的作者會讓奧德修斯和他那一大群人在這樣一個地方花上半天工夫殺掉一百多頭山羊，這種想法不值得認真討論；所以等我討論到奧德修斯的航程時，我將略過不提。人們竟然如此篤信它就是庫克羅普斯人土地外海的那座島，這對我自己倒是一個警惕，因為它顯示出：人只要打定主意，多麼容易就能說服自己，接受任何地點作為任何場景的所在。
> Dwellers on the East coast of Sicily believe the island here referred to to be Acitrezza, between Acireale and Catania. I have been all over it and do not believe that it contains more than two acres of land on which any goat could ever have fed. The idea that the writer of the *Odyssey* would make Ulysses and his large body of men spend half a day in killing over a hundred goats on such a site need not be discussed seriously, I shall therefore pass it over without notice when I come to discuss the voyage of Ulysses. That it should be so confidently believed to be the island off the land of the Cyclopes serves as a warning to myself, inasmuch as it shows how easily people can bring themselves to accept any site for any scene if they make up their minds to do so.

〔U0633 footnote〕
我為什麼把第 115–137 行加上方括號，理由見第十五章。
> See Chapter xv. for reasons why I have bracketed lines 115—137.

〔U0634 footnote〕
奧德修斯要去平息波賽頓的怒氣，方法是去當一名傳教士，到一個不認識波賽頓的民族當中宣揚他的名號。
> Ulysses was to appease Neptune's anger by going as a missionary to preach his name among a people that did not know him.

〔U0635 footnote〕
這裡的前後不連貫是很明顯的；但由於奧德修斯照理應該來到漂移之岩時（他其實從未到過），同樣的情形又出現了一次，所以這必須歸因於一處 *lacuna*（脫漏）──不是文本中的脫漏，而是作者資料來源中的脫漏；對此她似乎完全心知肚明。
> The want of coherence here is obvions, but as it is repeated when Ulysses ought to come to the wandering cliffs (which he never does) it must be referred to a *lacuna* not in the text, but in the writer's sources of information—of which she seems fully aware.

〔U0636 footnote〕
我認為這一行是在本卷第 426–446 行添入時一併加上的。
> I suppose this line to have been added when lines 426—446 of this book were added.

〔U0637 footnote〕
這裡所指的無疑是漂移之岩，因為奧德修斯在卷二十三重述自己的歷險時，明確提到他在遇見塞壬之後、遇見斯庫拉與卡律布狄斯之前，到過 πλαγκτὰς πέτρας（漂移之岩；卷二十三 327 行）。不管作者對它們所知多麼有限，她都決意要把它們寫進故事裡。
> The wandering cliffs are certainly intended, for when Ulysses is recapitulating his adventures in Book xxiii. he expressly mentions having reached the πλαγκτὰς πέτρας, just after the Sirens, and before Scylla and Charybdis (xxiii. 327). The writer is determined to have them in her story however little she may know about them.

〔U0638 footnote〕
我傾向於認為這幾行是事後才添上的，而且是作者本人加的。
> I incline to think that these lines are an after thought, added by the writer herself.

〔U0639 footnote〕
σὺν ἀρτεμέεσσι φίλοισιν（與安然無恙的親友們一起）。
> σὺν ἀρτεμέεσσι φίλοισιν.

〔U0640 footnote〕
雅典娜一心想把求婚者在奧德修斯家盤踞的時間說得短些，似乎忘了他們從忒勒馬科斯還是個小孩子起就一直在那裡了（《奧德賽》卷二 312–314 行）。
> Minerva, in her desire to minimise the time during which the suitors had been at Ulysses' house, seems to have forgotten that they had been there ever since Telemachus was quite a child (*Od*. ii. 312-14).

〔U0641 footnote〕
即那些看起來從他們身旁飛掠而過的。
> *i.e*. which seemed to fly past them.

〔U0642 footnote〕
據傳說，她是聽到兒子的死訊後上吊自盡的。
> According to tradition, she had hanged herself on hearing a report of the death of her son.

〔U0643 footnote〕
見第十二章開頭附近。
> See Chapter XII, near the beginning.

〔U0644 footnote〕
在幾乎所有其他地方，他都叫作墨蘭提俄斯（Melanthius）。
> In almost all other places he is called Melanthius.

〔U0645 footnote〕
如果場景設在露天庭院，這一切都完全說得通；但如果是在宅邸內的大廳裡，就很難說得通了。
> All this might very well be, if the scene is laid in an open court, but hardly if it was in a hall inside a house.

〔U0646 footnote〕
ἐς μέσσον（到中間；第 447 行）。
> ἐς μέσσον (line 447).

〔U0647 footnote〕
在露天庭院的中央打鬥，完全說得通；但在有屋頂的大廳裡就很難。他們會到外面去打。
> They might very well fight in the middle of an open court, but hardly in a covered hall. They would go outside.

〔U0648 footnote〕
原文作 ἐν μεγάροισιν（在廳院裡），而不是 ἐν μεγάροισι σκιόεσσι（在有蔭的廳院裡）。
> ἐν μεγάροισιν, but not ἐν μεγάροισι σκιόεσσι.

〔U0649 footnote〕
文中沒有任何跡象顯示他們是走到外面去做這件事的；他們似乎就把灰燼倒在庭院露天的部分。
> There is no indication as though they went out to do this; they seem to have emptied the ashes on to the open part of the court.

〔U0650 footnote〕
我在特拉帕尼及其附近，一再看到鵝這樣覓食。夏天草全被曬枯了，所以牠們沒辦法像在英格蘭那樣吃草。
> I have repeatedly seen geese so feeding at Trapani and in the neighbourhood. In summer the grass is all burned up so that they cannot graze as in England.

〔U0651 footnote〕
這是《伊里亞德》與《奧德賽》中唯一提到薩丁尼亞的地方。
> This is the only reference to Sardinia in either *Iliad* or *Odyssey*.

〔U0652 footnote〕
如果忒勒馬科斯以前從沒見過這種東西，那麼《奧德賽》的作者大概也沒見過──無論如何，至今還沒有哪位注釋家能弄懂她的描述，我也懷疑她自己是否弄懂了。看來斧頭應該是楔進斧柄，或是用某種方式綁在斧柄上，讓斧頭上那個平常使用時插斧柄的孔露出來。這項比試顯然是雙重的：拉弓比的是力氣，射穿一排環孔比的是準頭。
> If Telemachus had never seen anything of the kind before, so probably, neither had the writer of the *Odyssey*—at any rate no commentator has yet been able to understand her description, and I doubt whether she understood it herself. It looks as though the axe heads must have been wedged into the handles or so bound on to them as to let the hole be visible through which the handle would go when the axe was in use. The trial is evidently a double one, of strength as regards the bending of the bow, and accuracy of aim as regards shooting through a row of rings.

〔U0653 footnote〕
原文並沒有明說這裡指的就是那片「石砌地面」。希臘原文只說 ἆλτο δ᾽ ἐπὶ μέγαν οὐδόν（他跳上那寬大的門檻），但我毫不懷疑這裡指的正是石砌地面。
> It is not expressly stated that the "stone pavement" is here intended. The Greek has simply ἆλτο δ᾽ ἐπὶ μέγαν οὐδόν, but I do not doubt that the stone pavement is intended.

〔U0654 footnote〕
這一點再次暗示──雖然不能證明──我們是在一座四周環繞迴廊的露天庭院裡，燕子常會停在迴廊的椽木上。第 297 行更強烈地暗示了這一點：「屋頂」無疑是指迴廊的屋頂，雅典娜從椽木飛上那裡，好讓她的神盾更能俯控整座庭院。
> This again suggests, though it does not prove, that we are in an open court surrounded by a cloister, on the rafters of which swallows would often perch. Line 297 suggests this even more strongly, "the roof" being, no doubt, the roof of the cloister, on to which Minerva flew from the rafter, that her ægis might better command the whole court.

〔U0655 footnote〕
大概就是菲羅提俄斯那天早上牽來的那頭小母牛的皮（卷二十 186 行）。
> Probably the hide of the heifer that Philœtius had brought in that morning (xx. 186).

〔U0656 footnote〕
這間房似乎不在宅邸主體之內。它肯定在底層，因為那張床是固定在一截樹樁上的；我強烈懷疑它就是那間圓頂屋，有罪女僕的屍體當時仍吊在它的外牆四周；我也懷疑，忒勒馬科斯之所以吊死而不刺死那些女人，正是為了像掛花綵那樣把那間屋子圍起來；不過這已踏入那種危險的臆測，而這種臆測，我向來極力反對別人去做。要不是第 129–151 行那場舞蹈令人毛骨悚然，我也不會有這樣的念頭。
> This room was apparently not within the body of the house. It was certainly on the ground floor, for the bed was fixed on to the stump of a tree; I strongly suspect it to be the vaulted room, round the outside of which the bodies of the guilty maids were still hanging, and I also suspect it was in order to thus festoon the room that Telemachus hanged the women instead of stabbing them, but this is treading on that perilous kind of speculation which I so strongly deprecate in others. If it were not for the gruesome horror of the dance, in lines 129—151, I should not have entertained it.

〔U0657 heading〕
第三章
> CHAPTER III.

〔U0658 heading〕
《奧德賽》中女性的壓倒性分量
> THE PREPONDERANCE OF WOMAN IN THE ODYSSEY.

〔U0659 para〕
在第一章裡，我已經回應了迄今為止有人向我提出的、針對我對作者性別看法的唯一一批 *à priori*（先驗的）反對意見；現在我要轉向剛才擺在讀者面前的這個故事，看看它本身提供了哪些出自女性之手的證據。
> Having in my first chapter met the only *à priori* objections to my views concerning the sex of the writer which have yet been presented to me, I now turn to the evidence of female authorship which is furnished by the story which I have just laid before the reader.

〔U0660 para〕
請容我一問：判斷作者是否為女性，最萬無一失的試金石是什麼？當然是女性的分量壓過一切，而且作者對女人通常要打交道的事物，比對那些多半屬於男人範圍的事物懂得更多。人總是偏愛寫自己最熟悉的東西，而人最熟悉的，正是自己本身最是什麼、最常和什麼打交道。這一點延伸到思考方式與性格上，比延伸到行動上還要明顯。倘若男人認為人類最高貴的研究對象是男人，那麼女人也同樣篤定地相信，那是女人。
> What, let me ask, is the most unerring test of female authorship? Surely a preponderance of female interest, and a fuller knowledge of those things which a woman generally has to deal with, than of those that fall more commonly within the province of man. People always write by preference of what they know best, and they know best what they most are, and have most to do with. This extends to ways of thought and to character, even more than to action. If man thinks the noblest study for mankind to be man, woman not less certainly believes it to be woman.

〔U0661 para〕
因此，在任何一部作品裡，如果女性寫得精彩而富於同情，男性卻寫得機械、相形之下敷衍了事，我想，我們就可以穩妥地推斷作者是女人；反過來，對男人也一樣成立。男人與女人從來不曾完全了解彼此，唯一的例外，或許是求愛與蜜月期間；而正如男人了解男人比女人所能了解的更透徹，女人了解女人也是如此。誠然，兩性都以盡可能了解對方為樂，而在這方面最成功的人，無論男女，都是最好也最幸福的人；但是，不管我們怎麼努力，這道藩籬永遠不可能完全打破，兩性各自主要──當然不是完全──都住在自己那個獨立的世界裡。再說，仔細想來，這道藩籬也不該打破，因為兩性彼此吸引的魅力，有很大一部分正是靠它的存在。
> Hence if in any work the women are found to be well and sympathetically drawn, while the men are mechanical and by comparison perfunctorily treated, it is, I imagine, safe to infer that the writer is a woman; and the converse holds good with man. Man and woman never fully understand one another save, perhaps, during courtship and honeymoon, and as a man understands man more fully than a woman can do, so does a woman, woman. Granted, it is the delight of either sex to understand the other as fully as it can, and those who succeed most in this respect are the best and happiest whether men or women; but do what we may the barriers can never be broken down completely, and each sex will dwell mainly, though not, of course, exclusively, within its own separate world. When, moreover, we come to think of it, it is not desirable that they should be broken down, for it is on their existence that much of the attraction of either sex to the other depends.

〔U0662 para〕
男人似乎沒有辦法描寫女人而不取笑她們或把她們漫畫化；同樣地，女人描寫男人，大概也從來沒有比把他寫得可笑時更傳神的了。她若存心把他寫得可笑，必定成功；她若無心如此，成功得還更有把握。其實兩性都能把對方寫成妙趣橫生的漫畫；而且，可以肯定的是，從來沒有哪位作者比《奧德賽》的作者更徹底地表明：除了頌揚女性之外，她認為男人的小毛病與小弱點，是最適合施展她天才的題材。不過我懷疑，在整個文學史上，有沒有哪位作者（我想莎士比亞要除外）曾經成功地為異性畫出一幅全身、真人大小、嚴肅的肖像。
> Men seem unable to draw women at all without either laughing at them or caricaturing them; and so, perhaps, a woman never draws a man so felicitously as when she is making him ridiculous. If she means to make him so she is certain to succeed; if she does not mean it she will succeed more surely still. Either sex, in fact, can caricature the other delightfully, and certainly no writer has ever shown more completely than the writer of the *Odyssey* has done that, next to the glorification of woman, she considers man's little ways and weaknesses to be the fittest theme on which her genius can be displayed. But I doubt whether any writer in the whole range of literature (excepting, I suppose, Shakspeare) has succeeded in drawing a full length, life-sized, serious portrait of a member of the sex opposite to the writer's own.

〔U0663 para〕
大家都承認，《伊里亞德》的興趣重心偏在男人一邊，《奧德賽》則偏在女人一邊。《伊里亞德》裡的女人為數不多，也很少站上舞台。不錯，女神們扮演了重要角色，但從來沒有人把她們當真。
> It is admitted on all hands that the preponderance of interest in the *Iliad* is on the side of man, and in the *Odyssey* on that of woman. Women in the *Iliad* are few in number and rarely occupy the stage. True, the goddesses play important parts, but they are never taken seriously.

〔U0664 para〕
再者，雪萊談到《伊里亞德》「最後七卷不斷攀升的壯麗」時說：「《奧德賽》是甜美的，但沒有任何東西比得上這個。」[註1]《奧德賽》的作者有時兇猛如母虎，但這部詩整體的感覺，正如雪萊所說。《伊里亞德》即使在最溫柔的段落，處處也都能感受到力量；而《奧德賽》從頭到尾令我們著迷的，與其說是力量，不如說是甜美。那是女人的魅力，而不是男人的。
> Shelley, again, speaking of the "perpetually increasing magnificence of the last seven books" of the *Iliad*, says, "The *Odyssey* is sweet, but there is nothing like this."[註1] The writer of the *Odyssey* is fierce as a tigress at times, but the feeling of the poem is on the whole exactly what Shelley says it is. Strength is felt everywhere even in the tenderest passages of the *Iliad*, but it is sweetness rather than strength that fascinates us throughout the *Odyssey*. It is the charm of a woman not of a man.

〔U0665 para〕
同樣地，再引一位較近的權威：格萊斯頓先生在前面提過的那部論荷馬的著作（即《荷馬與荷馬時代研究》）裡說（第 28 頁）：──
> So, again, to quote a more recent authority, Mr. Gladstone in his work on *Homer* already referred to, says (p. 28):—

〔U0666 para〕
在《伊里亞德》裡，宏偉或力量很少讓位給家庭親情的展現。反過來，在《奧德賽》中，家庭生活提供了織物的底布，詩的絲線便織進其中。
> It is rarely in the *Iliad* that grandeur or force give way to allow the exhibition of domestic affection. Conversely, in the *Odyssey* the family life supplies the tissue into which is woven the thread of the poem.

〔U0667 para〕
任何熟悉這兩部詩的人，必定都知道格萊斯頓先生說得對；他其實還可以補上一句同樣正確的話：在《伊里亞德》中，凡有家庭生活與親情的展現，男人總居於主導，女人則受他們保護；而在整部《奧德賽》裡，卻是女人在指揮、在出主意、在保護男人。
> Any one who is familiar with the two poems must know that what Mr. Gladstone has said is true; and he might have added, not less truly, that when there is any exhibition of domestic life and affection in the *Iliad* the men are dominant, and the women are under their protection, whereas throughout the *Odyssey* it is the women who are directing, counselling, and protecting the men.

〔U0668 para〕
《奧德賽》裡有哪些女人？有雅典娜，她無所不在，總守在奧德修斯與忒勒馬科斯的手肘邊，讓他們走正路，對他們時而責罵、時而奉承。在《伊里亞德》裡，她是偉大的戰士，卻不是女人；在《奧德賽》裡，她是偉大的女人，卻不是戰士。當然還有潘妮洛碧──幾乎直到最後都強勢專斷，而她一旦不再如此，幾乎立刻就被打發到側台去了。老女僕歐律克蕾亞也完全制得住忒勒馬科斯；她對他說：「孩子，沒有人可以怪罪的時候，就別怪罪了。」（卷二十 135 行）誰能懷疑海倫是墨涅拉俄斯家中的主人？她對墨涅拉俄斯能說的全部讚美，不過是他「無論相貌還是頭腦，都一點也不差」（卷四 264 行）。卷四的埃伊多忒亞，在整段普洛透斯插曲中，對墨涅拉俄斯都是 *de haut en bas*（居高臨下）。她待他和他的手下不錯，但他們必須完全照她吩咐的去做；而她顯然很享受「使喚」他們──我實在想不出更貼切的字眼了。主導全局的是卡呂普索，不是奧德修斯；而且請注意，無論她還是喀耳刻，住處似乎都沒有一個男僕。我有一次住在一家旅店，問那位氣派的老闆娘能否見見老闆。她把頭一昂，答道：「先生，我們這家店沒有老闆；我看不出男人在旅館裡有什麼用處，除了擦靴子和擦窗戶。」這正是喀耳刻與卡呂普索的口吻，只不過她們兩位似乎連這一點點讓步都沒有為男人保留。
> Who are the women in the *Odyssey*? There is Minerva, omnipresent at the elbows of Ulysses and Telemachus to keep them straight and alternately scold and flatter them. In the *Iliad* she is a great warrior but she is no woman: in the *Odyssey* she is a great woman but no warrior; we have, of course, Penelope—masterful nearly to the last and tossed off to the wings almost from the moment that she has ceased to be so; Euryclea, the old servant, is quite a match for Telemachus, "do not find fault, child," she says to him, "when there is no one to find fault with" (xx. 135). Who can doubt that Helen is master in the house of Menelaus—of whom all she can say in praise is that he is "not deficient either in person or understanding" (iv. 264)? Idothea in Book iv. treats Menelaus *de haut en bas*, all through the Proteus episode. She is good to him and his men, but they must do exactly what she tells them, and she evidently enjoys "running" them,—for I can think of no apter word. Calypso is the master mind, not Ulysses; and, be it noted, that neither she nor Circe seem to have a manservant on their premises. I was at an inn once and asked the stately landlady if I could see the landlord. She bridled up and answered, "We have no landlord, sir, in this house; I cannot see what use a man is in a hotel except to clean boots and windows." There spoke Circe and Calypso, but neither of them seem to have made even this much exception in man's favour.

〔U0669 para〕
請讀者去問問他認識的、住在自己房子裡的單身女士，她們比較喜歡讓男人還是女人伺候；如果他沒發現她們通常根本不想讓任何男人在屋裡走動──園丁當然除外──我會大感意外。不過話說回來，園丁通常有老婆，也有自己的房子。
> Let the reader ask any single ladies of his acquaintance, who live in a house of their own, whether they prefer being waited upon by men or by women, and I shall be much surprised if he does not find that they generally avoid having a man about the house at all—gardeners of course excepted. But then the gardener generally has a wife, and a house of his own.

〔U0670 para〕
再看瑙西卡：她固然可愛，但跟她唱反調可不是明智之舉；她知道什麼對奧德修斯有好處，只要他聽她的話，一切都會順順當當，但她必須是主人，他則是僕從。我發現我漏掉了卷五的伊諾。她不過是埃伊多忒亞的翻版，正如喀耳刻是卡呂普索的翻版，幾乎沒什麼變化。再問一次：誰是主人──阿瑞忒王后，還是阿爾基諾俄斯王？瑙西卡很清楚怎麼回答這個問題。她指點奧德修斯時說：
> Take Nausicaa again, delightful as she is, it would not be wise to contradict her; she knows what is good for Ulysses, and all will go well with him so long as he obeys her, but she must be master and he man. I see I have passed over Ino in Book v. She is Idothea over again, just as Circe is Calypso, with very little variation. Who again is master—Queen Arete or King Alcinous? Nausicaa knows well enough how to answer this question. When giving her instructions to Ulysses she says:

〔U0671 quote〕
「別管我父親，直接走到我母親面前，抱住她的膝蓋；只要她對你有好感，你就有幾分希望回到家鄉，再見到你的親友。」（卷六 310–315 行）
> "Never mind my father, but go up to my mother and embrace her knees; if she is well disposed towards you there is some chance of your getting home to see your friends again" (vi. 310-315).

〔U0672 para〕
在整段費埃克斯插曲中，阿瑞忒（Arēte）都比阿爾基諾俄斯重要──順帶一提，我認為她的名字是作者那些相當透明的偽裝之一，用意在讓人聯想到 Arĕte，也就是「美德」。我自己並不相信有她這個人；我相信，倘若阿瑞忒真像作者說的那麼好，潘妮洛碧就會被寫得更可親一些。不過，暫且把她擱在一邊：在《奧德賽》作者眼中，妻子比丈夫重要得多，重要到奧德修斯向費埃克斯人致告別辭時，她讓他說的是：願他們繼續讓自己的妻兒滿意（卷十三 44、45 行）；而不是願他們的妻兒繼續讓他們滿意。稍後，他又祝阿瑞忒王后與她的兒女、她的百姓，最後才是與阿爾基諾俄斯王，一切幸福美滿。至於阿爾基諾俄斯王，他幸不幸福無關緊要，只要他讓阿瑞忒王后滿意就行；不過，作為這樣一位令人讚佩的女人的丈夫，他想不幸福也難。
> Throughout the Phæacian episode Arēte (whose name, by the way, I take to be one of the writer's tolerably transparent disguises, and to be intended to suggest Arĕte, or "Goodness") is a more important person than Alcinous. I do not believe in her myself; I believe Penelope would have been made more amiable if Arete had been as nice a person as the writer says she was; leaving her, however, on one side, so much more important are wives than husbands in the eyes of the author of the *Odyssey* that when Ulysses makes his farewell speech to the Phæacians, she makes him say that he hopes they may continue to give satisfaction to their wives and children (xiii. 44, 45), instead of hoping that their wives and children will continue to give satisfaction to them. A little lower down he wishes Queen Arete all happiness with her children, her people, and lastly with King Alcinous. As for King Alcinous, it does not matter whether he is happy or no, provided he gives satisfaction to Queen Arete; but he was bound to be happy as the husband of such an admirable woman.

〔U0673 para〕
同樣地，約克公爵成婚的時候，我一再聽到女人說，希望瑪麗公主跟他在一起會非常幸福，卻從沒聽過哪個女人說，希望公爵跟瑪麗公主在一起會非常幸福。男人則說，希望這一對非常幸福，並不特別提到哪一方。
> So when the Duke of York was being married I heard women over and over again say they hoped the Princess May would be very happy with him, but I never heard one say that she hoped the Duke would be very happy with the Princess May. Men said they hoped the pair would be very happy, without naming one more than the other.

〔U0674 para〕
我已經把《奧德賽》中所有較突出的女性角色都簡略談過了。每一個例子的寓意似乎都是：男人懂得很少，而且即使在他懂得的那一點點事情上，也靠不住，難保不出洋相，除非手邊有個女人告訴他該怎麼做。全詩沒有一個例子是男人挺身而出，去解救身陷困境的美麗女子；情形總是恰恰相反。
> I have touched briefly on all the more prominent female characters of the *Odyssey*. The moral in every case seems to be that man knows very little, and cannot be trusted not to make a fool of himself even about the little that he does know, unless he has a woman at hand to tell him what he ought to do. There is not a single case in which a man comes to the rescue of female beauty in distress; it is invariably the other way about.

〔U0675 para〕
奧德修斯漂泊期間，給過他任何幫助的男性只有兩位：一位是埃俄羅斯，他並沒有真正幫上忙，而且第二次拒絕再幫他；另一位是赫耳墨斯，他給了奧德修斯藥草魔羅（卷十 305 行），保護他不受喀耳刻的魔法所害。不過在後一例中，我毫不懷疑作者是受了《伊里亞德》卷二十四那段可愛文字的誘惑──赫耳墨斯在那裡迎上普里阿摩斯，要引他到亞該亞人的營地去；事實上，上面提到的那段《伊里亞德》文字中，有一行漂亮的詩（還不止一行），被《奧德賽》的作者整句搬過來，用以描寫這位神的青春與俊美。[註2]除了這兩個例外，整部詩裡都是安德洛墨達救珀爾修斯，而不是珀爾修斯救安德洛墨達──是女基督徒做大勇先生的嚮導與守護者，而不是大勇先生做女基督徒的。
> The only males who give Ulysses any help while he is on his wanderings are Æolus, who does him no real service and refuses to help him a second time, and Mercury, who gives him the herb Moly (x. 305) to protect him against the spells of Circe. In this last case, however, I do not doubt that the writer was tempted by the lovely passage of *Il*. XXIV., where Mercury meets Priam to conduct him to the Achæan camp; one pretty line, indeed (and rather more), of the Iliadic passage above referred to is taken bodily by the writer of the *Odyssey* to describe the youth and beauty of the god.[註2] With these exceptions, throughout the poem Andromeda rescues Perseus, not Perseus Andromeda—Christiana is guide and guardian to Mr. Greatheart, not Mr. Greatheart to Christiana.

〔U0676 para〕
潘妮洛碧的情形似乎是個例外。也許有人會說，是奧德修斯前來解救她，而整部詩就繞著這件事打轉。但事實並非如此。奧德修斯殺死求婚者，第一是因為他們揮霍了他的家產──這從頭到尾都是最主要的怨恨；第二是因為他們姦污了他家中的女僕；第三，也只是第三，才是因為他還活著，他們就向他的妻子求婚（卷二十二 36–38 行）。從來沒有哪個女人比她更能在自己願意時守住自己的立場；而我很快就會詳細證明：她沒有守住的時候，是因為她寧可不守。
> The case of Penelope may seem to be an exception. It may be urged that Ulysses came to her rescue, and that the whole poem turns on his doing so. But this is not true. Ulysses kills the suitors, firstly, because they had wasted his substance—this from the first to last is the main grievance; secondly, because they had violated the female servants of his house; and only, thirdly, because they had offered marriage to his wife while he was still alive (xxii. 36-38). Never yet was woman better able to hold her own when she chose, and I will show at full length shortly that when she did not hold it it was because she preferred not to do so.

〔U0677 para〕
到目前為止，我談的是作者對陽世女性的態度。現在讓我們看看，在亡者的國度裡，她的本能驅使她認為什麼最有趣。奧德修斯到了黑帝斯的居所，遇見的第一個亡魂是他的同伴厄爾佩諾爾；此人喝醉了，就在奧德修斯一行即將啟航時，從喀耳刻家的屋頂上摔了下來。詩中明白告訴我們，他是個無足輕重的人，既不以頭腦也不以勇氣見長，所以讓他死了也無妨；而且顯而易見，這場意外之所以被允許發生，只是為了讓奧德修斯在冥府招呼這個亡魂時，能開個小玩笑，說厄爾佩諾爾走陸路，比奧德修斯走水路還早到。所以厄爾佩諾爾不算數。
> I have dealt so far with the writer's attitude towards women when in the world of the living. Let us now see what her instinct prompts her to consider most interesting in the kingdom of the dead. When Ulysses has reached the abode of Hades, the first ghost he meets is that of his comrade Elpenor, who had got drunk and fallen off the roof of Circe's house just as Ulysses and his men were about to set sail. We are expressly told that he was a person of no importance, being remarkable neither for sense nor courage, so that it does not matter about killing him, and it is transparent that the accident is only allowed to happen in order to enable Ulysses to make his little joke when he greets the ghost in Hades to the effect that Elpenor has got there more quickly by land than Ulysses had done by water. Elpenor therefore, does not count.

〔U0678 para〕
不過，成群亡魂走近奧德修斯的順序倒值得注意。奧德修斯獻祭的牲品之血流進他挖來承接的溝裡之後，作者寫道：──
> The order, however, in which the crowd of ghosts approach Ulysses, is noticeable. After the blood of the victims sacrificed by Ulysses had flowed into the trench which he had dug to receive it, the writer says:—

〔U0679 quote〕
「亡魂成群結隊從厄瑞玻斯湧上來──有新娘、年輕的單身漢、被勞苦磨盡的老人、為情所困的少女，還有戰死沙場的勇士，他們的鎧甲仍沾著血污；他們從四面八方而來，繞著那道溝飄來飄去，發出一種奇怪的尖叫聲，嚇得我臉色發白。」（卷十一 36–43 行）
> "The ghosts came trooping up from Erebus—brides, young bachelors, old men worn out with toil, maids who had been crossed in love, and brave men who had been killed in battle, with their armour still smirched with blood; they came from every quarter, and flitted round the trench with a strange kind of screaming sound that made me turn pale with fear" (xi. 36-43).

〔U0680 para〕
我不認為男性作者會把新娘排在第一，也不會把年輕單身漢排在第二。他會從國王、偉大的戰士或詩人開始；我也不相信他會只因為亡魂叫了幾聲，就讓奧德修斯嚇得臉色發白；它們得更嚴重地威脅他才行。
> I do not think a male writer would have put the brides first, nor yet the young bachelors second. He would have begun with kings or great warriors or poets, nor do I believe he would make Ulysses turn pale with fear merely because the ghosts screamed a little; they would have had to menace him more seriously.

〔U0681 para〕
班揚是怎麼做的？基督徒告訴易遷他在天堂裡會遇到什麼樣的同伴時，他說：──
> What does Bunyan do? When Christian tells Pliable what kind of company he will meet in Paradise, he says:—

〔U0682 quote〕
「在那裡，我們將看見長老們戴著金冠；在那裡，我們將看見聖潔的童女們手持金琴；在那裡，我們將看見那些為了愛那地方的主，而被世人砍成碎塊、在火焰中焚燒、被野獸吞食、在海中淹死的人；他們都安然無恙，並且披上不朽，如同穿上衣裳。」
> "There we shall see elders with their golden crowns; there we shall see holy virgins with their golden harps; there we shall see men that by the world were cut in pieces, burnt in flames, eaten of beasts, drowned in the seas, for the love they bore to the Lord of that place; all well and cloathed with immortality as with a garment."

〔U0683 para〕
最先出現在他腦中的，本能地是男人；雖然他離開他們片刻，卻立刻又回到他們身上，甚至沒有承認殉道者中也有女人。
> Men present themselves to him instinctively in the first instance, and though he quits them for a moment, he returns to them immediately without even recognising the existence of women among the martyrs.

〔U0684 para〕
此外，當基督徒和盼望渡過死亡之河、到達永恆之城時，前來迎接他們的，全是男人。
> Moreover, when Christian and Hopeful have passed through the river of death and readied the eternal city, it is none but men who greet them.

〔U0685 para〕
不錯，班揚把基督徒帶到永恆之城之後，在第二部裡也引領女基督徒和她的孩子們前往；但是，如果他是一個受了感召的女人而不是一個受了感召的男人，如果這個女人是照著自己本能所啟示的去寫，既不模仿男人，也不懼怕男人，那麼她無疑會先寫女基督徒，至於基督徒──如果她還要寫他的話──就放到她的附錄裡去。
> True, after having taken Christian to the Eternal City, Bunyan conducts Christiana also, and her children, in his Second Part; but surely if he had been an inspired woman and not an inspired man, and if this woman had been writing as it was borne in upon her by her own instinct, neither aping man nor fearing him, she would have taken Christiana first, and Christian, if she took him at all, in her appendix.

〔U0686 para〕
在厄爾佩諾爾之後，奧德修斯見到的第一個亡魂是他的母親安提克蕾亞；他非常難過，因為依照喀耳刻的指示，在聽完忒拜先知忒瑞西阿斯要告訴他的話之前，他不能跟母親說話。一聽完先知的話，他便問要怎樣才能讓母親認出他、跟他交談。這一點得到解答之後，接下來便是他與安提克蕾亞之間那段無比優美的場景，約有七八十行；最後，他的母親要他盡快回家，好講述他在冥府的種種經歷──講給誰聽？講給全世界？講給他的親族和同胞？不：是要講給他的妻子聽，而且顯然不講給別人聽（卷十一 223、224 行）。這當然十分正確得體；但它更像女性作者的特色，而不像男性作者的。
> Next to Elpenor the first ghost that Ulysses sees is that of his mother Anticlea, and he is sorely grieved that he may not, by Circe's instructions, speak to her till he has heard what the Theban prophet Tiresias had got to tell him. As soon as he has heard this, he enquires how he can make his mother recognise him, and converse with him. This point being answered there follows the incomparably beautiful scene between him and Anticlea, which occupies some seventy or eighty lines, and concludes by his mother's telling him to get home as fast as he can that he may tell of his adventures in Hades—to whom? To the world at large? To his kinsmen and countrymen? No: it is to his wife that he is to recount them and apparently to nobody else (xi. 223, 224). Very right and proper; but more characteristic of a female than of a male writer.

〔U0687 para〕
安提克蕾亞一離開，緊接著出場的是誰？珀耳塞福涅送上來「所有偉大君王的妻子與女兒」──薩爾摩紐斯的女兒堤羅、阿索波斯的女兒安提娥珮、阿爾克墨涅、厄庇卡斯忒（大家比較熟悉的名字是伊俄卡斯忒）、涅琉斯的妻子克羅麗絲、勒達、伊菲墨得亞、淮德拉、普洛克里斯、阿里阿德涅、邁拉、克呂墨涅，以及厄里費勒。奧德修斯說，還有許多英雄的妻女跟他交談過，但時間不容他再一一細說；不過，應阿爾基諾俄斯王的懇切要求，他才接著講他如何遇見阿伽門農、阿基里斯和埃阿斯（埃阿斯不肯跟他說話）；他也略略提到米諾斯、俄里翁、提堤俄斯、坦塔羅斯、西西弗斯和赫拉克勒斯。
> Who follow immediately on the departure of Anticlea? Proserpine sends up "all the wives and daughters of great princes"—Tyro, daughter of Salmoneus, Antiope daughter of Asopus, Alcmena, Epicaste (better known as Jocasta), Chloris wife of Neleus, Leda, Iphimedeia, Phædra, Procris, Ariadne, Mæra, Clymene, and Eriphyle. Ulysses says that there were many more wives and daughters of heroes whom he conversed with, but that time would not allow him to detail them further; in deference, however, to the urgent request of King Alcinous, he goes on to say how he met Agamemnon, Achilles, and Ajax (who would not speak to him); he touched lightly also on Minos, Orion, Tityus, Tantalus, Sisyphus, and Hercules.

〔U0688 para〕
我聽過一些女人說，冥府中女人比男人先出場這件事說明不了什麼，因為換作她們自己，多半會把男人排在女人前面；她們也能理解，男性作者最先被吸引的會是女性亡魂。女人一旦知道我的用意何在，通常就會這麼告訴我；但是，當我另找一位女士替我去探她們的口風，或者我自己小心翼翼地暗中試探時，我卻發現，她們寧可去見聖母瑪利亞、夏娃、伊莉莎白女王、克麗奧佩脫拉、莎芙、珍．奧斯汀、匈牙利的聖伊莉莎白、特洛伊的海倫、齊諾比婭以及其他偉大的女性，甚至勝過見荷馬與莎士比亞。有一位舒坦居家的婦人，我在她身上下了很大的工夫，她說她想不通我問這種問題是什麼意思；不過，要是我真想知道，她見伊莉莎白．拉曾比太太，和見伊莉莎白女王或她們當中的任何一位，同樣樂意。至於我自己，倘若要我挑選幾位想見的亡魂，我會把莎芙、珍．奧斯汀和《奧德賽》的女作者列入名單；但我大概會先要求見荷馬、莎士比亞、韓德爾、舒伯特、阿爾坎傑羅．柯瑞里、普賽爾、喬凡尼．貝里尼、林布蘭、霍爾班、德霍赫、唐納太羅、讓．德韋斯潘，以及其他許多男人──然而，《奧德賽》的作者讓我如此深感興趣，我也不敢說自己不會要求先見她，再見其他任何人。
> I have heard women say that nothing can be made out of the fact that the women in Hades are introduced before the men, inasmuch as they would themselves have been more likely to put the men before the women, and can understand that a male writer would be attracted in the first instance by the female shades. When women know what I am driving at, they generally tell me this, but when I have got another woman to sound them for me, or when I have stalked them warily, I find that they would rather meet the Virgin Mary, Eve, Queen Elizabeth, Cleopatra, Sappho, Jane Austen, St. Elizabeth of Hungary, Helen of Troy, Zenobia, and other great women than even Homer and Shakspeare. One comfortable homely woman with whom I had taken great pains said she could not think what I meant by asking such questions, but if I wanted to know, she would as lief meet Mrs. Elizabeth Lazenby as Queen Elizabeth or any one of them. For my own part, had I to choose a number of shades whom I would meet, I should include Sappho, Jane Austen, and the authoress of the *Odyssey* in my list, but I should probably ask first for Homer, Shakspeare, Handel, Schubert, Arcangelo Corelli, Purcell, Giovanni Bellini, Rembrandt, Holbein, De Hooghe, Donatello, Jean de Wespin and many another man—yet the writer of the *Odyssey* interests me so profoundly that I am not sure I should not ask to see her before any of the others.

〔U0689 para〕
我不知道還有哪位女作家曾把她的主人公送下冥府；但男人這麼做時，總是先處理男人，後處理女人。讓我們看看但丁。維吉爾告訴他，基督降入地獄時最先救了誰，我們發現他第一個救出的是亞當，關於夏娃卻隻字未提。接著獲救的是亞伯、挪亞、亞伯拉罕、大衛、雅各和他的兒子們──最後，就在 *et ceteri*（其餘眾人）之前──才有一個女人，拉結。維吉爾說完之後，但丁開始自己去遇見各種人物。最先來的是荷馬、賀拉斯、奧維德和盧坎；這幾位打發完了，接著是厄勒克特拉、赫克托耳、埃涅阿斯、凱撒、卡米拉、彭忒西勒亞、拉丁努斯、拉維尼亞、布魯圖斯、卡托的妻子瑪爾契婭、尤莉亞、科爾涅莉亞、薩拉丁、蘇格拉底、柏拉圖、德謨克利特、第歐根尼、赫拉克利特、恩培多克勒、阿那克薩哥拉、泰利斯、芝諾、狄奧斯科里德斯、俄耳甫斯、利諾斯、西塞羅、塞內卡、歐幾里得、托勒密、希波克拉底、蓋倫、阿維森納和阿威羅伊。七個女人對二十六個男人。這份名單讓我想起約翰．拉伯克爵士的百本好書，所以我不再追究但丁了；我之所以把它全文列出，是因為我不喜歡他。就我所見，義大利人自己也開始對他起疑了；最近不只一次有很夠格的評論家對我說：「Dante è un falso idolo」（但丁是一尊假偶像）。
> I know of no other women writers who have sent their heroes down to Hades, but when men have done so they deal with men first and women afterwards. Let us turn to Dante. When Virgil tells him whom Christ first saved when he descended into Hell, we find that he first rescued Adam. Not a word is there about Eve. Then are rescued Abel, Noah, Abraham, David, Jacob and his sons—and lastly, just before the *et ceteri*—one woman, Rachel. When Virgil has finished, Dante begins meeting people on his own account. First come Homer, Horace, Ovid, and Lucan; when these have been disposed of we have Electra, Hector, Æneas, Cæsar, Camilla, Penthesilea, Latinus, Lavinia, Brutus, Cato's wife Marcia, Julia, Cornelia, Saladin, Socrates, Plato, Democritus, Diogenes, Heraclitus, Empedocles, Anaxagoras, Thales, Zeno, Dioscorides, Orpheus, Linus, Cicero, Seneca, Euclid, Ptolemy, Hippocrates, Galen, Avicen, and Averroes. Seven women to twenty-six men. This list reminds me of Sir John Lubbock's hundred books, I shall therefore pursue Dante no further; I have given it in full because I do not like him. So far as I can see the Italians themselves are beginning to have their doubts about him; "Dante è un falso idolo," has been said to me more than once lately by highly competent critics.

〔U0690 para〕
現在來看看《埃涅阿斯紀》。埃涅阿斯和西比拉走近斯堤克斯河時，我們讀到：──
> Let us now look to the *Æneid*. When Æneas and the Sibyl approach the river Styx, we read:—

〔U0691 para〕
Huc omnis turba ad ripas effusa ruebat Matres atque viri, defunctaque corpora vitâ Magnanimûm heroum, pueri, innuptæque puellæ, Impositique rogis juvenes ante ora parentum.（這時全體群眾蜂擁而來，湧向河岸：母親與男人，還有已盡此生的心胸寬宏的英雄們的軀體，男孩和未嫁的少女，以及在父母眼前被放上火葬堆的青年。）《埃涅阿斯紀》卷六 305–308 行。
> Huc omnis turba ad ripas effusa ruebat Matres atque viri, defunctaque corpora vitâ Magnanimûm heroum, pueri, innuptæque puellæ, Impositique rogis juvenes ante ora parentum. *Æn*. vi. 305-308.

〔U0692 para〕
女人確實排在前面，但 *viri* 裡的 *i* 是短音，維吉爾也無可奈何；而且他最先認出的個別人物都是男人──也就是他手下兩位淹死的隊長琉卡斯庇斯和俄隆特斯──還有帕利努魯斯。渡過斯堤克斯河之後，他先穿過夭折嬰兒所居的地區；接著是含冤被判死刑者所居的地區；然後是自殺者的地區；再來是為愛而死者的地區，他在那裡看見幾個女人，其中有蒂朵，而她對待他的方式，就像埃阿斯對待奧德修斯一樣。埃涅阿斯在冥府見到或與之交談的其餘人物，全都是男人。
> The women indeed come first, but the *i* in *viri* being short Virgil could not help himself, and the first persons whom he recognises as individuals are men—namely two of his captains who had been drowned, Leucaspis and Orontes—and Palinurus. After crossing the Styx he first passes through the region inhabited by those who have died as infants; then that by those who have been unjustly condemned to die; then that by suicides; then that of those who have died for love, where he sees several women, and among them Dido, who treats him as Ajax treated Ulysses. The rest of those whom Æneas sees or converses with in Hades are all men.

〔U0693 para〕
琉善更不懂得對女士殷勤，他的《亡靈對話》裡連一個女人都沒有。
> Lucian is still more ungallant, for in his dialogues of the dead he does not introduce a single woman.

〔U0694 para〕
在許多本該浮現在我腦海的例子中，我只想得起另外一個：菲爾丁的《來世之旅》。他在驛車上最先交談的三個亡魂都是男人。到了旅程終點，他與自己的小女兒有一段簡短卻極為感人的場面──她在菲爾丁寫作的短短幾個月前，還是個小孩子就死了，因此與本題無關──接著他寫道：「我最先與之交談的靈魂，是斯巴達赫赫有名的列奧尼達。」當然；軍人總是先招呼軍人。下一段只給了莎芙一行字，說她正由俄耳甫斯伴奏而歌唱。然後就輪到荷馬[註3]、維吉爾、艾迪生、莎士比亞、貝特頓、布斯和米爾頓。
> One other case alone occurs to me among the many that ought to do so; I refer to Fielding's *Journey to the next World*. The three first ghosts whom he speaks to in the coach are men. When he gets to his journey's end, after a short but most touching scene with his own little daughter who had died a mere child only a few months before Fielding wrote, and who is therefore nothing to the point, he continues: "The first spirit with whom I entered into discourse, was the famous Leonidas of Sparta." Of course; soldier will greet soldier first. In the next paragraph one line is given to Sappho, who we are told was singing to the accompaniment of Orpheus. Then we go on to Homer,[註3] Virgil, Addison, Shakspeare, Betterton, Booth, and Milton.

〔U0695 para〕
再看笛福：他是個上了年紀的已婚男人，想安慰魯賓遜．克魯索時，除了給他另一個男人作伴，再也想不出更好的辦法，於是就把星期五送給他。換作女人，她會送給他一個和藹可親、容貌姣好的白人女孩，是食人族從某艘遇難船上擄來的俘虜。她會認為這樣對他可能有用得多。
> Defoe, again, being an elderly married man, and wanting to comfort Robinson Crusoe, can think of nothing better for him than the companionship of another man, whereon he sends him Friday. A woman would have sent him an amiable and good-looking white girl whom the cannibals had taken prisoner from some shipwrecked vessel. This she would have held as likely to be far more useful to him.

〔U0696 para〕
以上這些足以說明：男人的心思，除非他正年輕、害著相思病，否則本能地更傾向男人而不是女人。而我深信──其實每個人，無論他或她自己知不知道，也都這麼相信──除了上述例外，女人對女人更感興趣。正因如此，聖母瑪利亞才成了天后，而且在羅馬天主教國家的平民百姓眼中，實際上比三位一體本身還重要。因為支持神學家的，女人比男人多。再者，據說猶太男人有一段禱詞，男人在其中感謝上帝沒有讓他們生為女人，女人則感謝上帝沒有讓她們生為男人。每一種性別都最堅定地相信自己；而除非我們徹底廢除了個人主義，否則對於這種安排，我們找不出半點可以抱怨的理由。女人倘若嘗試寫史詩，幾乎不得不以男人作為中心人物，但她會把他縮小，把他的妻女放大，並以更細膩的筆觸描寫她們。《奧德賽》的作者這樣做了，是顯而易見的；光憑這一事實，就該讓我們強烈傾向於認為：我們面對的不是一個男人，而是一個女人。
> So much to show that the mind of man, unless when he is young and lovesick, turns more instinctively to man than to woman. And I am convinced, as indeed every one else is whether he or she knows it or no, that with the above exception, woman is more interested in woman. This is how the Virgin Mary has come to be Queen of Heaven, and practically of more importance than the Trinity itself in the eyes of the common people in Roman Catholic countries. For the women support the theologians more than the men do. The male Jews, again, so I am told, have a prayer in which the men thank God that they were not born women, and the women, that they were not born men. Each sex believes most firmly in itself, nor till we have done away with individualism altogether can we find the smallest reason to complain of this arrangement. A woman if she attempts an Epic is almost compelled to have a man for her central figure, but she will minimise him, and will maximise his wife and daughters, drawing them with subtler hand. That the writer of the *Odyssey* has done this is obvious; and this fact alone should make us incline strongly towards thinking that we are in the hands not of a man but of a woman.

〔U0697 footnote〕
理查．加內特編《雪萊書信選》，基根．保羅．特倫奇公司，1882 年，第 149 頁。
> *Select Letters of Percy Bysshe Shelley*, edited by Richard Garnett, Kegan Paul Trench & Co., 1882, p. 149.

〔U0698 footnote〕
《奧德賽》卷十 278、279 行；參照《伊里亞德》卷二十四 347、348 行。
> *Od*. x. 278, 279; cf. *Il*. XXIV. 347, 348.

〔U0699 footnote〕
菲爾丁談到荷馬時說：「我好奇地問他，他是否真的像傳聞所說，把那部詩〔《伊里亞德》〕寫成零散的篇章，撒遍了整個希臘。他聽了我的問題微微一笑，反問我這部詩看起來是否前後連貫；如果是，他想我自己就能回答這個問題了。」這段話初次發表於 1743 年，無疑是有意回應本特利。見傑布《荷馬導論》，1888 年版，第 106 頁註 1。
> Talking of *Homer* Fielding says, "I had the curiosity to ask him whether he had really writ that poem [the *Iliad*] in detached pieces and flung it about all over Greece, according to the report that went of him. He smiled at my question, and asked me whether there appeared any connection in the poem; for if there did he thought I might answer for myself." This was first published in 1743, and is no doubt intended as a reply to Bentley. See Jebb's *Introduction to Homer*, ed. 1888, note 1 on p. 106.

〔U0700 heading〕
第四章
> CHAPTER IV.

〔U0701 para〕
為女性的榮譽與尊嚴而戒慎──對玷辱同性者嚴厲無情──喜愛瑣細的宗教儀節──喜愛說教──喜愛善意的小謊與小小的作戲──喜愛兩面討好──以及喜愛錢財。
> JEALOUSY FOR THE HONOUR AND DIGNITY OF WOMAN—SEVERITY AGAINST THOSE WHO HAVE DISGRACED THEIR SEX—LOVE OF SMALL RELIGIOUS OBSERVANCES—OF PREACHING—OF WHITE LIES AND SMALL PLAY-ACTING—OF HAVING THINGS BOTH WAYS—AND OF MONEY.

〔U0702 para〕
作者處理女性時，不但顯露出明顯更多的興趣與更豐富的知識，而且她清楚表明，她對自己性別的榮譽極為戒慎，因此對那些玷辱了這份榮譽的女人，也就嚴厲得毫不留情。女神愛做什麼就做什麼，不受凡人的律法評判；但凡間女子一旦失足，就非死不可。
> Not only does the writer shew a markedly greater both interest and knowledge when dealing with women, but she makes it plain that she is exceedingly jealous for the honour of her sex, and by consequence inexorable in her severity against those women who have disgraced it. Goddesses may do what they like, they are not to be judged by mortal codes; but a mortal woman who has fallen must die.

〔U0703 para〕
整部《奧德賽》裡，從來沒有哪個女人被取笑。女人可以被吊死，卻不可以被取笑。男人可以被取笑；其實，阿爾基諾俄斯幾乎每次被提到，都是為了讓他多少顯得可笑。我們不能說卷四和卷十五裡的墨涅拉俄斯是被刻意寫得可笑，但他確實顯得可笑，而且被當成一個遠不如他妻子有趣、也遠不如她重要的人物。事實上，奧德修斯、阿爾基諾俄斯、墨涅拉俄斯和涅斯托爾彼此如此相像，我毫不懷疑他們是以同一個人為原型畫出來的，正如伊薩卡和斯刻里亞是以同一個地方為原型。那個人是誰，我們永遠無從知曉；不過我要指出，除非女兒崇拜她的父親，否則父親對她來說，通常是個神祕而有權勢的存在，他的行事與她的行事截然不同。她怕他，就像孩子怕黑房間；而如果他的妻子有點喜歡取笑他，女兒也不會放過他，無論她多麼會哄他，又以某種方式愛著他。
> No woman throughout the *Odyssey* is ever laughed at. Women may be hanged but they must not be laughed at. Men may be laughed at, indeed Alcinous is hardly mentioned at all except to be made more or less ridiculous. One cannot say that Menelaus in Books iv. and xv. is being deliberately made ridiculous, but made ridiculous he certainly is, and he is treated as a person of far less interest and importance than his wife is. Indeed Ulysses, Alcinous, Menelaus, and Nestor are all so like one another that I do not doubt they were drawn from the same person, just as Ithaca and Scheria are from the same place. Who that person was we shall never know; nevertheless I would point out that unless a girl adores her father he is generally, to her, a mysterious powerful being whose ways are not as her ways. He is feared as a dark room is feared by children; and if his wife is at all given to laughing at him, his daughter will not spare him, however much she may cajole and in a way love him.

〔U0704 para〕
但是，如我說過的，男人雖然可以被取笑，女人卻從來都被認真對待。阿芙蘿黛蒂倒是在某一段裡顯得有點可笑，但她是女神，所以無妨；況且，嘲笑的矛頭主要是由阿瑞斯承受的，而阿芙蘿黛蒂立刻就由美惠三女神重新裝扮、好言撫慰了。我想不起有哪一個例子，是讓女人去做一件她做了就會有失身分的事──當然，奴隸除外，我說的是上層社會。
> But, as I have said, though men may be laughed at, the women are never taken other than quite seriously. Venus is indeed, made a little ridiculous in one passage, but she was a goddess, so it does not matter; besides, the brunt of the ridicule was borne by Mars, and Venus was instantly readorned and comforted by the Graces. I cannot remember a single instance of a woman's being made to do anything which she could not do without loss of dignity—I except, of course, slaves, and am speaking of the higher social classes.

〔U0705 para〕
常有人指出，《伊里亞德》裡眾神的使者總是伊里斯，而在《奧德賽》裡，這個角色同樣一律是赫耳墨斯。我傾向於把這歸因於作者不喜歡讓伊里斯這樣一位高貴的女士替任何人跑腿聽差。因為顯然，一般人仍然認為伊里斯一向是眾神的使者。這從卷十八開頭可以看出：詩中告訴我們，伊羅斯的本名是阿爾奈俄斯，但人家叫他伊羅斯（這不過就是 Iris 加上一個陽性詞尾），「因為只要有人差遣，他就去傳話」。作家們除非有自己的特殊理由，是不會公然違背流行說法的。
> It has often been observed that the Messenger of the Gods in the *Iliad* is always Iris, while in the *Odyssey* he is no less invariably Mercury. I incline to attribute this to the author's dislike of the idea that so noble a lady as Iris should be made to fetch and carry for anybody. For it is evident Iris was still generally held to have been the messenger of the gods. This appears from the beginning of Book xviii., where we are told that Irus's real name was Arnæus, but that he was called Irus (which is nothing but Iris with a masculine termination) "because he used to carry messages when any one would send him." Writers do not fly in the face of current versions unless for some special reasons of their own.

〔U0706 para〕
然而，女人若是行為不端，就絕不寬貸。相關的例子只有三個，其中一個幾乎不算數，因為那個有罪的女人──克呂泰涅斯特拉──所受的懲罰，並不是女作者親手施加的。不過，克呂泰涅斯特拉罪行的故事緊緊抓住了她，她一再回到這個話題，她對此深感恐懼，卻又同時想盡量替克呂泰涅斯特拉卸下罪責──這一切使我確信，她深切感受到克呂泰涅斯特拉的背叛給全體女性帶來的恥辱，「連好女人也不能倖免」。不然她為什麼要費這麼大的力氣告訴我們，克呂泰涅斯特拉天性本來善良（卷三 266 行），而且在阿伽門農託付保護她的那位歌人被害死之前，她一直無可指摘？[註1]等到她孤身一人──既沒有丈夫，也沒有監護人，又有埃癸斯托斯這樣陰險的卑鄙小人用不斷的奉承來引誘她──她便屈服了；此外就沒什麼好說的了，只能說這實在太可怕，她只好聽任命運處置。我看到格萊斯頓先生曾納悶，是什麼促使荷馬（他認為《伊里亞德》與《奧德賽》都是荷馬所作）告訴我們克呂泰涅斯特拉起初是個好女人[註2]；但是，儘管我十分敬重他對荷馬文學的偉大貢獻，我卻不認為他找到了正確的解釋。此外也不該忘記，這種替克呂泰涅斯特拉開脫罪責的寫法，屬於《奧德賽》中後來嫁接到原始構想上的部分──在這部分裡，如我稍後將要證明的，還有另一個女人的罪過，它之所以沒有被開脫，只是因為它在壓倒性的證據面前被徹底否認了──我指的是潘妮洛碧的罪過。
> If, however, a woman has misconducted herself she is to be shewn no mercy. There are only three cases in point, and one of these hardly counts inasmuch as the punishment of the guilty woman, Clytemnestra, was not meted out to her by the authoress herself. The hold, however, which the story of Clytemnestra's guilt has upon her, the manner in which she repeatedly recurs to it, her horror at it, but at the same time her desire to remove as much of the blame as possible from Clytemnestra's shoulders, convinces me that she acutely feels the disgrace which Clytemnestra's treachery has inflicted upon all women "even on the good ones." Why should she be at such pains to tell us that Clytemnestra was a person of good natural disposition (iii. 266), and was irreproachable until death had removed the bard under whose protection Agamemnon had placed her?[註1] When she was left alone—without either husband or guardian, and with an insidious wretch like Ægisthus beguiling her with his incessant flattery, she yielded, and there is no more to be said, except that it was very dreadful and she must be abandoned to her fate. I see Mr. Gladstone has wondered what should have induced Homer (whom he holds to have written the *Odyssey* as well as the *Iliad*) to tell us that Clytemnestra was a good woman to start with,[註2] but with all my respect for his great services to Homeric literature, I cannot think that he has hit upon the right explanation. It should not be forgotten, moreover, that this extenuation of Clytemnestra's guilt belongs to a part of the *Odyssey* that was engrafted on to the original design—a part in which, as I shall show later, there was another woman's guilt, which was only not extenuated because it was absolutely denied in the face of overwhelming evidence—I mean Penelope's.

〔U0707 para〕
第二個例子，是歐邁俄斯小時候把他拐走的那個女人。她做了這件事、跟著腓尼基商人上了船之後沒幾天，就被阿蒂蜜絲殺死，屍體被拋下船去餵海豹和魚（卷十五 403–484 行）。
> The second case in point is that of the woman who stole Eumæus when he was a child. A few days after she has done this, and has gone on board the ship with the Phœnician traders, she is killed by Diana, and thrown overboard to the seals and fishes (xv. 403-484).

〔U0708 para〕
第三個例子，是奧德修斯家中那些在他離家期間與求婚者胡來的女人。詩中告訴我們，家裡有五十個女僕，其中只有十二個有罪。奇怪的是，女僕的數目竟與阿爾基諾俄斯王家中的侍女數目完全相同；另外也該注意，考慮到求婚者有一百多人，而女僕們似乎只要願意，夜裡就能離開宅邸（卷二十 6–8 行），十二個有罪的女僕實在是個很小的數目──不錯，詩中別處告訴我們，那些女人是遭到姦污、被迫屈從的，但這樣一來，她們人數之少就更令人驚奇了──而且我還可以補一句：吊死她們也就更加殘酷可怕。我想，史前時代風氣的鬆弛，會促使一個對女性榮譽並不特別戒慎的作者說，有罪的有三十八個，甚至更多，清白的只有十二個。另一方面，我們也必須記住：奧德修斯殺死求婚者之後，歐律克蕾亞把那三十八個清白的女人帶出來見他，奧德修斯一一認出了她們（卷二十二 501 行）。所以她們當中最年輕的，也很難說會在四十歲以下，有些無疑還更老──因為奧德修斯已經離家二十年了。
> The third case is that of the women of Ulysses' household who had misconducted themselves with the suitors during his absence. We are told that there were fifty women servants in the house, of whom twelve alone were guilty. It is curious that the number of servants should be exactly the same as that of the maidservants in the house of king Alcinous, and it should be also noted that twelve is a very small number for the guilty servants, considering that there were over a hundred suitors, and that the maids seem to have been able to leave the house by night when they chose to do so (xx. 6-8)—true, we are elsewhere told that the women had been violated and only yielded under compulsion, but this makes it more wonderful that they should be so few—and I may add, more terribly severe to hang them. I think the laxity of prehistoric times would have prompted a writer who was not particularly jealous for the honour of woman, to have said that there were thirty-eight, or even more, guilty, and only twelve innocent. We must bear in mind on the other hand that when Euryclea brought out the thirty-eight innocent women to see Ulysses after he had killed the suitors, Ulysses recognised them all (xxii. 501). The youngest of them therefore can hardly have been under forty, and so me no doubt were older—for Ulysses had been gone twenty years.

〔U0709 para〕
那麼，有罪的女僕受到怎樣的處置？有個男人把我「《奧德賽》出自女人之手」的理論當作純粹的 *mauvaise plaisanterie*（拙劣玩笑），有一次對我說，這種說法荒唐透頂，因為求婚者被殺之後，女人第一個會想到的，是餐廳的地毯。我說，*mutatis mutandis*（作必要的變通之後），這正是她確實想到的事。
> Now how are the guilty ones treated? A man who was speaking of my theory that the *Odyssey* was written by a woman as a mere *mauvaise plaisanterie*, once told me it was absurd, for the first thing a woman would have thought of after the suitors had been killed was the dining room carpet. I said that *mutatis mutandis* this was the very thing she did think of.

〔U0710 para〕
奧德修斯一確定沒有一個求婚者還活著，就吩咐歐律克蕾亞把有罪的女僕帶來；她們到了以後，他對忒勒馬科斯、歐邁俄斯和菲羅提俄斯說（卷二十二 437–443 行）：──
> As soon as Ulysses has satisfied himself that not a single suitor is left alive, he tells Euryclea to send him the guilty maidservants, and on their arrival he says to Telemachus, Eumæus and Philœtius (xxii. 437-443):—

〔U0711 quote〕
「動手把屍體搬走，叫那些女人幫你們。做完之後，把座椅和桌子都用海綿擦洗乾淨，直到整座宅子收拾妥當；然後把女僕們帶到外面去……用你們的劍刺穿她們。」
> "Begin to bear away the corpses, and make the women help you. When you have done this, sponge down the seats and tables, till you have set the whole house in order; then take the maids outside....and thrust them through with your swords."

〔U0712 para〕
這些命令都被忠實執行：女僕們幫忙搬走屍體，又用海綿擦洗椅子和桌子，直到擦乾淨為止──奧德修斯就站在一旁盯著，不讓她們浪費片刻。這些做完之後，忒勒馬科斯（詩中告訴我們〔卷二十二 426–427 行〕，他的母親一直不許他對女僕發號施令）把她們帶到外面，吊死了她們（卷二十二 462 行），認為這比他父親規定的死法更不光彩──或許他也想到，比起用劍刺死她們，這樣要清理的血會少一些──不過請參看第 98 頁的註。作者用一行大半借自《伊里亞德》的詩告訴我們[註3]，她們的腳抽搐了一會兒，但時間不長；然而她對忒勒馬科斯究竟怎麼吊死她們的，概念極其模糊。至今還沒有哪位評註家能弄懂這一段；唯一的解釋似乎是：作者自己也不懂，而且也懶得去懂。只要知道那些女人顯然是被吊死了，也就夠了。
> These orders are faithfully obeyed; the maids help in the work of removing the bodies and they sponge the chairs and tables till they are clean—Ulysses standing over them and seeing that they lose no time. This done, Telemachus (whose mother, we are told (xxii. 426-427) had never yet permitted him to give orders to the female servants) takes them outside and hangs them (xxii. 462), as a more dishonourable death than the one his father had prescribed for them—perhaps also he may have thought he should have less blood to clean up than if he stabbed them—but see note on p. 98. The writer tells us in a line which she borrows in great part from the *Iliad*,[註3] that their feet move convulsively for a short time though not for very long, but her ideas of the way in which Telemachus hanged them are of the vaguest. No commentator has ever yet been able to understand it; the only explanation seems to be that the writer did not understand it herself, and did not care to do so. Let it suffice that the women were obviously hanged.

〔U0713 para〕
在基督教以前的時代，沒有哪個男性作者會認為那些女人的罪過需要如此可怕的懲罰。他或許會下令處死她們，卻不會把憤慨推到這種地步，讓她們先去清洗自己情夫的血。作者對求婚者固然兇狠，對那些女人卻兇狠得多。求婚者全被殺死之後，歐律克蕾亞開始對著他們發出勝利的歡呼，奧德修斯卻制止了她。他說：「住嘴，老婦人，對著死人的屍體誇口是不對的。」（卷二十二 411 行）同樣地，一直到那些女人即將被處死的那一刻，還要逼她們去做最駭人的差事，也是不對的；但作者似乎全然沒有意識到這一點：凡是女性的榮譽遭到同性之人玷污，再重的懲罰對她們都不算過分。
> No man writing in pre-Christian times would have considered the guilt of the women to require so horrible a punishment. He might have ordered them to be killed, but he would not have carried his indignation to the point of making them first clean up the blood of their paramours. Fierce as the writer is against the suitors, she is far more so against the women. When the suitors are all killed, Euryclea begins to raise a cry of triumph over them, but Ulysses checks her. "Hold your tongue, woman," he says, "it is ill bragging over the bodies of dead men" (xxii. 411). So also it is ill getting the most hideous service out of women up to the very moment when they are to be executed; but the writer seems to have no sense of this; where female honour has been violated by those of woman's own sex, no punishment is too bad for them.

〔U0714 para〕
《奧德賽》中讓我傾向於把它歸於女人之手的其他主要特徵，是一種為藝術而藝術式的、對小小謊言的喜愛，以及只要合她的意，就決心兩面都要占的態度。這一點似乎從來不曾困擾她。故事就擺在那裡，讀者愛信不信。她喜愛那些一戳就破的偽裝，喜愛那些自相拆台、誰也騙不了的故弄玄虛。不必往遠處找，單看卷一和卷三：雅典娜在這兩卷裡各編了一套煞有介事、細節俱全的故事──一次說她正載著一船鐵要到忒墨塞去，打算換銅回來（卷一 184 行）；另一次說她第二天早上要到考科涅斯人那裡，去討一筆拖欠她很久的大債（卷三 366 行）──然後，她費盡心思編出來的謊話還沒說完，她就化成一隻鷹飛上天空，自己露了底。順帶一提，如果她是在一座有屋頂的大廳裡，這兩次她都很難這麼做；但如果像我所設想的，她兩次都是在一道環繞露天庭院、有屋頂的迴廊裡，那就可以想像她能這麼做了。
> The other chief characteristics of the *Odyssey* which incline me to ascribe it to a woman are a kind of art for art's sake love of a small lie, and a determination to have things both ways whenever it suits her purpose. This never seems to trouble her. There the story is, and the reader may take it or leave it. She loves flimsy disguises and mystifications that stultify themselves, and mystify nobody. To go no further than books i. and iii., Minerva in each of these tells plausible stories full of circumstantial details, about her being on her way to Temesa with a cargo of iron and how she meant to bring back copper (i. 184), and again how she was going to the Cauconians on the following morning to recover a large debt that had been long owing to her (iii. 366), and then, before the lies she had been at such pains to concoct are well out of her mouth she reveals herself by flying into the air in the form of an eagle. This, by the way, she could not well do in either case if she was in a roofed hall, but might be conceived as doing if, as I suppose her to have been in both cases, she was in a roofed cloister that ran round an open court.

〔U0715 para〕
這幾次飛走，頗有平行五度的味道[註4]，如果還不算是不折不扣的平行八度的話；我不禁認為，作者若肯費心去找，本可以找到更圓滑的進行方式。不過這一點暫且不談：善意的小謊從第一卷到最後一卷不斷出現；瑣細的宗教儀節處處被一絲不苟地堅持，而這些儀節一旦辦妥，卻又相當不擇手段；對神明與徵兆的敬畏，以及對一般 *convenances*（禮法規矩）的敬重──這一切在我看來，都更像女人的筆法，而不像男人的。
> There is a flavour of consecutive fifths in these flights,[註4] if indeed they are not downright octaves, and I cannot but think that the writer would have found a smoother progression open to her if she had cared to look for one; but letting this pass, the way in which white lies occur from the first book to the last, the punctiliousness, omnipresent, with which small religious observances are insisted upon, coupled with not a little unscrupulousness when these have been attended to, the respect for gods and omens, and for the *convenances* generally—all these seem to me to be more characteristic of a woman's writing than a man's.

〔U0716 para〕
再者，忒勒馬科斯被看待得那麼認真；他那麼緊緊遵守自己的計畫；凡是他父親、他母親、雅典娜或任何負責任的人告訴他該做什麼，他總是一絲不苟地照做──只有一段例外，而那一段幾乎是 *verbatim*（逐字）照搬《伊里亞德》的[註5]；雅典娜怎樣把他打扮得俊美、怎樣對他說教；他怎樣不聲不響卻堪為楷模地善盡一切宗教、道德與社交上的本分──這一切在我看來，都指向一個結論：作者是個女人，而且是個年輕的女人。
> The seriousness, again, with which Telemachus is taken, the closeness with which he adheres to his programme, the precision with which he invariably does what his father, his mother, Minerva, or any responsible person tells him that he should do, except in one passage which is taken almost *verbatim* from the *Iliad*,[註5] the way in which Minerva beautifies him and preaches to him; the unobtrusive but exemplary manner in which he discharges all his religious, moral, and social duties—all seem to me to point in the direction of thinking that the writer is a woman and a young one.

〔U0717 para〕
雅典娜是怎樣對他說教的？他在海裡洗了手之後，祈求她幫助他完成打算出海打聽父親消息的航程。女神於是化身為門托爾來到他面前，說了下面這番話：
> How does Minerva preach to him? When he has washed his hands in the sea he prays that she will help him on his intended voyage in search of news concerning his father. The goddess then comes up to him disguised as Mentor, and speaks as follows:

〔U0718 quote〕
「忒勒馬科斯，如果你和你父親是同樣的材料做成的，那麼從今以後你就既不會是傻瓜，也不會是懦夫，因為奧德修斯從來不食言，也從來不半途而廢。那麼，你若像他，你的航程就不會徒勞無功；但除非你的血管裡流著奧德修斯和潘妮洛碧的血，否則我看不出你有成功的希望。兒子很少比得上父親；他們通常更差，而不是更好；不過，既然你從今以後不會是傻瓜或懦夫，又並非完全沒有承襲你父親的明智眼光，我對你這番事業是抱著希望的。」（卷二 270–280 行）
> "Telemachus, if you are made of the same stuff as your father you will be neither fool or coward henceforward, for Ulysses never broke his word nor left his work half done. If, then, you take after him your voyage will not be fruitless, but unless you have the blood of Ulysses and Penelope in your veins I see no likelihood of your succeeding. Sons are seldom as good men as their fathers; they are generally worse not better; still, as you are not going to be either fool or coward henceforward, and are not entirely without some share of your father's wise discernment, I look with hope upon your undertaking" (ii. 270-280).

〔U0719 para〕
可憐的門托爾因此得了個老奶奶般的名聲，大概永遠甩不掉了。其實那不是門托爾，而是雅典娜。作者並沒有讓雅典娜說，女兒很少比得上母親。我小時候有一位非常親愛、慈祥的老姑媽，她正是這樣跟我說話的。「你放縱如水，」她會說，「必不得居首位。」我翻譯上面那段文字時，幾乎聽見她又在這麼說（還有更多同樣意思的話）。我的叔伯舅舅們跟我說話，卻完全不是這個樣子。
> Hence the grandmotherly reputation which poor Mentor is never likely to lose. It was not Mentor but Minerva. The writer does not make Minerva say that daughters were rarely as good women as their mothers were. I had a very dear kind old aunt who when I was a boy used to talk to me just in this way. "Unstable as water," she would say, "thou shalt not excel." I almost heard her saying it (and more to the same effect) when I was translating the passage above given. My uncles did not talk to me at all in the same way.

〔U0720 para〕
我不妨在這裡順帶一提──不過這個題目我會在後面一章更詳細地討論──雅典娜教訓忒勒馬科斯的整段時間裡，她一定知道他這一去不但無益，反而有害，因為照她自己的安排，奧德修斯就快要回來了；事實上，忒勒馬科斯還沒到家，他就已經回到伊薩卡了。
> I may add parenthetically here, but will deal with the subject more fully in a later chapter, that all the time Minerva was lecturing Telemachus she must have known that his going would be worse than useless, inasmuch as Ulysses was, by her own arrangements, on the very eve of his return; and indeed he was back again in Ithaca before Telemachus got home.

〔U0721 para〕
再看卷十八 215 行以下，潘妮洛碧因為他讓奧德修斯和伊羅斯打架而責備他的樣子。她說：──
> See, again, the manner in which Penelope scolds him in Book xviii. 215 &c., for having let Ulysses and Irus fight. She says:—

〔U0722 quote〕
「忒勒馬科斯，我怕你已不像從前那樣明理、那樣規矩了。你年紀小的時候，比現在更懂得分寸；可是現在你長大了，論身材和相貌，陌生人看了你，會以為你是富家子弟，你的行為卻遠不是它應有的樣子。剛才鬧成那樣，到底是怎麼回事？你怎麼能讓一個陌生人受到這麼可恥的虐待？他在我們家裡作為求援者，要是受了重傷，那會怎麼樣？這對你來說，必定是非常丟臉的事。」
> "Telemachus, I fear you are no longer so discreet and well conducted as you used to be. When you were younger you had a greater sense of propriety; now, however, that you are grown up, though a stranger to look at you would take you for the son of a well-to-do father as far as size and good looks go, your conduct is by no means what it should have been. What is all this disturbance that has been going on, and how came you to allow a stranger to be so disgracefully ill-treated? What would have happened if he had suffered serious injury while a suppliant in our house? Surely this would have been very discreditable to you."

〔U0723 para〕
我不相信有哪個男人能讓一位母親這樣女人味十足地責備她的兒子。
> I do not believe any man could make a mother rebuke her son so femininely.

〔U0724 para〕
再者，人們忠貞不渝地為一個失蹤了二十年的兒子哭個不停，儘管他們還剩下三個兒子[註6]；或者為一個從未見過面的兄弟哭泣[註7]──這正是那種對家庭生活之神聖的戒慎的一部分，而在這方面，女人往往比男人要求得更嚴。
> Again, the fidelity with which people go on crying incessantly for a son who has been lost to them for twenty years, though they have still three sons left,[註6] or for a brother whom they have never even seen,[註7] is part and parcel of that jealousy for the sanctity of domestic life, in respect of which women are apt to be more exacting than men.

〔U0725 para〕
然而，儘管如此，作者卻讓忒勒馬科斯毫不掩飾一個事實：他的怨恨主要不在於他母親據說受了虐待，也不在於他父親的死，而在於求婚者的揮霍在他自己口袋裡挖出的那個洞。他向同胞們求援時，談到降臨在他家的兩大災禍，說道：──
> And yet in spite of all this the writer makes Telemachus take no pains to hide the fact that his grievance is not so much the alleged ill-treatment of his mother, nor yet the death of his father, as the hole which the extravagance of the suitors is making in his own pocket. When demanding assistance from his fellow countrymen, he says, of the two great evils that have fallen upon his house:—

〔U0726 quote〕
「第一件，是我失去了我那位卓越的父親，他曾是在座各位的首領，對你們每一個人都像父親一樣。第二件嚴重得多，不久就會把我的家產徹底毀掉。你們當中所有首領的兒子都在糾纏我母親，要她違背自己的意願嫁給他們。他們不敢到她父親伊卡里俄斯那裡去，請他挑選他最中意的人，並為他的女兒備辦嫁妝；卻一天又一天賴在我父親的宅子裡，宰殺我們的牛、羊和肥山羊來大吃大喝，喝掉多少酒連想都不想一下。沒有哪份家產經得起這樣的揮霍（卷二 46–48 行）。」
> "The first of these is the loss of my excellent father, who was chief among all you here present and was like a father to every one of you. The second is much more serious, and ere long will be the utter ruin of my estate. The sons of all the chief men among you are pestering my mother to marry them against her will. They are afraid to go to her father Icarius, asking him to choose the one he likes best, and to provide marriage gifts for his daughter, but day after day they keep hanging about my father's house, sacrificing our oxen, sheep, and fat goats for their banquets, and never giving so much as a thought to the quantity of wine they drink. No estate can stand such recklessness (ii. 46-48)."

〔U0727 para〕
此外，在卷三和卷四裡，忒勒馬科斯設法打聽父親的消息，從頭到尾都很清楚：他真正想要的，是父親已死的證據，而不是父親還活著的證據；雖然這或許只是因為他對第二種可能已經絕望了。忒勒馬科斯對求婚者揮霍的憤慨，作者在整部詩中都明顯感同身受；她對此怒不可遏；或許是因為她在自己父親家中親眼見過這種浪費。在她對這件事所說的一切底下，我們似乎感覺到一股私人怨氣在隱隱作痛；我也常常想到：她在求婚者身上，同時看到了那些一夜又一夜來白吃白喝、占阿爾基諾俄斯那份不知節制的好脾氣的便宜的鄰居──他們最後大概會把他的家吃垮。
> Moreover it is clear throughout Books iii. and iv., in which Telemachus is trying to get news of his father, that what he really wants is evidence of his death, not of his being alive, though this may only be because he despairs of the second alternative. The indignation of Telemachus on the score of the extravagance of the suitors is noticeably shared by the writer all through the poem; she is furious about it; perhaps by reason of the waste she saw going on in her father's house. Under all she says on this head we seem to feel the rankling of a private grievance, and it often crosses my mind that in the suitors she also saw the neighbours who night after night came sponging on the reckless good nature of Alcinous, to the probable eventual ruin of his house.

〔U0728 para〕
女人、宗教和錢財，是《奧德賽》作者心中三個主導的念頭。在《伊里亞德》裡，*belli causa*（戰爭的起因）是一個女人，錢財只是細節，而最顯眼的是男人。在《奧德賽》裡，*belli causa* 主要是錢財，最顯眼的是女人──即使在她看似並不顯眼的時候，往往也是如此──這就像在《伊里亞德》中那些照理說是特洛伊人大勝亞該亞人的卷次裡，作者一直著墨最多的，卻是特洛伊人遭到的殺戮。
> Woman, religion, and money are the three dominant ideas in the mind of the writer of the *Odyssey*. In the *Iliad* the *belli causa* is a woman, money is a detail, and man is most in evidence. In the *Odyssey* the *belli causa* is mainly money, and woman is most in evidence—often when she does not appear to be so—just as in the books of the *Iliad* in which the Trojans are supposed to be most triumphant over the Achæans, it is the Trojans all the time whose slaughter is most dwelt upon.

〔U0729 para〕
奇怪的是，《奧德賽》的作者心中時時想著錢財，這部詩卻絲毫沒有顯示出它是從營利的角度寫成的；而《伊里亞德》裡錢財很少出現，卻處處顯示它是為了迎合某一批作者既厭惡又鄙視的聽眾而寫的──因此也就是著眼於錢財而寫的。
> It is strange that the *Odyssey*, in which money is so constantly present to the mind of the writer, should show not even the faintest signs of having been written from a business point of view, whereas the *Iliad*, in which money appears but little, abounds with evidence of its having been written to take with a certain audience whom the writer both disliked and despised—and hence of having been written with an eye to money.

〔U0730 para〕
現在我要進入這個問題：潘妮洛碧是不是正在被──容我這麼說──粉飾漂白？《奧德賽》給我們的、關於她行為的說法，是當時流行的說法嗎？還是作者在操弄一個截然不同的故事，給它換上另一副面孔──就像所有詩人在重述任何故事時都容易做的那樣？丁尼生──更不必提許多更早的作家──就對亞瑟王傳說做過這種事；那些傳說的原始形態，把我們帶進一種道德氛圍，與我們在《國王敘事詩》中遇到的那種氛圍，可以說是天差地別。
> I will now proceed to the question whether Penelope is being, if I may say so, whitewashed. Is the version of her conduct that is given us in the *Odyssey* the then current one, or is the writer manipulating a very different story, and putting another face on it—as all poets are apt to do with any story that they are re-telling? Tennyson, not to mention many earlier writers, has done this with the *Arthurian Legends*, the original form of which takes us into a moral atmosphere as different as can well be conceived from the one we meet with in the *Idylls of the King*.

〔U0731 para〕
假定《奧德賽》的作者可能選擇改寫一個她認為既侮辱她的性別、本身又令人作嘔的故事，這並沒有什麼不可能（因為其他例子讀者很容易就會想到，我無須引述）；問題在於：她究竟有沒有這麼做？在她覆蓋上去的那幅畫底下，是否透出一幅早先畫作的痕跡，而且清楚到足以讓人一眼看出原畫畫的是什麼？如果沒有，我就放棄我的論點；但如果有，我就會認為，荷馬時代的男人極不可能去承擔這項不可能的任務：把潘妮洛碧寫得既可信又貞潔。恐怕我認為，他更可能把她寫得比上一位處理這個題材的詩人筆下更黑，而不會費什麼力氣去把她漂白。
> There is no improbability (for other instances will occur to the reader so readily that I need not quote them) in the supposition that the writer of the *Odyssey* might choose to recast a story which she deemed insulting to her sex, as well as disgusting in itself; the question is, has she done so or not? Do traces of an earlier picture show up through the one she has painted over it, so distinctly as to make it obvious what the original picture represented? If they do not, I will give up my case, but if they do, I shall hold it highly improbable that a man in the Homeric age would undertake the impossible task of making Penelope at the same time plausible and virtuous. I am afraid I think he would be likely to make her out blacker than the last poet who had treated the subject, rather than be at any pains to whiten her.

〔U0732 para〕
最不可能被促使把潘妮洛碧寫得比傳聞中更好的，就是荷馬本人。她更好還是更壞，他根本不會在乎。他喜歡女人，但也喜歡逗弄她們；他絲毫沒有顯露出任何為女性榮譽而戒慎的跡象，也沒有想要普遍抬高女性的意思。他在這方面毫無跡象，正如他對奧德修斯家中女人所施懲罰的那種兇殘也毫無跡象──單憑這種兇殘本身，就足以讓人無法想像《伊里亞德》和《奧德賽》會出自同一人之手。
> Least of all would Homer himself have been prompted to make Penelope out better than report says she was. He would not have cared whether she was better or worse. He is fond of women, but he is also fond of teasing them, and he shows not the slightest signs of any jealousy for female honour, or of a desire to exalt women generally. He shows no more sign of this than he does of the ferocity with which punishment is inflicted on the women of Ulysses' household—a ferocity which is in itself sufficient to make it inconceivable that the *Iliad* and the *Odyssey* should be by the same person.

關於歌人的部分，我的縮譯中略去了。
> The part about the bard is omitted in my abridgement.

〔U0736 footnote〕
我得向不懂音樂的作家解釋一下：音樂中禁止同樣的兩個聲部之間出現連續的五度或八度。
> I should explain to the non-musical writer that it is forbidden in music to have consecutive fifths or octaves between the same parts.

〔U0740 heading〕
第五章
> CHAPTER V.

〔U0741 heading〕
論潘妮洛碧是否遭到粉飾
> ON THE QUESTION WHETHER OR NO PENELOPE IS BEING WHITEWASHED.

〔U0742 para〕
古人之間流傳著種種有損潘妮洛碧名節的說法，這是眾所周知的；事實上，這些說法似乎在史詩集成完成之前就已盛行。一般認為，《忒勒戈尼亞》在年代順序上緊接在《奧德賽》之後，而在這部詩裡，奧德修斯殺了求婚者以後，並沒有繼續和潘妮洛碧一起過日子，反倒到忒斯普洛提亞定居，娶了該國的女王卡莉狄刻。如此說來，他必定已經休了潘妮洛碧；倘若他相信《奧德賽》對她品行的那套說法，這種事他恐怕很難做得出來。照《忒勒戈尼亞》作者的說法，潘妮洛碧和忒勒馬科斯繼續住在伊薩卡，後來奧德修斯終於回到那裡，卻被忒勒戈諾斯殺死──忒勒戈諾斯是喀耳刻為他生的兒子。至於古代其他有損她名節的說法（雖然年代晚了許多），讀者可進一步參閱《史密斯辭典》的「潘妮洛碧」條。
> It is known that scandalous versions of Penelope's conduct were current among the ancients; indeed they seem to have prevailed before the completion of the Epic cycle, for in the *Telegony*, which is believed to have come next in chronological order after the *Odyssey*, we find that when Ulysses had killed the suitors he did not go on living with Penelope, but settled in Thesprotia, and married Callidice, the queen of the country. He must, therefore, have divorced Penelope, and he could hardly have done this if he accepted the Odyssean version of her conduct. According to the author of the *Telegony*, Penelope and Telemachus go on living in Ithaca, where eventually Ulysses returns and is killed by Telegonus, a son who had been born to him by Circe. For further reference to ancient, though a good deal later, scandalous versions, see *Smith's Dictionary* under "Penelope."

〔U0743 para〕
且看《奧德賽》要我們相信──或者不如說，要我們囫圇吞下──的是什麼。據說有一百多個年輕人，同時瘋狂地愛上了一位據信已經守寡的婦人；在他們的追求告一段落之前，她恐怕很難還不到四十歲，而且已有一個成年的兒子。他們明知她對自己的殷勤極其厭惡，卻一連糾纏了她好幾年。他們愛她愛得如此發狂，在她作出選擇之前，壓根兒想不到要向別人求婚（卷二 205–207 行）。等她選定了，他們就走；在那之前，為了報復她對他們的狠心，他們要把她的兒子忒勒馬科斯吃得傾家蕩產。於是他們便著手這麼做；而潘妮洛碧既是模範妻子，又是模範母親，痛苦得肝腸寸斷，一半是為了丈夫的死，一半是因為她沒辦法把求婚者趕出家門。
> Let us see what the *Odyssey* asks us to believe, or rather, swallow. We are told that more than a hundred young men fall violently in love, at the same time, with a supposed widow, who before the close of their suit can hardly have been under forty, and who had a grown up son—pestering her for several years with addresses that they know are most distasteful to her. They are so madly in love with her that they cannot think of proposing to any one else (ii. 205-207) till she has made her choice. When she has done this they will go; till then, they will pay her out for her cruel treatment of them by eating her son Telemachus out of house and home. This, therefore, they proceed to do, and Penelope, who is a model both wife and mother, suffers agonies of grief, partly because of the death of her husband, and partly because she cannot get the suitors out of the house.

〔U0744 para〕
照理說，她只要在求婚者夜裡離去之後立刻把門閂上，第二天早上拒不開門，也就成了；因為求婚者從來不和潘妮洛碧睡在同一座宅子裡。他們分散在城裡各處過夜，而奧德修斯的宅邸顯然就坐落在城的中央；他們既然溫順到肯讓人趕出去，只要屋裡的人拿出一點強硬的姿態，他們也就會溫順到肯讓人擋在門外。他們之中從來沒有一個人單獨見過潘妮洛碧；她每次出現在他們面前，總有兩名端莊的女僕隨侍，一左一右站在她身旁，而她則端端莊莊地拿一塊面紗或屏障遮在臉前──不錯，她已經四十歲了，可是她本人也好，女詩人也好，似乎都沒把這件事放在心上，所以我們可以斷定，讓她舉起面紗的確實是端莊，不是別的什麼。求婚者並不是講究分寸的人，他們儘管對潘妮洛碧一往情深，卻和她的女僕們有不正當的親密關係──而這些女僕事情敗露之後，似乎沒有一個立刻被逐出家門。說來有點奇怪：那些遠道而來的求婚者，竟然沒有一個堅持要在宅子裡連宿帶食；作者又費盡心思，唯恐潘妮洛碧沾上一絲一毫的流言，以致我們不禁推斷，作者自覺有必要把這份用心擺得明明白白。舉例來說，倘若潘妮洛碧真有作者竭力描繪的一半貞潔，我想第二十三卷裡她對奧德修斯歸來一事，就不會被寫得那樣半信半疑。作者給她安上的謹慎有多少，在某種程度上正好可以拿來衡量：作者認為非塗上不可的那層白粉究竟有多厚。潘妮洛碧對丈夫的種種忠貞之中，始終讓人覺得「這位夫人表白得太過火了」。
> One would have thought all she had to do was to bolt the doors as soon as the suitors had left for the night, and refuse to open them in the morning; for the suitors never sleep in the same house with Penelope. They sleep at various places in the town, in the middle of which Ulysses' house evidently stands, and if they were meek enough to let themselves be turned out, they would be meek enough to let themselves be kept out, if those inside showed anything of a firm front. Not one of them ever sees Penelope alone; when she comes into their presence she is attended by two respectable female servants who stand on either side of her, and she holds a screen or veil modestly before her face—true, she was forty, but neither she nor the poetess seem to bear this in mind, so we may take it as certain that it was modesty and nothing else that made her hold up the veil. The suitors were not men of scrupulous delicacy, and in spite of their devotion to Penelope lived on terms of improper intimacy with her women servants—none of whom appear to have been dismissed instantly on detection. It is a little strange that not one of those suitors who came from a long distance should have insisted on being found in bed as well as board, and so much care is taken that not one breath of scandal should attach to Penelope, that we infer a sense on the writer's part that it was necessary to put this care well in evidence. I cannot think, for example, that Penelope would have been represented as nearly so incredulous about the return of Ulysses in Book xxiii., if she had been nearly as virtuous as the writer tries to make her out. The amount of caution with which she is credited is to some extent a gauge of the thickness of the coat of whitewash which the writer considers necessary. In all Penelope's devotion to her husband there is an ever present sense that the lady doth protest too much.

〔U0745 para〕
然而更奇怪的是，這群熱烈激情的愛人，竟然從不為了爭奪這位中年的完美典範而彼此爭吵。適者生存在他們的體系裡似乎毫無地位。他們沒有半點嫉妒的跡象，反倒像一個非常和樂的大家庭，肩並肩、臉貼臉地過日子：擲矛打靶、下棋、在院子裡剝山羊皮、燎豬毛、喝下數不清的酒，大體上就是天天大擺筵席。他們堅持潘妮洛碧總得嫁給某個人，至於那個幸運的某人是誰，倒是無關緊要。[註1]似乎沒有人認為她非嫁給自己不可。他們之中也從沒有一個人發覺自己毫無希望，就此告辭；於是事情一年又一年地拖下去──這段期間潘妮洛碧可沒有越活越年輕──求婚者為潘妮洛碧相思欲死，潘妮洛碧則只為了擺脫他們而急得要死。
> Still stranger, however, is the fact that these ardent passionate lovers never quarrel among themselves for the possession of their middle-aged paragon. The survival of the fittest does not seem to have had any place in their system. They show no signs of jealousy, but jog along cheek by jowl as a very happy family, aiming spears at a mark, playing draughts, flaying goats and singeing pigs in the yard, drinking an untold quantity of wine, and generally holding high feast. They insist that Penelope should marry somebody, but who the happy somebody is to be is a matter of no importance.[註1] No one seems to think it essential that she shall marry himself in particular. Not one of them ever finds out that his case is hopeless and takes his leave; and thus matters drift on year after year—during all which time Penelope is not getting any younger—the suitors dying of love for Penelope, and Penelope dying only to be rid of them.

〔U0746 para〕
就算求婚者愛上忒勒馬科斯出錢供應的美酒佳餚，並不亞於愛上他的母親好了；可是這種完美情人兼完美食客的混合體根本不可能存在，若不是自知陷入了極大的困境，誰也不會求助於這種寫法──不管他（或她）是誰。人若是在戀愛，就不會白吃白喝；若是白吃白喝，就不是在戀愛；兩者可以擇一，卻不能兼得。因此，《奧德賽》的作者不但把這種不可能的行為加在求婚者身上，還要我們相信：一個聰明的女人，面對一百個愛人，即使他們多年來賴在她家裡，令她厭惡至極，她竟連其中寥寥幾個都擋不在門外──到了這個地步，我們就可以明白，作者正竭盡所能地矇騙我們；我們也就很願意相信，求婚者並不像我們被告知的那樣黑，潘妮洛碧也不像那樣白。
> Granted that the suitors are not less in love with the good cheer they enjoy at Telemachus's expense, than they are with his mother; but this mixture of perfect lover and perfect sponger is so impossible that no one could have recourse to it unless aware that he (or she) was in extreme difficulty. If men are in love they will not sponge; if they sponge they are not in love; we may have it either way but not both; when, therefore, the writer of the *Odyssey* not only attributes such impossible conduct to the suitors, but asks us also to believe that a clever woman could not keep at any rate some few of her hundred lovers out of the house, although their presence had been for many years in a high degree distasteful to her, we may know that we are being hoodwinked as far as the writer can hoodwink us, and shall be very inclinable to believe that the suitors were not so black, nor Penelope so white, as we are being given to understand.

〔U0747 para〕
至於說她被求婚者嚇住了，她有時候對他們說話可相當不客氣，例如卷十八 274–280 行；又如卷十九 322 行，她說起話來，彷彿完全有能力把任何一個惹她討厭的求婚者打發走。
> As for her being overawed by the suitors, she talks very plainly to them at times, as for example in xviii. 274-280, and again in xix. 322 where she speaks as though she were perfectly able to get rid of any suitor who was obnoxious to her.

〔U0748 para〕
不僅如此，我們還可以推斷：一個能板著臉講出這種故事的作者，對於男人如何對待自己所愛的女人，絕不可能有絲毫概念；對於女人通常如何看待自己所選中的男人（就我斗膽所能判斷的而言），也所知不多。因此我斷定，她當時還很年輕，而且尚未出嫁。無論如何，上面講的這個故事不可能出自荷馬之手；如果真是男人寫的，那必定是某位史前的安傑利科修士，他要嘛年輕時所知之少，要嘛老年時所忘之多，都遠非《伊里亞德》的作者所可能及。倘若他所知的仍足以寫出《奧德賽》，那麼他記得的東西，就會比《奧德賽》作者所顯露出來曾經知道的還要多。
> Over and above this we may infer that the writer who can tell us such a story with a grave face cannot have even the faintest conception of the way in which a man feels towards a woman he is in love with, nor yet much (so far as I may venture to form an opinion) of what women commonly feel towards the man of their choice; I conclude, therefore, that she was still very young, and unmarried. At any rate the story told above cannot have been written by Homer; if it is by a man at all it must be by some prehistoric Fra Angelico, who had known less in his youth, or forgotten more in his old age, than the writer of the *Iliad* is at all likely to have done. If he had still known enough to be able to write the *Odyssey*, he would have remembered more than the writer of the *Odyssey* shows any signs of having ever known.

〔U0749 para〕
一個男人，如果突發奇想（已故的丁尼生勳爵就很可能這麼做），要不顧當時流傳的種種醜聞，把潘妮洛碧寫成貞潔的女人──一個男人，是根本不會把求婚者寫成一群愛人的。他會立刻看出這絕不可行，而會把他們寫成一夥純粹的強盜，用威脅來壓制潘妮洛碧，只靠她天生的機智，再加上（譬如說）求婚者當中三四個暗地裡的盟友，才把他們牽制住。不管他怎麼寫，都無法讓求婚者日日長駐宅中顯得合情合理，但至少還有可能；反之，《奧德賽》作者端給我們的這種完美食客加完美情人的組合，卻荒唐得絕無可能。而且我想，若不是她一心要抬高自己的性別，要顯示一個聰明的女人如何能讓任意多的男人拜倒在她腳下，矇騙他們、寵壞他們，最後毀掉他們，她也不會要我們接受這種寫法。可是這肯定是女人的題材，而不是男人的──至少我不知道有哪一位男作家嘗試過類似的東西。
> A man, if he had taken it into his head (as the late Lord Tennyson might very conceivably have done) to represent Penelope as virtuous in spite of current scandalous stories to the contrary—a man, would not have made the suitors a band of lovers at all. He would have seen at once that this was out of the question, and would have made them mere marauders, who overawed Penelope by their threats, and were only held in check by her mother wit and by, say, some three or four covert allies among the suitors themselves. Do what he might he could not make the permanent daily presence of the suitors plausible, but it would be possible; whereas the combination of perfect sponger and perfect lover which is offered us by the writer of the *Odyssey* is grotesquely impossible, nor do I imagine that she would have asked us to accept it, but for her desire to exalt her sex by showing how a clever woman can bring any number of men to her feet, hoodwink them, spoil them, and in the end destroy them. This, however, is surely a woman's theme rather than a man's—at least I know of no male writer who has attempted anything like it.

〔U0750 para〕
我們已經看過從潘妮洛碧的觀點所講的故事；現在且聽聽求婚者那一方怎麼說。這見於第二卷開頭，我要把安提諾俄斯的話引得比我的縮譯更完整一些。他先說潘妮洛碧多年來一直給每一個求婚者捎去討好的口信，對他們個個加以鼓勵（順帶一提，雅典娜在卷十三 379–381 行完全證實了他的話），接著又說：──
> We have now seen the story as told from Penelope's point of view; let us proceed to hear it from that of the suitors. We find this at the beginning of Book ii., and I will give Antinous's speech at fuller length than I have done in my abridgement. After saying that Penelope had for years been encouraging every single suitor by sending him flattering messages (in which, by the way, Minerva fully corroborated him in Book xiii. 379-381) he continues:—

〔U0751 para〕
「然後她還對我們耍了另一個花招。她在房裡架起一座大繡架，開始做一件極大的精細針線活。『心肝們，』她說，『奧德修斯確實已經死了；不過，請別逼我馬上改嫁；等一等──我可不願我的針線手藝湮沒無聞──等我替英雄拉厄爾特斯做好一塊壽衣，好在死亡帶走他的時候備用。他非常富有，要是下葬時連一塊壽衣都沒有，地方上的婦人們會說閒話的。』
> "And then there was that other trick she played us. She set up a great tambour frame in her room, and began to work on an enormous piece of fine needlework. 'Sweethearts,' said she, 'Ulysses is indeed dead, still, do not press me to marry again immediately; wait—for I would not have my skill in needlework perish unrecorded—till I have completed a pall for the hero Laertes, to be ready against the time when death shall take him. He is very rich, and the women of the place will talk if he is laid out without a pall.'

〔U0752 para〕
「她就是這麼說的，我們也答應了；於是我們能看見她整天在那匹大布上忙著，到了夜裡卻點著火把把針腳拆掉。她就這樣愚弄了我們三年，我們始終沒有識破；可是時光流逝，到了第四年，她有個知情的女僕告訴了我們，我們當場撞見她在拆自己的活兒；於是不管願不願意，她都只好把它做完。
> "This was what she said, and we assented; whereon we could see her, working on her great web all day, but at night she would unpick the stiches again by torchlight. She fooled us in this way for three years and we never found her out, but as time wore on and she was now in her fourth year, one of her maids, who knew what she was doing, told us, and we caught her in the act of undoing her work; so she had to finish it, whether she would or no.

〔U0753 para〕
「因此，求婚者這樣答覆你，好讓你和亞該亞人都明白：『把你母親送走，叫她嫁給她自己和她父親所選中的人。』因為她若再仗著雅典娜教給她的那些才藝，再仗著自己聰明，擺著架子折磨我們下去，我真不知道會出什麼事。我們從沒聽說過這樣的女人。堤羅、阿爾克墨涅、密克涅和古代那些有名的女人，我們都知道，可是她們沒有一個比得上你母親。她那樣對待我們是不公平的；只要她一天還抱著上天如今賦予她的這副心思，我們就一天接著吃你的家產；我也看不出她有什麼理由要改變，因為榮耀和光彩是她得了，損失這一大筆家財的卻是你，不是她。可是我們不會回去做自己的事，也不到別處去，直到她作出選擇，嫁給我們當中的某一個為止。」（卷二 93–128 行）
> "The suitors, therefore, make you this answer, that both you and the Achæans may understand: 'Send your mother away, and bid her marry the man of her own and her father's choice,' for I do not know what will happen if she goes on plaguing us much longer with the airs she gives herself on the score of the accomplishments Minerva has taught her, and because she is so clever. We never yet heard of such a woman. We know all about Tyro, Alcmena, Mycene, and the famous women of old, but they were nothing to your mother any one of them. It was not fair of her to treat us in that way, and as long as she continues in the mind with which heaven has now endowed her, so long shall we go on eating up your estate; and I do not see why she should change, for it is she who gets the honour and glory, and it is you, not she, who lose all this substance. We however, will not go about our business, nor anywhere else, till she has made her choice and married some one or other of us" (ii. 93-128).

〔U0754 para〕
那麼，大致說來，女作者的版本是：潘妮洛碧是受了冤屈的無辜者；求婚者的版本則是：她是個狡猾無情的調情高手，寧可有一百個仰慕者，也不要一個丈夫。哪一個版本比較接近──不是接近真相，因為我們可以確定，求婚者能說的遠比作者肯讓他們說的多得多──而是接近她正在加工、正按自己更喜歡的方式重講的那個原始故事呢？讀者想必已經注意到，這一回求婚者似乎在入夜之後還待在宅子裡。
> Roughly, then, the authoress's version is that Penelope is an injured innocent, and the suitors', that she is an artful heartless flirt who prefers having a hundred admirers rather than one husband. Which comes nearest, not to the truth—for we may be sure the suitors could have said a great deal more than the writer chooses to say they said—but to the original story which she was sophisticating, and retelling in a way that was more to her liking? The reader will have noted that on this occasion the suitors seem to have been in the house after nightfall.

〔U0755 para〕
我們不能忘記，忒勒馬科斯第一次向雅典娜說起求婚者時，承認他母親並沒有斬釘截鐵地說她不會改嫁。「她既不拒絕那可恨的婚事，」他說，「也不把事情做個了結。」（卷一 249、250 行）顯然沒有；可是如果沒有，為什麼沒有？不立刻拒絕，就等於招人追求；她如果無意招人追求，以她矇騙男人的高明手段，似乎早就能找到辦法「把事情做個了結」了。
