> We cannot forget that when Telemachus first told Miranda about the suitors, he admitted that his mother had not point blank said that she would not marry again. "She does not," he says, "refuse the hateful marriage, nor yet does she bring matters to an end" (i. 249, 250). Apparently not; but if not, why not? Not to refuse at once is to court courtship, and if she had not meant to court it she seems to have been adept enough in the art of hoodwinking men to have found some means of "bringing the matter to an end."

〔U0756 para〕
給仰慕者捎些討人喜歡的小口信，可不是擺脫他們的好辦法。她可曾試過給他們碰釘子？史上沒有任何這類記載。她可曾說過：「好了，安提諾俄斯，不管我嫁給誰，你儘管放心，那人絕不會是你。」再來還有讓人厭煩這一招──她試過沒有？她可曾念她祖父的書信給他們聽？她可曾唱她自己作的歌給他們聽，或是彈奏她自己譜的曲子？每當我想擺脫什麼人，總覺得這些辦法很靈驗。確實有跡象顯示她在這方面下過一些功夫，因為求婚者說，她那些高雅藝術的胡鬧和唯美派的大話，他們再也受不了了；不過，她比較可能是想吸引人，而不是想把人嚇跑。她可曾把他們彼此在背後說的話添油加醋地轉述，挑得他們互相反目？她可曾請安提諾俄斯或歐律馬科斯坐下來給她的織物當模特兒──叫他們擺一個又僵又累的姿勢，非保持不可，同時嘴裡跟他們說個沒完？她可曾差他們去跑腿，然後又罵他們，說她根本不要他們？或者叫他們替她辦事卻忘了付錢，或者一再把他們打發回店裡去換東西，說他們付的錢多得太離譜，早知道她就自己去辦了？她可曾堅持要他們參加家庭禮拜？一句話，像她這樣精明的主婦，倘若真心不想被人追求，有成千種辦法可以信手拈來，她可曾用過其中任何一種？只要拿一點常識輕輕一碰，整座建築就化為齏粉。
> Sending pretty little messages to her admirers was not exactly the way to get rid of them. Did she ever try snubbing? Nothing of the kind is placed on record. Did she ever say, "Well Antinous, whoever else I may marry, you may make your mind easy that it will not be you." Then there was boring—did she ever try that? Did she ever read them any of her grandfather's letters? Did she sing them her own songs, or play them music of her own composition? I have always found these courses successful when I wanted to get rid of people. There are indeed signs that something had been done in this direction, for the suitors say that they cannot stand her high art nonsense and æsthetic rhodomontade any longer, but it is more likely she had been trying to attract than to repel. Did she set them by the ears by repeating with embellishments what they had said to her about one another? Did she ask Antinous or Eurymachus to sit to her for her web—give them a good stiff pose, make them stick to it, and talk to them all the time? Did she find errands for them to run, and then scold them, and say she did not want them? or make them do commissions for her and forget to pay them, or keep on sending them back to the shop to change things, and they had given ever so much too much money and she wished she had gone and done it herself? Did she insist on their attending family worship? In a word, did she do a single one of the thousand things so astute a matron would have been at no loss to hit upon if she had been in earnest about not wishing to be courted? With one touch of common sense the whole fabric crumbles into dust.

〔U0757 para〕
忒勒馬科斯在反駁求婚者時，沒有否認他們所說的任何一件事實。他沒有否認母親一向給他們捎去鼓勵的口信，也沒有試圖解釋她在織布一事上的行為。既然這一點已經承認，而潘妮洛碧在打發愛人們走路這件事上顯然也沒有盡到應盡的努力，我們怎能不懷疑某處有顆螺絲鬆了，懷疑有一個性質截然不同的故事正被人擺弄，好遷就作者的需要？倘若我們斷定，照原始版本的說法，潘妮洛碧拆她的織物，與其說是為了拖延一樁可恨的婚事，不如說是為了延長一段十分愜意的追求，難道會錯得很離譜嗎？
> Telemachus in his rejoinder to the suitors does not deny a single one of their facts. He does not deny that his mother had been in the habit of sending them encouraging messages, nor does he attempt to explain her conduct about the web. This, then, being admitted, and it being also transparent that Penelope had used no due diligence in sending her lovers to the right about, can we avoid suspecting that there is a screw loose somewhere, and that a story of very different character is being manipulated to meet the exigencies of the writer? And shall we go very far wrong if we conclude that according to the original version, Penelope picked out her web, not so much in order to delay a hateful marriage, as to prolong a very agreeable courtship?

〔U0758 para〕
拉厄爾特斯之所以搬到鄉下去住，不再進城（卷一 189、190 行），無疑是因為他看出了這裡正在發生什麼事；而潘妮洛碧之所以偏偏選了這種形式的活計，大概也是為了確保他不會走近她身邊。她為什麼不能替別人做壽衣呢？拉厄爾特斯每次來訪，都知道歐律克蕾亞只會告訴他，女主人正在樓上為他縫壽衣，馬上就下來──這樣他還可能繼續上門嗎？請讀者務必自己稍微想一想。
> It was no doubt because Laertes saw what was going on that he went to live in the country and left off coming into the town (i. 189, 190), and Penelope probably chose the particular form her work assumed in order to ensure that he should not come near her. Why could she not set about making a pall for somebody else? Was Laertes likely to continue calling, when every time he did so he knew that Euryclea would only tell him her mistress was upstairs working at his pall, but she would be down directly? Do let the reader try and think it out a little for himself.

〔U0759 para〕
至於說拉厄爾特斯像安提克蕾亞在第十一卷所說的那樣窮困潦倒，這話連一丁點真實都沒有。作者必須把他寫得很窮，才能解釋他為什麼沒有出面保護潘妮洛碧；可是潘妮洛碧做那匹布的藉口，卻是說他家財萬貫。求婚者的情形也一樣。要解釋忒勒馬科斯為什麼不設法向他們討回損失的時候，他們就窮得讓人覺得去告他們只是白花錢；反過來，作者要讓潘妮洛碧從他們身上弄到禮物、賣弄她女人的機智時（卷十八 274–280 行），也就是在奧德修斯殺死他們之前不久，他們又有花不完的錢了。再晚一天，她就來不及了。作者心裡很清楚這一點，但她可不打算讓潘妮洛碧錯失她的禮物。她顯然把男人看作可以任意獵取的對象，而男作家就不大會這樣看。不消說，她收到的第一件禮物是一件新衣裳。
> As for Laertes being so badly off as Anticlea says he was in Book xi., there is not one grain of truth in that story. The writer had to make him out poor in order to explain his not having interfered to protect Penelope, but Penelope's excuse for making her web was that he was a man of large property. It is the same with the suitors. When it is desired to explain Telemachus's not having tried in some way to recover from them, they are so poor that it would be a waste of money to sue them; when, on the other hand, the writer wants Penelope to air her woman's wit by getting presents out of them (xviii. 274-280), just before Ulysses kills them, they have any amount of money. One day more, and she would have been too late. The writer knew that very well, but she was not going to let Penelope lose her presents. She evidently looks upon man as fair game, which male writers are much less apt to do. Of course the first present she receives is a new dress.

〔U0760 para〕
回頭再說拉厄爾特斯：他必定有錢，否則奧德修斯怎麼會那麼富有？奧德修斯的錢是從哪裡來的？他在去特洛伊之前不大可能賺到多少，而且似乎也沒有從那裡寄過任何東西回家。家裡一直沒有他的音訊；而在第十卷，[註2]他似乎是隨身帶著自己那份戰利品回來的──果真如此，那些東西就在特里納基亞島外海的船難中損失了。他娶潘妮洛碧時似乎得過某種嫁妝，因為忒勒馬科斯說，他若把母親送走，就得把嫁妝退還給外祖父伊卡里俄斯，並以此作為不送她走的理由之一（卷二 132、133 行）；然而，奧德修斯的巨大財富，大部分必定來自拉厄爾特斯，而我們可以確定，拉厄爾特斯留給自己的，比給兒子的要多。那麼，這些錢都到哪裡去了？──因為拉厄爾特斯似乎是個生活極為節儉的人。答案是：錢仍在拉厄爾特斯手裡；至於他如今從不進城，原因一部分無疑是那塊壽衣，一部分是他兒媳過的那種醜聞不斷的生活，但主要是因為作者若不把他支開，就無法解釋他為什麼袖手不管。
> Returning to Laertes, he must have had money, or how could Ulysses be so rich? Where did Ulysses' money come from? He could hardly have made much before he went to Troy, and he does not appear to have sent anything home thence. Nothing has been heard from him, and in Book x.,[註2] he appears to be bringing back his share of the plunder with him—in which case it was lost in the shipwreck off the coast of the Thrinacian island. He seems to have had a dowry of some kind with Penelope, for Telemachus says that if he sends his mother away he shall have to refund it to his grandfather Icarius, and urges this fact as one of the reasons for not sending her (ii. 132, 133); the greater part, however, of Ulysses' enormous wealth must have come to him from Laertes, who we may be sure kept more for himself than he gave to his son. What, then, had become of all this money—for Laertes seems to have been a man of very frugal habits? The answer is that it was still in Laertes' hands, and the reason for his never coming to town now was partly, no doubt, the pall; partly the scandalous life which his daughter was leading; but mainly the writer's inability to explain his non-interference unless she got him out of the way.

〔U0761 para〕
再者，奧德修斯的母親在冥府向他講述伊薩卡近況的那番話（卷十一 180 行以下），也透露出有什麼事需要隱瞞。她對求婚者隻字不提。她只說忒勒馬科斯得應酬許多客人，而這也只是理所當然，因為他是地方官，自己也到處受人邀請（卷十一 185–187 行）。再沒有比這更冷淡的委婉說法了，也再沒有什麼比這更能顯示說話的人心知肚明：實際發生的事遠比她肯說的多。倘若安提克蕾亞相信兒媳是清白的，她早就把整個情況原原本本告訴奧德修斯了。
> The account, again, which Ulysses' mother gives him in Hades (xi. 180, &c.) of what is going on in Ithaca shows a sense that there is something to conceal. She says not one word about the suitors. All she says is that Telemachus has to see a good deal of company, which is only reasonable seeing that he is a magistrate and is asked out everywhere himself (xi. 185-187). Nothing can be more coldly euphemistic, nor show a fuller sense that there was a good deal more going on than the speaker chose to say. If Anticlea had believed her daughter-in-law to be innocent, she would have laid the whole situation before Ulysses.

〔U0762 para〕
或許有人會主張，那時求婚者還沒有大舉來到伊薩卡，因為冥府之行發生在奧德修斯漂泊的早期，在他與卡呂普索同住七年之前，所以安提克蕾亞或許真的對求婚者一無所知；可是作者忘了這一點，把忒勒馬科斯寫成已經長大成人。事實上，在這個時候，忒勒馬科斯頂多只有十二三歲，他要赴的宴、要辦的招待，頂多不過是一場兒童聚會而已。作者在年代上出了個紕漏，因為整部詩裡，忒勒馬科斯都被寫成在奧德修斯離家的第二十年才剛剛成年。顯然，作者描寫與安提克蕾亞會面的那一段時，心裡想的是奧德修斯歸來前夕的情況，那時求婚者正鬧得最兇。這一點在稍後更是明顯：同在冥府的阿伽門農說，特洛伊戰爭爆發時，忒勒馬科斯還是個抱在懷裡的嬰兒，如今想必已經長大成人了（卷十一 448、449 行）。
> It may be maintained that the suitors were not yet come to Ithaca in force, for the visit to Hades occurs early in the wanderings of Ulysses, and before his seven years' sojourn with Calypso, so that Anticlea may really have known nothing about the suitors; but the writer has forgotten this, and has represented Telemachus as already arrived at man's estate. In truth, at this point Telemachus was at the utmost only twelve or thirteen years old, and a children's party was all the entertainment he need either receive or give. The writer has made a slip in her chronology, for throughout the poem Telemachus is represented as only just arriving at man's estate in the twentieth year of Ulysses' absence. It is evident that in describing the interview with Anticlea the writer has in her mind the state of things existing just before Ulysses' return, when the suitors were in full riot. This, indeed, appears still more plainly lower down, when Agamemnon, also in Hades, says that Telemachus was a baby in arms when the Trojan war broke out, and that he must now be grown up (xi. 448, 449).

〔U0763 para〕
所以，就作者而言，奧德修斯母親的沉默是有意為之。在她的構想裡，安提克蕾亞必定對求婚者的事一清二楚──因為照安提克蕾亞自己的說法，她一直活到忒勒馬科斯年紀大得足以當地方官的時候才去世。我相信其中的解釋是：寫第十一卷的時候，作者還沒有打算添上第一至四卷，以及從第十三卷 187 行到第二十四卷的部分，而是打算對當時流傳的那些關於潘妮洛碧的醜聞置之不理，並且盡可能少提到她。等我談到這部詩的成形與發展時，再更詳細地處理這個問題；不過不妨現在就說明：上面指出的這個難題，除了解釋為一位把新作拼接到舊作上的年輕作者在年代上的疏忽之外，恐怕只能懸而不解。除了作者本人，任何人都會看出這個毛病而加以避免；事實上，她很可能後來也看出來了，只是覺得不值得費事去改。果真如此，至少我個人不會因此看輕她。
> The silence therefore of Ulysses' mother is wilful so far as the writer is concerned. She must have conceived of Anticlea as knowing all about the suitors perfectly well—for she did not die till Telemachus was, by her own account, old enough to be a magistrate. The explanation I believe to be, that at the time Book xi. was written, the writer had as yet no intention of adding Books i.-iv., and from line 187 of Book xiii. to Book xxiv. but proposed to ignore the current scandalous stories about Penelope, and to say as little as possible about her. I will deal with this more fully when I come to the genesis and development of the poem, but may as well say at once that the difficulty above pointed out will have to remain unexplained except as a slip in chronology on the part of a young writer who was piecing new work on to old. Any one but the writer herself would have seen it and avoided it; indeed it is quite possible that she came to see it, and did not think it worth her while to be at the trouble of altering it. If this is so I, for one, shall think none the worse of her.

〔U0764 footnote〕
《奧德賽》卷二 127–128 行及 203–207 行。
> *Od*. ii. 127-128 and 203-207.

〔U0765 footnote〕
《奧德賽》卷十 40 行；這一段在我的縮譯中沒有收錄。
> *Od*. x. 40, this passage is not given in my abridgement.

〔U0766 heading〕
第六章
> CHAPTER VI.

〔U0767 para〕
再論潘妮洛碧的品格──忒勒馬科斯的拉刻代蒙之行
> FURTHER CONSIDERATIONS REGARDING THE CHARACTER OF PENELOPE—THE JOURNEY OF TELEMACHUS TO LACEDÆMON.

〔U0768 para〕
《奧德賽》的作者，是否正把她同胞之間流傳的、關於潘妮洛碧品行的更不堪的說法，按她自己的意思重新詮釋？這個問題對於作者性別的問題關係重大，因此我要再提出一些證據，說明她發現要掩飾以下這個事實是多麼不可能：最了解潘妮洛碧的人，都對她沒有信心。
> The question whether or no the writer of the *Odyssey* is putting her own construction on grosser versions of Penelope's conduct current among her countrymen, has such an important bearing on that of the writer's sex, that I shall bring further evidence to show how impossible she finds it to conceal the fact that those who knew Penelope best had no confidence in her.

〔U0769 para〕
雅典娜憑著女人敏捷的直覺，一眼就看清了局面，直截了當地切中要害。她從忒勒馬科斯口中得知潘妮洛碧並沒有立刻表明自己不會改嫁，便不浪費唇舌，馬上說道：「如果你母親一心想要改嫁，」（這話無疑表示，說話的人毫不懷疑她確實一心如此）「那就讓她回到她父親那裡去。」（卷一 276 行）由此我們可以推斷，雅典娜不但對潘妮洛碧的意圖已有定見，還看出她打算從容不迫地享受這段追求；除非採取把她送回娘家這種斷然的手段，否則她不大可能被逼得下定決心。
> Minerva with quick womanly instinct took in the situation at a glance, and went straight to the point. On learning from Telemachus that Penelope did not at once say she would not marry again, she wastes no words, but says promptly, "If your mother's mind is set on marrying again" (and surely this implies that the speaker had no doubt that it was so set) "let her go back to her father" (i. 276). From this we may infer that Minerva had not only formed her own opinion about Penelope's intentions, but saw also that she meant taking her time about the courtship, and was not likely to be brought to the point by any measures less decisive than sending her back to her father's house.

〔U0770 para〕
此外，我們還可以從另一處得知雅典娜對潘妮洛碧的看法。忒勒馬科斯住在墨涅拉俄斯王那裡時，雅典娜在夢中向他顯現，讓他明白他母親就要嫁給求婚者之一的歐律馬科斯了（卷十五 1–42 行）。這是雅典娜一方肆意捏造的謊言（至少作者要我們這麼以為）。然而，事情若到此為止，大概再沒有什麼比這更讓忒勒馬科斯高興的了；因為他雖然把這樁婚事稱為「可恨的」，但毫無疑問，只要母親願意自己走，能把她送出家門，他只會感激不盡。潘妮洛碧說，由於求婚者沒完沒了地獻殷勤，害他破費，他一直催她改嫁離去（卷十九 530–534 行）。潘妮洛碧確實像是個撒謊的高手，很難知道什麼時候該相信她；但忒勒馬科斯在別處說的話已足以讓人毫不懷疑：儘管表面上得體地裝出幾分不情願，他其實很樂意母親離去。
> We know, moreover, what Minerva thought of Penelope from another source. Minerva appears to Telemachus in a dream when he is staying with King Menelaus, and gives him to understand that his mother is on the point of marrying Eurymachus, one of the suitors (xv. 1-42). This was (so at least we are intended to suppose) a wanton falsehood on Minerva's part. Nevertheless if the matter had ended there, nothing probably would have pleased Telemachus better; for in spite of his calling the marriage "hateful," there can be no question that he would have been only too thankful to get his mother out of the house, if she would go of her own free will. Penelope says he was continually urging her to marry and go, on the score of the expense he was being put to by the protracted attentions of the suitors (xix. 530-534). Penelope indeed seems to have been such an adept at lying that it is very difficult to know when to believe her, but Telemachus says enough elsewhere to leave no doubt that, in spite of a certain decent show of reluctance, he would have been glad that his mother should go.

〔U0771 para〕
可惜雅典娜的話並沒有在說潘妮洛碧打算嫁給歐律馬科斯之後就打住；她還補充說，到時候潘妮洛碧大概會偷走忒勒馬科斯的一些財物。她對他說：──
> Unfortunately Minerva's story does not end with saying that Penelope means marrying Eurymachus; she adds that in this case she will probably steal some of Telemachus's property. She says to him:—

〔U0772 quote〕
「你知道女人是什麼樣的；她們總想為眼下嫁的那個男人盡力張羅。她們把前夫和跟前夫生的孩子忘得一乾二淨。所以你立刻回家去，把一切交給你能找到的最可靠的女管家照管，直到上天高興賜給你一位自己的妻子為止。」（卷十五 20–26 行）
> "You know what women are; they always want to do the best they can for the man who is married to them at the moment. They forget all about their first husband and the children that they have had by him. Go home, therefore, at once, and put everything in charge of the most respectable housekeeper you can find, until it shall please heaven to send you a wife of your own" (xv. 20-26).

〔U0773 para〕
這段話不僅透露出對潘妮洛碧的不信任，與她表面上的過往不相稱，而且頗能說明雅典娜讀過《庫普里亞》──據說在這部（如今已失傳的）詩裡，海倫所做的，正是這裡說潘妮洛碧可能會做的事。不過此事暫且不論；倘若潘妮洛碧的過往真如作者希望我們接受的那樣，忒勒馬科斯的回答肯定會和他實際上的回答大不相同。他會說：「親愛的雅典娜，這是什麼話，竟從你的齒縫間溜出來！我母親會偷我的財物，跟歐律馬科斯那樣一個毫無廉恥的無賴私奔？沒有人比你更清楚，她是世上最不可能做出這種事的女人。」然後他就會像從一場可怕的噩夢中那樣醒來。
> This passage not only betrays a want of confidence in Penelope which is out of keeping with her ostensible antecedents, but it goes far to show that Minerva had read the *Cypria*, in which poem (now lost) we are told that Helen did exactly what is here represented as likely to be done by Penelope; but leaving this, surely if Penelope's antecedents had been such as the writer wishes us to accept, Telemachus would have made a very different answer to the one he actually made. He would have said, "My dear Minerva, what a word has escaped the boundary of your teeth. My mother steal my property and go off with an unprincipled scoundrel like Eurymachus? No one can know better than yourself that she is the last woman in the world to be capable of such conduct." And then he would have awoke as from a hideous dream.

〔U0774 para〕
可是實際上發生了什麼？忒勒馬科斯確實醒了過來（卷十五 43 行），而且十分苦惱，但他苦惱的是他的財物，不是他的母親。「偷了我母親的人，偷走的不過是廢物；可是誰要是從我這裡竊走我的傳家寶，云云。」他踢了可憐的佩西斯特拉托斯一腳，把他弄醒，說他們得套上馬，立刻動身回家。佩西斯特拉托斯回答說，外頭漆黑一片；無論如何，他們都非得等到天亮不可。況且，他們應該向墨涅拉俄斯道別，從他那裡拿一份禮物；只要忒勒馬科斯別這麼不講理地急著走，墨涅拉俄斯一定會送他們一份。還有什麼比這更清楚地顯示出雅典娜和忒勒馬科斯兩人對潘妮洛碧的真實想法──以及聽眾對她已經形成了什麼樣的看法呢？
> What, however, happens in reality? Telemachus does indeed wake up (xv. 43) in great distress, but it is about his property, not about his mother. "Who steals my mother steals trash, but whoso filches from me my family heirlooms &c." He kicks poor Pisistratus to wake him, and says they must harness the horses and be off home at once. Pisistratus rejoins that it is pitch dark; come what may they must really wait till morning. Besides, they ought to say good bye to Menelaus, and get a present out of him; he will be sure to give them one, if Telemachus will not be in such an unreasonable hurry. Can anything show more clearly what was the inner mind both of Minerva and Telemachus about Penelope—and also what kind of ideas the audience had formed about her?

〔U0775 para〕
再者，雅典娜和忒勒馬科斯對偷竊的看法又是多麼不同。忒勒馬科斯立刻就感到切膚之痛。雅典娜卻視為理所當然──不過話說回來，財物又不是她的。《奧德賽》的女作者對偷竊從來不苛責。雅典娜顯然認為，奧德修斯是否已死還沒有確定，潘妮洛碧就想改嫁，有點不大像話；但她解釋說，歐律馬科斯送的禮物比其他求婚者都要豐厚，而且最近又加碼了（卷十五 17、18 行）。說到底，潘妮洛碧有權讓自己稱心如意；只要她是 *bonâ fide*（真心誠意地）要出嫁，她儘管能偷多少就偷多少，既不失體面，也無損名節。作者讓雅典娜說出這種看法，當作女人可以合理持有的觀點。也許確實如此，但這不是男人的觀點。
> How differently, again, do Minerva and Telemachus regard the stealing. Telemachus feels it acutely and at once. Minerva takes it as a matter of course—but then the property was not hers. The authoress of the *Odyssey* is never severe about theft. Minerva evidently thinks it not nice of Penelope to want to marry again before it is known for certain that Ulysses is dead, but she explains that Eurymachus has been exceeding all the other suitors in the magnificence of his presents, and has lately increased them (xv. 17, 18). After all, Penelope had a right to please herself, and as long as she was going to be *bonâ fide* married, she might steal as much as she could, without loss of dignity or character. The writer put this view into Minerva's mouth as a reasonable one for a woman to take. So perhaps it was, but it is not a man's view.

〔U0776 para〕
我就在這裡結束我的論證──也就是說，在我所能使用的篇幅之內所能提出的那一部分──以支持《奧德賽》的作者是在粉飾潘妮洛碧這個看法。不過，既然我們恰好來到了拉刻代蒙，就讓我把自己想到的、與忒勒馬科斯拜訪墨涅拉俄斯王有關、又牽涉到作者是男是女這個問題的事，再說一說。
> Here I will close my case—as much of it, that is to say, as I have been able to give in the space at my disposal—for the view that the writer of the *Odyssey* was whitewashing Penelope. As, however, we happen to be at Lacedæmon let me say what more occurs to me in connection with the visit of Telemachus to King Menelaus that bears on the question whether the writer is a man or a woman.

〔U0777 para〕
忒勒馬科斯和涅斯托爾之子佩西斯特拉托斯在第四卷開頭抵達拉刻代蒙時，墨涅拉俄斯正在為他的兒子墨伽彭忒斯和女兒赫爾米俄涅舉行雙喜婚禮。作者寫道：──
> When Telemachus and Nestor's son Pisistratus reached Lacedæmon at the beginning of Book iv., Menelaus was celebrating the double marriage of his son Megapenthes and of his daughter Hermione. The writer says:—

〔U0778 quote〕
……他們抵達地勢低窪的拉刻代蒙城，直驅墨涅拉俄斯的住所，〔發現他正在自己家中與眾多族人歡宴，慶祝他兒子的婚禮，也慶祝他女兒的婚禮；他要把女兒嫁給那位英勇戰士阿基里斯的兒子。他還在特洛伊時就已應允，把她許配給他，如今眾神正促成這樁婚事，所以他要用戰車和馬匹把她送往阿基里斯之子所統治的密爾米頓人的城邦。他為獨子從斯巴達娶來阿勒克托爾的女兒當新娘。這個兒子墨伽彭忒斯是一個女奴為他生的，因為海倫生下赫爾米俄涅之後，上天就不再賜她子女了；赫爾米俄涅美得就像金色的阿芙蘿黛蒂本人（卷四 1–14 行）。〕
> ....they reached the low lying city of Lacedæmon, where they drove straight to the abode of Menelaus, [and found him in his own house feasting with his many clansmen in honour of the wedding of his son, and also that of his daughter whom he was giving in marriage to the son of that valiant warrior Achilles. He had given his consent and promised her to him while he was still at Troy, and now the gods were bringing the marriage about, so he was sending her with chariots and horses to the city of the Myrmidons over whom Achilles' son was reigning. For his only son he had found a bride from Sparta, the daughter of Alector. This son, Megapenthes, was born to him of a bondwoman, for heaven had vouchsafed Helen no more children after she had borne Hermione who was fair as golden Venus herself (iv. 1-14).]

〔U0779 para〕
我把上面引文的一部分放進括號裡，並不是因為我懷疑其中有哪一部分不是出自寫這部詩其餘部分的同一隻手，而是因為我確信，括號裡的詩行是作者在作品完成之後，或者至少是在第四卷和第十五卷定成現在的樣子之後，才插進去的。我認為插入的原因是：她不能原諒自己對赫爾米俄涅隻字未提；赫爾米俄涅在第十五卷以及第四卷其餘部分都沒有露面，於是她現在試圖插入上引這段來加以解釋，讓赫爾米俄涅就在第四卷一開頭一去不返地離開拉刻代蒙。可是不管插入的原因是什麼，這段話肯定是插入的；因為從第四卷其餘部分看來，再明白不過的是：當時並沒有什麼喜慶活動，唯一的客人是不請自來的──也就是忒勒馬科斯和佩西斯特拉托斯。
> I have enclosed part of the above quotation in brackets not because I have any doubt that the whole of it is by the same hand as the rest of the poem, but because I am convinced that the bracketed lines were interpolated by the writer after her work had been completed, or at any rate after Books iv. and xv. had assumed their present shape. The reason for the interpolation I take to be that she could not forgive herself for having said nothing about Hermione, whose non-appearance in Book xv. and in the rest of Book iv. she now attempts to explain by interpolating the passage above quoted, and thus making her quit Lacedæmon for good and all at the very beginning of this last named book. But whatever the cause of the interpolation may have been, an interpolation it certainly is, for nothing can be plainer from the rest of Book iv. than that there were no festivities going on, and that the only guests were uninvited ones—to wit Telemachus and Pisistratus.

〔U0780 para〕
不錯，作者曾試圖插入 621–624 行來修補這件事；這幾行在我們的各種版本裡總是被放進括號，列為可疑的詩行──我想是因為阿里斯塔克斯給它們標上了 *obeli*（存疑符號），儘管他沒有膽量把它們刪去。然而這塊補丁只讓事情更糟，因為它顯然補不了漏洞，而它的突兀也讓讀者摸不著頭腦。
> True, the writer tried to cobble the matter by introducing lines 621-624, which in our texts are always inclosed in brackets as suspected—I suppose because Aristarchus marked them with *obeli*, though he did not venture to exclude them. The cobble, however, only makes things worse, for it is obviously inadequate, and its abruptness puzzles the reader.

〔U0781 para〕
那麼，姑且承認第四卷 2–19 行和 621–624 行出自寫這部詩其餘部分的那位作者，讀者就會注意到，她覺得赫爾米俄涅的婚事比墨伽彭忒斯的婚事有趣得多──順帶一提，墨伽彭忒斯的新娘在第十五卷裡毫無蹤影。兒子的婚事確實比女兒的先提到；但這想必只是因為 υἱέος ἠδὲ θυγατρός（兒子與女兒）比把這兩個名詞對調的任何說法，都更容易放進六音步詩行裡。作者一提到兒子和女兒都要成婚，便立刻轉向赫爾米俄涅；她讓墨伽彭忒斯成婚，似乎只是因為既然他的姊妹要出嫁，他不妨也順便結婚算了。換了男作家，就會先讓墨伽彭忒斯成婚，再輪到赫爾米俄涅；他也不會僅僅為了把赫爾米俄涅帶進詩裡，就認為值得在詩中做一處非常笨拙的插入，因為到這時候她一定已經三十多歲了，大可假定她早在多年前、墨涅拉俄斯不在家的期間就已出嫁。
> Accepting, then, lines 2-19 and 621-624 of Book iv. as by the writer of the rest of the poem, the reader will note how far more interesting she finds the marriage of Hermione than that of Megapenthes—of whose bride, by the way, there is no trace in Book xv. The marriage of the son is indeed mentioned in the first instance before that of the daughter; but surely this is only because υἱέος ἠδὲ θυγατρός lends itself more readily to a hexameter verse than any transposition of the nouns would do. Having mentioned that both son and daughter are to be married, the writer at once turns to Hermione, and appears only to marry Megapenthes because, as his sister is being married, he may as well be married too. A male writer would have married Megapenthes first and Hermione afterwards; nor would he have thought it worth while to make a very awkward interpolation in his poem merely in order to bring Hermione into it, for by this time she must have been over thirty, and it would have been easy to suppose that she had been married years ago during Menelaus's absence.

〔U0782 para〕
至於第二處、也較短的那段插入（卷四 621–624 行），講的是那場假託的婚禮的次日，內容如下：──
> As regards the second and shorter interpolation (iv. 621-624), it refers to the day after the pretended marriages, and runs as follows:—

〔U0783 quote〕
他們就這樣交談著，〔而客人們陸續來到國王的宅邸。他們帶來了羊和酒，他們的妻子則為他們備好麵包，讓他們帶去。於是他們在庭院裡忙著烹煮自己的飯食。〕
> Thus did they converse [and guests kept coming to the king's house. They brought sheep and wine, while their wives had put up bread for them to take with them. So they were busy cooking their dinners in the courts.]

〔U0784 para〕
在兒女成婚這樣的大日子裡，墨涅拉俄斯大概不會指望客人自備糧食（雖然他也許會像阿爾基諾俄斯那樣，[註1]指望客人自己動手烹煮）──這一點且不去說它；我要請讀者注意的是，作者即使在一塊小小的補丁裡，也沒法不讓女人出場。男人也許會告訴我們，客人帶來了肉、酒和麵包，但他的心思不會本能地轉到客人的妻子為他們備好麵包這件事上。
> Passing over the fact that on such a great occasion as the marriage of his son and daughter, Menelaus would hardly expect his guests to bring their own provisions with them (though he might expect them as Alcinous did[註1] to do their own cooking) I would ask the reader to note that the writer cannot keep the women out even from a mere cobble. A man might have told us that the guests brought meat and wine and bread, but his mind would not instinctively turn to the guests' wives putting up the bread for them.

〔U0785 para〕
忒勒馬科斯斯巴達之行的時間順序，和奧德修斯從卡呂普索的島嶼回到伊薩卡的時間順序之間有出入──奧德修斯比忒勒馬科斯早一天抵達──這一點我就不談了。讀者可以在繆爾上校的《古希臘語言文學批判史》第一卷 439、440 頁看到詳細的討論。我認為這不過是一位作者的疏忽，而她覺得這類疏忽無關緊要；不過我倒要提請讀者注意：第四卷所描寫的墨涅拉俄斯府邸那般富麗堂皇，到了第十五卷就已經垮了下來；而照我所能判定的忒勒馬科斯在墨涅拉俄斯那裡逗留的時間來看，兩卷之間相隔應該不超過整整一天。
> I say nothing about the discrepancy between the chronology of Telemachus's visit to Sparta, and of Ulysses' journey from the island of Calypso to Ithaca where he arrives one day before Telemachus does. The reader will find it dwelt on in Colonel Mure's *Language and Literature of Ancient Greece*, Vol. I., pp. 439, 440. I regard it as nothing more than a slip on the part of a writer who felt that such slips are matters of very small importance; but I will call attention to the manner in which the gorgeousness of Menelaus's establishment as described in Book iv. has collapsed by the time we reach Book xv., though as far as I can determine the length of Telemachus's stay with Menelaus, the interval between the two books should not exceed one entire day.

〔U0786 para〕
忒勒馬科斯告訴墨涅拉俄斯他必須立刻回家之後，墨涅拉俄斯力勸兩位客人留下來吃點東西再上路；他告訴他們，這樣不但比較合乎禮數，對他們也比較舒服，而且還比較省錢。
> When Telemachus has informed Menelaus that he must go home at once, Menelaus presses his guests to stay and have something to eat before they start; this, he tells them, will be not only more proper and more comfortable for them, but also cheaper.

〔U0787 para〕
從《伊里亞德》卷七 470–475 行我們知道，墨涅拉俄斯在特洛伊城下時曾經賣過酒，阿伽門農也一樣；可是這番邀請裡有一種毫不掩飾的 *bourgoisie*（市民階級的小家子氣），這是男作家會避免的。然而更露骨的，是墨涅拉俄斯提議親自帶隊，領他們環遊伯羅奔尼撒一圈。這趟旅行會非常有賺頭，因為沒有人會讓他們空手而歸；每個人都會送他們一只青銅三足鼎，或一口大鍋，或兩頭騾子，或一只金杯（卷十五 75–85 行）。至於馬上要給他們吃的點心，國王解釋說他們只好有什麼吃什麼，但說他會吩咐女人們，從家裡碰巧有的東西當中，確保給他們準備足夠的分量。
> We know from *Il*. VII. 470-475 that Menelaus used to sell wine when he was before Troy, as also did Agamemnon, but there is a frank *bourgoisie* about this invitation which a male writer would have avoided. Still franker, however, is the offer of Menelaus to take them on a personally conducted tour round the Peloponesus. It will be very profitable, for no one will send them away empty handed; every one will give them either a bronze tripod or a cauldron, or two mules, or a gold chalice (xv. 75-85). As for the refreshments which they are to have immediately, the king explains that they will have to take potluck, but says he will tell the women to see that there is enough for them, of what there might happen to be in the house.

〔U0788 para〕
這正是墨涅拉俄斯一貫的瑣碎囉唆。他為什麼不能把這一切都交給海倫去辦呢？讀過《奧德賽》之後，我對她跟帕里斯私奔一點也不驚訝；唯一奇怪的是，特洛伊那件事了結之後不久，竟然沒有必要再打第二場大戰。兩個年輕的單身漢要留下來吃飯，這肯定不是什麼刺耳得可怕的不協和音，非得事先預備不可；即使不加預備就直接奏出，或至少不勞國王親自插手，也無不可。「從家裡碰巧有的東西當中」，說得真好聽！我們看得出這頓飯不會豐盛到哪裡去。如果家裡碰巧沒有什麼好東西──我猜情況正是如此──墨涅拉俄斯就該放心讓海倫差人出去買點什麼。可是根本就不該有差人出去買這回事；墨涅拉俄斯和海倫每一餐都應該有能想像得到的種種珍饈美味。
> That is just like Menelaus's usual fussiness. Why could he not have left it all to Helen? After reading the *Odyssey* I am not surprised at her having run away with Paris; the only wonder is that a second great war did not become necessary very shortly after the Trojan matter had been ended. Surely the fact that two young bachelors were going to stay and dine was not such a frightful discord but that it might have been taken unprepared, or at any rate without the monarch's personal interference. "Of what there may be in the house" indeed. We can see that the dinner is not going to be profusely sumptuous. If there did not happen to be anything good in the house—and I suspect this to have been the case—Menelaus should have trusted Helen to send out and get something. But there should have been no sending out about it; Menelaus and Helen ought never to have had a meal without every conceivable delicacy.

〔U0789 para〕
再說，管家埃特俄紐斯的身分不也是一落千丈嗎？他根本不是真正的管家──只是個打零工的鐘點管家；他不住在宅子裡（卷十五 96 行），說不定他一邊在墨涅拉俄斯家裡當差，一邊還在街角開著一家小店。更糟的是，他手下沒有男僕，連個小廝都沒有，因為墨涅拉俄斯叫他生火做飯（卷十五 97、98 行），而他連一個字的異議都沒有就照辦了。阿斯法利翁哪裡去了？忒勒馬科斯和佩西斯特拉托斯到達時服侍他們的那些男僕都到哪裡去了？他們現在得自己套馬了。出現在《奧德賽》每一頓飯上的上等和下等女僕照例在場，但我們再也沒有聽到阿德瑞斯忒、阿爾基珀和菲羅的消息。看來作者在第四卷描寫了墨涅拉俄斯府邸的富麗堂皇之後，就洩了膽氣，到了第十五卷，她本能的節儉又重新抬頭了。
> What a come down, again, is there not as regards the butler Eteoneus. He was not a real butler at all—he was only a kind of char-butler; he did not sleep in the house (xv. 96), and for aught we know may have combined a shop round the corner with his position in Menelaus' household. Worse than this, he had no footman, not even a boy, under him, for Menelaus tells him to light the fire and set about cooking dinner (xv. 97, 98), which he proceeds to do without one syllable of remonstrance. What has become of Asphaliou? Where are the men servants who attended to Telemachus and Pisistratus on their arrival? They have to yoke their own horses now. The upper and under women servants who appear at all Odyssean meals are here as usual, but we hear nothing more of Adraste, Alcippe, and Phylo. It seems as though after describing the splendour of Menelaus's house in Book iv. the writer's nerve has failed her, and by Book xv. her instinctive thrift has re-asserted itself.

〔U0790 para〕
現在，讓我照第四章裡說過的打算，回頭談談雅典娜在她的被保護人前往皮洛斯和拉刻代蒙的航行一事上所做的安排；這些安排的性質非常奇特──我不想說是女性化的。
> And now let me return, as I said in Chapter IV. that I intended doing, to the very singular—for I do not like to say feminine—nature of the arrangements made by Minerva for her protégé in the matter of his voyage to Pylos and Lacedæmon.

〔U0791 para〕
雅典娜最初向他提出這個主意時，她知道奧德修斯即將從卡呂普索的島上啟程前往斯刻里亞，幾乎馬上就要回到伊薩卡了。可是她偏偏要挑這個時候，而不是別的任何時候，派忒勒馬科斯去作一趟危險的航行，打聽他父親的消息。我們已經看過她如何對他說教；可是，倘若忒勒馬科斯知道她一直在竭盡全力讓他的航行白費，他大可相當有理地反唇相譏：不管他今後會不會當個傻瓜，他都不會像她現在耍弄他這樣，把自己的哪個年輕朋友當傻瓜耍。況且，必要的話，他是要離家十二個月的；然而他走了還不到四五天，雅典娜就讓他為自己的財物憂心如焚，催他火速趕回伊薩卡。
> When Minerva first suggested it to him, she knew that Ulysses was on the point of starting from Calypso's island for Scheria, and would be back in Ithaca almost immediately. Yet she must needs choose this particular moment, of all others, for sending Telemachus on a perilons voyage in quest of news concerning him. We have seen how she preached to him; but surely if Telemachus had known that she was all the time doing her very utmost to make his voyage useless, he might have retorted with some justice that whether he was going to be a fool henceforward or no, he should not make such a fool of any young friend of his own as she was now making of himself. Besides, he was to be away, if necessary, for twelve months; yet here before he has been gone more than four or five days, Minerva fills him with an agony of apprehension about his property and sends him post haste back to Ithaca again.

〔U0792 para〕
女作者似乎也感受到了這一點的分量，因為在卷十三 416–419 行，她讓奧德修斯正是以這個意思向雅典娜抗議，問道：──
> The authoress seems to have felt the force of this, for in xiii. 416-419 she makes Ulysses remonstrate with Minerva in this very sense, and ask:—

〔U0793 para〕
「你既然什麼都知道，為什麼不告訴他？難道你也要他在別人吃光他家產的時候，在海上四處漂泊，歷盡種種艱辛嗎？」
> "Why did you not tell him, for you know all about it? Did you want him, too, to go sailing about amid all kinds of hardships when others were eating up his estate?"

〔U0794 para〕
雅典娜回答說：「你不必為他操心。我派他去，是要他因為這趟遠行而博得好名聲。他沒有遇上任何困難，正舒舒服服地待在墨涅拉俄斯那裡，身邊各樣東西應有盡有。求婚者已經出海，正埋伏著等他，因為他們打算在他到家之前殺了他。我不大相信他們會得逞，倒是那些如今正在吃你家產的人當中，有一些會先替自己找到墳墓。」
> Minerva answered, "Do not trouble yourself about him. I sent him that he might be well spoken about for having gone. He is in no sort of difficulty, but is staying comfortably with Menelaus, and is surrounded with abundance of every kind. The suitors have put out to sea and are on the watch for him, for they mean to kill him before he can got home. I do not much think they will succeed, but rather that some of those who are now eating up your estate will first find a grave themselves."

〔U0795 para〕
她其實應該這麼說：──
> What she ought to have said was:—

〔U0796 quote〕
「你這笨人，難道你還不明白，我的女詩人一心想把特洛伊的海倫寫進她的詩裡，而除了派忒勒馬科斯到斯巴達去，她想不出別的辦法？我向你保證，他一跟海倫和墨涅拉俄斯見過面，我就採取了──或者說將會採取，因為我那女詩人的年代順序把我這可憐的腦袋搞得糊塗極了──措施，立刻把他帶回來。」
> "You stupid man, can you not understand that my poetess had set her heart on bringing Helen of Troy into her poem, and could not see her way to this without sending Telemachus to Sparta? I assure you that as soon as ever he had interviewed Helen and Menelaus, I took—or will take, for my poetess's chronology puzzles my poor head dreadfully—stops to bring him back at once."

〔U0797 para〕
在第四卷末尾，潘妮洛碧也表現出類似的傾向，抱怨別人讓她蒙在鼓裡，一些本來可以輕易透露給她的消息，卻偏偏不告訴她。
> At the end of Book iv. Penelope shows a like tendency to complain of the manner in which she is kept in the dark about information that might easily have been vouchsafed to her.

〔U0798 para〕
雅典娜給她送去一個幻影，模樣像她的姊妹伊芙提墨。這個幻影來到潘妮洛碧床邊，告訴她，她的兒子會平安回家。她立刻說道：──
> Minerva has sent her a vision in the likeness of her sister Ipthime. This vision comes to Penelope's bedside and tells her that her son shall come safely home again. She immediately says:—

〔U0799 para〕
「那麼，如果你是一位女神，或是聽到了上天傳來的消息，就告訴我那另一個不幸的人的事吧。他還活著嗎？還是已經死了，在黑帝斯的宅邸裡？」
> "If, then, you are a goddess, or have heard news from Heaven tell me about that other unhappy one. Is he still alive, or is he dead and in the house of Hades?"

〔U0800 para〕
幻影回答說：「他究竟是生是死，我不會確切告訴你；說些空話閒談，也沒有什麼用處。」
> And the vision answered, "I shall not tell you for certain whether he is alive or dead, and there is no use in idle conversation."

〔U0801 para〕
說完，它便從門上穿皮條的孔隙中消失了。
> On this it vanished through the thong-hole of the door.

〔U0802 para〕
我還可以補充一句：《奧德賽》裡臥房門的閂法，我一直沒有完全弄懂，直到我發現自己在特拉帕尼中央旅館住的那間臥房，正是用《奧德賽》裡的方式閂門的。
> I may add that I never quite understood the fastening of the Odyssean bedroom door, till I found my bedroom at the Hotel Centrale, Trapani, fastened in the Odyssean manner.

〔U0803 footnote〕
《奧德賽》卷八 38–40 行，另參見 61 行。看來，比較貧窮的客人自己烹煮、在外面庭院裡吃的那些糧食，是由阿爾基諾俄斯供給的。權貴們則在有頂蓋的迴廊裡用餐，想必有人替他們烹煮。
> *Od*. viii. 38-40, *cf*. also 61. It would seem that Alcinous found the provisions which the poorer guests cooked for themselves and ate outside in the court yards. The magnates ate in the covered cloister, and were no doubt cooked for.

〔U0804 heading〕
第七章
> CHAPTER VII.

〔U0805 para〕
更多跡象顯示作者是女性──年輕──任性──而且未婚。
> FURTHER INDICATIONS THAT THE WRITER IS A WOMAN—YOUNG—HEADSTRONG—AND UNMARRIED.

〔U0806 para〕
除了前面那些大方向的一般考量，以及我已經提請讀者注意的種種細節之外，我現在要簡略談談幾處主要的段落；在我看來，這些段落透露的是女人的手筆，而不是男人的。許許多多比較可疑的例子，我一概略去不談；其中不少讀者想必早已注意到，或者不難自行發現。
> I will now touch briefly on the principal passages, over and above large general considerations and the details to which I have already called attention, which seem to me to suggest a woman's hand rather than a man's. I shall omit countless more doubtful instances, many of which the reader will have noted, or easily discover.

〔U0807 para〕
全詩一開頭（卷一 13 行），作者就把奧德修斯寫成一心渴望回到妻子身邊。可是他在喀耳刻那裡整整待了一年，要不是手下一再勸諫，天曉得他還會再待多久。他在卡呂普索那裡待了七年，而且看來一直和她相處得極為融洽，直到這部詩的情節需要，才不得不把他送回伊薩卡。像他那樣精明的人，大可以早在好幾年前就把卡呂普索的斧頭和鑽子順手摸走，到島的另一頭砍下樹木，把木筏造好，而她根本不會察覺；畢竟，她總不至於常常要用到斧頭或鑽子吧。
> At the very outset of the poem (i. 13) the writer represents Ulysses as longing to get back to his wife. He had stayed a whole year with Circe, and but for the remonstrances of his men would have stayed no one can say how much longer. He had stayed seven years with Calypso, and seems to have remained on excellent terms with her until the exigencies of the poem made it necessary to send him back to Ithaca. Surely a man of his sagacity might have subtracted Calypso's axe and auger, cut down the trees at the far end of the island, and made his raft years ago without her finding out anything about it; for she can hardly have wanted either axe or auger very often.

〔U0808 para〕
至於糧食，奧德修斯若連私下攢一點存糧的本事都沒有，那他的狡黠可就被大大高估了。他若真心想回到潘妮洛碧身邊，詩中有目共睹的那一點小狡黠，早就該用在這上頭了。因此我深信，女作者儘管故意假裝奧德修斯歸心似箭、恨不得立刻回到潘妮洛碧身邊，她心裡其實很清楚，他一點也不急；只是她無論如何不肯承認這種事，因為那太有損婚姻生活的神聖──或者至少有損妻子駕馭丈夫的權力。
> As for the provisions, if Ulysses was not capable of accumulating a private hoard, his cunning has been much overrated. If he had seriously wanted to get back to Penelope his little cunning that is put in evidence would have been exercised in this direction. I am convinced, therefore, that though the authoress chooses to pretend that Ulysses was dying to get back to Penelope, she knew perfectly well that he was in no great hurry to do so; she was not, however, going to admit anything so derogatory to the sanctity of married life, or at any rate to the power which a wife has over her husband.

〔U0809 para〕
年紀大一點的女人，或許就不會那麼費心去掩飾這個事實：潘妮洛碧對奧德修斯的牽繫，其實非常薄弱。可是《奧德賽》的作者偏偏再年輕、再任性、再未婚不過。沒有哪一位主婦會坐下來動筆寫《奧德賽》。她太懂事，又太缺乏膽量。她會得到太多──而在得到的過程中又失去太多。這部詩是一部 *tour de force*（絕技之作），除了一個慣於隨心所欲、意氣昂揚而又倔強的少女，誰也不會去嘗試，更別說把它完成得如此光彩奪目、如此成功了；因此，我無法想像作者的年紀會比本書卷首插圖那幅像的原型更大──假如她真有那麼大的話。
> An older woman might have been at less pains to conceal the fact that Penelope's hold on Ulysses was in reality very slight, but the writer of the *Odyssey* is nothing if she is not young, self-willed, and unmarried. No matron would set herself down to write the *Odyssey* at all. She would have too much sense, and too little daring. She would have gained too much—and lost too greatly in the gaining. The poem is such a *tour de force* as none but a high-spirited, headstrong girl who had been accustomed to have her own way would have attempted, much less carried to such a brilliantly successful conclusion; I cannot, therefore, conceive the writer as older than the original of the frontispiece at the beginning of this book—if indeed she was so old.

〔U0810 para〕
老奶媽歐律克蕾亞替忒勒馬科斯持燈照路、送他就寢，又替他把衣服疊好的那幾行（卷一 428–442 行）寫得非常優美，透露的是女人的手筆，而不是男人的。特意強調拉厄爾特斯顧及妻子的感受（卷一 430–433 行），也是如此。這種為妻子權利而戒慎的心思，暗示作者一心要淨化她的時代，在夫妻關係上標舉一個更高的理想，比荷馬時代的男人可能堅持的要高。
> The very beautiful lines in which the old nurse Euryclea lights Telemachus to bed, and folds up his clothes for him (i. 428-442), suggest a woman's hand rather than a man's. So also does the emphasising Laertes' respect for his wife's feelings (i. 430-433). This jealousy for a wife's rights suggests a writer who was bent on purifying her age, and upholding a higher ideal as regards the relations between husband and wife than a man in the Homeric age would be likely to insist on.

〔U0811 para〕
歐律克蕾亞的身價（卷一 431 行），我毫不懷疑，是對《伊里亞德》那些侮辱的反擊：卷二十三 262–264 行把一個女人和一只三腳鼎合成一份，當作獎品；卷二十三 702–705 行又說一只三腳鼎值十二頭牛，而一個能幹耐用、什麼雜活都做的女僕只值四頭牛。主婦會對荷馬這段話敬而遠之、碰都不碰；男人則不至於憤慨到特地寫上一筆來回敬，宣稱歐律克蕾亞是用二十頭牛買來的。
> The price paid for Euryclea (i. 431) is, I do not doubt, a rejoinder to the Iliadic insults of XXIII. 262-264, in which a woman and a tripod are put up in one lot as a prize, and also of XXIII. 702-705, in which a tripod is represented as worth twelve oxen, and a good serviceable maid of all work only four oxen. A matron would have let Homer's passage severely alone, and a man would not have resented it so strongly as to make him write at it by declaring Euryclea to have been bought for twenty oxen.

〔U0812 para〕
有一段篇幅不短、取自《伊里亞德》的文字被打斷了（卷三 448–455 行），為的是把涅斯托爾的妻子和女兒們帶進來，描寫她們看見一頭小母牛被宰殺時的歡喜；然後那段《伊里亞德》式的文字才又接續下去。男人或年紀大些的女人，一旦著手寫一段《伊里亞德》式的文字，就會一路寫下去，直到寫不下去為止。涅斯托爾家裡的女眷愛不愛看宰小母牛，他們才不在乎。
> An Iliadic passage of some length is interrupted (iii. 448-455) for the purpose of bringing in Nestor's wife and daughters, and describing their delight at seeing a heifer killed; the Iliadic passage is then resumed. A man, or older woman, once launched on an Iliadic passage would have stuck to it till it failed them. They would not have cared whether the ladies of Nestor's household liked seeing the heifer killed or no.

〔U0813 para〕
海倫把忘憂藥摻進要分給墨涅拉俄斯、忒勒馬科斯和佩西斯特拉托斯的酒裡時，我們得知這藥的效力之強：一個人喝下之後，一整天都流不出眼淚，即使他失去了父親和母親，或者親眼看見兄弟或兒子被砍成碎塊，也是如此（卷四 220–226 行）。從這些親屬關係在作者心中浮現的先後次序，我推斷她的父親和母親是她世界裡最重要的人，因而她當時還年輕、還沒有出嫁。
> When Helen mixes Nepenthe with the wine which was to be handed round to Menelaus, Telemachus, and Pisistratus, we learn its virtues to be so powerful that a man could not weep during all the day on which he had drunk it, not even though he had lost both his father and his mother, or had seen a brother or a son cut to pieces before his eyes (iv. 220-226). From the order in which these relationships present themselves to the writer's mind I opine that her father and mother were the most important persons in her world, and hence that she was still young and unmarried.

〔U0814 para〕
再往下一點，我們發現海倫對自己跟帕里斯私奔多少有些悔意。海倫是宙斯的親生女兒，因此差不多有權愛怎麼做就怎麼做；儘管如此，大家還是認為最好盡可能替她挽回一點，讓她多少帶點悔恨。然而，這悔恨卻是非常奇特的一種。看來，該懺悔的倒是阿芙蘿黛蒂，因為她對海倫犯下了這麼大的過錯。海倫哀嘆的是別人加諸她的過錯，而不是她自己有什麼不是。
> A little lower we find Helen more or less penitent for having run away with Paris. Helen was Jove's own daughter, and therefore had a right to do pretty much as she chose; still it was held better to redeem her as far as possible, by making her more or less contrite. The contrition, however, is of a very curious kind. It was Venus, it seems, who ought to be penitent for having done Helen so great a wrong. It is the wrong that has been done to her that she laments, rather than any misdoing of her own.

〔U0815 para〕
一個男人，或者一位主婦，會想得出這樣的點子嗎：讓海倫繞著木馬走，拍拍它，喊出藏在裡面的英雄們的名字，還模仿他們妻子的聲音（卷四 274–279 行）？奧德修斯喬裝潛入特洛伊、和她交談時，想必已經告訴她木馬要來，以及裡面會裝些什麼。男人或許會讓海倫繞著木馬走、拍拍它，甚至喊出英雄們的名字，但他絕對想不到要讓她模仿他們的妻子。
> Is a man, or matron, likely to have conceived the idea of making Helen walk round the wooden horse, pat it, call out the names of the heroes who were inside, and mimick the voices of their wives (iv. 274-279)? Ulysses must have told her that the horse was coming, and what it would contain, when he entered Troy in disguise and talked with her. A man might have made Helen walk round the horse, pat it, and even call out the names of the heroes, but he would never have thought of making her mimick their wives.

〔U0816 para〕
作者覺得魚腥味令人無法忍受，認為有必要替墨涅拉俄斯和他的三個手下解除這番難受的處境，於是讓埃伊多忒亞在每個人的鼻子底下抹上一點香料（卷四 441–446 行）。不過，這裡另有一層 *arrière pensée*（言外之意），我稍後會再提醒讀者注意（見第十二章接近結尾處）。埃伊多忒亞捉弄自己父親時那顯而易見的得意，也十足是做女兒的樣子。父親向來是可以捉弄的對象──至少對年輕的女神來說是如此。
> The writer finds the smell of fish intolerable, and thinks it necessary to relieve Menelaus and his three men from a distressing situation, by getting Idothea to put some scent under each man's nostrils (iv. 441-446). There is, however, an *arrière pensée* here to which I will call attention later (see Chapter XII. near the end). Very daughterly also is the pleasure which Idothea evidently feels in playing a trick upon her father. Fathers are fair game—at all events for young goddesses.

〔U0817 para〕
卷四 625–847 行整段都強烈暗示出自女人的筆下，但我不能指望任何人不讀原文或某個完整的譯本，就同意這一點。
> The whole of iv. 625-847 is strongly suggestive of a woman's writing, but I cannot expect any one to admit this without reading either the original or some complete translation.

〔U0818 para〕
卡呂普索對潘妮洛碧的嫉妒（卷五 203 行以下）寫得太過嬌俏，不像男人的手筆。換了男人，一定會寫過頭。
> Calypso's jealously of Penelope (v. 203, &c.) is too prettily done for a man. A man would be sure to overdo it.

〔U0819 para〕
卷六或許是全詩最可愛的一卷；但我幾乎毫不懷疑，假如把它當作佚名作品交給今天《泰晤士報》的某位評論家，要他判斷作者的性別，他會毫不猶豫地斷定這出自一位年輕未婚女子之手。請讀者留意：瑙西卡得督促父親，在該換乾淨襯衫的時候換上一件（卷六 60 行）；而她的弟弟們要乾淨襯衫時，看來倒是由他們來督促她替他們備好。這些小小的筆觸，暗示這是阿爾基諾俄斯自家某位女性成員的寫生之作，她在幕後熟知他那些小習慣。
> Book vi. is perhaps the loveliest in the whole poem, but I can hardly doubt that if it were given to a *Times* critic of to-day as an anonymous work, and he was told to determine the sex of the writer he would ascribe it to a young unmarried woman without a moment's hesitation. Let the reader note how Nausicaa has to keep her father up to having a clean shirt on when he ought to have one (vi. 60), whereas her younger brothers appear to keep her up to having one for them when they want one. These little touches suggest drawing from life by a female member of Alcinous' own family who knew his little ways from behind the scenes.

〔U0820 para〕
再看奧德修斯初遇瑙西卡的那一場。一個少女──正像瑙西卡本人那樣，年輕、未婚、心無所屬，也不知道男人在這種事情上通常有什麼感受──既然藉著命運一場殘酷的惡作劇，把她的英雄弄進了如此尷尬的窘境，或許真能想像他會像《奧德賽》作者讓奧德修斯那樣去說理；但沒有哪個男人──除非是那種專給女人做衣裳、絕不可能寫出《奧德賽》的男人──會讓他的英雄落到這麼有失體面的境地，更別說讓他像奧德修斯那樣說話了。
> Take, again, the scene in which Ulysses first meets Nausicaa. A girl, such a girl as Nausicaa herself, young, unmarried, unattached, and without knowledge of what men commonly feel on such points, having by a cruel freak of fortune got her hero into such an awkward predicament, might conceivably imagine that he would argue as the writer of the *Odyssey* has made Ulysses do, but no man, except such a woman's tailor as could never have written the *Odyssey*, would have got his hero into such an undignified position at all, much less have made him talk as Ulysses is made to talk.

〔U0821 para〕
再說，瑙西卡想要表明，她在奧德修斯身上找到了一個她真的可能會傾心的男人──假如他們的結合沒有任何阻礙的話──這又多麼像一個厭惡男人的女子會做的事！
> How characteristic, again, of the man-hatress is Nausicaa's attempt to make out that in Ulysses she had found a man to whom she really might become attached—if there were no obstacle to their union.

〔U0822 para〕
卷七我實在很難略過不提，尤其是第 230 行以下，阿瑞忒想知道奧德修斯身上的衣服是從哪裡來的；還有第 294 行，說年輕人往往思慮不周。這分明是一個少女在敲長輩的指節，拿她平日聽慣了的長輩說話來回敬他們。
> I find it hard to pass over Book vii., especially line 230, &c., where Arete wants to know how Ulysses came by his clothes, and 294, in which it is said that young people are apt to be thoughtless. Surely this is a girl giving a rap on the knuckles to older people, by echoing what she is accustomed to hear them say.

〔U0823 para〕
卷八裡的競技，無疑是受了《伊里亞德》卷二十三的啟發，卻只被籠統標為「運動」，一點細節也沒有，只說奧德修斯擲出的鐵餅飛過空中時發出某種聲響（卷八 190 行）──我才不相信它會發出什麼聲響。在《伊里亞德》裡，每一項比賽都交代了細節，而且競技也不像《奧德賽》那樣，緊接在一頓豐盛的大餐之後舉行；我們很難設想參賽者沒有參加那頓飯。
> In Book viii. the games, which are no doubt suggested by those in *Il*. XXIII. are merely labelled "sports," not a single detail being given except that Ulysses' disc made a sound of some sort as it went through the air (viii. 190), which I do not believe it would do. In the *Iliad* details are given of every contest, and the games do not take place as they do in the *Odyssey* immediately after a heavy meal, from which we can hardly suppose that the competitors would be excluded.

〔U0824 para〕
至於女神們出於端莊，沒有前來觀看阿瑞斯與阿芙蘿黛蒂落入赫淮斯托斯的羅網（卷八 324 行），以及阿芙蘿黛蒂獲釋之後美惠三女神拿來安慰她的那件美麗新衣（卷八 366 行），我都不置一詞，因為我在縮譯中把這整段插曲略去了。
> I say nothing about the modesty of the female goddesses in not coming to see Mars and Venus caught in the toils of Vulcan (viii. 324), nor yet about the lovely new dress with which the Graces consoled Venus when she had been liberated (viii. 366), for I have omitted the whole of this episode in my abridgement.

〔U0825 para〕
全詩處處可見她對自己家園和父母的愛，而這份愛表現得最明顯的，莫過於奧德修斯的那段話（卷九 34–36 行）。他說，一個人在異鄉不管住著多好的房子，離開了父母，就永遠不會真正快樂。這和阿里斯托芬借赫耳墨斯之口說的話（《財神》1151 行）多麼不同──那句話大意是說，哪裡能賺錢，哪裡就是一個人的祖國；也和歐里庇得斯大不相同，他在《法厄同》的一段殘篇裡說，哪片土地養得活人，哪裡就是祖國。只有一個多情的年輕少女，才能讓奧德修斯（他並不怎麼多愁善感）說得如此情深意切。中年人，無論男女，都已沾染了太多世俗的汙點，說不出這種話來。他們的想法和阿里斯托芬、歐里庇得斯一樣。
> The love of her own home and parents which is so obvious throughout the poem is never more apparent than in the speech of Ulysses (ix. 34-36). He says that however fine a house a man may have in a foreign land, he can never be really happy away from his father and mother. How different this from the saying which Aristophanes puts into the mouth of Mercury (*Plut*. 1151) to the effect that a man's fatherland is any place in which he is making money; or again from Euripides, who in a fragment of *Phaethon* says that a man's fatherland is any land that will feed him. It is only a young and affectionate girl who could have made Ulysses (who is not much given to sentiment) speak so warmly. Middle-aged people, whether men or women, are too much spotted with the world to be able to say such things. They think as Aristophanes and Euripides do.

〔U0826 para〕
卷九 120、121 行，作者告訴我們，獵人一般都肯在森林裡、在山巔上忍受種種艱苦。這話一點不假，可是男人談起獵岩羚羊的人，不會是這種說法。
> In lines 120, 121 of Book ix. the writer tells us that huntsmen as a general rule will face all sorts of hardship in forest and on mountain top. This is quite true, but it is not the way in which men speak of chamois-hunters.

〔U0827 para〕
至於庫克羅普斯那段插曲，固然妙趣橫生，卻不可能出自男人或主婦的筆下。波呂斐摩斯雖然喝醉了，卻肯一動不動、連一塊肌肉都不抽一下，一直等到奧德修斯和他的手下用一根大得足以當船桅的燒紅木樁把他的眼睛鑽完，這可真是體貼極了；可是能端出這種東西給我們的男作家，只有閔希豪森男爵一人，而他的情形不能拿來相比。說到底，《奧德賽》卷九也不能拿來相比，因為作者並沒有把波呂斐摩斯當真。
> As for the Cyclops incident, delightful as it is, it is impossible as a man or matron's writing. It was very kind of Polyphemus, drunk though he was, to stay without moving a muscle, till Ulysses and his men had quite finished boring out his eye with a burning beam that was big enough for a ship's mast, but Baron Munchausen is the only male writer who could offer us anything of the kind, and his is not a case in point. Neither, after all, is Book ix. of the *Odyssey*, for the writer is not taking Polyphemus seriously.

〔U0828 para〕
波呂斐摩斯扔下一捆柴薪，把奧德修斯和他的人嚇得魂飛魄散，這一筆太過生動，不可能不是據實寫生。我常常幻想，整段庫克羅普斯插曲，也許是由一次 *merende*（野餐）引發的──義大利人和西西里人至今仍非常喜愛這種野餐──《奧德賽》的作者和朋友們一起去了皮佐隆戈，去了故事發生的那個洞穴；那裡當時其實和今天的 *alpe*（高山牧場）差不多──是牧人的住處，他們就在洞裡做乳酪。我喜歡幻想（因為我知道這不過是幻想而已），《奧德賽》的作者對所見的一切都非常著迷，但就在她看著那些奶盆的時候，有個蓬頭垢面的高大牧人背著一捆柴走進來，用力過猛地往地上一扔，讓她的神經受了一場突如其來的驚嚇。她便把他變成了當地的巨人──這位巨人如今仍以孔圖拉諾之名存在於埃律克斯山上。[註1]
> The distress which Polyphemus caused to Ulysses and his men by flinging down a bundle of firewood is too graphic a touch not to have been drawn from life. I have often fancied that the whole Cyclops incident may have been suggested by one of those *merende*, or pic-nics which Italians and Sicilians are still so fond of, and that the writer of the *Odyssey* went with her friends to Pizzolungo and the cave where the scene is laid, which was then really much what an *alpe* is now—an abode of shepherds who made cheese in the cave itself. I like to fancy (for I know that it is nothing more than fancy) that the writer of the *Odyssey* was delighted with all she saw, but that as she was looking at the milk dishes some huge unkempt shepherd came in with a load of firewood on his back, and gave a sudden shock to her nervous system by flinging it down too violently. Him she transformed into the local giant that exists on Mt. Eryx now under the name of Conturràno.[註1]

〔U0829 para〕
女作者以為波呂斐摩斯是怎麼處置他的母羊和羔羊的，實在很難說。羔羊整天都待在圈欄裡，因為奧德修斯的手下看見牠們在那裡，還想把牠們偷走（卷九 226、227 行）。況且，羔羊若白天跟母羊在一起，波呂斐摩斯就擠不到奶了。他把母羊趕進洞裡（為了簡便，母山羊我略去不提）之後，擠了奶，然後把羔羊放到母羊身邊（卷九 245 行）。問題是：羔羊從擠過奶的母羊身上吸到所能吸到的奶之後，他是再把牠們帶走，還是讓牠們整夜留在母親身邊？
> It is very hard to say what the authoress thought that Polyphemus did in the matter of his ewes and lambs. The lambs were in the yards all day, for Ulysses' men saw them there and wanted to steal them (ix. 226, 227). Besides, Polyphemus could not have got any milk from the ewes if their lambs had been with them in the day-time. Having driven the ewes into his cave (I omit the she-goats for brevity) he milked them, and then put their lambs with them (ix. 245). The question is, did he take them away again after they had got what they could from a milked ewe, or did he leave them with their mothers all night?

〔U0830 para〕
一方面，詩中毫無暗示說他把羔羊帶走，而那會是一件費時又麻煩的工作；另一方面，第 309 行又告訴我們，他早上擠了母羊的奶，又把各自的羔羊還給每一頭母羊；同一天傍晚，他又重複了這套程序（第 342 行）──而他若不是先把羔羊帶走，就很難再把羔羊「給」母羊。
> On the one hand we have no hint of their removal, which would be a long and troublesome task; on the other we are told in line 309 that he milked the ewes in the morning, and again gave each one of them her lamb; on the evening of the same day he repeats this process (line 342), and he could hardly give the ewes their lambs unless he had first removed them.

〔U0831 para〕
難處在於：他若把羔羊帶走，以波呂斐摩斯給牠們的那種伙食，牠們不出幾天就必死無疑，因為他做的一切都是 κατὰ μοῖραν，也就是照他平日的做法；可他若不把羔羊帶走，就擠不到奶。不管他怎麼做，我們都可以確定《奧德賽》的作者搞錯了；而想弄清她當時怎麼想，也得不到多少好處，因為她顯然根本沒有想。
> The difficulty is that if he removed them they would certainly die in a very few days of such diet as Polyphemus allows them, for whatever he did was κατὰ μοῖραν, according to his usual practice; while if he did not remove them, he could not have got any milk. Whatever he did, we may be sure that the writer of the *Odyssey* had got it wrong, and there is not much to be gained by trying to find out what she thought, for it is obvious that she did not think.

〔U0832 para〕
我請教了我的朋友、埃律克斯山的朱塞佩．帕戈托先生，問他現在西西里牧羊人的做法如何，得到以下的答覆：
> I asked my friend, Sigr. Giuseppe Pagoto of Mt. Eryx, what was the practise of Sicilian shepherds now, and received the following answer:—

〔U0833 para〕
在西西里，剛生過小羊的母羊是不擠奶的；人們把羔羊關起來，把母羊帶出去吃草。傍晚讓羔羊吸奶，然後再把牠們關起來。夜裡母羊會分泌大量的奶，早上這些奶又讓羔羊吸掉，並不擠出來。我們的牧羊人要等到羔羊宰了以後，才會取用母羊的奶。也許在那個時代，牧草非常豐美，母羊的奶既足夠餵飽羔羊，又還有剩餘可以擠。波呂斐摩斯的做法，只有這樣才解釋得通。
> In Sicily they do not milk ewes that have lately lambed; they keep the lambs shut up and take the ewes to feed. In the evening they let the lambs suck, and then shut them up again. During the night the ewes make a great deal of milk, and this is again sucked by the lambs in the morning, and not milked. Our shepherds do not take any of the milk until the lamb has been killed. Perhaps in those days the pastures were so abundant that the ewes gave milk enough to nourish the lambs, and still have some for milking. This is the only way in which what Polyphemus did can be explained.

〔U0834 para〕
我認為真正的解釋是：這個場景部分取材自某位牧人的 *alpe*，他趕進來一群母羊，其中有些帶著羔羊，另一些的羔羊則早已被宰來吃掉了。女作者看見牧人給一些母羊擠奶，然後又帶進一些羔羊，卻不明白擠過奶的母羊已經沒有羔羊，而羔羊還活著的母羊並沒有被擠奶。我想她自己也知道對此一知半解，否則她就不會用一句 πάντα κατὰ μοῖραν（一切按部就班），把她的敘述草草收尾了。
> I believe the true explanation to be that the shepherd from whose *alpe* the scene was in part drawn, drove in a number of ewes some of which had lambs, while the lambs of others had been already killed and eaten. The authoress saw the shepherd milk a number of ewes, and then bring in a number of lambs, but she did not understand that the ewes which had been milked had got no lambs, while those that had lambs still living had not been milked. I think she knew she was hazy about it, otherwise she would not have cut her version short with a πάντα κατὰ μοῖραν—"all in due course."

〔U0835 para〕
既然喀耳刻顯然和忒瑞西阿斯一樣是個稱職的預言者，可憐的奧德修斯為什麼還得被打發到冥府去？顯然是因為作者一心想要寫進與亡靈的對話。就算如此吧；但一個不那麼急於證明女人懂得和男人一樣多的作者，就不會讓喀耳刻知道得那麼多。奧德修斯一從冥府回來，她為什麼要用同樣的話，把忒瑞西阿斯已經警告過他的赫利俄斯牛群之事再對他重複一遍，還添上一大堆她自己的話？還有，漂移之岩的事，她既然自己承認所知不足，為什麼不叮囑奧德修斯特別記得向忒瑞西阿斯打聽？奧德修斯看來什麼也沒說，但他心裡想必想了不少。年輕人對這種小節是沒有耐性的。說真的，如果每一筆、每一轉折都要受常識的提醒來干擾，誰還能讓想像、*naiveté*（天真）與自然流露的魅力自由而優雅地發揮呢？年輕人太過輕蔑地鄙夷精確；而年紀大的人則往往除了精確什麼也不想。
> It being evident that Circe is quite as capable a prophet as Tiresias, why should poor Ulysses be sent down to Hades? Obviously because the writer had set her heart on introducing colloquies with the dead. Granted; but a writer who was less desirous of making out that women know as much as men would not have made Circe know quite so much. Why, as soon as Ulysses has returned from Hades, repeat to him the warning about the cattle of the Sun which Tiresias had given him in the same words, and add a great deal more of her own? Why, again, did she not tell Ulysses to be particularly careful to ask Tiresias about the Wandering Cliffs, in respect of which she had confessed that her information was deficient? Ulysses does not appear to have said anything, but he must have thought a good deal. Young people are impatient of such small considerations. Who, indeed, can let fancy, *naiveté*, and the charm of spontaneity have free and graceful play, if he or she is to be troubled at every touch and turn by the suggestions of common sense? The young disdain precision too contemptuously; while older people are apt to think of nothing else.

〔U0836 para〕
同樣抬高女性能耐的心思，也表現在讓赫利俄斯把他的綿羊和牛群全權交給蘭佩提亞與法厄圖薩兩位寧芙看管（卷十二 132 行）；順帶一提，她們後來證明完全保護不了那些牲口。不過話說回來，赫利俄斯是個男的，什麼蠢事都做得出來。
> The same desire to exalt the capabilities of woman appears in making the Sun leave his sheep and cattle in the sole charge of the two nymphs Lampetie and Phaëthusa (xii. 132) who, by the way, proved quite unable to protect them. But then the Sun was a man, and capable of any folly.

〔U0837 para〕
把奧德修斯比作一個飢腸轆轆的法官（卷十二 439、440 行），顯然帶有幽默意味，但對如此驚心動魄的情境而言，這既不是男人會用的比喻，也不是主婦會用的。在我看來，這暗示出自某位法官女兒之手：她常常看見父親因為在法庭上被耽擱，回到家時又累又暴躁。
> The comparison of Ulysses to a hungry magistrate (xii. 439, 440), which is obviously humorous, is neither a man's nor a matron's simile for such a thrilling situation. To me it suggests the hand of a magistrate's daughter who had often seen her father come home tired and cross at having been detained in court.

〔U0838 para〕
海倫送忒勒馬科斯一件新娘禮服當禮物（卷十五 125–129 行），這種事比較可能是年輕女子想得到的，而不是男人。我也認為，男作家會送點東西給可憐的佩西斯特拉托斯，他從頭到尾都那麼好心、那麼和善。看不出忒勒馬科斯給過埃特俄紐斯或墨涅拉俄斯的其他僕人任何小費，儘管從卷二十 296、297 行可以明白看出，客人送點東西給借住人家的僕人，是相當平常的事。他不去向涅斯托爾辭行，十分失禮（卷十五 199–201 行）；而且他也始終沒有照禮數向佩西斯特拉托斯道別。
> The present from Helen to Telemachus of a wedding dress (xv. 125-129) was more likely to occur to a young woman than to a man. I think also that a male writer would have given something to poor Pisistratus, who has been very good and amiable all through. It does not appear that Telemachus tipped Eteoneus or any other of Menelaus' servants, though from xx. 296, 297 it is plain that it was quite usual for visitors to give something to the servants of a house at which they were staying. He is very rude about not saying good-bye to Nestor (xv. 199-201), and he never says good-bye to Pisistratus as he ought.

〔U0839 para〕
再說奧德修斯，他對喀耳刻或卡呂普索似乎毫無感恩之意。他心裡只有一個念頭：盡量多拿，盡量少給。所以，他在冥府裡開口問的，不是潘妮洛碧好不好，而是她守不守本分，有沒有在保護他的家產（卷十一 177 行以下）。
> Ulysses, again, seems to have no sense of obligation whatever to Circe or Calypso. He has no other idea than that of taking as much and giving as little as he can. So, in Hades he does not begin by asking how Penelope is, but how she is behaving, and whether she is protecting his estate (xi. 177, &c.).

〔U0840 para〕
卷十七 495 行，那位一直以來都叫歐律克蕾亞的老奶媽兼管家，忽然變成了歐律諾墨──這個名字我們先前從未見過。從此以後，歐律諾墨就經常出現，儘管上下文總是暗示、有時甚至迫使我們相信，指的其實是歐律克蕾亞。舉例來說，卷二十 4 行告訴我們，奧德修斯躺下休息後，是歐律諾墨給他蓋上了一件斗篷；可是同一卷第 143 行，歐律克蕾亞卻說斗篷是她自己給他蓋的──這裡想必是這個意思，雖然依照非常普遍的慣例，用的是複數而不是單數。兩個名字交替出現，愈來愈令人摸不著頭腦，直到最後在卷二十三 289–293 行，歐律諾墨和歐律克蕾亞同時登場，這算是把難題勉強補綴起來，卻補得並不高明──因為那裡該做的事，一個女人就足以應付了。
> In Book xvii. 495 the old nurse and housekeeper, who has hitherto always been Euryclea, suddenly becomes Eurynome, a name which we have not yet had. Eurynome from this point is frequently mentioned, though the context always suggests, and sometimes compels, the belief that Euryclea is intended. In Book xx. 4, for example, we are told that Eurynome threw a cloak over Ulysses after he had lain down to rest, but in line 143 of the same Book, Euryclea says she threw the cloak over him herself—for surely this is intended, though the plural according to very common custom is used instead of the singular. The alternation of the two names becomes very baffling, till finally in Book xxiii. 289-293 both Eurynome and Euryclea appear on the scene together, which cobbles the difficulty, but does not make a good job of it—for one woman would have been quite enough to do all that there was to do.

〔U0841 para〕
依我看，事情是這樣的。在我們初次遇見歐律諾墨的那一行，「歐律克蕾亞」這個名字不容易排進格律，作者心想以後再改，就寫下了歐律諾墨。既然用過一次，她便看格律方便，時而用歐律諾墨，時而用歐律克蕾亞。這樣持續了一陣子，到後來她發現，要把歐律諾墨出現過的段落全部改寫，實在太費事了；於是她決定硬著頭皮撐下去，假裝她從頭到尾就打算讓歐律克蕾亞和歐律諾墨是兩個人。為了讓兩人各自存在這件事無可置疑，她就讓兩人一起登場。我不是說這有什麼女性特質，但在《伊里亞德》裡我找不到類似的例子。我有時想，最後六、七卷雖然包含了全詩一些最精妙的段落，卻比前面各卷寫得匆忙；而卷二十四最後一百行左右，則顯示作者決心把作品收尾，至於怎麼收尾，就不太在乎了。我也曾納悶，卷六裡那位尚待覓得的丈夫，會不會在卷二十四寫成之前就已經覓得了；不過，這番臆測我提不出任何佐證。
> What happened, I take it, was this. In the first line where we meet with Eurynome, the name Euryclea could not be made to scan very easily, and the writer, thinking she would alter it later, wrote Eurynome. Having done so once, she used the names Eurynome and Euryclea according as metrical convenience inspired her. This went on for some time, till in the end she found it would be a great deal of trouble to re-write all the passages in which Eurynome had appeared; she therefore determined to brazen it out, and pretend that she had all along meant Euryclea and Eurynome to be two people. To put their separate existence beyond question, she brings them both on together. I do not say that this is feminine, but I can find nothing like it in the *Iliad*. I have sometimes thought the last six or seven Books, though they contain some of the most exquisite passages in the whole poem, were written in greater haste than the earlier ones, while the last hundred lines or so of Book xxiv. suggest that the writer was determined to end her work without much caring how. I have also wondered whether the husband who in Book vi. was yet to find may not have been found before Book xxiv. was written; but I have nothing to urge in support of this speculation.

〔U0842 para〕
阿爾戈斯（卷十七 292 行）不算是給狗取的好名字。這個字是《伊里亞德》與《奧德賽》中形容獵犬的固定修飾語，意思是「迅捷的」。奧德修斯與阿爾戈斯之間的整場戲，或許是《奧德賽》裡最令人失望的一段。那條狗若是太老太弱，走不到奧德修斯身邊，奧德修斯就該走過去抱住牠──管牠有沒有跳蚤；而在這一幕清楚呈現之前，不該讓阿爾戈斯死去。不錯，奧德修斯確實拭去了一滴眼淚，但他應該徹底崩潰才對──他卻還有心思去問歐邁俄斯，阿爾戈斯究竟中不中用，還是只是一條擺著好看的狗──這就算當作演戲也說不過去。這場戲構想得好，寫得卻差；它洩露了作者性情中較冷硬的一面，荷馬會注入其中的那種悲憫之情，在這裡幾乎看不到。
> Argus (xvii. 292) is not a very good name for a dog. It is the stock epithet for hounds in both *Iliad* and *Odyssey*, and means "fleet." The whole scene between Ulysses and Argus is perhaps the most disappointing in the *Odyssey*. If the dog was too old or feeble to come to Ulysses, Ulysses should have gone up to him and hugged him—fleas or no fleas; and Argus should not have been allowed to die till this had been put in evidence. True, Ulysses does wipe away one tear, but he should have broken utterly down—and then to ask Eumæus whether Argus was any use, or whether he was only a show dog—this will not do even as acting. The scene is well conceived but badly executed; it betrays the harder side of the writer's nature, and has little of the pathos which Homer would have infused into it.

〔U0843 para〕
歐邁俄斯說起，假如由他自由選擇，他會請哪種人到家裡來時，他把神職之士排在第一，醫生其次，然後是木匠，再來才是吟遊詩人（卷十七 384 行）。唯一奇怪的是，作者沒有把吟遊詩人排在木匠前面；而她若不是想讓吟遊詩人獨佔一整行，無疑會這麼排的。今天寫作的女人，很容易會認為牧師和醫生是最該邀請的人；但荷馬時代的男人，就不太可能像歐邁俄斯被寫成的那樣挑選了。
> When Eumæus says what kind of man he would be likely to ask to the house if he was free to choose, he puts a divine first, a physician next, then a carpenter, and then a bard (xvii. 384). The only wonder is that the writer did not put the bard before the carpenter, and doubtless she would have done so had she not wanted to give the bard a whole line to himself. A woman, writing at the present day would be apt to consider the clergyman, and the doctor, as the first people who should be invited, but a man in the Homeric age would hardly have chosen as Eumæus is made to do.

〔U0844 para〕
我不相信世上有哪個男人，能像歐律克蕾亞那樣（卷十九 386 行以下）給奧德修斯洗腳、又把腳盆打翻得那麼妙趣橫生，同時還讓潘妮洛碧坐在一旁，對發生的事一無所察。他不會有那份膽量，說是雅典娜把潘妮洛碧的注意力引到別處去了──儘管奧德修斯的腿弄出了聲響，一整盆水也被打翻，水想必流遍了迴廊的那一帶。男人會讓潘妮洛碧在意外發生前一刻，忽然想離開迴廊，去躺在那張她始終以淚水浸濕的臥榻上云云；然後，等腳盆重新盛滿水、地上收拾乾淨之後，她才想起自己忘了什麼東西，再回到原處。但其實，他根本寫不出這一段。
> I do not believe that any man living could wash Ulysses' feet and upset the bath so delightfully as Euryclea does (xix. 386, &c.), and at the same time make Penelope sit by and observe nothing of what was going on. He could not rise to the audacity of saying that Minerva had directed Penelope's attention elsewhere, notwithstanding the noise which Ulysses' leg made, and the upsetting of a bath full of water, which must have run over all that part of the cloister. A man would have made Penelope desire suddenly to leave the cloister, just before the accident happened, and lie down upon that couch which she had never ceased to water with her tears, &c.; she could then have come back, remembering that she had forgotten something, after the foot-bath had been refilled and the mess cleaned up. But he could not have done it at all.

〔U0845 para〕
讀者會注意到，奧德修斯即將歸來的跡象愈是明顯，潘妮洛碧就愈覺得非得一天也不耽擱地嫁給某個求婚者不可。她聽說了老鷹撕裂鴿子的事；她聽見忒勒馬科斯打了噴嚏；有人向她保證奧德修斯就在忒斯普洛提亞人那裡，近在咫尺，馬上就會回到伊薩卡；她還做了一個夢，這個夢足以讓任何人再等上，比方說，至少一個星期，除非這人存心要把局面看得要多悲觀就有多悲觀；可是不行，第二天她就非嫁人、離開這個家不可。在我看來，她的話就像一個女人沉溺於悲苦的奢侈享受，而描寫她的，是另一個從未嘗過真正苦難的女人。再沒有什麼比這更能顯出潘妮洛碧的愁苦從頭到尾都是空洞的了。一個真覺得自己快要淹死的女人，會抓住上面提到的任何一根稻草，靠它撐上幾個星期甚至幾個月；而任何嘗過真正悲傷的作者，也會知道她必然會這麼做。男人若這樣描寫他的女主角，只能是在暗中笑她；而《奧德賽》的作者卻是竭盡全力要嚴肅看待她自己。
> It will be observed that the stronger the indications become that Ulysses is on the point of returning, the more imperative Penelope finds it to marry one of the suitors without a day's delay. She has heard about the hawk tearing the dove; she has heard Telemachus sneeze; she has been assured that Ulysses was among the Thesprotians, quite near, and would be in Ithaca immediately; she has had a dream which would have made any one wait, say, for at least a week longer, unless determined to take the gloomiest possible view of the situation; but no; on the following day she must marry and leave the house. Her words seem to me like those of a woman gloating over the luxury of woe, as drawn by another woman who has never known real trouble. Nothing can better show the hollowness of Penelope's distress from first to last. A woman who felt herself really drowning would have clutched at any one of the straws above mentioned, and made it buoy her up for weeks or months; and any writer who had known real sorrow would also know how certain she would be to do this. A man could only so draw his heroine if he was laughing at her in his sleeve; whereas the writer of the *Odyssey* is doing her very utmost to take herself seriously.

〔U0846 para〕
潘妮洛碧似乎堅信，她一直把兒子的家產守護得好好的，要是她離開這個家，一切都會敗落崩毀。奧德修斯喬裝成乞丐時，她就向他暗示了這一點（卷十九 524 行）。真不知道奧德修斯怎麼忍得住不說：「哎，夫人，你這個守護人若不能比過去這許多年做得更成功，那你愈早離開這個家，對忒勒馬科斯就愈好。」
> Penelope seems firmly convinced that she is keeping excellent guard over her son's estate all the time, and that if she were to leave the house everything would go to rack and ruin. She implies this to Ulysses when he is disguised as a beggar (xix. 524). One wonders how Ulysses could restrain himself from saying, "Well, Madam, if you cannot prove more successful as a guardian than you have been doing this many years past, the sooner you leave the house the better for Telemachus."

〔U0847 para〕
沒有哪位偉大的詩人會把他的英雄比作一個裝滿血和脂肪、正在火前烤著的肚囊（卷二十 24–28 行）。這種幽默──因為它當然是存心幽默──不是男人的幽默，除非他寫的是滑稽諷仿之作。而《奧德賽》的作者在這裡並不是在寫諷仿，儘管在費埃克斯那段插曲的大部分篇幅裡，她都有意寫得非常接近諷仿。
> No great poet would compare his hero to a paunch full of blood and fat, cooking before the fire (xx. 24-28). The humour, for of course it is humorously intended, is not man's humour, unless he is writing burlesque. This the writer of the *Odyssey* is not doing here, though she has intentionally approached it very nearly in a great part of the Phæacian episode.

〔U0848 para〕
卷二十裡另外只有兩處透露出女性的手筆──我是說特別有力地透露──一是作者對那個完不成工作的可憐虛弱女人流露的同情（105 行以下），一是忒勒馬科斯說他母親太容易抬舉二流人物的那段話（129–133 行）。
> The only other two points which suggest a female hand in Book xx.—I mean with especial force—are the sympathy which the writer betrays with the poor weakly woman who could not finish her task (105, &c.), and the speech of Telemachus about his mother being too apt to make much of second rate people (129-133).

〔U0849 para〕
卷二十一裡立起的十二把斧頭，在求婚者遭殺戮的整段時間裡一直留在庭院中。我真納悶，怎麼沒有一個求婚者撿起一把斧頭？十二個人拿著十二把斧頭，早該把奧德修斯和他的人收拾掉了。不錯，依照我自己的假說，斧頭已經從斧柄上卸下來了，但它們一定被楔牢或綁緊在斧柄上，或者綁在別的類似木頭上，好把它們墊得夠高，讓人能從柄孔中射箭穿過。要把斧頭重新裝回柄上，或者臨時湊出新的柄，應該是很容易的事。作者若不是急著要開始射殺、把斧頭忘得一乾二淨，她就會捏造出某種困難來。也許她以為聽眾既然不再聽到斧頭的事，也會把斧頭忘得一乾二淨──而她想得也不算大錯。
> The twelve axes set up in Book xxi. remain in the court during the whole time that the suitors are being killed. How, I wonder, is it that not one of the suitors picked up a single axe? A dozen men with a dozen axes should have made short work of Ulysses and his men. True, by my own hypothesis the heads had been taken off the handles, but they must have been wedged, or bound, either on to the handles or to some other like pieces of wood, so as to raise them high enough for any one to shoot through the handle-holes. It should have been an easy matter either to fix the heads on to the handles again, or to extemporise new ones. If the writer had not forgotten all about the axes in her desire to begin with the shooting, she would have trumped up a difficulty of some kind. Perhaps she thought that the audience, hearing nothing more about them, would forget all about the axes too—and she was not far wrong.

〔U0850 para〕
作者出於本能的持家節儉，表現得最明顯的，莫過於卷二十二開頭附近：在安提諾俄斯和歐律馬科斯垂死掙扎之際，她仍念念不忘那些好肉好酒，因求婚者坐的桌子被打翻而糟蹋了。
> The instinctive house-wifely thrift of the writer is nowhere more marked than near the beginning of Book xxii., where amid the death-throes of Antinous and Eurymachus she cannot forget the good meat and wine that were spoiled by the upsetting of the tables at which the suitors had been sitting.

〔U0851 para〕
求婚者遭殺戮這一段，不合情理到了咄咄逼人的地步。墨蘭提俄斯既然能到庫房去──不管他是怎麼去的──其他求婚者也能跟著他去，從背後攻擊奧德修斯；因為顯然有一條通道，從庫房通到奧德修斯站立的地方。
> The killing of the suitors is aggressive in its want of plausibility. If Melanthius could go to the store-room, no matter how, the other suitors could have followed him and attacked Ulysses from behind; for there is evidently a passage from the store-room to the place where Ulysses is standing.

〔U0852 para〕
再者，外院自始至終都對求婚者敞開著。他們手邊既然還有斧頭可用，想必能砍斷那條紙莎草纜繩──那是大門唯一的門閂；至少有一些人可能逃得出去。這一卷的頭九十行和《伊里亞德》一樣精彩，但從（比方說）第 100 行到第 330 行，作者已經力不從心，而且她自己也知道。最明顯帶著女性色彩的部分，是雅典娜化身門托爾來幫助奧德修斯的那一段（卷二十二 205–240 行）。求婚者威嚇她，她勃然大怒，罵的卻不是他們，而是奧德修斯，把他狠狠數落了一頓。罵完之後，她就飛走，像燕子一樣停在一根椽木上。
> Again, the outer yard was open to the suitors all the time. Surely with the axes still at command they could have cut the Byblus-fibre rope that was the only fastening of the main gate; some of them at any rate might have got out. The first ninety lines of the Book are as fine as the *Iliad*, but from line (say) 100 to line 330 the writer is out of her depth, and knows it. The most palpably feminine part is where Minerva comes to help Ulysses disguised as Mentor (xxii. 205-240). The suitors menace her, and in a rage she scolds not them but Ulysses, whom she rates roundly. Having done this, she flies away and sits on a rafter like a swallow.

〔U0853 para〕
所有讀者都願意幫詩人、劇作家和小說家的忙，在相當程度上配合著信以為真；但我們總想知道，自己落在什麼樣的人手裡：是會鞭策我們爬坡的，還是會盡量少要求我們的。卷二十二的這一部分，作者就是在鞭策我們爬坡。讀者為了相信她而費盡力氣、苦不堪言，她毫不在意──她真正在意的只有她的報復。她非要每一個求婚者都死得透透的，所有有罪的女人都吊死，墨蘭提俄斯先受盡酷刑再被大卸八塊不可。只要達到這些目的，讀者能不能相信、甚至能不能跟上通往這些結果的種種來龍去脈，都無關緊要。
> All readers will help poets, playwrights, and novelists, by making believe a good deal, but we like to know whether we are in the hands of one who will flog us uphill, or who will make as little demand upon us as possible. In this portion of Book xxii. the writer is flogging us uphill. She does not care how much she may afflict the reader in his efforts to believe her—the only thing she really cares for is her revenge. She must have every one of the suitors killed stone dead, and all the guilty women hanged, and Melanthius first horribly tortured and then cut in pieces. Provided these objects are attained, it is not necessary that the reader should be able to believe, or even follow, all the ins and outs of the processes that lead up to them.

〔U0854 para〕
因此，求婚者遭殺戮一段中多不勝數的荒謬之處，我就不再追究了。不過，我倒要指出：卷十六 281 行以下，初次提出把武器從迴廊牆上撤走時，曾提議留下足夠奧德修斯和忒勒馬科斯武裝的分量，放在拿得到的地方，好讓他們一把就能抓起，而不必一路下到庫房去拿，還冒著忒勒馬科斯忘記關門的風險──年輕人就是常常這樣。我想，到了卷十九開頭，奧德修斯和忒勒馬科斯藏匿武器時，奧德修斯把這個明智的預防措施忘得一乾二淨了。或者，我們該這樣設想：女詩人寫卷十六時，還沒想到要在庫房裡逮住墨蘭提俄斯，但在寫到卷十九之前，這個點子已經浮現在她腦中，而她不是忘了，就是認為不值得，或是覺得不方便，總之沒有刪掉卷十六 295、296 行？就我對這位女作者的認識，我傾向於最後這個看法，並且認為：她之所以讓奧德修斯沒給自己和忒勒馬科斯留下一點拿得到的武器，是因為她此時已經決定要在庫房裡把墨蘭提俄斯吊起來；而她想不出怎樣讓他進到庫房裡，除非先讓忒勒馬科斯去那裡、把門開著不關；再者，她要是在近處留下武器讓他隨手抓起，又想不出怎樣讓忒勒馬科斯下到庫房去。
> I will therefore not pursue the absurdities with which the killing of the suitors abounds. I would, however, point out that in Book xvi. 281, &c., where the taking away of the armour from the cloister walls was first mooted, it was proposed that enough to arm Ulysses and Telemachus should be left accessible, so that they might snatch it up in a moment without having to go all the way down into the store-room after it, at the risk of Telemachus's forgetting to shut the door—as young people so often do. I suppose Ulysses forgot all about this sensible precaution, when he and Telemachus were hiding the armour at the beginning of Book xix. Or shall we suppose that the idea of catching Melanthius in the store-room had not occurred to the poetess when she was writing Book xvi., but had struck her before she reached Book xix., and that she either forgot, or did not think it worth while, or found it inconvenient, to cancel lines 295, 296 of Book xvi.? From what I have seen of the authoress I incline to this last opinion, and hold that she made Ulysses omit to leave a little of the armour accessible to himself and Telemachus, because she had by this time determined to string Melanthius up in the store-room, and did not see how to get him inside it unless she made Telemachus go there first and leave the door open; and, again, did not see how to get Telemachus down to the store-room if she left armour near at hand, for him to snatch up.

〔U0855 para〕
至於忒勒馬科斯拿上來四頂頭盔、四面盾牌、八支長矛，他在打鬥開始時就已經全副武裝了（卷二十一 434 行），所以三頂頭盔、三面盾牌和六支長矛就夠了。四頂頭盔、四面盾牌、八支長矛，已經是很重的負擔；可是墨蘭提俄斯扛了十二面盾牌、十二頂頭盔和十二支長矛，而且看來是一趟全扛上來的。
> As for Telemachus bringing up four helmets, four shields, eight spears, he was already fully armed when the fight began (xxi. 434), so three helmets, three shields and six spears should have done. Four helmets, four shields, and eight spears is a heavy load; but Melanthius carried twelve shields, twelve helmets, and twelve spears apparently all at one time.

〔U0856 para〕
我們置身於倫敦泰晤士河南岸劇院那種通俗煽情劇的氛圍裡；但唯一奇怪的是，這些荒謬之處竟然沒有比現在更離譜──想想看，作者可是一個意志堅強、極有主見的年輕女子。她必定是女人；沒有哪個男作家抵擋得住殺掉歐邁俄斯的誘惑。在這種場合身受致命重傷，本來就是忠僕的角色。剩下要殺的求婚者已經沒有幾個，雅典娜又馬上就要舉起她的神盾，所以完全可以讓他活下來；也許作者覺得，清洗血跡、搬運屍體時會人手不足，但更可能的是，她對求婚者恨之入骨，連一分都不肯讓他們得。
> We are in an atmosphere of transpontine melodrama, but the only wonder is that the absurdities are not even grosser than they are, seeing that the writer was a young woman with a strong will of her own. Woman she must have been; no male writer could have resisted the temptation to kill Eumæus. It is the faithful servant's rôle to be mortally wounded on occasions of this sort. There are very few more suitors to be killed, and Minerva is going to raise her ægis immediately, so that he could be perfectly well spared; possibly the writer felt that she should be shorthanded with the cleaning up of the blood and the removal of the dead bodies, but more probably she hated the suitors so bitterly that she would not let them score a single point.

〔U0857 para〕
等她把殺戮寫完，能回到她擅長的領域──例如饒過費彌俄斯和墨東、把屋子打掃乾淨──她顯然多麼如釋重負啊。
> How evidently relieved she feels when she has got the killing over, and can return to ground on which she is strong, such as the saving of Phemius and Medon, and the cleaning down of the house.

〔U0858 para〕
讓潘妮洛碧──她的房間正對著迴廊──在整場殺戮求婚者的過程中一直酣睡，我們該怎麼說呢？她被叫醒後對歐律克蕾亞說的那些話，又該怎麼說？還有她和奧德修斯的會面，以及奧德修斯隨即建議的那場舞蹈，又該怎麼說？說真的，整卷又該怎麼說？出自我卷首插圖所畫的那樣一個人之手，這一切當然再恰當不過；可是誰能想像，什麼樣的男人或主婦寫得出這樣的東西？卷二十四也是如此。哪個男人或中年婦女，寫得出奧德修斯與拉厄爾特斯在果園裡那場美得無法言喻的戲？或者讓奧德修斯和多利俄斯一起吃飯──而前一天他才殺了多利俄斯的兒子和女兒？男人一定會讓奧德修斯告訴多利俄斯，他非常抱歉，但除了吊死他的女兒，割掉他兒子的鼻子和耳朵、掏出他的內臟、再砍掉他的手腳之外，實在別無他法。不過，更可能的是，男人會乾脆讓多利俄斯和他的兒子們根本不出現在這一卷裡。
> What are we to say of making Penelope, whose room looked out upon the cloister, sleep soundly all through the killing of the suitors? What of her remarks to Euryclea when she has been waked? What, again, of her interview with Ulysses, and the dance which Ulysses presently advises? what, indeed, of the whole Book? Surely it is all perfectly right as coining from some such person as the one portrayed in my frontispiece, but who can conceive the kind of man or matron who could write it? The same applies to Book xxiv. What man or middle-aged woman could have written the ineffably lovely scene between Ulysses and Laertes in the garden? or have made Ulysses eat along with Dolius, whose son and daughter he had killed on the preceding day? A man would have been certain to make Ulysses tell Dolius that he was very sorry, but there had been nothing for it but to hang his daughter and to cut his son's nose and ears off, draw out his vitals, and then cut off his hands and feet. Probably, however, he would have kept Dolius and his sons out of the Book altogether.

〔U0859 para〕
奧德修斯與潘妮洛碧上床之後（卷二十三 300–343 行），互相訴說各自的經歷時，是潘妮洛碧先說。我相信，男作家會讓奧德修斯的故事先講，潘妮洛碧的放在後頭。
> When Ulysses and Penelope are in bed (xxiii. 300-343) and are telling their stories to one another, Penelope tells hers first. I believe a male writer would have made Ulysses' story come first and Penelope's second.

〔U0860 footnote〕
見第十章。
> See Chapter X.

〔U0861 heading〕
第八章
> CHAPTER VIII.

〔U0862 para〕
伊薩卡與斯刻里亞都取材自特拉帕尼及其近郊
> THAT ITHACA AND SCHERIA ARE BOTH OF THEM DRAWN FROM TRAPANI AND ITS IMMEDIATE NEIGHBOURHOOD.

〔U0863 para〕
以上我已陳述了一些理由，說明我何以相信，從《奧德賽》第一卷到最後一卷，我們都是在一位年輕女子的手中──雖然比起這個題目所需要的篇幅，我講得簡略太多了。那麼，她是誰？她住在哪裡、在哪裡寫作？她是有血有肉的人，活在某個時間、某個地方，看過海，看過天，在某處某時來來去去──可是在哪裡？在什麼時候？而最要緊的，她是誰？接下來幾章，我的目標就是盡我所能，替這些問題投下一點光。
> I have now given, though far more briefly than the subject requires, some of my reasons for believing that from the first Book of the *Odyssey* to the last we are in the hands of a young woman. Who, then, was she? Where did she live and write? She was of flesh and blood, lived in time and place, looked on sea and sky, came and went somewhither and somewhen—but where? and when? and above all, who? It will be my object to throw what light I can upon these subjects in the following chapters.

〔U0864 para〕
我會沿用前面的做法，把自己一步步走到這些結論的路徑重走一遍。
> I will follow the same course that I have taken earlier, and retrace the steps whereby I was led to my conclusions.

〔U0865 para〕
讀完第十卷時，我已確信《奧德賽》不是男人的作品，但還沒看到任何跡象，讓我覺得它是在某個地方而非別的地方寫成的。然而，讀到卷十三 159–164 行，波賽頓把費埃克斯人的船變成斯刻里亞港口入口處的一塊岩石，我便深信作者是照著一處實有的地貌在寫，於是記下一筆：任何地方，不管它多麼恰好位於兩個港口之間，若不是其中一個港口盡頭有一塊半沒入水中的小岩礁，就不可能是斯刻里亞。不久，我著手思考：假定斯刻里亞是真實的地方，我應該去找哪些自然地貌的組合？我把它們列成下面這張清單：──
> By the time I had finished Book x. I was satisfied that the *Odyssey* was not a man's work, but I had seen nothing to make me think that it was written rather at one place than at another. When, however, I reached xiii. 159-164, in which passage Neptune turns the Phæacian ship into a rock at the entrance of the Scherian harbour, I felt sure that an actual feature was being drawn from, and made a note that no place, however much it might lie between two harbours, would do for Scheria, unless at the end of one of them there was a small half sunken rock. Presently I set myself to consider what combination of natural features I ought to look for on the supposition that Scheria was a real place, and made a list of them as follows:—

〔U0866 para〕
一、城鎮必須坐落在一片伸入海中、宛如天涯盡頭的岬角上，兩側各有一個港口，或可供船隻停泊的海灣（卷六 263 行）。它必須以一條狹窄的地峽與大陸相連。而且，如我剛才所說，其中一個港口的入口處必須有一塊半沒入水中、令人生畏的岩礁。
> 1. The town must be placed on a point of land jutting out as a land's end into the sea between two harbours, or bays in which ships could ride (vi. 263); it must be connected with the mainland by a narrow neck of land, and as I have just said, must have a half sunken formidable rock at the entrance of one of the harbours.

〔U0867 para〕
二、兩個港口都不能有河流注入，否則瑙西卡就不必走那麼遠去洗衣服了。走到那條「河」時，它也許不過是一片潟湖，有一兩道淡水泉流入其中。因為那些衣服看來並不是在河裡洗的，而是在公共的洗衣池裡洗的（《奧德賽》卷六 40、86、92 行），一道小泉就足以讓池子保持水量充足，「即使衣服很髒也洗得乾淨」。場景就設在緊鄰海岸處，因為衣服是攤在海浪堆起的高高礫石灘上晾乾的；而瑙西卡的侍女們躲避奧德修斯時，也是沿著海灘和伸入海中的沙嘴四散奔逃。
> 2. There must be no river running into either harbour, or Nausicaa would not have had to go so far to wash her clothes. The river when reached might be nothing but a lagoon with a spring or two of fresh water running into it, for the clothes were not, so it would seem, washed in a river; they were washed in public washing cisterns (*Od*. vi. 40, 86, 92) which a small spring would keep full enough of water "to wash clothes even though they were very dirty." The scene is laid close on the sea shore, for the clothes are put out to dry on a high bank of shingle which the sea had raised, and Nausicaa's maidens fly from Ulysses along the beach and spits that run into the sea.

〔U0868 para〕
三、離城不遠處必須有一座顯眼的高山，波賽頓揚言要用一座高山把城埋掉，這威脅才有著落。此外，上面描述的整個組合，它在埃維亞島以西的距離，必須遠遠超過伊薩卡，因而也遠在伊薩卡以西（卷七 321 行）。說真的，如果作者是照著一個真實的地方在寫，這些線索綽綽有餘，足以讓人輕易找到它。
> 3. There must be a notable mountain at no great distance from the town so as to give point to Neptune's threat that he would bury it under a high mountain. Furthermore, the whole combination above described must lie greatly further west of Eubœa than Ithaca was, and hence greatly west of Ithaca (vii. 321). Surely, if a real place is being drawn from, these indications are ample to ensure its being easily found.

〔U0869 para〕
