就我所觀察，科學界人士生怕倉促下結論，往往忘了世上還有一種事叫做倉促避開結論；荷馬學者們在這方面似乎也向他們取了經。我不禁要問：一個真實的地方與一部小說裡描寫的地方之間，要有多少處醒目的吻合，才足以讓人合理地確信，集這些特徵於一身的那個地方，就是小說取材的地方？依我說，四處鮮明的吻合，就足以使人極難在別處找到同樣的組合；若是五處，那麼除非我們找到什麼理由，足以抵銷真實地方與小說所描寫的地方如此密切的吻合所引出的推論，或者除非能證明，由於某種離奇的巧合，同樣的組合連同一切細節，也出現在另一個更說得通的地點，否則我們就可以確定，那部小說是取材自那個地方；因為所要求的組合中，每多一項細節，只要它確實符合，出錯的機率就以幾何級數遞減。
> Men of science, so far as I have observed them, are apt in their fear of jumping to a conclusion to forget that there is such a thing as jumping away from one, and Homeric scholars seem to have taken a leaf out of their book in this respect. How many striking points of correspondence, I wonder, between an actual place and one described in a novel, would be enough to create a reasonable assurance that the place in which they were combined was the one that was drawn from? I should say four well marked ones would be sufficient to make it extremely improbable that a like combination could be found elsewhere; make it five, and unless we find something to outweigh the considerations which so close a correspondence between the actual place and the one described in the novel would suggest, or unless by some strange coincidence the same combination in all its details can be shown to occur in some other and more probable locality, we may be sure that the novel was drawn from the place; for every fresh detail in the combination required decreases the probability of error in geometrical ratio if it be duly complied with.

〔U0870 para〕
假設有個警察奉命留意一位約六十歲的老先生。這人是外國人，會講一點英語但不多，左腳跛，藍眼睛，酒糟鼻，身高約五英尺十英寸。這些特徵，警察要湊齊幾項，才會頗有把握自己找對了人？只要看到外國人，他就會多看兩眼。看到一個身高約五英尺十英寸的外國人，他會留意對方的年紀；如果年紀看來約莫六十，而且這人左腳一跛一跛的，他大概就會覺得把人攔下來並無不妥。等他發現──他一定會發現──這人還有個酒糟鼻，他就不必再管什麼藍眼睛、破英語了，直接把人帶到治安法官面前。
> Let us suppose that a policeman is told to look out for an elderly gentleman of about sixty; he is a foreigner, speaks a little English but not much, is lame in his left foot, has blue eyes, a bottle nose, and is about 5 ft. 10 in. high. How many of these features will the policeman require before he feels pretty sure that he has found his man? If he sees any foreigner he will look at him. If he sees one who is about 5 ft. 10 in. high he will note his age, if this proves to be about sixty years, and further, if the man limps on his left foot, he will probably feel safe in stopping him. If, as he is sure to do, he finds he has a bottle nose, he will leave the blue eyes and broken English alone, and will bring the man before the magistrate.

〔U0871 para〕
假如隨後發現這人的眼睛是淡褐色的，而且他不是英語講得很流利，就是根本不會講──那麼，既然所要求的其他特徵這麼多項都對得上，治安法官會因為此人與通緝描述之間這幾處小小出入就把他放了嗎？他難道不會反過來要求被告拿出極有說服力的證據，證明這是認錯了人？
> If it is then found that the man's eyes are hazel, and that he either speaks English fluently or does not speak it at all—is the magistrate likely to discharge the prisoner on account of these small discrepancies between him and the description given of him, when so many other of the required characteristics are found present? Will he not rather require the prisoner to bring forward very convincing proof that it is a case of mistaken identity?

〔U0872 para〕
再舉一個例子，也許更切中要點，因為它牽涉到真實地方與小說所描寫的地方之間的比較。下面是一段小說摘錄：──
> Or to take another illustration, which is perhaps more strictly to the point as involving comparison between an actual place and one described in a novel. Here is an extract from a novel:—

〔U0873 quote〕
格拉默頓和其他美麗的城市一樣，建在一座山丘上。最高處是那所古老典雅的伊莉莎白時代學校，當時、乃至今日，都享譽全歐。學校對面是那座殘破頹圮的古堡，俯瞰著寬闊湍急的薩伯河那翻騰沸湧的淺灘。……城鎮從山丘向四面八方陡然傾斜而下，直抵河邊。河水把它圍成一座半島，上面星羅棋布地散落著住家。（查爾斯．克拉克著《博克勒父子》，查普曼與霍爾出版，第一卷第 28 頁。）
> Grammerton, like other fair cities, was built on a hill. The highest point was the fine old Elizabethan School, then, and now, of European reputation. Opposite it was the old shattered and ruined castle, overlooking the bubbling and boiling shallows of the broad and rapid river Saber.... From the hill the town sloped rapidly down on every side towards the river, which made it a peninsula studded with habitations. (*The Beaucleres, father and son*, by Charles Clarke, Chapman and Hall. Vol. I. p. 28.)

〔U0874 para〕
凡是智力尋常、又熟悉什魯斯伯里的人，有誰會懷疑克拉克先生寫的就是什魯斯伯里？
> Is there any man of ordinary intelligence and acquainted with Shrewsbury who will doubt that Shrewsbury was the place that Mr. Clarke was drawing from?

〔U0875 para〕
我把《奧德賽》所描寫的斯刻里亞與至今猶在的特拉帕尼之間那些多得多、分量也重得多的吻合之處提出來時，傑出的荷馬學者們不止一次、兩次對我說──而且說得一點也不謙和，倒像是一副要把我壓垮的架勢──我的論證主要建立在海岸其他地方也並非沒有的地理特徵上，以及同樣屬於別處的傳說上。
> When I have urged the much more numerous and weightier points of agreement between Scheria as described in the *Odyssey*, and Trapani as it still exists, eminent Homeric scholars have told me, not once nor twice—and not meekly, but with an air as though they were crushing me—that my case rests in the main on geographical features that are not unknown to other parts of the coast, and upon legends which also belong to other places.

〔U0876 para〕
回到我那個例子，他們會這樣論證：格拉默頓不可認定為什魯斯伯里，因為哈羅和什魯斯伯里一樣，學校也在城裡地勢最高處。再說，伊頓也有一條河，所以伊頓很可能才是作者心目中的地方。設想薩伯這個名字不過是薩布麗娜在文學上的戲仿變形，實在異想天開。諾丁漢有座城堡，幾年前還是一片廢墟，從那裡也看得見特倫特河。所以，諾丁漢和什魯斯伯里一樣，都可能是格拉默頓的原型。
> Grammerton, they argue—to return to my illustration—must not be held as Shrewsbury, for at Harrow as well as Shrewsbury the School is on the highest part of the town. There is a river, again, at Eton, so that Eton may very well have been the place intended. It is highly fanciful to suppose that the name Saber may have been a mere literary travesty of Sabrina. At Nottingham there is a castle which was in ruins but a few years since, and from which one can see the Trent. Nottingham, therefore, is quite as likely to be the original of Grammerton as Shrewsbury is.

〔U0877 para〕
如此這般，沒完沒了（*ad infinitum*）。這種論證路線的毛病，在於無視對方論證的力量所在：對方證明了一個高度複雜的組合確實存在，其中各個組成項目，全都出現在同一處時威力強大，若不組合在一起，就毫無力量。傑出的荷馬學者們討論我的《奧德賽》理論時，幾乎無一例外都走這條路線；但這條路線無法說服另一群人──在他們面前，即使最傑出的荷馬學者，最終也得低頭──我指的是具有尋常常識的人。這些人會明白：要把格拉默頓從什魯斯伯里身上拉開，只有兩條路，要不證明什魯斯伯里的特徵其實與格拉默頓並不相符，要不證明英格蘭另有一座城鎮具備同樣的組合，而且在其他方面更令人信服。
> And so on *ad infinitum*. This line of argument consists in ignoring that the force of the one opposed to it lies in the demonstrable existence of a highly complex combination, the component items of which are potent when they are all found in the same place, but impotent unless combined. It is a line which eminent Homeric scholars almost invariably take when discussing my Odyssean theory, but it is not one which will satisfy those before whom even the most eminent of Homeric scholars must in the end bow—I mean, men of ordinary common sense. These last will know that Grammerton can only be dislodged from Shrewsbury on proof either that the features of Shrewsbury do not in reality correspond with those of Grammerton, or else that there is another town in England which offers the same combination, and is otherwise more acceptable.

〔U0878 para〕
特拉帕尼與斯刻里亞也是如此。傑出的荷馬學者們必須證明我誇大了兩地之間的吻合之處──面對海軍部海圖和《奧德賽》，他們恐怕不太敢這麼做；或者，他們必須另外提出一個地方，同樣集這些吻合之處於一身──這他們不會去嘗試；再不然，他們就必須拿出理由，讓人相信特拉帕尼與斯刻里亞、伊薩卡所有場景之間那些為數極多而又精確的吻合，都只能歸諸純粹的偶然──而這種說法，只能說服那些肯相信有人連續三局手上都握著十三張王牌、卻沒在牌上動過手腳的人。最後這種假定，我不必討論；至於其他兩種，我只能向讀者保證：自從我的理論首次公諸於世，將近六年來，從沒有人試圖證成其中任何一種。
> So with Trapani and Scheria. Eminent Homeric scholars must show that I have exaggerated the points of correspondence between the two places—which in the face of Admiralty charts and of the *Odyssey* they will hardly venture; or they must bring forward some other place in which the same points of correspondence are found combined—which they will not attempt; or they must show reason for thinking that the very numerous and precise correspondences between Trapani and all Scherian and Ithacan scenes are referable to mere accident—and this will satisfy those only who will believe that a man has held thirteen trumps in his hand three deals running, without having tampered with the cards. I need not discuss this last supposition, and as for the other two, I can only assure the reader that no attempt has been made to establish either of them during the close on six years since my theory was first put before the public.

〔U0879 para〕
將來也永遠不會有人去試。因為斯刻里亞應該到希臘以西的某道西岸上去找，而除了義大利和西西里的西岸，並沒有這樣的西岸；這兩處我都夠熟悉，足以確定，假如斯刻里亞的組合能在特拉帕尼以外的地方找到，我早就找到了。即使真能找到這樣一個地方，連同它的馬爾孔西利奧岩、傳說和一切都齊全，它要和特拉帕尼爭奪《奧德賽》的歸屬之前，也還得在斯刻里亞的組合之外，再拿出伊薩卡的組合；因為一個地方若能同時提供伊薩卡與斯刻里亞的地形，當然比只能提供斯刻里亞細節的地方，更有資格被視為《奧德賽》的取材之地。
> Neither will it ever be made. For Scheria should be looked for on some West coast to the West of Greece, and there are no such West coasts except those of Italy and Sicily, both of which I know well enough to be sure that if the Scherian combination could be found elsewhere than at Trapani I should long since have found it. Even could such a place be found with its rock Malconsiglio, legend and all, before it could compete with Trapani in claiming the *Odyssey* it would have to offer the Ithacan combination as well as the Scherian; for surely a place which provides us with both Ithacan and Scherian topography would have a greater right to be considered as that from which the *Odyssey* was drawn, than one which could only offer the details of Scheria.

〔U0880 para〕
再者，就算他們找得到另一個兼具斯刻里亞與伊薩卡全部特徵的地方，我的論敵們的麻煩也才解決了一半；因為特拉帕尼仍可與它分庭抗禮，除非那地方外海也有四座島嶼（不多也不少），其中一座又長又窄，而且全都與《奧德賽》對愛奧尼亞群島那不準確的描述相符。即使如此，它也還談不上與特拉帕尼平起平坐，直到有人證明，奧德修斯航程中真正要緊的部分是從那裡開始、又在那裡結束的。等這一切都做到了──但不能在那之前──才是權衡兩地孰是孰非的時候。
> Furthermore, could they find another place with all both Scherian and Ithacan features, my opponents would be only half way through their troubles; for Trapani could still hold its own against it, unless it also had four islands (neither more nor fewer) lying off it, one of them long and narrow, and all of them corresponding with the inaccurate Odyssean description of the Ionian islands. Nor would it even then begin to be on equal terms with Trapani, till it was shown that the effective part of the voyages of Ulysses begins and ends with it. When all this has been done, but not before, it will be time to weigh the comparative claims of the two sites.

〔U0881 para〕
因為我的論證，是建立在四條論證路線的和諧並進之上；每一條都要求滿足許多極其嚴格的條件，而每一條單獨拿出來，都足以有力地推定，特拉帕尼是《奧德賽》作者心中最突出的那個地方。這四條是：──
> For I rest my case on the harmonious concurrence of four lines of argument, each requiring the fulfilment of many and very rigorous conditions, and each by itself sufficient to raise a strong presumption that Trapani was the place which was most prominent in the mind of the writer of the *Odyssey*. They are:—

〔U0882 para〕
一、斯刻里亞取材自特拉帕尼。我將提出一組比格拉默頓與什魯斯伯里之間強得多的吻合之處，來證實這一點。
> 1. That Scheria is drawn from Trapani. This I will substantiate by bringing forward a much stronger combination of correspondences than exists between Grammerton and Shrewsbury.

〔U0883 para〕
二、伊薩卡也取材自特拉帕尼及其近郊。讀者會發現，我在這一點上的論證，若有可能，甚至比我用來證明斯刻里亞就是特拉帕尼的論證還要有力。
> 2. That Ithaca also is drawn from Trapani and its immediate neighbourhood. My case for this will be found even stronger if possible than that by which I established that Scheria was Trapani.

〔U0884 para〕
三、《奧德賽》所描述的愛奧尼亞群島，不可能取材自真正的愛奧尼亞群島，也不可能取材自特拉帕尼外海那幾座島以外的任何島嶼；而且作者為了把這些島硬塞進她的敘事，不惜違背自己所知的實情。
> 3. That the Ionian islands as described in the *Odyssey* cannot have been drawn from the actual Ionian islands, nor from any others but those off Trapani; and that the writer sinned against her own knowledge in order to force these islands into her narrative.

〔U0885 para〕
四、奧德修斯的航程，實際上可以歸結為一段從特洛伊到西西里附近的航行，此後則是一趟環繞西西里的航行，從特拉帕尼出發，又回到特拉帕尼結束。
> 4. That the voyages of Ulysses practically resolve themselves into a voyage from Troy to the neighbourhood of Sicily, and thenceforward into a sail round Sicily, beginning with Trapani and ending with the same place.

〔U0886 para〕
為支持其中任何一個命題而提出的論據，都必須不與支持其他命題的論據相牴觸；四條論證路線還必須彼此印證，像兒童拼圖的碎片那樣互相嵌合。除了真實的理論，不可想像還有什麼能滿足如此苛刻的條件。現在我就來證明斯刻里亞就是特拉帕尼，並回頭講述我得出這個結論的步驟。
> It will be necessary that no argument adduced in support of any of these propositions should clash with those in support of any other, but all the four lines of argument must corroborate each other, so that they fit into one another as the pieces of a child's puzzle. It is inconceivable that anything but a true theory should comply with conditions so exacting. I will now proceed to show that Scheria is Trapani, and will return to the steps by which I arrived at this conclusion.

〔U0887 para〕
我帶著本章開頭列出、必須找到其組合的那份要點清單，前往大英博物館的地圖室，打算必要時從特洛阿斯一路搜尋地中海直到直布羅陀；但我記得（基於前面已說明的理由）我應該到希臘以西相當距離處去找，又記得《奧德賽》的作者似乎住在一處面向西方而非東方的海岸，於是決定先搜尋西岸。我知道繆爾上校以及相當有分量的古代證言，都把庫克羅普斯人安置在埃律克斯山上；而且在我看來，奧德修斯獵山羊的那座島，以及整段庫克羅普斯插曲，比航程中任何其他部分都更鮮明地讓人覺得是取材自實景。此外，我知道作者是一位不大可能出過遠門的年輕女子，因此確信只要找到其中一個地方，其他地方也就不難找到了；所以我調閱了利呂拜翁岬的地圖，因為在希臘以西的西岸之中，它最有成功的希望。地圖剛拿到手，我就發現斯刻里亞所需的組合，正好就躺在埃律克斯山腳下。伸入海中的天涯岬角──兩側各一的兩個港口──兩片沼澤之間的狹窄入口──近在咫尺的高山──其中一個港口入口處的岩礁──沒有任何河流──這些都可以在這裡附上的地圖中看到；這張地圖是沃克與布托爾公司根據義大利政府的測量和我國海軍部的海圖替我繪製的。
> Armed with the list of points I had to find in combination, as given at the beginning of this chapter, I went down to the map room of the British Museum intending to search the Mediterranean from the Troad to Gibraltar if necessary; but remembering that I ought to look (for reasons already given) some distance West of Greece, and also that the writer of the *Odyssey* appeared to have lived on a coast that looked West not East, I resolved to search the West coasts first. I knew that Colonel Mure and a respectable weight of ancient testimony had placed the Cyclopes on Mt. Eryx, and it seemed to me that the island where Ulysses hunted the goats, and the whole Cyclopes incident suggested drawing from life more vividly than any other part of the voyages. I knew, moreover, that the writer was a young woman who was little likely to have travelled, and hence felt sure that if one place could be found, none of the others would be long in finding; I asked, therefore, for the map of the Lilybæan promontory, as the West coast West of Greece that offered the greatest prospect of success, and hardly had I got it in my hand before I found the combination I wanted for Scheria lying right under Mt. Eryx. The land's end jutting into the sea—the two harbours one on either side of it—the narrow entrance between two marshes—the high mountain hard by—the rock at the entrance of one of the harbours—the absence of any river—will be found in the map here given, which Messrs. Walker & Boutall have made for me from the Italian Government survey, and from our own Admiralty chart.

〔U0888 figure〕
〔圖版 6：特拉帕尼與埃律克斯山──沃克與布托爾刻製。〕
> TRAPANI AND MT. ERYX — Walker & Boutall sc .

〔U0889 para〕
但這還不是全部。那塊岩礁不但高度恰到好處，而且其方位轉向，正好讓人聯想到一艘正駛入港口的船；更奇的是，它帶著一個古怪的名字：馬爾孔西利奧（Malconsiglio），也就是「惡謀」。這讓我大為震動，於是寫信到特拉帕尼，打聽有沒有和這塊岩礁有關的地方傳說；回信說有兩則──一則荒誕不經，另一則大意是說，這塊岩礁原是一艘土耳其海盜船，海盜們前來攻打特拉帕尼，卻在港口入口處被特拉帕尼聖母（Madonna di Trapani）變成了石頭。我毫不懷疑，這個名字和這則傳說合起來，以基督教化的形式保存了《奧德賽》的版本──傳說記錄了一艘船在駛入港口時被變成石頭這件事，名字則告訴我們另一件事：這是一場「惡謀」所招致的後果。
> But this was not all. Not only was the rock of the right height, and so turned as to give the idea of a ship coming into port, but it it bore the strange name of Malconsiglio, or "Evil counsel." I was so much struck with this that I wrote to Trapani enquiring whether there existed any local tradition in connection with the rock, and was told that there were two—the one absurd, and the other to the effect that the rock had been a ship of Turkish Pirates who were coming to attack Trapani, but were turned into stone at the entrance of the harbour by the Madonna di Trapani. I did not doubt that the name and the legend between them preserved the Odyssean version, in a Christianised form—the legend recording the fact of a ship's having been turned into stone as it was entering harbour, and the name telling us the other fact that this had been brought about in consequence of an evil counsel.

〔U0890 para〕
我相信，以上所述已足以讓人合理地確信斯刻里亞取材自特拉帕尼；因此我接著要證明，伊薩卡的場景也取材自同一個地方及其近郊。
> I believe the above sufficient for reasonable assurance that Scheria was drawn from Trapani, and will, therefore, proceed to establish that the Ithaca scenes are drawn also from the same place and its immediate neighbourhood.

〔U0891 para〕
為此，我有責任在特拉帕尼附近──雖然不是就在城裡──找到一個港口：它現在存在，或可以證明在一切合理的可能性上曾經存在；港內現在有、或過去有水流；而且它緊靠在一座山的山腳下。這個港口的底部應該是緩坡，因為送奧德修斯到伊薩卡的費埃克斯船員，在船勢未止之前，就讓船身衝上岸達半個船長。不遠處必須有兩個彼此相近的洞穴（卷十三 103–112 行及 347–349 行）。其中一個必須有兩個入口──一個朝北，人可以由此下到洞裡；另一個朝南，只有眾神才能從那裡進去。洞裡必須有水，還應該能找到史前的器物。從洞附近必須看得見港口（複數），而且洞應該在山腰上。奧德修斯就是在這裡藏起費埃克斯人送給他的財寶。另一個洞則不必有什麼特殊之處。
> To this end it will be incumbent upon me to find that near Trapani, though not actually at the town, there exists, or can be shown to have in all reasonable probability existed, a harbour which has, or had, a current in it, and which lies hard by the foot of a mountain. This harbour should have a shelving bottom, for the Phæacian crew which brought Ulysses to Ithaca ran half the ship's length on shore before the way was off it. At no great distance there must be two caves near together (xiii. 103-112 and 347-349). One of them must have two entrances—one turned towards the North, by which people can go down into the cave, and the other towards the South, by which the gods alone can enter. It must have water in it, and also prehistoric implements should be found there. From near it one must be able to see harbours (in the plural), and it should be on the side of a mountain. Here Ulysses hid the treasures that the Phæacians had given him. The other cave need present no special features.

〔U0892 para〕
從這些洞穴往山上爬、再沿著山頂走的人，應該會來到一處視野遼闊的高地，那裡有一道泉水和一塊名叫「烏鴉」的岩石（即科拉科斯岩）。這個地點冬天必定冷得刺骨，而且離特拉帕尼必定約兩小時路程；通往城裡的小路必須崎嶇到一個體力尋常的人不拄根手杖就不想走；最後，這條路必須經過一座顯眼的土丘或小山，它比上述高地離特拉帕尼近得多，而且可以把城市和港口盡收眼底。讀者若翻閱本書所收卷十三、十四、十五、十六、十七的縮譯，就會發現這些要點樣樣都不可少。
> A man ascending the mountain from these caves, and keeping along the top of it should come to a place on ground commanding an extensive prospect, where there is a spring and a rock that is called Raven. This site must be bitterly cold in winter, and must be about two hours' walk from Trapani; the path to the town must be so rugged that a man in ordinary vigour would not like to take it without having a stick; and lastly, it must pass a notable mound or hill much nearer Trapani than the high ground above alluded to, and commanding a full view of the city and harbour. The reader who turns to the abridgement of Books xiii., xiv., xv., xvi. and xvii. given in this work, will find that all these points are necessary.

〔U0893 para〕
這些要點至今全都存在，連那塊岩石被叫做「烏鴉」也不例外，只有一項除外──我指的是費埃克斯人駛入的那個港口的口子；它如今已經淤塞，就像塞利努斯港一樣[註1]──塞利努斯我幾乎可以說是在同一段海岸上。港口內側仍滿是海水，但已改建成鹽場[註2]，略低於海平面。舊出口的河床清晰可見，雖然已經完全乾涸，裡面仍長著燈心草：它非常狹窄，冬天常常積滿水；義大利官方地形圖上用虛線標出了它，我國海軍部海圖上卻沒有。
> They all of them exist at this day, even to the calling of the rock "Raven," except one—I mean the mouth of the harbour where the Phæacians entered; this is now silted up, like the harbour of Selinunte,[註1] which I might almost call on the same coast. The inner part of the harbour is still full of sea water, but has been converted into Salt Works[註2] which are slightly below the level of the sea. The bed of the old exit is clearly seen, and there are still rushes in it though it is quite dry: it is very narrow, is often full in winter, and is marked with dotted lines in the Italian Ordnance Map, but not so in our Admiralty Chart.

〔U0894 para〕
這道河床的存在，是特拉帕尼的蘇加梅利先生指給我看的。他向我保證，直到 1848 年開闢鹽場之前，鹽場所占的整片地方原是一片開闊的湖沼，他父親常去那裡打野鴨。開闢鹽場的一大難題，是淡水泉太多，必須在鹽田上抹水泥，才能防止淡水混進鹹水裡。卷六 40 行的 πλυνοί，也就是洗衣池，或許就是由其中某幾道泉水供水的──除非瑙西卡其實是用海水洗衣服，就像我在潘泰萊里亞島上看到婦女們至今仍在做的那樣。
> The existence of this bed was pointed out to me by Signor Sugameli, of Trapani. He assured me that till 1848 when the Salt Works were made, the whole space covered by them was an open mere where his father used to go to shoot wild ducks. One great difficulty in making the Salt Works was the abundance of fresh water springs, which made it necessary to cement the salt pans in order to keep the fresh water from mixing with the salt. It was perhaps from some of these springs that the πλυνοί, or washing cisterns, of vi. 40 were supplied—unless indeed Nausicaa washed the clothes in sea water as I have seen women in the island of Pantellaria still do.

〔U0895 figure〕
〔圖版 7：瑞特隆港，今為聖庫蘇馬諾鹽場。〕
> THE HARBOUR RHEITHRON. NOW SALT WORKS OF S. CUSUMANO.

〔U0896 figure〕
〔圖版 8：瑞特隆港的口子，今已淤塞。〕
> MOUTH OF THE HARBOUR RHEITHRON, NOW SILTED UP.

〔U0897 para〕
設想有一片長將近一英里、寬四分之一英里的水域，出口狹窄，而此地潮水的漲落有二到三英尺，就會造成一股時而相當強勁的水流，足以讓人把它既描述為一條河，又描述為一個有水流的港口。暫且回到斯刻里亞：我推想，這就是奧德修斯在其河口登岸的那條河；而所謂河流為他止住了水勢（卷五 451 行），我認為意思是他抵達那裡時正好碰上潮水轉向。我還可以說，這個港口在《奧德賽》中被用了五次：──
> Given a mass of water, nearly a mile long and a quarter of a mile broad, with a narrow exit, and the tide, which here has a rise and fall of from two to three feet, would cause a current that at times would be strong, and justify its being described as a river and also as a harbour with a current in it; returning for a moment to Scheria, I suppose this to be the river at the mouth of which Ulysses landed, and the river's staying his flow (v. 451), I take to mean that he arrived there just at the turn of the tide. I may also say that this harbour is used five times in the *Odyssey*:—

〔U0898 para〕
一、作為「城外鄉間、涅里通山下那個有水流的港口」。如前所述，此處讀作 Νηρίτῳ 而非 Νηίῳ。雅典娜與忒勒馬科斯交談時，說她把船停在那裡（卷一 185、186 行）。二、作為奧德修斯在斯刻里亞登岸、瑙西卡洗衣服的地方。三、作為奧德修斯在伊薩卡登岸的地方。四、作為忒勒馬科斯從皮洛斯歸來、在伊薩卡登岸的地方（卷十五 495 行以下）。五、作為奧德修斯所指、他的船停泊「在城外鄉間」的那個地點（卷二十四 308 行）。
> 1. As the "flowing harbour, in the country beyond the town, under Mt. Neritum"—reading, as explained earlier, Νηρίτῳ for Νηίῳ—where Minerva said she left her ship, when she was talking with Telemachus i. 185, 186. 2. As the place where Ulysses landed in Scheria and where Nausicaa washed her clothes. 3. As the place where Ulysses landed in Ithaca. 4. As the place where Telemachus landed in Ithaca on his return from Pylos (xv. 495 &c.). 5. As the spot pointed to by Ulysses as the one where his ship was lying "in the country beyond the town" (xxiv. 308).

〔U0899 para〕
現在我回頭談那兩個洞穴，它們應該就在離這個港口頂端不遠的地方。從原文看得很清楚，洞穴是兩個而不是一個；但有人把提到第二個洞穴的那兩行用括號括了起來，我猜想，是因為直到這裡為止，他都只聽說有一個洞，忽然又冒出第二個來，讓他摸不著頭腦。
> I will now return to the two caves which ought to be found at no great distance from the head of this harbour. It is clear from the text that there were two not one, but some one has enclosed in brackets the two lines in which the second cave is mentioned, I presume because he found himself puzzled by having a second cave sprung upon him when up to this point he has been only told of one.

〔U0900 para〕
我斗膽認為，他若熟悉實地，就不會摸不著頭腦了，因為在地圖上標為「公牛洞」（*grotta del toro*）的地方，正有兩個洞穴，彼此相距約八十到一百碼。其中一個很顯眼，但沒什麼特別之處；另一個則不太容易看到，被農民們稱為「公牛洞」，洞口正朝北，大家都相信裡面藏著一筆財寶，有一頭住在洞穴深處的公牛不停地在磨它，但只有處女才找得到，而且她得吃下一整顆石榴，一粒籽也不掉。我懷疑 *toro*（公牛）是孩子們把 *tesoro*（財寶）講走了樣。公牛就這樣進了洞，再也沒出來；而既然大家又確知財寶是有的──那麼──公牛待在那裡，除了推磨磨財寶，還能做什麼呢？
> I venture to think that if he had known the ground he would not have been puzzled, for there are two caves, distant about 80 or 100 yards from one another, at the place marked in the map as the *grotta del toro*. The one is conspicuous, but without special feature; the other, which is not very easily seen, and which is called by the peasants the *grotta del toro*, looks due North, and is universally believed to contain a treasure, which a bull who lives in its recesses is continually grinding, but which can only be found by a virgin, who will eat a whole pomegranate without spilling a single pip. I suspect the *toro* to be a children's corruption of *tesoro*. The bull having thus got into the cave has never got out again, and as the treasure is also confidently known to exist—well—what can the bull be there for but to turn a mill and grind the treasure?

〔U0901 para〕
這個洞穴朝正南方深入岩石之中，通道既崎嶇又狹窄，誰都不大可能順著它往裡走上超過寥寥幾英尺。因此，沒有人能從南面進入這個洞穴──只有眾神才能從那裡進去。
> The cave runs due South into the rock by a passage so rough and narrow that no one is likely to go more than a very few feet with it. No one, therefore, can enter the cavern from the South—it is only the gods who can do so.

〔U0902 para〕
1894 年 8 月，我和幾位西西里朋友一同造訪實地，與掌管洞穴所在農莊的農人（*contadino*）攀談。正談著，一個討人喜歡、看來挺機靈的小夥子騎著驢子過來，似乎對我們的談話很感興趣。
> In August, 1894, I visited the ground with some Sicilian friends, and we discoursed with the *contadino* who had charge of the farm on which the caves are found. While we were talking there came up a nice intelligent lad on a donkey, and he seemed much interested in our conversation.

〔U0903 para〕
「那個洞裡，」我們指著公牛洞問道，「有財寶嗎？」
> "Is there," we asked, pointing to the *grotta del toro*, "a treasure in the cave?"

〔U0904 para〕
「當然有，」他立刻回答。這時那男孩插嘴了。他十分肯定有財寶。人人都知道。這是無庸置疑的。
> "Certainly," was the immediate answer. Here the boy broke in. He was quite sure there was one. Everybody knew it. It could not be doubted.

〔U0905 para〕
「另一個洞裡有財寶嗎？」
> "Is there a treasure in the other cave?"

〔U0906 para〕
「噢，沒有。」
> "Oh, no."

〔U0907 para〕
「兩個洞裡，哪一個叫公牛洞？」
> "Which of the two caves is called the *grotta del toro*?"

〔U0908 para〕
「那一個」──農人和男孩異口同聲，立刻指向與《奧德賽》相符的那個洞。
> "That one"—from both peasant and boy, who pointed at once to the cave that corresponded with the *Odyssey*.

〔U0909 para〕
「你們很確定，另一個洞不叫『公牛洞』（la grotta del toro）？」
> "You are quite sure that the other cave is not called 'la grotta del toro'?"

〔U0910 para〕
「很確定。」
> "Quite."

〔U0911 para〕
「公牛洞通到哪裡？」
> "Where does the *grotta del toro* go to?"

〔U0912 para〕
「越往裡越窄，一直深入到岩石裡去。」
> "It gets narrow and goes far into the rock."

〔U0913 para〕
「有沒有人走到過盡頭？」
> "Has any one ever been to the end of it?"

〔U0914 para〕
「沒有，沒有；沒人知道它到哪裡為止。從前有個趕牛的進去探過一次，就再也沒回來；據說那以後就砌了一道牆，不讓任何人再往裡走。」
> "No, no; no one knows where it ends. There was a cattle driver who went in once to explore it, but he never came back, and they say that after this there was a wall built to stop any one from going further."

〔U0915 para〕
「你自己進過洞裡嗎？」
> "Have you ever been inside the cave yourself?"

〔U0916 para〕
「進過。」
> "Yes."

〔U0917 para〕
「走到那道牆了嗎？」
> "Have you been as far as the wall?"

〔U0918 para〕
「沒有。」
> "No."

〔U0919 para〕
「你走了多遠？」
> "How far did you go?"

〔U0920 para〕
「沒多遠；我害怕。」
> "Not very far; I was afraid."

〔U0921 para〕
「那你根本不知道這個洞有多深了？」
> "Then you have no idea how far the cave goes?"

〔U0922 para〕
「不知道。」
> "No."

〔U0923 para〕
「洞裡一年到頭都有水嗎？」
> "Is there water in the cave at all times?"

〔U0924 para〕
「有。」
> "Yes."

〔U0925 para〕
「你親眼見過嗎？」
> "Have you seen it?"

〔U0926 para〕
「今年五月我去過，那時有水。」
> "I was there in May last, and there was water then."

〔U0927 para〕
「現在那裡有水嗎？」
> "Is there water there now?"

〔U0928 para〕
「我想應該有，但不敢肯定。」
> "I should think so, but cannot be certain."

〔U0929 para〕
「你能帶我們去看看嗎？」
> "Can you take us to it?"

〔U0930 para〕
「不行；那塊地的鑰匙在特拉帕尼。」
> "No; the key of the ground is at Trapani."

〔U0931 para〕
「聽說洞穴深處有一頭公牛？」
> "They say there is a bull in the further recesses of the cavern?"

〔U0932 para〕
「是這麼說，但我們從沒見過牠；我們唯一確知的，就是那裡有財寶。」
> "They say so, but we have never seen him; all we know for certain is that there is a treasure."

〔U0933 para〕
這時男孩又來了精神，說這是千真萬確的。
> Here the boy again brightened up, and said that this was certain.

〔U0934 para〕
等我們問完了，那位農人把我們當中一人拉到一旁，悄悄地說：「放心交給我吧，我的肚子很能裝」（意思是：我守得住祕密）。「你們來搬財寶的時候──我看得出你們是打算這麼做的──一定要帶上我，分我一份。你們要是夜裡來，狗會叫，我就知道你們來了。那時我會下來幫你們，可是一定要分我一份。」
> When we had finished our questions the *contadino* took one of our party aside, and said, confidentially, "Be sure of me, for I have a strong stomach" (*i.e*., I can keep a secret). "When you come to remove the treasure, which I can see that you intend to do, you must take me with you and give me my share. If you come by night the dogs will bark, and I shall know that you are there. I will then come down and help you, but you must give me my share."

〔U0935 para〕
一回到特拉帕尼，我立刻用義大利文把上面這段對話寫了下來；我的西西里朋友們應我的要求在上面簽名，證明記錄無誤。我還想到要補充一句：特拉帕尼附近沒有別的洞穴附會著任何藏寶的故事。
> I wrote the above conversation down, in Italian, immediately on my return to Trapani, and my Sicilian friends signed it, at my request, as a correct report. It occurs to me to add that there is no other cave near Trapani to which any story of a hidden treasure attaches.

〔U0936 para〕
去年（1896 年 5 月）我再度造訪這個洞穴，這一回是和我的朋友 H．費斯廷．瓊斯先生同去；他走遍了本書所描述的整片地方，為的是確認我沒有誇大其詞。陪同我們的是特拉帕尼的蘇加梅利先生；多虧他，我才改正了一個錯誤：我原以為兩個洞穴中較顯眼的那一個叫公牛洞（*grotta del toro*）──因為 1892 年我第一次到特拉帕尼時，城裡的朋友們就是這麼向我保證的，他們並不知道真正叫這個名字的那個洞的存在。瓊斯和蘇加梅利先生手腳並用地爬進了洞穴內部，我則因為上了年紀、腿腳又有點跛，沒敢冒險。他們發現洞穴大約在三十英尺後就止於一整塊堅實的岩石；但走過十或十二英尺的人，很少會願意再往裡走，所以我完全相信，《奧德賽》的作者和今天的農民一樣，相信誰也走不到洞的盡頭。我的朋友們在洞裡發現了水。
> Last year (May, 1896) I visited the cave again, this time with my friend Mr. H. Festing Jones, who has gone over the whole of the ground described in this book, to make sure that I have not overstated my case. We were accompanied by Signor Sugameli of Trapani, to whom I owe the correction of my error in believing the more conspicuous of the two caves to be called the *grotta del toro*—for so, on my first visit to Trapani in 1892, my friends in the town had assured me, not knowing the existence of the one which really bears the name. Jones and Signor Sugameli scrambled into the interior of the cavern, but I, being elderly and somewhat lame, did not venture. They found the cave end, after about thirty feet, in a mass of solid rock; but few who have gone above ten or twelve feet will be likely to go any further, and I can well believe that the writer of the *Odyssey*, like the peasants of to-day, believed that no one could get to the end of it. My friends found water.

〔U0937 para〕
夏天這個洞穴裡滿是蜂巢，這一帶所有的洞穴都是如此。這些蜂巢很小，孤零零的，由紅土築成，大約一個橡實殼斗那麼大。埃律克斯山附近的所有洞穴都富藏石器時代人類的遺物，其中一些精品可以在巴勒莫的博物館看到。三千年左右以前，這類遺物無疑會比現在更常見、也更醒目；所以我認為不難想像，《奧德賽》作者富於詩意的想像力，把它們歸於寧芙與那伊阿得斯仙女。
> The cave is full of bees' nests in summer, as are all the caves hereabouts. They are small, solitary, of red clay, and about the size of the cup of an acorn. All the caves in the neighbourhood of Mt. Eryx abound in remains of stone-age man, some fine examples of which may be seen in the museum at Palermo. These remains would doubtless be more common and more striking three thousand years or so ago than they are at present, and I find no difficulty in thinking that the poetic imagination of the writer of the Odyssey ascribed them to the nymphs and naiads.

〔U0938 para〕
從兩個洞穴附近，當然都看得到埃律克斯山的懸崖──我認為那就是作者心目中的涅里通山（卷十三 351 行）──看得到下方約兩百英尺處耕地上筆直的小路，看得到海之老人福爾庫斯的港灣，也看得到特拉帕尼的兩個港口；這一切都是卷十三 195、196 行與 345–351 行所要求的。
> From hard by both the caves one can see, of course, the precipices of Mt. Eryx, which I suppose to be Neritum in the mind of the writer (xiii. 351), the straight paths on the cultivated land some couple of hundred feet below, the harbour of the old merman Phorcys, and also the harbours of Trapani, all which are requisite by lines xiii. 195, 196, and 345-351.

〔U0939 para〕
讀者會注意到，雖然有不只一處斯刻里亞的細節是隨口帶出、也許是無意間透露的──例如奧德修斯在斯刻里亞登岸的那個港口，以及那些港口（我毫不懷疑就是特拉帕尼的兩個港口）──但到目前為止，沒有任何一處伊薩卡的細節與斯刻里亞描述中的任何特徵相牴觸。
> The reader will note that while more than one Scherian detail is given casually and perhaps unintentionally, as for example the harbour where Ulysses landed in Scheria, and the harbours, which I do not doubt are the two harbours of Trapani, there is no Ithacan detail given so far which conflicts with any feature in the description of Scheria.

〔U0940 para〕
奧德修斯藏寶的那個洞穴與公牛洞之間的吻合之處，無論數量還是分量，都遠遠超過格拉默頓與什魯斯伯里之間的吻合。儘管如此，最好還是看看他離開洞穴後的行蹤，究竟是證實了我的看法，還是與之相悖。
> The number and value of the points of correspondence between the cave in which Ulysses hid his treasure, and the *grotta del toro* greatly exceed those between Grammerton and Shrewsbury. Nevertheless it will be well to see whether his movements on leaving the cave confirm my view or make against it.

〔U0941 para〕
我推想，他爬上了埃律克斯山陡峭的山坡──那時無疑林木茂密。然後他沿著它高而近乎平坦的山頂（δἰ ἄκριας，卷十四 2 行）走到山的另一頭，也就是今天諾曼城堡矗立、海拔二千五百英尺的地方。他在這裡往下走了約兩三百英尺，來到今天叫做 *i runzi*（倫齊）的地點，那裡在一處懸崖附近有一道泉水，那懸崖至今仍叫 *il ruccazzù dei corvi*，也就是「烏鴉岩」，因為這些鳥最愛在山的這一帶繁殖。這段路他大約要走兩個小時，或多或少。
> I suppose him to have ascended the steep, and then, doubtless, wooded slopes of Mt. Eryx and to have passed along its high and nearly level summit (δἰ ἄκριας, xiv. 2) to the other end of the mountain, where the Norman Castle stands now 2500 feet above the sea level. Here he descended some two or three hundred feet to the spot now called *i runzi*, where there is a spring near a precipice which is still called *il ruccazzù dei corvi, i.e*. "the rock of the ravens," it being on this part of the mountain that these birds breed most freely. This walk would take him about two hours, more or less.

〔U0942 para〕
這個地點從四面八方老遠都看得見，冬天冷得刺骨，有一條崎嶇的山路與特拉帕尼相連，奧德修斯沒有手杖不敢走這條路，也是很自然的（卷十七 195 行）[註3]。這條路緊挨著圓頂的聖安娜丘（*Colle di Sta Anna*）經過，它與卷十六 471 行的 Ἕρμαιος λόφος（赫耳墨斯山丘）完全相符。從倫齊走到特拉帕尼所需的時間，與《奧德賽》所提供的一切線索都相符，這些線索說的是歐邁俄斯的小屋與伊薩卡城之間的距離──大致說來，似乎是冬日裡一天之內走一趟來回的路程。
> The site is seen from far and wide, it is bitterly cold in winter, and is connected with Trapani by a rough mountain path which Ulysses may well have been afraid to travel without a stick (xvii. 195).[註3] The path passes close to the round-topped _Colle di Sta Anna_ which answers perfectly to the Ἕρμαιος λόφος of xvi. 471. The time it takes to walk from the *runzi* to Trapani corresponds with all the indications furnished us in the *Odyssey* concerning the distance between Eumæus's hut and the town of Ithaca—which seems roughly to have been a winter's day walk there and back.

〔U0943 para〕
因此讀者會看到，奧德修斯所走的整條路線──從他在福爾庫斯港登岸，到他藏起禮物的洞穴（連同其一切複雜的要求），再登上涅里通山，沿著它長長的山頂走到泉水與科拉科斯岩，最後走那條經過赫耳墨斯山丘、下到伊薩卡的小路──都由一條從聖庫蘇馬諾鹽場（*saline di S. Cusumano*）到公牛洞、埃律克斯山、泉水、烏鴉岩，再到通往特拉帕尼之路的路線，精確地呈現在我們眼前，彷彿《奧德賽》是昨天才寫成的。讀者若能在古今一切文學中，替我找出任何類似的一連串吻合，存在於一個真實的地方與一部虛構作品所描寫的地方之間，而又純屬偶然的結果，我就承認我所指出的這些巧合或許只是偶然。但我深信，這樣的例子，甚至任何差可比擬的例子，都舉不出來。
> The reader will see, therefore, that we have the whole road taken by Ulysses from his landing in the harbour of Phorcys to the cave (with all its complex requirements) in which he hid his presents, up Mt. Neritum, along its long top to the spring and the Haven Rock, and finally the path passing the hill of Mercury down to Ithaca, as accurately presented to us by the road from the *saline di S. Cusumano* to the *grotta del toro*, Mt. Eryx, the fountain, the Raven Rock, and the road to Trapani, as though the *Odyssey* had been written yesterday. When the reader can find me in all literature, ancient or modern, any like chain of correspondences between an actual place and one described in a work of fiction as an effect of mere chance, I will accept the coincidences to which I have called attention as possibly accidental only; but I am convinced that no such case nor anything approaching it can be adduced.

〔U0944 para〕
因此我主張，伊薩卡和斯刻里亞一樣，必須視為取材自特拉帕尼。不過，有一個要點必須記住：作者在從外部（*ab extra*）看待伊薩卡、只把它當成一座島而別無其他時，心中想像的是離特拉帕尼約二十二英里、高聳醒目的馬雷蒂莫島；但她需要細節時，就從大陸上她自己的近鄰取材。
> I, therefore, claim that Ithaca, like Scheria, must be taken as drawn from Trapani. There is, however, this important point to be remembered, that though the writer, when she has to consider Ithaca *ab extra*, as an island and nothing more, pictures it to herself as the high and striking island of Marettimo some 22 miles off Trapani, when she wants details she takes them from her own immediate neighbourhood on the mainland.

〔U0945 para〕
年輕人把熟悉的故事搬到自己家鄉來的時候──幾乎所有年輕人都會這麼做──從不在乎前後矛盾。他們就像傑出的荷馬學者，一旦打定主意要讓事情照某種樣子進行，就不會讓「決心的本色」被思慮所阻撓；他們會覺得，讓伊薩卡既在這裡、又在那裡，同樣輕而易舉；在結冰的池塘上溜冰時，也能看見哥倫布駛向熱帶的航程。塞爾本勳爵就這樣寫道：──
> Young people when transferring familiar stories to their own neighbourhood, as almost all young people do, never stick at inconsistencies. They are like eminent Homeric scholars, and when they mean to have things in any given way they will not let the native hue of resolution be balked by thought, and will find it equally easy to have an Ithaca in one place and also in another, and to see the voyages of Columbus to the tropics in their own sliding over a frozen pool. So Lord Selborne writes:—

〔U0946 quote〕
隨著我們長大，想像力也越來越強。它替我們童年所有的樂趣添上色彩；它陪著我們上冰場、進樹林；它把超自然的夢境摻進最平凡的事物裡。我們在結冰的池塘上溜冰時歇腳的地方，就是哥倫布或庫克所發現的島嶼──我們最愛讀他們的航海記了。
> As we grew, the faculty of imagination increased in power. It coloured all our childish pleasures; it accompanied us on the ice and into the woods; it mixed the dreams of the supernatural with the most ordinary things. Our resting-places when sliding over a frozen pool were the islands discovered by Columbus or Cook, in whose voyages we delighted.

〔U0947 quote〕
（朗德爾．帕爾默，塞爾本伯爵著，《回憶錄》等，麥克米倫，1896 年，第一部第 66 頁。）
> (*Memorials &*., by Roundell Palmer, Earl of Selborne, Macmillan 1896, pt. I. p. 66.)

〔U0948 para〕
在離開伊薩卡的場景之前，我應該說明：特拉帕尼過去很可能有一片水域，讓牛群需要搭船渡過，就像《奧德賽》卷二十 186–188 行所描述的那樣。特拉帕尼東側的土地在 1860 年經人工墊高；在那之前，北風連吹一陣之後，城鎮兩側的海水在冬天常常連成一片。好幾個人向我保證，他們記得自己曾得坐車渡過特拉帕尼與大陸之間的水面。起初我很想相信，菲羅提俄斯是在通往城裡的狹窄入口被水淹沒時進城的；但往上幾行，我們就讀到歐邁俄斯也帶了三頭豬進城，墨蘭提俄斯也帶了幾頭山羊。這兩人無疑都是從埃律克斯山來的，而原文似乎排除了他們也得渡水的可能。然而，沒有任何跡象暗示菲羅提俄斯是從埃律克斯山來的；事實上，他的牛群比較可能在港口南邊的平地上吃草，而他是搭船渡過港口，省得非繞一大段遠路（*détour*）不可。如果那片水是小亞細亞、希臘或西西里任何一條河的水，大概一個擺渡人就夠了；然而看來卻有好幾個人在那裡載客收費，就像在海港或港口那樣。
> Before I leave the Ithaca scenes I ought to show that there may well have existed at Trapani a sheet of water which cattle would be likely to cross in a boat, as described in *Od*. XX. 186-188. The land on the East side of Trapani was artificially raised in 1860, till which time the two seas on either side the town were often joined in winter after a continuance of Northerly Winds. Several people have assured me that they remember having to be carted over the water between Trapani and the mainland. I was at first tempted to believe that Philœtius had come to the town when the narrow entrance to it was flooded; but a few lines above we find that Eumæus had also come to the town with three pigs, and Melanthius with some goats. These men had both unquestionably come from Mt. Eryx, and the text seems to forbid the idea that they too had had to cross the water. There is nothing, however, to imply that Philœtius had come from Mt. Eryx; indeed, it is more likely that his cattle would feed on the flat land south of the harbour, which he had crossed by boat to save the long *détour* which would have been otherwise necessary. If the water had been that of any such river as is to be found in Asia Minor, Greece, or Sicily, one man would probably have been enough, whereas there seem to have been several plying for hire, as in a port or harbour.

〔U0949 para〕
聽眾一定很清楚，斯刻里亞和伊薩卡都取材自他們自己的家鄉；這個事實又解釋了另一個難題。某個吹毛求疵的聽眾也許會問：「像奧德修斯這麼精明老練的水手，怎麼會沒注意到他從斯刻里亞到伊薩卡只航行了兩英里，甚至不到兩英里？他又怎麼會認不出自己上岸的地方，正是幾天前遇見瑙西卡的地方？」
> The fact that Scheria and Ithaca would be perfectly well-known by the audience as drawn from their own neighbourhood explains another difficulty. "How," some hypercritical listener might ask, "could so sagacious and experienced a mariner as Ulysses have failed to note that he was only travelling two miles, or even less, from Scheria to Ithaca? And how again could he fail to recognise the place at which he landed as the one where he had met Nausicaa a few days previously?"

〔U0950 para〕
《奧德賽》的作者帶點天真（*naiveté*）地承認，費埃克斯水手們早就熟悉他們把奧德修斯留下的那個港口。他們大概確實熟悉。可是怎樣才能讓奧德修斯在航行途中、以及被送上岸時都不提出異議？作者實際採取的辦法是：奧德修斯一上船就讓他睡著，等水手們都走了才讓他醒來；很難看出她還能採取什麼更好的辦法。於是，替他預備了一場沉睡（卷七 318 行、卷八 445 行），而他似乎在船還沒出港就睡著了；這場睡眠如此深沉，與其說像睡眠，不如說更像死亡（卷十三 80 行）。什麼都吵不醒他──就連水手們第二天早上把他從船上抬下來，把他所有的財寶放在他身旁，然後離去，也吵不醒他──直到費埃克斯水手們遠在一切盤問所能及的範圍之外為止。那之後，當然是他醒得越早越好。
> The writer of the *Odyssey* admits with some *naiveté* that the Phæacian mariners were already acquainted with the harbour in which they left Ulysses. They probably would be. But how prevent Ulysses from remonstrating both during the voyage and on being landed? It is not easy to see what better course the writer could take than the one she actually did take, *i.e*., put Ulysses to sleep as soon as ever he was on board, and not wake him till after the sailors were gone. A sleep, therefore, is prepared for him (vii. 318, and viii. 445) and he falls into it apparently before even leaving the harbour; it is so profound that it is more like death than sleep (xiii. 80). Nothing, not even the men lifting him off the ship next morning, laying all his treasures hard by him and going away, can disturb him till the Phæacian sailors are beyond all reach of question. Then, of course, the sooner he wakes up the better.

〔U0951 para〕
至於另一個難題──他沒看出自己所在之處，不過就是兩天前遇見瑙西卡的地方──作者的解決辦法是：讓那天早上大霧瀰漫，並讓奧德修斯整個人糊里糊塗，連這地方是伊薩卡都認不出來，更別說認出是斯刻里亞了；直到雅典娜與他相遇，和他長談了一番，聽眾就在這番長談之間不知不覺進入了情境，不再多加異議，便把特拉帕尼一帶當成了伊薩卡一帶。
> As for the other difficulty of his not seeing that he was only at the spot where he had met Nausicaa two days earlier, this was got over by making it a misty morning, and muddling Ulysses generally so that he does not even recognise the place as Ithaca, much less as Scheria, till Minerva meets him and has a long talk with him, in the course of which the audience slides into the situation, and accepts the neighbourhood of Trapani for that of Ithaca without more demur.

〔U0952 footnote〕
幾年前，塞利努斯港入口處的石砌工程曾被挖掘出來，但只過了一個冬天就又被淤沙掩埋。
> A few years ago the stone work at the entrance to the harbour of Selinunte was excavated, but it was silted over again in a single winter.

〔U0953 footnote〕
平面圖上標為聖庫蘇馬諾鹽場。
> Shown in the plan as the Salt Works of S. Cusumano.

〔U0954 footnote〕
近年來，從特拉帕尼到埃律克斯山頂小鎮已修築了一條極好的馬車道，但步行者仍走那條舊路，有些地方非常崎嶇。
> Of recent years an excellent carriage road has been made from Trapani to the town on the top of Mt. Eryx, but pedestrians still use the old path, which in places is very rough.

〔U0955 heading〕
第九章
> CHAPTER IX.

〔U0956 para〕
愛奧尼亞群島與埃加迪群島──奧德修斯的航程實際上是一趟從特拉帕尼出發、又回到特拉帕尼的環西西里航行
> THE IONIAN AND THE ÆGADEAN ISLANDS—THE VOYAGES OF ULYSSES SHOWN TO BE PRACTICALLY A SAIL ROUND SICILY FROM TRAPANI TO TRAPANI.

〔U0957 para〕
在後面的一章裡，我打算證明：《奧德賽》的作者手上的《伊里亞德》，就是我們今天所見的樣子，只有無關緊要的抄寫錯誤除外。我將指出，歐陸與英國的知名荷馬學者最為懷疑的那幾卷，她引用起來，和那些公認出自荷馬手筆的卷次一樣完整、一樣隨意。我沒看過有誰提出充分的理由，足以懷疑《伊里亞德》卷二 484–877 行的〈船表〉不屬於荷馬身後所留的原詩──雖然他很可能找過某個比他更熟悉希臘的人幫忙。這個問題我打算以後再談，眼下只請讀者接受我的假定：《奧德賽》的作者知道上述的〈船表〉。在亞該亞大軍的〈船表〉中，埃基納伊諸島與愛奧尼亞群島是這樣描述的：
> In a later chapter I propose to show that the writer of the *Odyssey* had the *Iliad* before her in the state in which we have it now, unimportant copyists' errors alone excepted. I shall show that those Books on which most doubt has been cast by eminent Homeric scholars both on the Continent and in England, are just as fully and freely quoted from as those that are admitted to have been by Homer. I have seen no sufficient reason alleged for doubting that the Catalogues of *Il*. II. 484-877 formed part of the poem as Homer left it, though it is quite likely that he may have got some one with greater knowledge of Greece to help him. I intend returning to this question, but for the present will ask the reader to accept my assumption that the writer of the *Odyssey* knew the Catalogues above referred to. The group of the Echinades and the Ionian islands are described as follows in the Catalogue of the Achæan forces:—

〔U0958 quote〕
還有杜利基昂島的人，以及神聖的埃基納伊諸島的人，他們住在隔海與埃利斯相望之處──率領他們的是堪比阿瑞斯的墨革斯，菲勒烏斯之子；菲勒烏斯從前因與父親失和，遷居杜利基昂島。隨墨革斯而來的有四十艘船。
> And they of Dulichium, with the sacred Echinean islands, who dwelt beyond the sea off Elis—these were led by Meges, peer of Mars, the son of Phyleus, who had erewhile migrated to Dulichium in consequence of a quarrel with his father. And with him there came forty ships.

〔U0959 quote〕
奧德修斯率領勇敢的凱法勒尼亞人，他們據有伊薩卡、林木茂密的涅里通、克羅庫勒亞、崎嶇的埃基利普斯、薩摩斯[註1]與扎金索斯，以及與這些島嶼隔海相對的大陸。率領他們的是謀略堪比眾神的奧德修斯，隨他而來的有十二艘船。（《伊里亞德》卷二 625–637 行。）
> Ulysses led the brave Cephallenians, who held Ithaca, wooded Neritum, Crocylea, rugged Ægilips, Samos,[註1] and Zacynthus, with the mainland also that is over against the islands. These were led by Ulysses, peer of gods in counsel, and with him came twelve ships. (*Il*. II. 625-637.)

〔U0960 para〕
讀者會注意到，杜利基昂島（意思是「長島」）並不屬於愛奧尼亞群島，而屬於鄰近的埃基納伊諸島。現在來看看《奧德賽》怎麼描述伊薩卡附近的島嶼。奧德修斯說（卷九 21–26 行）：
> The reader will note that Dulichium, which means "Long Island," does not belong to the Ionian islands, but to the neighbouring group of the Echinades. Let us now see how the islands in the neighbourhood of Ithaca are described in the *Odyssey*. Ulysses says (ix. 21-26):

〔U0961 quote〕
「我住在伊薩卡，島上有一座高山，名叫涅里通。附近還有幾座彼此相距不遠的島嶼：杜利基昂島、薩墨與扎金索斯。伊薩卡橫在天際，在海中位置最高，朝向西方；其他島嶼則遠在它的東邊。」
> "I dwell in Ithaca, an island which contains a high mountain called Neritum. In its neighbourhood there are other islands near to one another, Dulichium, Same and Zacynthus. It lies on the horizon all highest up in the sea towards the west, while the other islands lie away from it to the east."

〔U0962 figure〕
〔圖版 9：愛奧尼亞群島地圖──沃克與布托爾。〕
> MAP OF THE ÆONIAN ISLANDS. — Walker & Boutall .

〔U0963 para〕
在《奧德賽》裡，伊薩卡之外的島嶼從來不超過三座。三座島一起提到時，總是依上面的順序排列──大概是格律上的緣故；但在作者眼中，杜利基昂島最重要：它被單獨提起的次數比較多，而且派出了五十二名求婚者，相比之下，薩墨只有二十四名，扎金索斯二十名，伊薩卡本島十二名（卷十六 247–251 行）。
> In the *Odyssey* there are never more than three islands besides Ithaca. When mentioned all together they are always named in the order given above—probably for reasons of scansion—but Dulichium is the most important in the eyes of the writer, being more frequently mentioned separately, and sending fifty-two suitors as against twenty-four from Same, twenty from Zacynthus, and twelve from Ithaca itself (xvi. 247-251).

〔U0964 para〕
只要瞄一眼上面的地圖就會明白，伊薩卡附近沒有哪一座島，能以詩意上說得過去的方式，被當成派出了將近其他三島總和那麼多的求婚者。最說不過去的就是杜利基昂島。這樣看來，作者要的是這個名字，而不是這座島；而且她想要得十分迫切，竟不惜動粗，從另一個群島把它搶了過來。
> A glance at the map given above will show that there is no island in the neighbourhood of Ithaca which can with poetical propriety be held to have sent nearly as many suitors as the other three put together. Least of all could Dulichium be so held. It seems, then, that it was the name, and not the island, that the writer wanted; and further that she wanted this so badly as to lay violent hands upon it and raid it from another group.

〔U0965 para〕
她為什麼要這樣大費周章，非把它弄到手不可？《伊里亞德》的〈船表〉漏掉了三四座，仍給我們留下六座愛奧尼亞島嶼。去掉克羅庫勒亞與埃基利普斯這兩座小島，還剩四座，看來這正是《奧德賽》作者打算引入的確切數目；既然如此，涅里通為什麼不夠格？顯然它不合她的用途，否則她不會無視〈船表〉，把它塞進伊薩卡島內，再跑到更遠的地方去找她的首要島嶼。這種事從來不會無緣無故地做，而這一次尤其需要理由，因為真正的杜利基昂島既然微不足道，讓五十二名求婚者來自頗具規模的涅里通島，不但容易得多，也合適得多。
> Why should she strain so considerable a point in order to get hold of it? The Iliadic catalogue omits three or four but leaves us six Ionian islands. After suppressing the small islands of Crocylea and Ægilips, there remained four, which it seems was the exact number that the writer of the *Odyssey* meant to introduce; why, then, should not Neritum have been good enough for her? It evidently did not answer her purpose, or she would not, in the face of the catalogue, have stowed it away inside Ithaca and gone further afield for her dominant island. These things are never done without a reason, and in this case a reason is particularly necessary, for it would have been more easy and also suitable, considering the insignificance of the real Dulichium, to make the fifty-two suitors come from the very considerable island of Neritum.

〔U0966 para〕
只要假定作者住在特拉帕尼，是拿她所熟悉的埃加迪群島來描寫愛奧尼亞群島，一切困難便迎刃而解。瞄一眼前面的地圖就可看出，她不可能是照著真正的愛奧尼亞群島來寫的。伊薩卡無論怎樣硬拗，都不可能「在海中位置最高，朝向西方」。它完全被薩墨擋住了。其他島嶼也不是遠在它的東邊。由此可見，作者心中所想的並不是愛奧尼亞群島；不過我們可以順帶推論兩點：第一，她的聽眾住得離希臘夠遠，地形上不精確也無關緊要；第二，她實際取材的那些島嶼，和真正的愛奧尼亞群島一樣，都位於某處西岸之外。
> All difficulty is removed by supposing that the writer lived at Trapani and was drawing the Ionian islands from the, to her, familiar Ægadean group. A glance at the foregoing map will shew that she cannot have been drawing from the real Ionian islands. Ithaca cannot be tortured into lying "all highest up in the sea towards the West." It is completely covered by Samos. Nor do the other islands lie away from it to the East. It is clear, then, that the Ionian islands were not those present to the mind of the writer, but we may infer in passing, firstly, that her audience lived at a sufficient distance from Greece to make the infraction of topographical accuracy a matter of no importance, and secondly, that the islands from which she was in reality drawing lay, like the true Ionian group, off a West coast.

〔U0967 para〕
現在我附上一張特拉帕尼外海諸島的地圖。我注意到，弗里曼教授在他那張依他設想的古代樣貌所繪的西西里西岸地圖上，把大島（Isola Grande）和鄰近的本土連在一起，卻沒有提出任何根據。我在古代作家中找不到根據；親自勘察過那片地方之後，也看不出有任何跡象，顯示在離我們這個時代可以估量的時期之內，陸地與水域的分布曾經有過變化。
> I will now give a map of the islands off Trapani. I see that Professor Freeman, in his map of the West coast of Sicily, as he supposes it to have been in ancient times, has joined the Isola Grande to the neighbouring main land, but he gives no authority for doing so. I can find none in ancient writers, and having examined the ground see nothing to indicate any change in the distribution of land and water, as having taken place within measurable distance of our own times.

〔U0968 figure〕
〔圖版 10：埃加迪群島地圖。〕
> MAP OF THE ÆGADEAN ISLANDS.

〔U0969 para〕
高峻崎嶇的馬雷蒂莫島，在作者心中充當伊薩卡──不過正如我說過的，需要細節時，她取材於特拉帕尼與埃律克斯山。那座長島，也就是今天的大島──地勢低平、出產小麥──就是她的杜利基昂島；就特拉帕尼而言，這座島想必是四島中最重要的一座，因為任何天候都能前往，而且當時很可能已經孕育著後來赫赫有名的莫提亞城；這座城幾乎什麼也沒留下，原本位於大島與本土之間兩座小島中靠南的那一座上。另外兩座島分別代表薩墨與扎金索斯，但哪座是哪座，我始終無法斷定。從特拉帕尼幾乎看不到馬雷蒂莫，因為它幾乎完全被萊萬佐擋住了。然而，從埃律克斯山的高處望去──基於其他理由，我認為作者很熟悉這座山──它就「橫在天際，在海中位置最高，朝向西方」。我毫不懷疑，這位女詩人描寫的，正是她在埃律克斯山頂所熟知的景象，也是讀者至今仍能看到的景象。下一頁的草圖，會比文字更能說明 πανυπερτάτη εἰν ἁλὶ（在海中位置最高）的意思；圖中特拉帕尼正上方的兩座小島是福爾米凱群島，我認為那就是波呂斐摩斯擲出的第二塊巨石。
> The lofty and rugged island of Marettimo did duty in the writer's mind for Ithaca, though, as I have said, when details are wanted they are taken from Trapani and Mt. Eryx. The long island, now the Isola Grande—low lying and wheat growing—was her Dulichium; this must have been for the most important of the four as regards Trapani, being accessible in all weathers, and probably already pregnant with the subsequently famous city of Motya, of which hardly anything remains, but which stood on the Southernmost of the two islands that lie between Isola Grande and the mainland. The other two islands stood for Same and Zacynthus, but which was which I have not been able to determine. Marettimo can hardly be seen from Trapani, being almost entirely hidden by Levanzo. From the heights, however, of Mt. Eryx, with which, for other reasons, I suppose the writer to have been familiar, it is seen "on the horizon, all highest up in the sea towards the West." I do not doubt the poetess was describing it as she knew it from the top of Mt. Eryx, and as the reader may still see it. The rough sketch on the following page will explain πανυπερτάτη εἰν ἁλὶ better than words can do; the two small islands shown just over Trapani are the Formiche, which I take to be the second rock thrown by Polyphemus.

〔U0970 figure〕
〔圖版 11：從埃律克斯山望特拉帕尼。可見馬雷蒂莫「在海中位置最高」。《奧德賽》卷九 25 行。大島（杜利基昂島）未能收入畫面。〕
> TRAPANI FROM MT. ERYX. Showing Marettimo all highest up in the sea. Od . ix 25. The Isola Grande (Dulichium) could not be got into the picture.

〔U0971 para〕
如果以上所說還不足以讓讀者相信，《奧德賽》的作者是拿埃加迪群島來描寫愛奧尼亞群島，那麼我再補充什麼，大概也說服不了他。因此，我現在進入第四點：奧德修斯的航程，在作者能力所及的範圍內，就是一趟環西西里的航行──從特拉帕尼出發，沿北岸穿過墨西拿海峽，到潘泰萊里亞島，再回到特拉帕尼；再往後就不必追了，因為伊薩卡與斯刻里亞兩者都是特拉帕尼，這一點我已經證明過了。
> If what I have said above is not enough to satisfy the reader that the writer of the *Odyssey* was drawing the Ionian islands from the Ægadean, nothing that I can add is likely to convince him. I will therefore now go on to my fourth point, namely, that the voyages of Ulysses are, as nearly as the writer could make them, a voyage round Sicily, from Trapani by the North coast, through the straits of Messina, to the island of Pantellaria, and so back to Trapani, beyond which we need not go, for Ithaca and Scheria are, both of them, Trapani, as I have already shown.

〔U0972 para〕
航程的主要情節依下列順序發生。一、基科涅斯人。二、食蓮人，途經基西拉島之後抵達。三、奧德修斯與同伴獵山羊的那座島，以及與波呂斐摩斯的遭遇。四、埃俄羅斯的島，接著順風向東航行十天，風勢始終不改，直到伊薩卡清楚在望，隨即又被吹回奧德修斯出發的那座島。這段越過義大利腳尖與薩墨島、駛向伊薩卡的航行，在地形上沒有意義，只說明作者設想埃俄羅斯的島遠在伊薩卡以西。這段插曲的用意，不過是把酒杯送到奧德修斯唇邊，再一把打落。五、萊斯特律戈涅斯人。六、喀耳刻的島與冥府之行；後者同樣沒有地形上的意義，只是一根掛釘，用來掛上與亡魂的對話，之後又把我們帶回喀耳刻的島。七、塞壬。八、斯庫拉與卡律布狄斯。九、赫利俄斯的牛群。十、卡呂普索的島。十一、斯刻里亞與伊薩卡。
> The main episodes of the voyage occur in the following order. 1. The Cicons. 2. The Lotus-eaters, arrived at after passing the island of Cythera. 3. The island where Ulysses and his men hunted the goats, and the adventure with Polyphemus. 4. The island of Æolus, and a ten days' sail towards the East with a fair wind all the time, till Ithaca is well in sight, followed by an immediate return to the island from which Ulysses had started. This sail to Ithaca over the toe of Italy and the island of Samos has no topographical significance except as showing that the writer conceived of the island of Æolus as lying a long way West of Ithaca. The episode is introduced merely for the purpose of bringing the cup close to Ulysses' lips and then dashing it from them. 5. The Læstrygonians. 6. The island of Circe and the journey to Hades, which last is again without topographical significance, being nothing but a peg on which to hang colloquies with the dead, and bringing us back to the island of Circe. 7. The Sirens. 8. Scylla and Charybdis. 9. The cattle of the Sun. 10. The island of Calypso. 11. Scheria and Ithaca.

〔U0973 para〕
至於基科涅斯人、基西拉島與食蓮人的位置，學者之間並無異議；所以讀者會看到，我們一點時間也沒浪費，就被帶到離西西里不遠的地方──馬勒亞岬外的逆風（卷九 81 行），似乎就是特意颳起來把我們帶離希臘的。為什麼要安排基科涅斯人出場，不太容易看出來，除非是要讓奧德修斯得到伊斯馬羅斯的奇妙美酒，好用它灌醉波呂斐摩斯。這一帶的酒，在《奧德賽》寫成之後許多世紀仍然有名，想必在《奧德賽》本身的時代也是如此。特拉帕尼人之間很可能流傳著某個關於這種酒的吹牛故事，促使作者把它寫進來，拿它打趣，並用它把波呂斐摩斯灌醉。
> There is no difference of opinion among scholars as to the sites of the Cicons, the island of Cythera, and the Lotus-eaters; the reader will, therefore, see that we are taken without waste of time to a point at no great distance from Sicily—the contrary winds off Cape Malea (ix. 81) being apparently raised on purpose to take us away from Greece. It is not quite easy to see why the Cicons were introduced unless it was that Ulysses might become possessed of the wondrous wine of Ismarus with which lie intoxicated Polyphemus. The wine of this neighbourhood was famous many centuries after the *Odyssey* was written, and presumably was so in the time of the *Odyssey* itself. A gasconading story of this wine may well have existed among the people of Trapani which might prompt the reader to introduce it, poke fun at it and make Polyphemus drunk with it.

〔U0974 para〕
又或者，我們從修昔底德（卷六第 2 章）得知，西西里這一帶原有的西卡尼居民，在特洛伊陷落之後，接納了一批從特洛伊附近逃來的難民；因此，作者的聽眾之間可能流傳著某種傳說，說他們當中有些人源自基科涅斯人，而她或許想奉承他們，告訴他們是他們打退了奧德修斯。這一點無法有把握地說什麼；我們只能注意到，那片地方毫無特色，因此和食蓮人之地一樣，看不出是根據親身見聞描寫的。
> Or again, knowing as we do from Thucydides (vi. 2) that the original Sican inhabitants of this part of Sicily received an influx of fugitives from the neighbourhood of Troy after the fall of that city, it is possible that traditions may have existed among the writer's audience to the effect that some of them were of Cicon origin, and she may have wished to flatter them by telling them that they had repulsed Ulysses. Nothing can be said with any confidence upon this head; all we may note is that the country is quite featureless, and hence does not suggest drawing from personal knowledge, any more than does the land of the Lotus-eaters.

〔U0975 para〕
離開食蓮人之地以後，一路陪伴我們的一致意見就沒有了；不過，古代、中世紀與現代都有相當分量的權威，把我們帶到法維尼亞納島──古稱埃古薩，即「山羊島」──認定那就是奧德修斯與同伴獵山羊的島。的確，這段情節的安排，就像是故意要讓聽眾聯想到埃加迪群島，也就是「山羊」群島；卷四 606 行也是如此：那裡說伊薩卡──其實也就是馬雷蒂莫島──是一座適合山羊的島。[註2]
> On leaving the land of the Lotus-eaters the full consent which has accompanied us so far fails us; nevertheless a considerable weight of authority, ancient, medieval, and modern, carries us to the island of Favognana, anciently called Ægusa or Goat Island, as the one on which Ulysses and his men hunted the goats. Indeed this incident seems introduced as though purposely to suggest the Ægadean or "goat" islands to the audience, as also does the line iv. 606 in which Ithaca—that is to say, in reality, the island of Marettimo—is said to be an island fit for goats.[註2]

〔U0976 para〕
把埃律克斯山當作與波呂斐摩斯遭遇之地，也有相當廣泛的共識。在這裡，以及在獵山羊的那座島上，地方色彩比奧德修斯航程中任何其他地方都濃；對一位我已證明對特拉帕尼一帶瞭如指掌的作者來說，這本來就是可以合理預期的。
> A very considerable consent accompanies us also to Mt. Eryx as the site of the adventure with Polyphemus. Here, and with the island on which the goats were hunted, the local colour is stronger than anywhere else in Ulysses' voyages, as indeed might be reasonably expected from a writer whom I have shown to have been so intimately acquainted with the neighbourhood of Trapani.

〔U0977 figure〕
〔圖版 12：奧德修斯的航程，略去……〕
> THE VOYAGES OF ULYSSES, omitting...

〔U0978 para〕
然而，到了這裡，連部分的共識也沒有了。埃俄羅斯的島與萊斯特律戈涅斯人的國度，被安放的地點幾乎和論《奧德賽》的作者一樣多。這兩處，以及法維尼亞納島與庫克羅普斯人，我會在後面再談。我眼下的目的，是說明航程中有多少可以視為已知，多少有相當的權威支持，還有多少尚待尋找。
> Even partial consent, however, now fails us. The island of Æolus and the country of the Læstrygonians have been placed in almost as many sites as there have been writers upon the *Odyssey*. I shall return to these on a later page, as also to the island of Favognana and the Cyclopes. My present object is to show how much of the voyage we may consider as known, how much as supported by considerable authority, and how much we have yet to find.

〔U0979 para〕
我們在波呂斐摩斯的洞穴失去的部分共識，到了喀耳刻的島、塞壬與漂移之岩又回來了──這幾處一般認為就是利帕里群島；到了斯庫拉與卡律布狄斯，則重新獲得一致的共識。把赫利俄斯的牛群放在西西里西岸、大約陶羅美尼翁（今陶爾米納）一帶，我不敢說意見一致；但這個看法非常普遍，根據又顯然十分充分，所以我要把這一點當作確定的：因為島名已足以指向西西里，困住奧德修斯的風向表明他在一處西岸，而一夜之間把他吹回卡律布狄斯的南風，則表明他被設想為在墨西拿海峽以南不遠處。
> The partial consent which we lost at the cave of Polyphemus returns to us with the island of Circe, the Sirens and the Wandering Cliffs, which are generally considered to have been the Lipari islands, and universal consent rejoins us for Scylla and Charybdis. I can hardly say that consent is universal for placing the cattle of the Sun on the West coast of Sicily, somewhere about Tauromenium now Taormina; but it is very general, and is so obviously well founded that I shall claim this point as certain; for the name of the island sufficiently indicates Sicily, the winds that detain Ulysses show him to have been on a West coast, and the South wind that blew him back to Charybdis in a night shows that he was supposed to be at no great distance South of the Straits of Messina.

〔U0980 para〕
卡呂普索的島一般被認為是馬爾他，但無論在《奧德賽》內部或外部都找不到根據，因此我將把卡呂普索的島視為尚待尋找。
> The island of Calypso has been generally held to be Malta, but on no foundation either internal or external to the *Odyssey*, I shall, therefore, consider Calypso's island as yet to find.

〔U0981 para〕
斯刻里亞就是特拉帕尼，這一點我沒有任何共識可依，但有了上面所寫的，我也要把這一點視為確定。因此，我在這裡附上的地圖，可以讓讀者看出：就共識與其他已確定的地點而言，我們的處境如何。可以視為確定的航段，我用粗實線；有相當權威支持的航段，用斷續線；而我自己要補上的航段，則用點線。我讓奧德修斯從南方駛近特拉帕尼，依據的是卡呂普索給他的指示：要他朝大熊星座航行，讓它保持在左手邊（卷五 276、277 行）。[註3]這無疑表示一條向北的航線，甚至可以說是北北東的航線；無論如何，這樣的航線與卡呂普索的指示毫不衝突。或許我最好把詩中的原句引出來：
> I have no consent for Scheria being Trapani, but after what I have written above shall claim this point too as certain. The map, therefore, which I here give will show the reader how we stand as regards assent and otherwise ascertained points. I have used strong lines for the parts of the voyage that may be claimed as certain, interrupted lines for the parts that are backed by considerable authority, and dotted lines for those which I would supply. I have made Ulysses approach Trapani from the South, on the strength of Calypso's directions to him that he was to sail towards the Great Bear, keeping it on his left hand (v. 276, 277).[註3] This indicates certainly a Northerly, and one would say a N.N. Easterly, course; at any rate such a course would in no way conflict with Calypso's instructions. Perhaps I had better give the words of the poem which run:—

〔U0982 quote〕
他坐著，目光注視著普勒阿得斯星團[註4]、遲落的牧夫座，以及又名大車的大熊星座──它面向俄里翁不停旋轉，唯獨它從不沉入海中──因為卡呂普索吩咐過他，要以它為準掌舵，讓它保持在左手邊（卷五 272–277 行）。
> He sat keeping his eyes upon the Pleiades,[註4] late setting Boötes, and on the Bear, also called the Wain, which turns round and round facing Orion, and alone never sinks beneath the sea—for Calypso had bidden him steer by this, keeping it on his left hand (v. 272-277).

〔U0983 para〕
奧德修斯航程中的所有地點，一般都被歸到某個實際的地方，那個地方以它本來的名字或某個虛構的名字，存在於作者心中；而一旦我們進入西西里海域，凡是有相當共識的地點，或不論有無共識、如今都可以宣稱已經確定的地點──我指的是喀耳刻的島、塞壬、斯庫拉與卡律布狄斯、特里納基亞島、斯刻里亞與伊薩卡──全都位於西西里沿岸或近旁。那麼，這三個尚未確知的地點──埃俄羅斯的島、萊斯特律戈涅斯人之地與卡呂普索的島──不管名字多麼虛構，也都是真實的地方，而且應該依照書中描述的順序，到西西里沿岸或附近去找；這樣的念頭豈不是叫人無法抗拒？
> All the places in Ulysses' voyage have been generally referred to some actual locality, which was present to the writer's mind either under its own or a fictitious name; and when we have once got into Sicilian waters, all those about which is there is any considerable amount of consent, or which we may now, with or without consent, claim as ascertained—I mean Circe's island, the Sirens, Scylla and Charybdis, the Thrinacian island, Scheria and Ithaca are on, or hard by, the coast of Sicily. Is not the temptation irresistible to think that the three unknown sites—the island of Æolus, the Land of the Læstrygonians and the island of Calypso—are also real places however fictitious the names may be, and to hold that they should be looked for on, or near, the coast of Sicily in the same order as that in which we find them described?

〔U0984 para〕
假如我們假設法維尼亞納與埃律克斯山就是奧德修斯與同伴獵山羊之島和波呂斐摩斯洞穴的真正所在，便立刻被依序引向其他地點，而這些地點又令人信服地像是埃俄羅斯的島、萊斯特律戈涅斯人之地、喀耳刻的島、其他已確立的地點，最後是卡呂普索的島──那麼，我們難道不該斷定，至少暫時斷定，這個假設是正確的嗎？
> If, on the hypothesis that Favognana and Mt. Eryx are the true sites of the island on which Ulysses and his men hunted the goats, and of the cave of Polyphemus, we are immediately led to others, in due order of sequence, which commend themselves as being those of the island of Æolus, the Land of the Læstrygonians, Circe's island, the other established sites, and lastly Calypso's island, should we not conclude, at any rate provisionally, that the hypothesis is a true one?

〔U0985 para〕
我就這樣斷定，並著手尋找埃俄羅斯的島：它應該是某座看來孤懸的島，位於利帕里群島以西相當遠處，離埃律克斯山不太遠。
> I will so conclude, and proceed to look for the island of Æolus in some island, apparently solitary, a good way to the West of the Lipari islands, and at no great distance from Mt. Eryx.

〔U0986 para〕
首先我得糾正一個非常普遍的誤解。πλωτῇ（卷十 3 行）一詞被過度引申，解釋成這座島四處漂浮，因而會改變位置。但如果原意真是這樣奇特的現象，作者應該會大做文章，不會只用一個字帶過，還留下輕易就能解釋為單純比喻的餘地。沒有人會把卷十五 299 行那些「飛快移動」的島，硬解釋成島嶼真的在移動。那裡的意思只是：船從旁飛馳而過時，那些島「看起來在移動」；埃俄羅斯的島也是如此──「它看起來在天際漂浮」。奧德修斯在島上住了一個月，看不出它有任何移動的跡象；他離開三個星期後又回到島上，書中也沒有絲毫暗示它換了位置。[註5]因此我的結論是：它和任何其他島嶼一樣固定不動；接著我便去尋找它。
> I should first correct a very general misapprehension. The word πλωτῇ (x. 3) has been unduly pressed into meaning that the island floated about, and thus changed its place. But if so singular a phenomenon were intended more would have been made of it. It would not have been dealt with in a single word, admitting easy explanation as mere metaphor. No one presses the "swiftly moving" islands of xv. 299 into meaning that the islands actually moved. All that is meant is that they "seemed to move" as the ship flew past them, and so with the island of Æolus—"it seemed to float on the horizon." It shows no signs of having moved during the month that Ulysses stayed on it, and when he returns to it after an absence of three weeks, we have no hint given of its having changed its place.[註5] I conclude, therefore, that it was as fixed as any other island, and proceed to look for it.

〔U0987 para〕
這並不難，因為烏斯蒂卡島立刻就自己送上門來。天氣晴朗時，從埃律克斯山可以隱約望見它；對一位把埃律克斯及其周遭當作整個天地的作者來說，它自然會在心中留下印象。冬季那幾個月，颳向特拉帕尼最猛烈的風正是從它那個方向吹來，特拉帕尼的作者把它選為風的家園，再恰當不過。它的距離──在利帕里群島以西相當遠，在伊薩卡以西更遠得多──完全符合要求。因此我接受它，接著去尋找萊斯特律戈涅斯人之地及其城市忒勒皮洛斯，地點應在西西里北岸、烏斯蒂卡與利帕里群島之間的某處。
> This is no hard matter, for the island of Ustica offers itself at once. In clear weather it can be faintly seen from Mt. Eryx, and would naturally have impressed itself on the mind of a writer to whom Eryx and its neighbourhood was all in all. It is in the quarter from which the winds blow most fiercely on Trapani during the winter months, and may fitly have been selected by a Trapanese writer as the home of the winds. The distance, a long way West of the Lipari islands, and a greatly longer distance West of Ithaca, is all as it should be. I accept it, therefore, and go on to look for the land of the Læstrygonians, and their city Telepylus, at some point on the North coast of Sicily between Ustica and the Lipari islands.

〔U0988 para〕
萊斯特律戈涅斯人，亦即「石匠」[註6]，這個名字就像西西里境內所有的地名與民族名一樣，是虛構的。如果西西里真有過叫這個名字的民族，修昔底德至少能打聽到一點關於他們的事；然而他明白宣稱（卷六第 2 章）他辦不到，還帶著一絲微笑，請讀者去找詩人，或去找他們能找到的其他資料來源。顯然，他只把他們當作詩人對西西里最古老居民的虛構；在他所知的居民中，沒有比西卡尼人更古老的了。
> The name of the Læstrygonians or Workers in Stone,[註6] like all names of places or people inside Sicily, is fictitious. If there had ever been any people really so called in Sicily Thucydides would have been able to find out some little, at any rate, about them; whereas he declares (vi. 2) that he cannot do so, and subrisively refers his readers to the poets, or whatever other source of information they can command. Clearly he does not believe in them except as poetical fictions concerning the most ancient inhabitants of Sicily—of whom none are known to him as more ancient than the Sicans.

〔U0989 para〕
可是，《奧德賽》的作者為什麼不可能是用她自己杜撰的名字──無論是庫克羅普斯人還是萊斯特律戈涅斯人──來指這些西卡尼人呢？萊斯特律戈涅斯人的城市名忒勒皮洛斯，肯定是虛構的。它的意思是「城門相距遙遠」，這只可能是事後（*ex post facto*）才取的名字：一座城市取名，遠在人們知道它日後會發展到多大之前。從這個名字我們只能看出，《奧德賽》的作者想讓聽眾明白，這是一座大城。
> But why should not the writer of the *Odyssey* be referring under names of her own coinage to these same Sicans, for both the Cyclopes and the Læstrygonians? The name of the Læstrygonian city, Telepylus, is certainly fictitious. It means "with gates far asunder," which can only be an *ex post facto* name: a city receives its name long before it is known what it will prove to be in the matter of growth. All that we can gather from the name is that the writer of the *Odyssey* intended her audience to understand that the city was large.

〔U0990 para〕
城裡的居民和庫克羅普斯人一樣，是巨人，也是吃人的妖魔。既然他們是巨人，我們就該去找巨石建築的遺跡；既然他們是吃人的妖魔，我們就該懷疑，他們和與之如此相像的庫克羅普斯人屬於同一種族。作者兩者都厭惡，而她看不起庫克羅普斯人的樣子，很像諾曼征服之後好幾個世代裡，諾曼人看不起撒克遜人那樣。
> Its inhabitants, like the Cyclopes, are giants and ogres. They being giants, we should look for remains of megalithic buildings, and being ogres we should suspect identity of race between them and the Cyclopes whom they so closely resemble. The writer hates them both, and looks down upon the Cyclopes much as the Normans looked down upon the Saxons for some generations after the Conquest.

〔U0991 para〕
庫克羅普斯人似乎已被征服，淪為化外之民；萊斯特律戈涅斯人卻不然。後者是個興旺而非常勤勞的民族，夜裡和白天一樣工作（卷十 84–86 行）。關於他們有一個可憐兮兮的史前小笑話，大意是：在他們的國度裡，一個人只要能不睡覺，就能賺兩份工錢。此外，他們既富裕又講究，鮮奶一批接一批地送來（卷十 82、83 行），而不像西西里的城鎮直到今天大多那樣，只滿足於早上送來的一批。除此之外，我從《奧德賽》裡再也搜集不到關於他們的什麼了。
> The Cyclopes appear to have been subdued and outlawed; not so the Læstrygonians. These last are a flourishing and very industrious people, who work by night as well as by day (x. 84-86). There is a poor little prehistoric joke about them, to the effect that in their country a man could earn double wages if he could only do without sleep. Moreover they were so wealthy and luxurious that they used to have relays of fresh milk (x. 82, 83), instead of being contented with a morning supply, as Sicilian towns generally are even at the present day. More than this I cannot collect about them from the *Odyssey*.

〔U0992 figure〕
〔圖版 13：切法盧從海中升起的城牆。〕
> WALL AT CEFALÙ, RISING FROM THE SEA.

〔U0993 figure〕
〔圖版 14：切法盧後山上的巨石遺跡。〕
> MEGALITHIC REMAINS ON THE MOUNTAIN BEHIND CEFALÙ.

〔U0994 para〕
那麼，在西西里北岸、烏斯蒂卡與利帕里島之間，我們能不能找到一個符合忒勒皮洛斯描述的地方？我可以毫不猶豫地說，切法盧能滿足我們的一切要求。那裡有兩處上好的巨石建築實例，兩者必定都比《奧德賽》早好幾個世紀。兩處相距約四分之三英里：一處是從海中升起的城牆，另一處是鎮後山丘上的一座建築，一部分是多邊形石塊砌成，極為粗糙，另一部分則是晚得多、異常精美的作品──這較晚的部分一般認為屬於邁錫尼時代。
> Can we, then, find a place answering to the description of Telepylus, on the North coast of Sicily between Ustica and the island of Lipari? I have no hesitation in saying that Cefalù will give us all we want. It has two fine examples of megalithic work. They must both of them be centuries earlier than the *Odyssey*. They are about three quarters of a mile apart, one, a wall rising from the sea, the other a building on the hill, behind the town, in part polygonal, and very rude, and in part of much later and singularly exquisite work—the later work being generally held to be of the Mycenæan age.

〔U0995 para〕
因此，這座城在當時想必規模很大。整個現代市鎮，老百姓都叫它波爾塔扎（Portazza），也就是 *portaccia*，意思是「寬門」；這太像是忒勒皮洛斯一名訛傳走樣的誤譯，我不能略過不提。
> The city, therefore, must have been for those days extensive. The whole modern town is called among the common people Portazza, *i.e., portaccia*, or "wide gate," which is too like a corrupt mistranslation of Telepylus to allow of my passing it over.

〔U0996 para〕
我認為，埃律克斯與切法盧無疑是在極其遙遠的年代，由同一種族的人建造的。除了剛提到的這兩處，我在西西里沒有見過其他巨石遺跡；我在切法盧西南約十五英里的科萊薩諾見過一些古代建築遺跡，一般稱為庫克羅普斯式，但非常可疑，奧爾西博士懷疑它們屬於拜占庭時代，我幾乎毫不懷疑他是對的。我也在陶爾米納附近的斯基索角見過少數幾處，既不醒目，也不確定。塞傑斯塔城牆殘存的那一點點，年代要晚得多，而我很難想像，《奧德賽》寫作的時候塞傑斯塔已經存在。[註7]我聽說泰爾米尼後方有一座庫克羅普斯式衛城的遺跡，路易吉．毛切里先生寫過一本關於它的專論，可在大英博物館找到。在科萊薩諾往內陸兩小時路程的伊斯內洛，不久前發現了一處年代極早的墓地；我相信，當地考古學者的努力，還會在西西里北部許多鮮為人知的山區地點或其附近發現其他遺跡；事實上，奧爾西博士最近就在敘拉古內陸約四十英里的潘塔利卡，發現了一座巨石房屋的遺跡。然而，迄今發現的巨石建築，沒有一處在重要性上比得上埃律克斯與切法盧的遺跡，看來也不太可能再發現這樣的遺跡了。
> There can, I think, be no doubt that Eryx and Cefalù were built in a very remote age by people of the same race. I have seen no other megalithic remains in Sicily than at the two places just named; I have seen remains of ancient buildings at Collesano about fifteen miles S.W. of Cefalù, which are commonly called Cyclopean, but they are very doubtful, and Dr. Orsi suspects them, I have little doubt correctly, to be Byzantine. I have also seen a few, neither striking nor yet certain ones, at Capo Schisò near Taormina. What little is left of the walls of Segesta is of a greatly later age, and I find it very difficult to think that Segesta was in existence when the *Odyssey* was being written.[註7] I have heard of the remains of a Cyclopean acropolis behind Termini, a monograph about which by Sigr. Luigi Mauceri will be found in the British Museum. At Isnello two hours inland from Collesano a very early necropolis has been discovered not long since, and the efforts of local archæologists will, I doubt not, lead to the finding of others at or near many of the little known mountain sites in the North of Sicily; Dr. Orsi, indeed, has recently discovered the remains of a megalithic house at Pantalica some forty miles inland from Syracuse. No megalithic work, however, that has yet been found will compare in importance with the remains at Eryx and Cefalù, nor does it seem likely that any other such remains will be discovered.

〔U0997 para〕
因此，考慮到切法盧相對於烏斯蒂卡與利帕里的位置、現存遺跡所證明的它與埃律克斯兩地建城者之間的親緣、它廣大的規模，以及它至今在老百姓口中的名字，我毫不猶豫地接受它就是萊斯特律戈涅斯人的城市；至於《奧德賽》所描述的港口細節與當地實況毫不相符，這並不影響我的看法。我不妨一提：1896 年春天，我和我的朋友 H．F．瓊斯先生在切法盧時，大約下午五點，遇到一群山羊進城擠奶；散步回來時，又遇到另一群剛擠完奶正要出城的山羊。這兩群羊必定相遇過，牧人們也必定像卷十 82、83 行那樣互相打過招呼，可惜我們恰好不在牠們相遇的地點。
> Bearing in mind, then, the situation of Cefalù both as regards Ustica and Lipari, the affinity between its founders and those of Eryx as evidenced by existing remains, its great extent, and the name it still bears among the common people, I do not hesitate to accept it as the city of the Læstrygonians, nor does it affect me that the details of the harbour as given in the *Odyssey* have no correspondence with the place itself. I may mention that when my friend, Mr. H. F. Jones, and myself were at Cefalù in the spring of 1896, we met a flock of goats coming into the town to be milked about five in the afternoon, and on our return from a walk we met another flock coming out after having been just milked. These two flocks must have met, and the shepherds must have saluted one another as in x. 82, 83, but unfortunately we did not happen to be at their point of meeting.

〔U0998 para〕
經過打聽，我們得知鮮奶在早上六點到八點、下午五點到七點分批送進城裡；提供消息的人還告訴我們，就他所知，沒有別的城鎮在早上那一批之外還有奶送。在人口三萬的特拉帕尼，傍晚就沒有鮮奶；雖然我不懷疑在巴勒莫、卡塔尼亞與敘拉古傍晚也能買到鮮奶，但即使在這幾座大城也不容易買到，而在較小的城鎮，就我所知，根本買不到。在羅馬，我問下榻旅館的老闆，山羊是不是傍晚也像早上一樣來擠奶，他說只有在特殊情況下才會這樣。
> On enquiry we found that relays of fresh milk come into the town from six till eight in the morning, and from five till seven in the afternoon, and were told that there was no other town known to our informant which had more than a morning supply. At Trapani, a town with 30,000 inhabitants, there is no evening supply, and though I have no doubt that fresh milk can be had in the evening at Palermo, Catania, and Syracuse, it is not easily procurable even in these large towns, while in smaller ones, so far as I know them, it is not to be had at all. At Rome I asked the landlord of my hotel whether the goats came to be milked in the evening as in the morning, and he said it would be only in exceptional cases that they would do so.

〔U0999 para〕
現在我只剩下卡呂普索的島要找。這座島在《奧德賽》中被稱為大海的「肚臍」（卷一 50 行）；這個比喻絕對不可能用在孤島以外的任何島上，也排除了戈佐或馬爾他，以及同一群島中另外兩座小島。卡呂普索獨自居住，與其他所有人隔絕──奧德修斯坐在海岸上哭泣、眺望海浪時，不能設想他看得到其他島嶼。此外，既然斯刻里亞已確定在特拉帕尼，奧德修斯如果遵照卡呂普索的指示，朝大熊星座航行並讓它保持在左手邊，那麼無論從戈佐或馬爾他出發，都永遠到不了那裡。因此，我們不得不另找一座島，它要更孤立，而且更偏向特拉帕尼的南南西方。
> I have now only to find the island of Calypso, which in the *Odyssey* is called the "navel" of the sea (i. 50), a metaphor absolutely impossible of application to any but a solitary island, and prohibitive of either Gozo or Malta, or of the other two small islands of the same group. Calypso lives by herself and is cut off from every one else—Ulysses cannot be supposed to have other islands in sight as he sits on the sea shore weeping and looking out upon the waves. Moreover, Scheria being fixed at Trapani, Ulysses could never get there from either Gozo or Malta if he followed the directions of Calypso and steered towards the Great Bear, keeping it on his left hand. We are, therefore, compelled to look for some other island, which shall be more solitary and more S.S.W. of Trapani.

〔U1000 para〕
潘泰萊里亞島滿足這兩個條件；誠然，天氣晴朗時，有時勉強可以看出非洲海岸──我就曾從潘泰萊里亞望見過──但它既不夠近，也不夠常見，不至於硬闖進奧德修斯的眼簾；埃律克斯山就更不會了，它有時也看得見，但極為罕見。毫無疑問，詩中把這座島寫得比實際上離斯刻里亞遠得多，但在這方面所取的自由，並不比詩歌一般被容許的更大。
> The island of Pantellaria fulfils both these conditions; true, in clear weather the coast of Africa can sometimes be just made out—I have seen it from Pantellaria, but it is not sufficiently near or sufficiently often seen to have obtruded itself on Ulysses' notice; still less so is Mt. Eryx, which can also be seen sometimes, but very rarely. No doubt the island is represented as being a good deal further off Scheria than it really was, but the liberty taken in this respect is not greater than is generally conceded in poetry.

〔U1001 para〕
因此，作者讓奧德修斯一離開特拉帕尼，就以一座她自己和聽眾都感興趣、因為就在他們眼界之內的島嶼展開航程；同樣地，她也以另一座同樣值得她和聽眾關注的島嶼結束這趟航程。
> As, therefore, the writer begins the voyage, when Ulysses is once clear of Trapani, with an island interesting to herself and her audience as being well within their ken, so she ends it with another island which has like claims on her and their attention.

〔U1002 footnote〕
此島在《奧德賽》中較常稱作薩墨（Same）。
> In the *Odyssey* more generally called Same.

〔U1003 footnote〕
法維尼亞納（Favognana）這個名字源自法沃尼烏斯（Favonius，西風），因為這股風從該島方向吹向特拉帕尼。不過，它也叫 Favignana，而且或許這個名字最常用。
> The name Favognana is derived from Favonius, this wind blowing on to Trapani from off the island. It is, however, also and perhaps most frequently called Favignana.

〔U1004 footnote〕
希臘原文：Τὴν γὰρ δή μιν ἄνωγε Καλυψὼ δῖα θεάων ποντοπορευέμεναι ἐπ᾽ ἀριστερὰ χειρὸς ἔχοντα.（因為卡呂普索，那神聖的女神，曾吩咐他，航行時要讓它保持在左手邊。）
> Gr. Τὴν γὰρ δή μιν ἄνωγε Καλυψὼ δῖα θεάων ποντοπορευέμεναι ἐπ᾽ ἀριστερὰ χειρὸς ἔχοντα.

〔U1005 footnote〕
普勒阿得斯星團我們可以不管，它們之所以被提到，只是因為它們出現在《伊里亞德》的那段文字裡（卷十八 486–489 行）；《奧德賽》的作者照搬這段，唯一的改動是拿掉許阿得斯星團與俄里翁，換上牧夫座。她非這樣做不可，因為她不能讓奧德修斯同時朝大熊星座和俄里翁航行，而她接著就要像《伊里亞德》那樣告訴我們，俄里翁在天空的另一邊。普勒阿得斯星團她就讓它留著了──我們之中有誰知道（且不說歲差）該到天空哪一方去找它們呢？──而詩中講得很清楚，奧德修斯是以大熊星座為準掌舵的。
> We may neglect the Pleiades, as introduced simply because they are in the Iliadic passage (xviii. 486-489) which the writer of the *Odyssey* is adopting with no other change than taking out the Hyades and Orion, and substituting Boötes. This she was bound to do, for she could not make Ulysses steer towards both the Bear and Orion, when she is just going to tell us, as the *Iliad* does, that Orion is on the other side of the sky. The Pleiades she has allowed to stand—which of us knows in what quarter of the heavens (let alone the Precession of the Equinoxes) they are to be looked for?—and it is made quite clear that the Bear is the constellation by which Ulysses is steering.

〔U1006 footnote〕
幾個月前，我在墨西拿看到一張印刷的傳單，上面寫著開往雷焦的船班時刻，傳單裡把義大利稱為「Terra firma」（堅實之地），彷彿任何島嶼都帶有一種不穩定的感覺。
> At Messina a few months since I saw a printed handbill about the hours when the boat would start for Reggio, in which Italy was called "Terra firma," as though a sense of instability attached itself to any island.

〔U1007 footnote〕
這個名字似乎源自 λᾶας（石頭）、τρυγάω（採收）與 αἶα（土地）；俄諾特里亞則源自 οἶνος（酒）、τρυγάω 與 αἶα。我讀過──但忘了在哪裡──說俄諾特里亞只是 Italia（義大利）的希臘譯名，而 Italia 源自 *vites*（葡萄藤）、*alo*（養育），以及某個相當於 αἶα 的拉丁字。現代義大利文的 *lastricare*，意思是「用石頭鋪路」，大概和 *Læstrygonian*（萊斯特律戈涅斯的）出自同樣的字根。
> The name seems derived from λᾶας, τρυγάω, and αἶα, Œnotria is from οἶνος, τρυγάω, and αἶα. I have read, but forget where, that Œnotria is only a Greek rendering of Italia, which is derived from *vites, alo*, and some Latin equivalent for αἶα. The modern Italian word *lastricare*, "to pave roads with stone," is probably derived from the same roots as *Læstrygonian*.

〔U1008 footnote〕
從埃律克斯山頂，應該望得見塞傑斯塔在夏日夕照中閃閃發光；我認為，詩中應該會有某種暗示提到它。
> Segesta would have been seen from the top of Mt. Eryx gleaming in the summer sunset, and I think there would have been some kind of allusion to it.

〔U1009 heading〕
第十章
> CHAPTER X.

〔U1010 para〕
關於奧德修斯航程的進一步細節，以證實其航程主要是一趟以埃律克斯山與特拉帕尼為起點和終點的環西西里航行
> FURTHER DETAILS REGARDING THE VOYAGES OF ULYSSES, TO CONFIRM THE VIEW THAT THEY WERE MAINLY A SAIL ROUND SICILY, BEGINNING AND ENDING WITH MT. ERYX AND TRAPANI.

〔U1011 para〕
前一章所說的，應足以確立奧德修斯所走的航線就是我地圖上標示的那一條；不過關於庫克羅普斯人、埃俄羅斯島周圍的城牆、塞壬、漂移之岩，以及與航程有關的其他事項，我還有些話要說；為了讓整體輪廓更寬廣、更簡明，我先把它們保留下來。
> What I have said in the preceding chapter should be enough to establish that the course taken by Ulysses was the one indicated in my map, but I have remarks to make on the Cyclopes, the wall round the island of Æolus, the Sirens, the Wandering Cliffs, and other matters connected with the voyages which I have reserved in order to keep the general view more broad and simple.

〔U1012 para〕
庫克羅普斯人住在埃律克斯山，這是我最有責任證實的一點，因為只要這一點獲得承認，再把斯庫拉與卡律布狄斯以及斯刻里亞都當作已經找到，其他地點便都會自然而然地落到我為它們標定的位置上，我不擔心會有什麼爭議。那麼，讓我們轉向法維尼亞納，姑且接受它就是奧德修斯獵山羊的那座島。
> The habitat of the Cyclopes on Mt. Eryx is the point which it is most incumbent on me to establish, for if this be conceded, and both Scylla and Charybdis, and Scheria be taken as found, all the other places fall so spontaneously on to the sites I have marked for them, that I fear no dispute concerning them. Let us turn, then, to Favognana and accept it for the moment as the island on which Ulysses hunted the goats.

〔U1013 para〕
我不禁納悶：作者為什麼那麼用心地召來一片濃重的黑暗，寫得那樣堂皇而詳盡，還藉神明之助把奧德修斯領進這座島的港口，而不讓他四處張望，看看那片「並不很遠」的陸地呢？
> Why, I wonder, was the author so careful to invoke a thick darkness, so pompous and circumstantial, and to pilot Ulysses into the harbour of this island by divine assistance, rather than permit him to look about him and see the land, which was "not very far" off.

〔U1014 para〕
答案其實「並不很遠」，一找就到。如果奧德修斯從食蓮人之地駛近時看到了西西里本土，他一定會沿著海岸一路走下去，那樣他就會被直接帶進特拉帕尼港。而雖然所有聽眾都知道，作者早已把特拉帕尼處理成奧德修斯整個航程的最後一站，奧德修斯本人卻不該在按部就班抵達之前，就對它有所知曉。
> The answer is "not very far" to seek. If Ulysses had seen the main land of Sicily as he approached it from that of the Lotus-eaters, he would have been sure to have followed it up, and in this case he would have been taken straight into Trapani harbour. Now, though the writer, as all the audience would know, had already dealt with Trapani, as the last point in all Ulysses' voyages, Ulysses himself ought not to know anything about it till he comes to it in due course.

〔U1015 para〕
波呂斐摩斯的洞穴──農民至今仍稱之為 *la grotta di Polifemo*（波呂斐摩斯洞）──位於特拉帕尼以北約六七英里處；奧德修斯必須被帶到那裡，而且可能的話，不能讓他看到一座八年後他將認得是斯刻里亞的城市，以免攪亂他自己或聽眾的心思。藉著一點霧氣，可以勉強設想他從法維尼亞納島前往皮佐隆戈岬與波呂斐摩斯的洞穴，而沒看見費埃克斯人的城市──只要他不特別使勁往那個方向看；但如果讓他一路駛到斯刻里亞的港口，即使是奧德修斯，也不得不注意到它。因此，最好讓某位神明把他帶到那座島，而完全不讓他看到任何其他陸地；這就是作者如此用心描寫那片濃黑的緣故。我們將會看到，後來（就故事設定的時間而言；在她詩的結構上則較早）她在完全相同的地方、出於相同的理由，又召來一片黑暗，讓奧德修斯完全無法判斷自己身在何處（卷五 291–294 行）──因為在那裡，同樣必須讓奧德修斯從特拉帕尼以南某處，到達城北另一處，而不看見這座城鎮。
> The cave of Polyphemus—still called among the peasants *la grotta di Polifemo*—was some six or seven miles North of Trapani; Ulysses had got to be taken there, and if possible, without unsettling either his own mind or that of the audience by showing him a city which eight years later he was to know as Scheria. He could, with the help of a little mist, be just supposed to go from the island of Favognana to the promontory of Pizzolungo and the cave of Polyphemus, without seeing the city of the Phæacians if he did not look particularly hard in that direction, but even Ulysses would have been compelled to take note of Scheria if he had been allowed to go on till he reached its harbour. It was better, therefore, that some god should take him to the island without letting him see any other land at all, and hence the intense darkness which the writer has been so careful to describe. We shall see that later on (as regards the supposed time, though earlier in the structure of her poem) she invokes a darkness which makes it impossible for Ulysses to form any idea of his whereabouts, in exactly the same place, and for the same reasons (v. 291-294)—for here too it is necessary to get Ulysses from a point South of Trapani, to another on the North side of it without seeing the town.

〔U1016 para〕
