把我那張埃加迪群島地圖（第 177 頁）和特拉帕尼與埃律克斯山地圖（第 164 頁）合起來看，就能看出奧德修斯從法維尼亞納到 *Grotta di Polifemo*（波呂斐摩斯洞）會走的路線──這是特拉帕尼附近最大的洞穴，比其他洞穴大得多，至今仍用來在夜裡圈養一大群綿羊。波呂斐摩斯擲出的兩塊巨石，應當看作：第一塊是阿西內利[註1]，第二塊是名為福爾米凱的兩座小島，由於兩島緊挨在一起，算作一塊。
> My map of the Ægadean islands (p. 177) combined with that of Trapani and Mt. Eryx (p. 164) will show the course Ulysses would make from Favognana to the *Grotta di Polifemo*—which is [by] far the largest cave near Trapani, and is still used as a place in which to keep a large flock of sheep by night. The two rocks which Polyphemus threw should be seen, the first as the Asinelli,[註1] and the second as the two small islands called Formiche, which, being close together, are taken as one.

〔U1017 para〕
因此，我在防止奧德修斯看見特拉帕尼的這份用心中，找到了一個相當有力的論據，支持這樣的看法：法維尼亞納就是奧德修斯獵山羊的島，而波呂斐摩斯的洞穴位於埃律克斯山。
> I find, therefore, in the care taken to prevent Ulysses from seeing Trapani, a considerable argument for the belief that Favognana was the island where Ulysses hunted the goats, and that the cave of Polyphemus was on Mt. Eryx.

〔U1018 para〕
另有一項跡象，雖然力道不大，似乎也暗示庫克羅普斯人當時仍是費埃克斯人的近鄰。
> Another indication, though one of no great strength, seems to suggest that the Cyclopes were still near neighbours of the Phæacians.

〔U1019 para〕
在卷六開頭，我們得知費埃克斯人過去住在一個叫許珀瑞亞的地方，「靠近無法無天的庫克羅普斯人」，但近年已遷到斯刻里亞──正如我說過的，斯刻里亞的意思是「日德蘭」。在一段我的縮譯沒有收入的文字中，阿爾基諾俄斯隨口說道（卷七 205、206 行），費埃克斯人與眾神的親緣，就和庫克羅普斯人及巨人族一樣近。且不說阿爾基諾俄斯身為波賽頓的孫子，算是波呂斐摩斯的半個姪子；庫克羅普斯人如此自然地浮上他的心頭，表示他們雖然不像從前那麼近，大概仍是他最近的鄰居。
> At the beginning of Book vi. we learn that the Phæacians used to live at a place called Hypereia, "near the lawless Cyclopes," but had of late years been moved to Scheria, which, as I have said, means Jutland. In a passage which I have not given in my abridgement Alcinous says casually (vii. 205, 206) that the Phæacians are as closely related to the gods as the Cyclopes and the giants are. Passing over the fact that Alcinous, being grandson to Neptune, was half nephew to Polyphemus, the spontaneousness with which the Cyclopes rise to his mind suggests that though less near than they had been, they were still about the nearest neighbours that he had.

〔U1020 para〕
巨人族不過是庫克羅普斯人換個說法，無疑就是建造埃律克斯那些雄偉巨石城牆的人的後裔。許珀瑞亞，即「上城」，大概位於埃律克斯山頂的東端；那裡至今仍可追尋到一道極古老的城牆，性質與山西端西卡尼城市的城牆完全不同。這道城牆的遺跡就在 *Ruccazzù dei Corvi*（烏鴉岩）上方，位於佩波利伯爵的園地內，最初是伯爵本人指給我看的。外地人除非由見過的人帶路，否則不太可能找得到。
> The giants are only the Cyclopes over again, and are doubtless the descendants of the people who built the noble megalithic walls of Eryx. Hypereia, or Upper-town, was probably at the Eastern end of the top of Mt. Eryx on a site where a very ancient wall, of totally different character to those of the Sican city at the West end of the mountain, may yet be traced. The remains of this wall are just above the *Ruccazzù dei Corvi*, in Count Pepoli's grounds, and were first shown me by the Count. A stranger is little likely to find them unless conducted by one who has seen them.

〔U1021 para〕
關於許珀瑞亞，我要重申：西西里境內的地名，除了一個部分的例外之外，全是虛構的，連特里納克里亞也是──修昔底德告訴我們，那是西西里最古老的名字──它變成了「特里納基亞」島，即「三尖」島；然而一出了西西里，地名便都是真實的。這讓人有理由認為，作者描寫的是真實的人物與真實的地方，只是用一層她知道聽眾一眼就能看穿的薄薄偽裝，把他們改頭換面。面具只摘下過一次，而且只有片刻：奧德修斯說他剛從西卡尼亞來（卷二十四 307 行）；但這不算數，因為奧德修斯這時被設定是在說謊。
> As regards Hypereia I would repeat that all the names of places in Sicily with one partial exception are fictitious, even Trinacria, which Thucydides tells us was the most ancient name of Sicily, becoming "the Thrinacian," or "three-pointed," island; whereas as soon as we are outside Sicily the names are real. This affords ground for thinking that the writer was drawing real people as well as real places, and travestying them under flimsy disguises that she knew her audience would see through. Once only is the mask dropped for a moment, when Ulysses says that he had just come from Sicania (xxiv. 307), but this does not count, for Ulysses is supposed to be lying.

〔U1022 para〕
舉例來說，庫克羅普斯人（Cyclopes）這個名字，也就是「圓臉」──這個字本身看不出還有任何別的意思，而我從利德爾與斯科特的辭典中看到，巴門尼德把月亮稱為 Cyclops──不過是作者取的綽號。如果 μήλωψ 意為「蘋果臉」，κύκλωψ 就該是「圓圈臉」。這個字本身沒有任何暗示，《奧德賽》裡也沒有任何暗示，說庫克羅普斯人是額頭中央只有一隻圓眼的民族。這樣顯著的特徵，不可能不言自明[註2]；而在最早的希臘藝術家眼中，它也並非不言自明，從他們總是給波呂斐摩斯畫兩隻眼睛就看得出來。要到羅馬時代，他才變成獨眼；而《奧德賽》給他的眉毛是複數（卷九 389 行），這就意味著眼睛也是複數。誠然，作者只弄瞎了一隻眼睛，但她可以指望如此嚴重的傷勢在另一隻眼睛引起交感性發炎，並認為燒烤了其中一隻，就足以讓兩隻都瞎掉。之所以只弄瞎一隻眼睛，不是因為波呂斐摩斯沒有兩隻眼，而是因為他那根木樁沒有兩個尖頭；作者既想不出怎麼把一件分叉的工具弄進洞裡，也想不出在這麼多人已被吃掉之後，剩下的人要怎麼揮舞它。
> The name Cyclopes, for example, or "round faces"—for there is nothing in the word to show that it means anything else than this, and I see from Liddell & Scott that Parmenides calls the moon Cyclops—is merely an author's nick name. If μήλωψ means "apple-faced," κύκλωψ should mean "circle-faced." As there is nothing in the word, so neither is there in the *Odyssey*, to suggest that the Cyclopes were a people with only one round eye in the middle of their foreheads. Such a marked feature does not go without saying,[註2] and that it did not go with the earliest Greek artists appears from the fact that they always gave Polyphemus two eyes. It is not till Roman times that he becomes monophthalmic, and the *Odyssey* gives him eyebrows in the plural (ix. 389), which involve eyes in the plural also. True, the writer only blinds one eye, but she could trust to the sympathetic inflammation which so serious an injury would excite in the other eye, and would consider that she had sufficiently blinded both by roasting one of them. One eye alone was blinded, not because Polyphemus had not got two, but because his pole had not got two prongs, and the writer saw neither how to get a bifurcated instrument into the cave, nor how to wield it now that so many of the men had been eaten.

〔U1023 para〕
所以我們可以確定，「庫克羅普斯人」的意思不過是「月亮臉」。波呂斐摩斯這個名字，在《伊里亞德》中是一位英雄的名字，或許是從那部詩借來，作為當地巨人（如果有的話）的化名。無論他原本叫什麼名字，也無論他是《奧德賽》之前就有的巨人，還是《奧德賽》的作者讓他誕生的，他如今以孔圖拉諾之名存在。我有時會想，這個名字會不會與希臘字 κόντος（長竿）和 οὐρανός（天空）有關，表示這個巨人高到能用他的手杖在天上戳個洞。果真如此，他在《奧德賽》的時代很可能就叫孔圖拉諾，或類似的名字，而奧德修斯弄瞎他所用的，正是他自己名字裡紀念的那根 κόντος。自《奧德賽》寫成以來，這個巨人已長大許多，*grotta di Polifemo*（波呂斐摩斯洞）再大，他也絕對擠不進去；因為他雙腳踩在平原上，肚子撐在埃律克斯山頂，身子前傾，把巨大的雙手伸進博納賈與科法諾之間的海裡捉鮪魚。有人打擾他時，他就從埃律克斯山頂扯下大石塊，朝所有打擾他的人砸去。
> "Cyclopes," therefore, we may be sure, means nothing more than "moon-faced." The name Polyphemus is found as that of a hero in the *Iliad*, and is perhaps a pseudonym for the local giant (if there was one) taken from that poem. "Whatever his name may have been, and whether he was a pre-Odyssean giant, or whether the writer of the *Odyssey* called him into being, he exists now under the name of Conturràno. I have sometimes wondered whether this name may have any connection with the Greek words κόντος and οὐρανός, and may indicate that the giant was so tall as to be able to knock a hole in the sky with his staff. Should this be so, his name, as likely as not was Conturràno, or something near it, in the days of the *Odyssey*, and it was with the κόντος commemorated in his own name that Ulysses blinded him. The giant has grown greatly since the *Odyssey* was written, and large as the *grotta di Polifemo* is, he could never get inside it; for he rests his feet on the plain while he props his stomach on the top of Mt. Eryx, and bending forward plunges his huge hands into the sea between Bonagia and Cofàno, to catch tunnies. When disturbed he tears great rocks from the top of Mt. Eryx, and dashes them at all who interrupt him.

〔U1024 para〕
重複並總結一下──這一點我不再多辯了：我認為庫克羅普斯人是埃律克斯山古老西卡尼居民被征服後的殘餘。他們之所以身材巨大，是因為他們在埃律克斯山上築城所用的石塊巨大無比。這些石塊幾乎看不出用硬化青銅或鐵製工具加工過的痕跡，只有腓尼基人局部重建城牆時，可能在這裡那裡修整過；腓尼基人重建時，部分是從頭（*de novo*）砌起，所用的石塊有些刻著腓尼基文字的採石工標記。[註3]古老的西卡尼工程有很大一部分被保留了下來，屬於真正的巨石時代，那時搬運比切割便宜；後來的世代沒有考慮到改良的切割方法帶來了怎樣的革命，便推論說用這麼大的石塊築城的人必定是大個子，其實他們只是精打細算的人。
> To repeat and to sum up, for I will argue this point no further; I take the Cyclopes to be the conquered remnant of the old Sican inhabitants of Mt. Eryx. They owe their gigantic stature to the huge size of the stones with which the walls of their city on Mt. Eryx were built. These stones show few or no signs of having been worked with a tool of hardened bronze or iron, save in so far as the Phœnicians may have trimmed them here and there when they rebuilt the walls, in part, *de novo*, with stones some of which bear quarry-men's marks in Phœnician characters.[註3] The old Sican work, a good deal of which has been allowed to stand, belongs to the true megalithic age, when it was cheaper to carry than to cut; later generations, failing to consider the revolution which the introduction of improved methods of cutting had effected, argued that the men who built with such large stones must have been large men, whereas in reality they were only economical men.

〔U1025 para〕
一旦切割變得比搬運便宜，我們在埃律克斯、切法盧，以及塞尼、阿爾皮諾、阿拉特里和南義大利許多其他城市看到的那些笨重巨塊，就過時了；但要等到砌層的種種不規則完全被拋棄、換成我們在敘拉古、塞利努斯、塞傑斯塔神廟以及歷史時期幾乎所有希臘與羅馬建築中所見的那種完美規則，還要經過很長的時間。事實上，我知道許多晚至西元十世紀的建築，砌層仍遠不規則；然而，幾乎就在切割變得較便宜之後，趨勢便是朝向砌層更規則、石塊更小，直到另一種巨石主義興起，與早期建造者的巨石主義截然相反──我指的是炫耀的巨石主義。
> As soon as it became cheaper to cut than to carry, the huge unwieldy blocks that we see at Eryx, at Cefalù, and at Segni, Arpino, Allatri, and many another city in Southern Italy, became obsolete, but it was still long before all irregularity in the courses was abandoned for that perfect regularity which we find at Syracuse, Selinunte, the temple of Segesta, and nearly all the Greek and Roman architecture of historic times. Indeed I know many buildings as late as the tenth century after Christ, in which the courses are far from regular; nevertheless the tendency, almost immediately after cutting had become cheaper, was towards greater regularity of courses and the use of smaller stones, until there arose another megalithicism, of a kind diametrically opposed to that of the earlier builders—I mean the megalithicism of display.

〔U1026 figure〕
〔圖版 15：H．費斯廷．瓊斯先生（身高 6 英尺 2 英寸）站在塞利努斯的石柱凹槽中。〕
> H. FESTING JONES, ESQ. (height 6 ft. 2 in.) IN FLUTE OF COLUMN AT SELINUNTE.

〔U1027 figure〕
〔圖版 16：埃律克斯山上的巨石城牆遺跡。〕
> REMAINS OF MEGALITHIC WALLS ON MT. ERYX.

〔U1028 para〕
塞利努斯有些用於西元前五世紀建築的石塊，比埃律克斯或切法盧最大的石塊還大；那裡有些石柱底部直徑十三英尺，我的朋友 H．F．瓊斯先生能站進柱子的凹槽裡；然而這些石頭上，用清晰卻看不見的字體寫滿了「榮耀」一詞，而埃律克斯的石頭上，則同樣清楚地寫著「節約」。我認為，任何真正未經金屬工具加工的巨石多邊形城牆，年代都不可能比西元前 2000 年晚很多。等到邁錫尼的阿特柔斯寶庫，或希沙利克那道《伊里亞德》時代的城牆（不過後者的砌層遠不如前者規則）這樣的建築出現時，切割──無論是用硬化青銅的鑿子，或者更可能在那時已經用鐵──已不再費事；然而，直到赫西俄德的時代──一般認為他不早於西元前 1000 年──人們仍未忘記那個「黝黑的鐵尚未出現」的時代。（《工作與時日》148–151 行。）
> There are stones at Selinunte, used in buildings of the fifth century before Christ, that are larger than the largest at Eryx or Cefalù; there are columns thirteen feet in diameter at the base, and in a flute of which my friend Mr. H. F. Jones could stand; but they are written all over in clear though invisible characters with the word "Glory," whereas the stones at Eryx bear not less clearly the word "Economy." I do not think that any true megalithic polygonal walls not worked with metal can be dated much later than 2000 B.C. By the time we reach such buildings as the Treasury of Atreus at Mycene, or the Iliadic wall of Hissarlik (which, however, is built in far less regular courses), cutting, whether with chisels of hardened bronze, or more probably by that time with iron, has ceased to be troublesome; nevertheless as late as Hesiod, who is not generally dated earlier than 1000 B.C., the memory of an age when "as yet swart iron was not," had not been lost. (*Works and Days*, 148-151.)

〔U1029 para〕
此外，我想請讀者留意，《奧德賽》對庫克羅普斯人的描述，與 1892 年 9 月 24 日《泰晤士報》刊載的現代西西里土匪的描述，吻合得何等緊密。
> Furthermore, I would ask the reader to remark how closely the description of the Cyclopes in the *Odyssey* tallies with that of the modern Sicilian brigands published in the *Times* of September 24th, 1892.

〔U1030 para〕
撰文者──斯蒂甘德先生──寫道：
> The writer—Mr. Stigand—says:—

〔U1031 quote〕
聖毛羅（S. Mauro）是土匪的大本營，是一座位於三千英尺高山頂上的城鎮，望得見傑拉奇西庫洛──另一座高度差不多的城鎮──以及同樣位於另一座山頂的波利納。山間連接這些城鎮的道路，只是騾子走的小徑。群山之中洞穴遍布，只有土匪和牧人知道。
> S. Mauro, the headquarters of the brigands, is a town on the top of a mountain 3000 feet high, and in sight of Geraci Siculo, another town of about the same height, and of Pollina, also on the summit of another mountain. The roads among the mountains, connecting these towns, are mere mule paths. The mountains abound in caves known only to the brigands and shepherds.

〔U1032 para〕
《奧德賽》這樣描述庫克羅普斯人：
> The *Odyssey* says of the Cyclopes:—

〔U1033 quote〕
他們既沒有議事的集會，也沒有法律，而是住在高山頂上的洞穴裡；每個人管自己的妻子兒女，對別人一概不理（卷九 112–115 行）。
> They have neither places of assembly nor laws, but they live in caves on the tops of high mountains; each one of them rules over his own wife and children, and they take no account of any one else (ix. 112-115).

〔U1034 para〕
我在特拉帕尼以北約十五英里的科法諾山上，一個叫 *le grotte degli Scurati*（斯庫拉蒂洞穴）的地方，見過好幾戶穴居人家。不過，他們身上毫無庫克羅普斯的氣息。他們的洞穴乾淨極了，舒適程度不輸英國最好的農舍。這些人非常親切好客，膚色偏白而不偏黑，說是英國人也完全過得去。我們從特拉帕尼帶了午餐，他們為我們拿出雪白的桌布和餐巾，還給了我們數不清的杏仁，用一點鹽和奶油炒過；最出人意料的是，他們拿來的鹽裡摻了細葉芹籽。在我們野餐大約兩週之後，科法諾發生了一起駭人的綁架案。一名巴勒莫商人被扣在山上整整一個月，直到付了贖金才獲釋；但我確信，我們那些穴居人與此事無關。這些洞穴留有史前人類的痕跡，是一些如今已經石化的古代餐食殘跡。
> I saw several families of cave-dwellers at a place called *le grotte degli Scurati* on Cofàno about fifteen miles North of Trapani. There was, however, nothing of the Cyclops about them. Their caves were most beautifully clean and as comfortable as the best class of English cottages. The people, who were most kind and hospitable, were more fair than dark, and might very well have passed for English. They provided us with snow white table cloths and napkins for the lunch which we had brought from Trapani, and they gave us any quantity of almonds fried in a little salt and butter; most unexpected of all, the salt they brought us was mixed with chervil seed. There was an atrocious case of brigandage on Cofàno about a fortnight later than our pic-nic. A Palermo merchant was kept a whole month on the mountain till he was ransomed, but I am sure that our cave-dwellers had nothing to do with it. The caves bore traces of prehistoric man by way of ancient meals now petrified.

〔U1035 para〕
值得注意的是，σπέος 或 ἄντρον（洞穴）這兩個字的各種形式，在《奧德賽》中出現四十五次，在《伊里亞德》中卻只有六次；考慮到後者篇幅較長，比例大約是 10 比 1。因此我們可以推測，《奧德賽》出自一個洞穴遍布的地區。
> It is noticeable that forms of the word σπέος or ἄντρον (cave) appear forty-five times in the *Odyssey* as against only six in the *Iliad*, which, allowing for the greater length of the last named poem, is about in the proportion of 10:1. We may surmise, therefore, that the *Odyssey* hails from a district in which caves abounded.

〔U1036 para〕
至於《奧德賽》作者告訴我們環繞埃俄羅斯島的那道「青銅城牆」，很難說究竟是不是純屬虛構。我們可以確定，它並非真是青銅造的，正如埃俄羅斯並非真是風之王；不過，在馬雷蒂莫島四周，凡是懸崖未能天然屏護之處，都曾有一道遠在《奧德賽》之前就建成的城牆；泰德斯科先生與斯帕達羅教授帶我看了它的遺跡，要不是他們幫忙，我是不會注意到的。我有時會想，作者會不會把這道城牆移到了烏斯蒂卡，就像我們後面將看到的，她把忒耳西忒斯背上的駝峰移到了歐律巴特斯的背上；但就我所知，烏斯蒂卡上並沒有這樣一道城牆的遺跡，法維尼亞納島或萊萬佐島上也沒有。馬雷蒂莫的古名是希耶拉；在海拔約 1,900 英尺處，有人帶我看了一片小台地上極為古老的城牆廢墟（並不醒目）；居民夜裡不敢穿越那片台地，據信那裡就是那個讓此島得名的祭祀所在之地。
> As regards "the wall of bronze" which the writer of the *Odyssey* tells us ran round the island of Æolus, it is hard to say whether it was purely fiction or no. We may be sure that it was no more made of bronze than Æolus was king of the winds, but all round the island of Marettimo, wherever the cliffs do not protect it naturally, there existed a wall of long pre-Odyssean construction, traces of which were shown me by Sigr. Tedesco and Professor Spadaro, without whose assistance I should not have observed them. I have sometimes wondered whether the writer may not have transferred this wall to Ustica, as we shall see later that she transferred the hump on Thersites' back to that of Eurybates; but no traces of any such wall exist so far as I know on Ustica, nor yet on the islands of Favognana or Levanzo. The ancient name of Marettimo was Hiera, and about 1,900 feet above the sea I was shown ruins (not striking) of exceedingly ancient walls on a small plateau which the inhabitants dare not cross by night, and which is believed to have been the site of the cult that gave its name to the island.

〔U1037 para〕
我對喀耳刻之島要說的話，推測成分太重，寫的時候不免戰戰兢兢。我注意到，喀耳刻的住所和歐邁俄斯的養豬場一樣，「位於從遠處就看得見的地方」（卷十 211 行）；我也注意到，奧德修斯是「越過山頂」去接近它的（卷十 281 行），正如他走近歐邁俄斯的小屋時一樣（卷十四 2 行）。我記得那些豬，於是忍不住想：雖然作者一開始告訴我們這座島地勢低平（卷十 196 行），但她終究擺脫不了自己身邊的環境，不知不覺地漂向埃律克斯山頂與歐邁俄斯的養豬場。她起初並不打算寫豬──從前被喀耳刻施法的人，變成的是狼和獅子──但聯想的力量太強，奧德修斯的同伴終究還是被變成了豬。
> What I have to say about Circe's island is so speculative that I write it in fear and trembling. I see that Circe's house is, like Eumæus's pig farm, "in a place that can be seen from far" (x. 211), and I see also that Ulysses approaches it "over the top of the mountain" (x. 281), as he does Eumæus's hut (xiv. 2). I remember the pigs, and I cannot refrain from thinking that though the writer tells us in the first instance that the island was a low one (x. 196), her inability to get away from her own surroundings is too much for her, and she is drifting on to the top of Mt. Eryx and Eumæus's pig farm. She does not mean to have pigs at first—the men whom Circe bewitched on previous occasions were turned into wolves and lions—but the force of association is too strong for her, and Ulysses' men are turned into pigs after all.

〔U1038 para〕
讓厄爾佩諾爾從喀耳刻的屋頂上摔下來，是一種非常奇特的殺死他的方式。如果他是在歐邁俄斯的小屋，她讓他從小屋上方的懸崖跌落，就是再自然不過的殺法了；後來的時代，阿芙蘿黛蒂的神廟就矗立在那座懸崖頂上。我懷疑──說來不無慚愧──喀耳刻屋子的牆，是被拿來充當這座懸崖的。
> The fall of Elpenor from the top of Circe's house is a very singular way of killing him. If he had been at Eumæus's hut she could not have killed him more naturally than by letting him tumble off the precipice that overhangs it, and on the top of which the temple of Venus stood in later ages. I suspect, not without shame, that the wall of Circe's house is made to do duty for this precipice.

〔U1039 para〕
在利帕里群島中的帕納雷阿島（古稱厄諾倪摩斯）上，有一個小海灣叫 La Caletta dei Zummari（祖馬里小灣），令人聯想到 Cimmerii（基墨里安人）的訛音；不過我已經說明過，不應試圖為冥府之行定出地點。
> On the island of Panaria, anciently Enonymus, among the Lipari group, there is a small bay called La Caletta dei Zummari, which suggests a corruption of Cimmerii, but I have already explained that no attempt should be made to localise the journey to Hades.

〔U1040 para〕
兩位塞壬可以──我得說──很有把握地安置在薩利納島上；此島古稱狄迪墨，得名於構成全島的兩座高山，每座高約三千英尺。在地中海這一帶，突如其來、極其猛烈的陣風，不時會從海邊所有的高處撲下來，例如特拉帕尼附近的科法諾，當地漁民之間就有一句俗話：「當心科法諾。」我的朋友 E．比亞吉尼先生──我不得不哀悼他在這十二個月內辭世──一再為我提供地方上的細節；他告訴我，有一次他幾乎被一陣從科法諾撲下的狂風掀翻，那陣風在天氣完全平靜時突然撲向他的船，只持續了短短幾秒。我認為，那兩位塞壬──在較早的希臘藝術表現中總是生有翅膀──正如她們的名字所暗示的，就是那些毫無預兆、從狄迪墨兩座山上撲下的呼嘯陣風，或者說空氣的雪崩。把這故事從詩翻成散文，意思就是：「靠近狄迪墨那兩座險惡山峰的人有禍了；海岸上散滿了船隻殘骸，他若聽見從山上吹下的風在他的索具間尖嘯，他的骨頭就要在岸上曬白。」讀者會記得，塞壬的島離喀耳刻的島非常近。
> The two Sirens can be placed with, I should say, confidence, on the island of Salina anciently called Didyme from the two high mountains, each about 3000 feet high, of which it consists. Sudden cat's paws of very violent wind descend at times from all high points near the sea in this part of the Mediterranean, as from Cofàno near Trapani, where there is a saying among the fishermen "ware Cofàno." My friend, Signor E. Biaggini, whose loss I have to deplore within the last twelve months, and who has furnished me over and over again with local details, told me that he once was all but capsized by a gust from Cofàno, that came down on his boat in perfectly calm weather, and lasted hardly more than a few seconds. I take it that the two Sirens—who are always winged in the earlier Greek representations of them—were, as indeed their name suggests, the whistling gusts or avalanches of air that descended without the slightest warning from the two mountains of Didyme. The story turned from poetry into prose means, "Woe to him who draws near the two treacherous mountains of Didyme; the coast is strewn with wreckage, and if he hears the wind from off them shriek in his rigging his bones will whiten the shore." The reader will remember that the Sirens' island is very near Circe's.

〔U1041 para〕
談到埃俄利斯群島時，史邁思海軍上將說：
> Speaking of the Æolian islands Admiral Smyth says:—

〔U1042 quote〕
無論是由於火山溫泉使海水變熱、武爾卡內洛的蒸氣、斯特龍博利不斷噴出的熱氣，還是這一切再加上一般的氣溫，可以確定的是，這一群島的天氣變化比鄰近地區更頻繁（《地中海》，帕克出版，1854 年，第 250 頁）。
> Whether from the heat of the water by volcanic springs, the steam of Vulcanella, the incessant hot injections from Stromboli, or all of them added to the general temperature, it is certain that there are more frequent changes in this group than in the neighbourhood (*The Mediterranean*, Parkers, 1854, p. 250).

〔U1043 para〕
他談到墨西拿海峽時又說：
> Speaking, again, of the Straits of Messina, he says:—

〔U1044 quote〕
也應提防猛烈的陣風；由於海岸多山，這些陣風有時會順著急流河床（Fuimare）直衝而下，對小船十分危險。我曾兩度痛心地目睹，忽視這些陣風的人因此喪命（《西西里及其島嶼》，默里出版，1824 年，第 111 頁）。
> Precautions should also be taken against the heavy gusts, which at times, from the mountainous nature of the coasts, rush down the Fuimare, and are dangerous to small vessels. I have twice, with grief, seen the neglect of them prove fatal (*Sicily and its Islands*, Murray, 1824, p. 111).

〔U1045 para〕
這位女詩人之所以在漂移之岩上遇到這樣的困難，是因為正如布特曼所說，這個故事並不是指哪兩座特定的島，而是源自旅人講述利帕里群島整體航行之難的傳聞。據說：「它們一座接一座逼攏過來，快得連一隻鳥都幾乎飛不過去。」此外，「火的狂風」（卷十二 68 行）暗示是在影射埃俄利斯群島普遍的火山性質。斯庫拉岩頂那片無論夏冬都不散去的烏雲（卷十二 74 行），就更是如此了。
> The reason why the poetess found herself in such difficulties about the Wandering Cliffs, is because the story, as Buttmann has said, does not refer to any two islands in particular, but is derived from traveller's tales about the difficulties of navigating the Lipari islands as a whole. "They close in upon you," it was said, "so quickly one after another that a bird can hardly get through them." The "hurricanes of fire," moreover (xii. 68), suggest an allusion to the volcanic nature of the Æolian islands generally. Still more so does the dark cloud that never leaves the top of Scylla's rock (xii. 74) neither in summer nor winter.

〔U1046 para〕
斯庫拉與卡律布狄斯的恐怖，和塞壬、漂移之岩一樣，是以同樣的詩意筆調誇大的。兩者之間根本不可能一箭射到，實際上相距約八英里。對一篇告訴我們有個怪物生著六個頭、三排牙齒的敘事，我們不該期望它有默里先生旅遊手冊那樣的精確。只要有幾粒真實就夠了，而這些真實確實存在：斯庫拉是一塊面向西方的高岩，卡律布狄斯則是（就那個時代而言）一個可怕的漩渦，位於海峽另一側、地勢較低的岸外，靠近通往一座「三尖」島的入口。根據史邁思海軍上將的說法，它就在墨西拿港外，現在叫作加洛法羅。史邁思海軍上將這樣描述它：
> The terrors of Scylla and Charybdis are exaggerated in the same poetic vein as the Sirens and the Wandering Cliffs. Instead of its being possible to shoot an arrow from the one to the other, they are about eight miles apart. We ought not to look for the accuracy of one of Mr. Murray's handbooks in a narrative that tells us of a monster with six heads and three rows of teeth. It is enough if there are a few grains of truth, and these there are: for Scylla is a high rock looking West, and Charybdis is (for those days) a formidable whirlpool, on the other side the Straits, off lower ground, and hard by the approach to a three pointed island. According to Admiral Smyth it is just outside Messina harbour, and is now called Galofaro. Admiral Smyth says of it:—

〔U1047 quote〕
對雷吉翁人、洛克里斯人、贊克勒人與希臘人那些沒有甲板的船來說，它必定十分可怕，因為即使在今天，小船有時仍會遭它威脅；我也曾見過好幾艘軍艦，甚至一艘七十四門砲的戰艦，在它的水面上被捲得團團轉；不過只要謹慎從事，一般說來並沒有什麼危險或不便需要擔心（《西西里及其島嶼》，默里出版，1824 年，第 123 頁）。
> To the undecked boats of the Rhegians, Locrians, Zancleans and Greeks, it must have been formidable for even in the present day small craft are sometimes endangered by it, and I have seen several men-of-war, and even a seventy-four-gun, ship, whirled round on its surface; but by using due caution there is generally very little danger or inconvenience to be apprehended (*Sicily and its Islands*, Murray, 1824, p. 123).

〔U1048 para〕
我毫不懷疑，加洛法羅就是卡律布狄斯故事凝聚而成的核心，但我在整個墨西拿海峽都見過海面相當大的擾動。這在很大程度上取決於風的情況：風有時會把海水堆積在義大利腳尖與西西里北岸之間的角落，於是墨西拿海峽便出現一股海流和強烈的漩渦。其他時候則幾乎看不出什麼。
> I do not doubt that the Galofaro is the nucleus round which the story of Charybdis gathered, but I have seen considerable disturbance in the sea all through the Straits of Messina. Very much depends upon the state of the winds, which sometimes bank the water up in the angle between the toe of Italy and the North coast of Sicily, on which a current and strong eddies occur in the Straits of Messina. At other times there is hardly anything noticeable.

〔U1049 para〕
奧德修斯在海上漂流九天，從卡律布狄斯被帶到卡呂普索的島，也就是潘泰萊里亞──我們可以確定，如果作者敢讓他再久一點不吃不喝，一定會讓他花更長的時間──這段且不談；我只剩下要把從潘泰萊里亞到特拉帕尼的航程，比先前更詳細一點地處理一下。奧德修斯離開潘泰萊里亞後的第十八天，朝著大熊星座航行，但讓它保持在左手邊，望見利呂拜翁海岸那條又長又低的線在天際升起。他似乎沒有看到法維尼亞納島，那座島想必就在近處；他沒看到或許倒好，因為他幾乎不可能認不出，這就是他八九年前獵山羊的那座島，而這可能會讓他困惑。
> Passing over the nine days drifting in the sea, which take Ulysses from Charybdis to the island of Calypso, *i.e*. Pantellaria—and we may be sure he would have been made to take longer time if the writer had dared to keep him longer without food and water—it only remains for me to deal at somewhat fuller length than I have yet done with the voyage from Pantelleria to Trapani. On the eighteenth day after Ulysses had left Pantellaria, steering towards the Great Bear, but keeping it on his left, he saw the long low line of the Lilybæan coast rising on the horizon. He does not appear to have seen the island of Favognana, which must have been quite near, and it was perhaps as well that he did not, for he could hardly have failed to recognise it as the one on which he had hunted the goats some eight or nine years previously, and this might have puzzled him.

〔U1050 para〕
不過，雖然允許他看到陸地，卻不能允許他沿著海岸走，否則，正如我已經解釋過的，他就會直接駛進斯刻里亞的港口；而作者特意要他在城的北邊遇見瑙西卡，並且在瑙西卡帶他進城之前，對斯刻里亞一無所知。因此，作者讓波賽頓恰在此刻瞥見他，掀起一場可怕的風暴；海天被雲層遮蔽，黑得如同夜晚（卷五 291–294 行），奧德修斯就這樣被帶到很遠的地方，看來是往北，因為等他被帶得夠遠了，雅典娜便讓北風吹送他兩天兩夜，也就是往南吹，直到他抵達那個可以讓瑙西卡遇見他的港口附近。
> But though he is allowed to see the land he must not be permitted to follow it up, or, as I have explained already, he would have gone straight into the harbour of Scheria, whereas he is particularly wanted to meet Nausicaa on the North side of the town, and to know nothing about Scheria till she brings him to it. Neptune, therefore, is made to catch sight of him at this moment and to raise a frightful hurricane; sea and sky become obscured in clouds, with a darkness as dense as night (v. 291-294), and thus Ulysses is carried a long distance apparently to the North, for when he has been taken far enough, Minerva blows him two days and two nights before a North wind, and hence Southwards, till he reaches the harbour near which Nausicaa can meet him.

〔U1051 para〕
《奧德賽》裡再沒有其他如此引人注意的黑暗，除了這一次和卷九 144 行那一次（第 188 頁已提過）。兩者發生在同一地方，出於同一理由──把斯刻里亞城保留到後面。
> There are no other such noticeable darknesses in the *Odyssey*, as this and the one of Book ix. 144, alluded to on p. 188. They both occur in the same place, and for the same reason—to keep the town of Scheria in reserve.

〔U1052 para〕
現在我已經證明，《奧德賽》中所有伊薩卡的場景，都極其忠實地取材自特拉帕尼及其近郊，斯刻里亞的場景也全都如此；此外，我也證明了愛奧尼亞群島其實取材自特拉帕尼外海的埃加迪群島；最後，我證明了奧德修斯的航程實際上始於特拉帕尼，又終於特拉帕尼。至於皮洛斯與拉刻代蒙，我只需指出它們遠在作者與聽眾的知識範圍之外，不必多談。
> I have now shown that all the Ithacan scenes of the *Odyssey* are drawn with singular fidelity from Trapani and its neighbourhood, as also all the Scherian; moreover, I have shown that the Ionian islands are in reality drawn from the Ægadean group off Trapani; lastly I have shown that the voyage of Ulysses in effect begins with Trapani and ends with Trapani again. I need not deal with Pylos and Lacedæmon beyond showing that they were far removed from the knowledge of either writer or audience.

〔U1053 para〕
兩處都沒有提到任何一項自然地貌。忒勒馬科斯與佩西斯特拉托斯乘一輛雙馬戰車，從費拉伊到拉刻代蒙，越過高峻、至今仍無道路的塔宇革托斯山脈──這段不可能的旅程，作者寫來毫無不安。她完全沒有暗示有山要翻越──由此我們可以推斷：要嘛她對皮洛斯與拉刻代蒙之間的地方一無所知，要嘛至少她的聽眾一無所知。不過，或許值得一提的是，雅典娜為了把忒勒馬科斯從伊薩卡送到皮洛斯而提供的西風，更適合把他從西西里送去。如果他是從真正的伊薩卡出發，北風會對他更有利；但雅典娜行事如此古怪，這一點我不打算深究。
> There is not a single natural feature mentioned in either case. The impossible journey of Telemachus and Pisistratus from Pheræ to Lacedæmon in a chariot and pair over the lofty, and even now roadless, range of Mt. Taygetus, causes no uneasiness to the writer. She gives no hint of any mountain to be crossed—from which we may infer, either that she knew nothing of the country between Pylos and Lacedæmon, or that at any rate her audience would not do so. It may, however, be remarked that the West wind which Minerva provided in order to take Telemachus from Ithaca to Pylos, was more suitable for taking him from Sicily. A North wind would have been better for him if he had been coming from the real Ithaca, but Minerva manages things so strangely that I would not press this point.

〔U1054 footnote〕
阿西內利（Asinelli）是一座單獨的小島，形狀很像一艘直朝法維尼亞納駛去的船。它沒有任何複數的成分，看不出為什麼要用複數的名字。那些「驢子」或「傻瓜」到底是誰？
> The Asinelli is a single islet much in the shape of a ship heading straight for Favognana. There is nothing plural about it, and one does not see why it should have a plural name. Who were the "asses" or "fools"?

〔U1055 footnote〕
維吉爾可沒有放過這一點。他寫道：
> Virgil does not let it pass unnoticed. He writes:—

〔U1056 footnote〕
「Cernimus adstantes nequidquam lumine torvo Ætnæos fratres,....」（我們看見他們站在那裡，目光兇狠卻無可奈何，那些埃特納的兄弟……）《埃涅阿斯紀》卷三 667、678 行。
> "Cernimus adstantes nequidquam lumine torvo Ætnæos fratres,.... Æn. III. 667, 678.

〔U1057 footnote〕
他稱庫克羅普斯人為「埃特納的」，因為他把他們安置在埃特納山上。
> He calls the Cyclopes "Ætnæan" because he places them on Mt. Etna.

〔U1058 footnote〕
我插圖中所示的那座稜堡裡沒有任何腓尼基工程，這裡的修補是中世紀的。
> There is no Phœnician work in the bastion shown in my illustration, the restorations here are medieval.

〔U1059 heading〕
第十一章
> CHAPTER XI.

〔U1060 heading〕
作者是誰？
> WHO WAS THE WRITER?

〔U1061 para〕
我相信，讀到這裡，讀者想必已不再懷疑：從我前面幾章看來，《奧德賽》出自一個女人之手；從後面幾章看來，這個女人除了特拉帕尼一帶，別的地方一概不識，因此必須認定她就住在那裡，也在那裡寫作。
> I believe the reader will by this time feel no doubt, from my earlier Chapters that the *Odyssey* was written by one woman, and from my later ones that this woman knew no other neighbourhood than that of Trapani, and therefore must be held to have lived and written there.

〔U1062 para〕
那麼，她究竟是誰？
> Who, then, was she?

〔U1063 para〕
這個問題，我無法像先前那樣滿懷把握地回答。到目前為止，我論證的要點都能一一證明；但在這個最引人入勝的問題上，我所能提出的，頂多只是推定而已。
> I cannot answer this question with the confidence that I have felt hitherto. So far I have been able to demonstrate the main points of my argument; on this, the most interesting question of all, I can offer nothing stronger than presumption.

〔U1064 para〕
我們要找的是一位特拉帕尼的女子：年輕、無所畏懼、任性，而且對女性的榮譽極為敏感、極力維護。她似乎出入於她那個時代、那個地方的上流社會，因為就《奧德賽》時代的西西里西岸而言，我們想像不出還有哪個圈子，比作者顯然熟悉的那一個更為優雅。她一定有閒暇，否則不可能完成如此宏大的作品。男人若是有其必要，或是赫赫有名，她也就容忍他們；但她從不為男人著迷，最喜歡的是拿他們取笑；反之，對美麗而有名的女子，她卻是由衷地關切。
> We have to find a woman of Trapani, young, fearless, self-willed, and exceedingly jealous for the honour of her sex. She seems to have moved in the best society of her age and country, for we can imagine none more polished on the West coast of Sicily in Odyssean times than the one with which the writer shews herself familiar. She must have had leisure, or she could not have carried through so great a work. She puts up with men when they are necessary or illustrious, but she is never enthusiastic about them, and likes them best when she is laughing at them; but she is cordially interested in fair and famous women.

〔U1065 para〕
我認為，應該到她以阿爾基諾俄斯王之名加以戲仿的那個人家中去找她。阿爾基諾俄斯和他妻子阿瑞忒的家世交代得那樣仔細（卷七 54–77 行），而詩中提到阿瑞忒時，總是帶著那樣溫暖的敬愛之情；這一切都散發著同一種真切的氣味──正是這種氣味，使學者們在《伊里亞德》卷二十 200–241 行所記埃涅阿斯的家世中，看出了真實的歷史和個人的關切。再者，她必定是這一家相當親近的成員，才能隨心所欲地取笑一家之主。《奧德賽》的其他 *dramatis personæ*（劇中人物）都沒有交代家世，唯一的例外是忒俄克呂墨諾斯；而他為什麼會出現在詩中，需要的解釋比我所能提供的還多。我只能指出，他出身顯貴，身分比下級神職人員還要高一等，與我們在詩中結識的其他任何人物都不是同一路人。
> I think she should be looked for in the household of the person whom she is travestying under the name of King Alcinous. The care with which his pedigree and that of his wife Arete is explained (vii. 54-77), and the warmth of affectionate admiration with which Arete is always treated, have the same genuine flavour that has led scholars to see true history and personal interest in the pedigree of Æneas given in *Il*. xx. 200-241. Moreover, she must be a sufficiently intimate member of the household to be able to laugh at its head as much as she chose. No pedigree of any of the other *dramatis personæ* of the *Odyssey* is given save that of Theoclymenus, whose presence in the poem at all requires more explanation than I can give. I can only note that he was of august descent, more than sub-clerical, and of a different stamp from any other character to whom we are introduced.

〔U1066 para〕
作者應到阿爾基諾俄斯王的家人當中去找，這一點還有一個佐證：詩中描寫這戶人家和這座花園時（卷七 81–132 行）興致勃勃，儘管通篇顯然帶著一種似笑非笑的誇張。描寫其他任何一戶人家，都沒有這種興致；作者在這裡顯而易見的樂在其中，更像是一個人在描畫自己的家，而不像在描述別人的家，或憑空虛構一個場景。請看她起先用的是過去式，一寫到這家的女人和花園，便不由自主地滑進了現在式。在她其他任何一段描寫裡，她都不曾這樣。
> The fact that the writer should be looked for in a member of King Alcinous' household seems further supported by the zest with which this household and garden are described (vii. 81-132), despite the obviously subrisive exaggeration which pervades the telling. There is no such zest in the description of any other household, and the evident pleasure which the writer takes in it is more like that of a person drawing her own home, than either describing some one else's or creating an imaginary scene. See how having begun in the past tense she slides involuntarily into the present as soon as she comes to the women of the house and to the garden. She never does this in any other of her descriptions.

〔U1067 para〕
最後，應該到這樣一個人當中去找她：對這個人來說，那位被描寫成瑙西卡的少女就是她的一切。詩中再沒有別的人物寫得這樣栩栩如生、這樣熱情洋溢；也再沒有別的段落，能像瑙西卡把家裡的衣物送到洗衣池去的那一段那樣意氣飛揚，脈搏與動作那樣富於彈性──連差不多都談不上──或者在把場景帶到我們眼前時，有一絲半點相近的清新。整個卷六，只可能出自一個全心全意投入其中的人之手。
> Lastly, she must be looked for in one to whom the girl described as Nausicaa was all in all. No one else is drawn with like livingness and enthusiasm, and no other episode is written with the same, or nearly the same, buoyancy of spirits and resiliency of pulse and movement, or brings the scene before us with anything approaching the same freshness, as that in which Nausicaa takes the family linen to the washing cisterns. The whole of Book vi. can only have been written by one who was throwing herself into it heart and soul.

〔U1068 para〕
前面這三段，全都建立在一個假設上：作者描畫的是真人。至於她描畫的是一個真實的地方、住在那個地方、別處一概不識，這一點已無須再議；我們之所以能把作者釘在這裡，是因為她緊緊依循那些自然地貌，而這些地貌至今大致仍是她當年描繪時的模樣。可是，阿爾基諾俄斯的宅邸和花園，以及他家中的成員，任何頭腦清醒的人連去找都不會去找它們的遺跡；因此，反對者大可以主張：作者固然看來是照著實物描畫那些永久的地貌，但我們並沒有證據顯示，她描畫房屋、花園和男男女女時，憑的不只是她自己的想像。
> All the three last paragraphs are based on the supposition that the writer was drawing real people. That she was drawing a real place, lived at that place, and knew no other, does not admit of further question; we can pin the writer down here by reason of the closeness with which she has kept to natural features that remain much as they were when she pourtrayed them; but no traces of Alcinous's house and garden, nor of the inmates of his household will be even looked for by any sane person; it is open, therefore, to an objector to contend that though the writer does indeed appear to have drawn permanent features from life, we have no evidence that she drew houses and gardens and men and women from anything but her own imagination.

〔U1069 para〕
姑且承認；但首先，凡是地貌的永久性讓我們得以確定她做了什麼的地方，我們都發現她盡可能緊扣自己的家鄉一帶；那麼，在證據過於短暫易逝、無法讓我們拿她問罪的情形下，自然可以推定她做的也是同一件事。其次，我們有充分的證據，證明作者不喜歡虛構。
> Granted; but surely, in the first place, if we find her keeping to her own neighbourhood as closely as she can whenever the permanency of the features described enables us to be certain of what she did, there is a presumption that she was doing the same thing in cases where the evidence has been too fleeting to allow of our bringing her to book. And secondly, we have abundant evidence that the writer did not like inventing.

〔U1070 para〕
她得天獨厚，擁有最高一等的想像力──那種想像力在於明智地挑選、審慎地運用取自生活的材料；一旦脫離了以自身環境為根據地的作戰基地，她就失手了，這也是必然的。這一點在敘述奧德修斯離開埃律克斯山和庫克羅普斯人之後種種歷險的那三卷裡，表現得最為明顯。書中描述的那些地方，沒有任何在地的細節；事實上，除了一份大致的行程之外什麼也沒有，而這樣的行程，她很容易從家鄉的水手那裡得來。她就靠這份行程勉強應付過去，再拿一些取自近處的零碎材料來補綴；可是，這裡完全看不到我們在《格列佛遊記》《魯賓遜漂流記》或《天路歷程》裡見到的那種逼真的虛構。當她借喀耳刻之口描述冥府的景象（卷十 508–515 行）時──她心知肚明，那必定和她見過的一切都不一樣──她便招架不住，給了我們一個毫無說服力的場景。幸好這裡用不著多少細節；可是在伊薩卡，需要的細節就多得很了，她自覺虛構非己力所能及，乾脆連試都不試，而是極其坦然地借用她熟悉的地方。
> Richly endowed with that highest kind of imagination which consists in wise selection and judicious application of materials derived from life, she fails, as she was sure to do, when cut off from a base of operation in her own surroundings. This appears most plainly in the three books which tell of the adventures of Ulysses after he has left Mt. Eryx and the Cyclopes. There is no local detail in the places described; nothing, in fact, but a general itinerary such as she could easily get from the mariners of her native town. With this she manages to rub along, helping herself out with fragments taken from nearer home, but there is no approach to such plausible invention as we find in *Gulliver's Travels, Robinson Crusoe*, or *Pilgrim's Progress*; and when she puts a description of the land of Hades into the mouth of Circe (x. 508-515)—which she is aware must be something unlike anything she had ever witnessed—she breaks down and gives us a scene which carries no conviction. Fortunately not much detail is necessary here; in Ithaca, however, a great deal is wanted, and feeling invention beyond her strength she does not even attempt it, but has recourse with the utmost frankness to places with which she is familiar.

〔U1071 para〕
她不只在自然地貌上盡量逃避虛構，在情節上也是如此。她可以詆毀埃律克斯山上的鄰居，正如特拉帕尼人至今仍在做的那樣，因為特拉帕尼人和聖朱利亞諾山（埃律克斯山今名）的人彼此毫無好感。她知道烏斯蒂卡：風從那裡吹來，她可以拿這一點做點文章；然後還有另一座偉大的西卡尼城市切法盧──這裡也可以發揮一下；可是到了利帕里群島，她的材料就快用完了。她有十年光陰要打發，不過，用八年或八年半也就可以充作十年了。在讓主角登上喀耳刻的島之前，她頂多只打發掉三個月；那麼，看在老天分上，就讓他在這裡至少待上十二個月吧──他也就真的待了這麼久。
> Not only does she shirk invention as much as possible in respect of natural features, but she does so also as regards incident. She can vilipend her neighbours on Mt. Eryx as the people at Trapani continue doing to this day, for there is no love lost between the men of Trapani and those of Mte. S. Giuliano, as Eryx is now called. She knows Ustica: the wind comes thence, and she can make something out of that; then there is the other great Sican city of Cefalù—a point can be made here; but with the Lipari islands her material is running short. She has ten years to kill, for which, however, eight or eight-and-a-half may be made to pass. She cannot have killed more than three months before she lands her hero on Circe's island; here, then, in pity's name let him stay for at any rate twelve months—which he accordingly does.

〔U1072 para〕
塞壬之島、斯庫拉和卡律布狄斯，很快就把她的本錢花光了；她知道西西里西岸陶爾米納以南沒有什麼能引起她興趣的東西──因為敘拉古（這一點我稍後再談）那時還只是科林斯人到來以前的一個小聚落，而在南岸，我們也沒有理由相信當時有什麼前希臘時代的城市。她自問：除了把奧德修斯關進她所能想到最孤絕的島上──一個他最沒有機會逃脫的島──讓他在那裡度過剩下的刑期，她還能怎麼辦？於是她選了一座島，正是現代義大利政府基於完全相同的理由，選來拘禁那些不能放任在外的人的那座島──潘泰萊里亞島；可是，她可不打算讓卡呂普索整整七年都背著奧德修斯所有手下這個包袱，所以他的船最好還是沉了吧。
> She soon runs through her resources for the Sirens' island, and Scylla and Charybdis; she knows that there is nothing to interest her on the West coast of Sicily below Taormina—for Syracuse (to which I will return) was still a small pre-Corinthian settlement, while on the South coast we have no reason to believe that there was any pre-Hellenic city. What, she asked herself, could she do but shut Ulysses up in the most lonely island she could think of—the one from which he would have the least chance of escaping—for the remainder of his term? She chose, therefore, the island which the modern Italian Government has chosen, for exactly the same reasons, as the one in which to confine those who cannot be left at large—the island of Pantellaria; but she was not going to burden Calypso for seven long years with all Ulysses' men, so his ship had better be wrecked.

〔U1073 para〕
用這種方法擺脫困境，顯示這位作者的虛構能力既不豐沛，也不成熟。她知道有薩丁尼亞，因為奧德修斯曾露出一抹陰沉的薩丁尼亞式冷笑（卷二十 302 行）。何不把他送到那裡去，再用取自特拉帕尼南邊而非北邊的細節來描寫它？或者，她根本不必給任何細節──她大可以只用半頁篇幅，就把他送上漫長無比、歷時不知多少年的旅程。她若是富於發明的性情，有的是辦法讓他忙個不停，用不著訴諸那種廉價又有失體面的權宜之計：先把他關在一座島上一年，再關在另一座島上七年。她既然替自己做了這樣一根絕佳的掛釘，可以在上面掛更多的遊歷和冒險，那麼，只要她的力氣或興致有一樣指向那個方向，她早就會掛上更多了。荷馬學者的老生常談之一，就是把奧德修斯的航程稱為「一部冒險遊歷的故事」。就某種意義而言確實如此，但我們從頭到尾都看得出來，作者正竭力把冒險遊歷壓到最少。
> This way out of the difficulty does not indicate a writer of fecund or mature invention. She knew the existence of Sardinia, for Ulysses smiles a grim Sardinian smile (xx. 302). Why not send him there, and describe it with details taken not from the North side of Trapani but from the South? Or she need not have given details at all—she might have sent him very long journeys extending over ever so many years in half a page. If she had been of an inventive turn there were abundant means of keeping him occupied without having recourse to the cheap and undignified expedient of shutting him up first for a year in one island, and then for seven in another. Having made herself so noble a peg on which to hang more travel and adventure, she would have hung more upon it, had either strength or inclination pointed in that direction. It is one of the commonplaces of Homeric scholars to speak of the voyages of Ulysses as "a story of adventurous travel." So in a way they are, but one can see all through that the writer is trying to reduce the adventurous travel to a minimum.

〔U1074 para〕
請看她一離開自己實際的環境，就何等捉襟見肘。只要她待在家裡，她就不會重複自己的情節，因為她有大量的材料可取；一旦離開家，任她怎麼努力，都無法那麼輕易地把事情寫得真切，而且容易退回到她已經用過的東西上。例如，在皮洛斯，她重複了雅典娜奇蹟般飛去的那一幕（卷三 372 行），而這一幕她在卷一 320 行已經用過。抵達萊斯特律戈涅斯人的國土時，奧德修斯爬上一塊高岩去偵察，除了從地面升起的煙之外，看不到任何有人居住的跡象──到了緊接著的下一個地方，他又爬上一塊高岩去偵察，而且顯然除了喀耳刻宅邸從樹林中央升起的煙之外，同樣看不到任何有人居住的跡象。他由一個出城來打一罐水的女孩領到阿爾基諾俄斯的宅邸（卷七 20 行）；這一幕又重複了一次（卷十 105 行）：奧德修斯的手下由一個出城來打一罐水的女孩，領到萊斯特律戈涅斯人安提法特斯的宅邸。作者虛構了一場睡眠，讓奧德修斯就在伊薩卡已清楚在望之際功虧一簣（卷十 31 行等處）。這不是高明的虛構，因為在這樣的時刻，奧德修斯最不可能覺得睏──可是，當他的手下對赫利俄斯的牛群垂涎三尺時，要再虛構別的東西來毀掉他，這份力氣對她來說實在太吃力了，於是她重複了（卷十二 366 行）那場已證明非常有效的睡眠。同樣，正如我前面說過的，每當奧德修斯眼看就要撞上特拉帕尼時，她就重複那場黑暗。卡呂普索既然已經虛構過一次，就得再充當一回喀耳刻──或者 *vice versâ*（反過來說也行），因為卷十大概寫在卷五之前。
> See how hard put to it she is when she is away from her own actual surroundings. She does not repeat her incidents so long as she is at home, for she has plenty of material to draw from; when she is away from home, do what she may, she cannot realise things so easily, and has a tendency to fall back on something she has already done. Thus, at Pylos, she repeats the miraculous flight of Minerva (iii. 372) which she had used i. 320. On reaching the land of the Læstrygonians Ulysses climbs a high rock to reconnoitre, and sees no sign of inhabitants save only smoke rising from the ground—at the very next place he comes to he again climbs a high rock to reconnoitre, and apparently sees no sign of inhabitants but only the smoke of Circe's house rising from the middle of a wood. He is conducted to the house of Alcinous by a girl who had come out of the town to fetch a pitcher of water (vii. 20); this is repeated (x. 105) when Ulysses' men are conducted to the house of the Læstrygonian Antiphates, by a girl who had come out of the town to fetch a pitcher of water. The writer has invented a sleep to ruin Ulysses just as he was well in sight of Ithaca (x. 31, &c.). This is not good invention, for such a moment is the very last in which Ulysses would be likely to feel sleepy—but the effort of inventing something else to ruin him when his men are hankering after the cattle of the Sun is quite too much for her, and she repeats (xii. 366) the sleep which had proved so effectual already. So, as I have said above, she repeats the darkness on each occasion when Ulysses seems likely to stumble upon Trapani. Calypso, having been invented once, must do duty again as Circe—or *vice versâ*, for Book x. was probably written before Book v.

〔U1075 para〕
這類一再出現、我只能稱之為「連續八度」的例子，顯示這位作者並不擅長虛構，因此除非萬不得已，不大會訴諸虛構。地方和情節兩方面既已說明，剩下要指出的只是：作者厭惡虛構，不僅及於地方，也及於人物。《奧德賽》的主要人物，一個個全是斯刻里亞人。涅斯托爾、奧德修斯、墨涅拉俄斯和阿爾基諾俄斯，每一個都是同一個人在扮演不同的角色；而阿爾基諾俄斯寫得格外興致盎然，正如我在前面某一章說過的，這暗示他們共同的原型，作者在阿爾基諾俄斯這個角色上，比在其他三個角色上都更熟悉。潘妮洛碧、海倫和阿瑞忒只是同一個人，而我始終懷疑，潘妮洛碧比另外兩位都更貼近原型。埃伊多忒亞和伊諾，兩個都是瑙西卡；喀耳刻和卡呂普索也是，只不過扮得老一點，而且做的是作者認為瑙西卡要是當了女神、有了自己一份家業時會做的事。對後面這兩位我比較沒有把握，因為她們兩個似乎都比較沒有那種仇視男人的心理──瑙西卡幾乎不打算掩飾這種心理。話雖如此，瑙西卡一找到合適的人選就打算嫁人；而且，正如我們已經看到的，喀耳刻和卡呂普索自己連一個男僕都沒有，喀耳刻還習慣把所有接近她的男人變成豬或野獸。再者，卡呂普索被迫送走奧德修斯，也只是有點生氣而已。她似乎並沒有為此心碎。因此，她們兩個對男人的喜愛，都不該認定超過了這部詩為了方便所需要的程度。連伊薩卡的平民百姓也是斯刻里亞人，他們對潘妮洛碧說的那些吹毛求疵、不懷好意的閒話（卷二十三 149–151 行），和費埃克斯人在卷六 273–288 行對瑙西卡說的如出一轍。
> Such frequent examples of what I can only call consecutive octaves indicate a writer to whom invention does not come easily, and who is not likely to have recourse to it more than she can help. Having shown this as regards both places and incidents, it only remains to point out that the writer's dislike of invention extends to the invention of people as well as places. The principal characters in the *Odyssey* are of all of them Scherian. Nestor, Ulysses, Menelaus and Alcinous are every one of them the same person playing other parts, and the greater zest with which Alcinous is drawn suggests, as I have said in an earlier Chapter, that the original from whom they are all taken was better known to the writer in the part of Alcinous than in that of any of the other three. Penelope, Helen, and Arete are only one person, and I always suspect Penelope to be truer to the original than either of the other two. Idothea and Ino are both of them Nausicaa; so also are Circe and Calypso, only made up a little older, and doing as the writer thinks Nausicaa would do if she were a goddess and had an establishment of her own. I am more doubtful about these two last, for they both seem somewhat more free from that man-hatred which Nausicaa hardly attempts to conceal. Still, Nausicaa contemplates marrying as soon as she can find the right person, and, as we have seen, neither Circe nor Calypso had a single man-servant of their own, while Circe was in the habit of turning all men who came near her into pigs or wild beasts. Calypso, moreover, is only made a little angry by being compelled to send Ulysses away. She does not seem to have been broken-hearted about it. Neither of them, therefore, must be held to be more fond of men than the convenience of the poem dictated. Even the common people of Ithaca are Scherians, and make exactly the same fault-finding ill-natured remarks about Penelope (xxiii. 149-151) as the Phæacians did about Nausicaa in Book vi. 273-288.

〔U1076 para〕
那麼，如果我們注意到：作者的虛構較為吃力之處，描寫的都是她家鄉以外的地方，而她寫得令人信服的時候，都是在她家中或家附近；那麼，我們就有極強的理由推定，那些較為自然流露的場景，與其說是虛構，不如說是作者對自身環境的描摹──顯然，無論她有時怎樣譏刺自己的環境，她都熱烈地眷戀著它。因此，我駁回我假想中那位反對者的假設，即作者描畫阿爾基諾俄斯的家和花園並非取自實物；而我想起奧德修斯的宅邸與阿爾基諾俄斯的宅邸完全吻合──連兩家所用女僕的人數都一樣──就更加堅定了這個看法。
> If, then, we observe that where the writer's invention is more laboured she is describing places foreign to her own neighbourhood, while when she carries conviction she is at or near her own home, the presumption becomes very strong that the more spontaneous scenes are not so much invention as a rendering of the writer's environment, to which it is plain that she is passionately attached, however much she may sometimes gird at it. I, therefore, dismiss the supposition of my supposed objector that the writer was not drawing Alcinous' household and garden from life, and am confirmed in this opinion by remembering that the house of Ulysses corresponds perfectly with that of Alcinous—even to the number of the women servants kept in each establishment.

〔U1077 para〕
既然範圍限定在一位與阿爾基諾俄斯家關係親密的年輕女子身上，我們就只需在兩者之間擇一：一是某個崇拜瑙西卡、想連同她周遭的一切一併頌揚她的家中僕從，一是瑙西卡本人（不管她的真名叫什麼）。或者，也有人會主張，這部詩是瑙西卡某位與她家極為親近的閨中密友寫的，例如狄馬斯船長的女兒。
> Being limited to a young woman who was an intimate member of Alcinous' household, we have only to choose between some dependant who idolised Nausicaa and wished to celebrate her with all her surroundings, or Nausicaa (whatever her real name may have been) herself. Or again, it may be urged that the poem was written by some bosom friend of Nausicaa's who was very intimate with the family, as for example Captain Dymas's daughter.

〔U1078 para〕
密友之說可以立刻排除。聰明到足以寫出《奧德賽》的那種意氣昂揚的女孩，不會謙退到把自己完全藏起來。假如是某位朋友寫了洗衣那一段，那位朋友一定也會跟著去洗衣服──尤其是她在瑙西卡的夢裡已經說過她要去。
> The intimate friend theory may be dismissed at once. High spirited girls, brilliant enough to write the *Odyssey* are not so self effacing as to keep themselves entirely out of sight. If a friend had written the washing day episode, the friend would have come a washing too—especially after having said she would in Nausicaa's dream.

〔U1079 para〕
再者，假如是家中僕從寫的，瑙西卡既不會忍心，也沒有能力把她完全抹去；因為，她若想這麼做，那位僕從──作者已證明自己是那樣大膽、那樣任性──必定比她的女主人更勝一籌。我們可以確定，特拉帕尼不會有兩個這樣的人物；而假如我們讓瑙西卡有本事使《奧德賽》的女作者屈從於她的意志，就非得這樣假定不可。在洗衣那一段的女性 *dramatis personæ*（劇中人物）之中，我們盡可能只見到瑙西卡、全是瑙西卡、除了瑙西卡別無他人；這個事實顯示，她本人就是我們要找的那位特拉帕尼年輕女子、阿爾基諾俄斯王家中的一員，而她在作品裡給自己留了一個小小的位置──一個寫這種作品的女孩必定會給自己留下的位置。
> If, again, a dependant had written it, Nausicaa would neither have had the heart nor the power to suppress her altogether; for if she tried to do so the dependant—so daring and self-willed as the writer proves herself to be—would have been more than a match for her mistress. We may be sure that there were not two such spirits in Trapani, as we must suppose if we make Nausicaa able to bow the will of the authoress of the *Odyssey*. The fact that in the washing day episode, so far as possible, we find Nausicaa, all Nausicaa, and nothing but Nausicaa, among the female *dramatis personæ*, indicates that she was herself the young woman of Trapani, a member of the household of King Alcinous, whom we have got to find, and that she was giving herself the little niche in her work which a girl who was writing such a work was sure to give herself.

〔U1080 para〕
家中僕從不敢像作者那樣，帶著那般戲謔的惡意取笑阿爾基諾俄斯。再者，在接下來的場景裡，僕從會讓瑙西卡本人有更多戲分。如今瑙西卡卻像一道參差不齊的毛邊被撇在一旁；她在卷八 460 行等處向奧德修斯道別，無論是她自己的話，還是奧德修斯的回答，細節都比一個善於逢迎的僕從所容許的少得多。她沒有聽奧德修斯講述他的歷險──其實她大可以躲在母親的羽翼下聽。她似乎根本不和家人一起用餐。她洗衣回來以後，是歐律墨杜薩把晚飯送進她自己的房間。阿爾基諾俄斯的宴會，她一次也沒有出席，比賽也沒有到場；而卷十三的送別場面中，她的缺席太過顯眼，除了有意為之，別無解釋。看來她似乎希望讀者明白：她是獨自過活的；不管她多麼喜歡和侍女們出遊，那些夜復一夜來喝她父親的好酒、糟蹋他家產的男人，她可一概不想沾惹。她說他們每晚最後一次奠酒，總是不獻給宙斯，而是獻給竊賊之神赫耳墨斯（卷七 137 行），這幾乎等於當面罵這些人是無賴了。順帶一提，瑙西卡向奧德修斯道別的方式如此古怪，正是讓我深信《奧德賽》從未經後人改寫的眾多理由之一。改寫這部作品的人，十之八九會把這場道別挪到卷十三去。
> A dependant would not have dared to laugh at Alcinous with such playful malice as the writer has done. Again she would have made more of Nausicaa herself in the scenes that follow. At present she is left rather as a ragged edge, and says good bye to Ulysses in Book viii. 460, &c., with much less detail, both as regards her own speech and that of Ulysses in reply, than a courtier-like dependant would have permitted. She does not hear Ulysses' account of his adventures—which she might perfectly well have done under her mother's wing. She does not appear to take her meals with the rest of the family at all. When she returns from washing, Eurymedusa brings her supper into her own room. She is not present at any of Alcinous' banquets, nor yet at the games, and her absence from the farewell scene in Book xiii. is too marked to be anything but intentional. It seems as though she wished the reader to understand that she lived apart, and however much she might enjoy an outing with her maids, would have nothing to do with the men who came night after night drinking her father's best wine, and making havoc of his estate. She almost calls these people scoundrels to their faces by saying that they always made the final drink offering of the evening not to Jove but to Mercury, the god of thieves (vii. 137) In passing, I may say that the strangeness of the manner in which Nausicaa says good bye to Ulysses is one of the many things which convince me that the *Odyssey* has never been recast by a later hand. A person recasting the work would have been tolerably sure to have transferred the leave-taking to Book xiii.

〔U1081 para〕
再者，瑙西卡要是容許自己以外的任何人，把那些她聲稱在費埃克斯人之間流傳、關於她的惡毒閒話塞進她嘴裡，那她就未免超乎常人了。由她自己說出來，她倒不在乎，因為她的聽眾，不論私下的還是公開的，都會知道那是她自己在說；可是換成僕從，就會被要求說得不要那麼辛辣。
> Nausicaa, again, would have been more than human if she had permitted any one but herself to put into her month the ill-natured talk about her which she alleges to pass current among the Phæacians. She would not mind saying it herself when her audience, private or public, would know that she was doing so, but a dependant would have been requested to be less pungent.

〔U1082 para〕
我承認──我先前也已承認過──這些論證並非絕對確鑿；不過，既然我可以說已經證明，我們必須在瑙西卡與另一位住在阿爾基諾俄斯王家中、或與這家極為親密的特拉帕尼年輕女子之間擇一，那麼我毫不猶豫地說：我認為瑙西卡本人比那位不知名的年輕女子，更可能是我們要找的作者。
> I admit as I have already done that these arguments are not absolutely demonstrative, but it being, I may say, demonstrated that we must choose between Nausicaa and some other young woman of Trapani who lived in, or was very closely intimate with, the household of King Alcinous, I have no hesitation in saying that I think Nausicaa herself more likely than this other unknown young woman to have been the writer we are seeking.

〔U1083 para〕
請讀者看看我的卷首插圖，然後說說：要相信這幅畫像的原型有能力寫出《奧德賽》，他是否會覺得有絲毫困難？只因為他碰巧確知她的事情，不過是她曾經和侍女們一起出門洗衣服，他就不肯這樣相信她嗎？瑙西卡在晴朗的春日早晨享受一趟出遊，還在洗衣池邊幫侍女們的忙；所以，假定她能寫出《奧德賽》是荒謬的。我斗膽認為，這種論證，除了在我們那一大群普通的荷馬派學者之間，不會有多少分量──儘管我極不願意把「荷馬派」這個詞與《奧德賽》扯上任何關係，哪怕是再遙遠的關係。
> Let the reader look at my frontispiece and say whether he would find the smallest difficulty in crediting the original of the portrait with being able to write the *Odyssey*. Would he refuse so to credit her merely because all he happened to know about her for certain was that she once went out washing clothes with her attendants? Nausicaa enjoyed a jaunt on a fine spring morning and helped her maids at the washing cisterns; therefore it is absurd to suppose that she could have written the *Odyssey*. I venture to think that this argument will carry little weight outside the rank and file of our Homerists—greatly as I dislike connecting this word however remotely with the *Odyssey*.

〔U1084 para〕
沒有任何藝術家能達到比他自身最好的實際環境更高的理想。凡是想在自己相當熟悉的事物上大加改進的，無論男女，無一例外都以失敗告終。可以安排、可以變化的只有附屬之物──本質可以取用，也可以捨棄，但不可以加以改善。設法取法自然、安於自然，只在終究無關緊要的細節上有所變動，這條路通往唐納太羅、喬凡尼．貝里尼、霍爾班、林布蘭和德霍赫；設法改善自然，這條路則直通米開朗基羅和 *barocco*（巴洛克），直通透納和現代劇院的布景幕畫。我的卷首插圖裡沒有一絲 *barocco* 的痕跡；因此我們可以確信，這樣的女子儘管無疑相對罕見，當時卻是存在的，而且數量相當可觀，正如今天在義大利一樣。假定她們當中有一位，不論是當車夫、當洗衣婦，還是當女詩人，都能同樣出色，這需要多大的想像力嗎？同時，我認為很有可能，《奧德賽》的作者其實又矮又不好看，她把自己描寫成高挑美麗，是在拿自己開玩笑，也是要讓聽眾跟著笑。她不論是相貌平平還是美麗動人，都不影響這個論證。
> No artist can reach an ideal higher than his own best actual environment. Trying to materially improve upon that with which he or she is fairly familiar invariably ends in failure. It is only adjuncts that may be arranged and varied—the essence may be taken or left, but it must not be bettered. The attempt to take nature and be content with her save in respect of details which after all are unimportant, leads to Donatello, Giovanni Bellini, Holbein, Rembrandt, and De Hooghe—the attempt to improve upon her leads straight to Michael Angelo and the *barocco*, to Turner and the modern drop scene. There is not a trace of the *barocco* in my frontispiece; we may be confident, therefore, that such women, though doubtless comparatively rare, yet existed, as they exist in Italy now, in considerable numbers. Is it a very great stretch of imagination to suppose that one among them may have shown to equal advantage whether as driver, washerwoman, or poetess? At the same time I think it highly probable that the writer of the *Odyssey* was both short and plain, and was laughing at herself, and intending to make her audience laugh also, by describing herself as tall and beautiful. She may have been either plain or beautiful without its affecting the argument.

〔U1085 para〕
但願我能找到一個人，給我任何一個正經的理由，說明瑙西卡為什麼不可能寫出《奧德賽》。過去五年來，凡是能攀上一點交情的學者，我都去纏著他，請他解釋《奧德賽》為什麼不可能是一位年輕女子寫的。有一兩位說，他們一點理由也看不出來，但在重讀這部作品之前，不願明確表態。一位知名而且非常能幹的作家說，他第一次聽說有人提出這個問題時，還以為是我自己的什麼奇談怪論，可是拿起《奧德賽》來，讀不到一百行，就發現自己在說：「哎呀，當然是這樣。」然而，大多數人都讓我明白，要不是他們另有要事，揭穿我這套主張的荒謬並不是什麼難事，只是眼下他們只好祝我早安、恕不奉陪了。他們什麼也沒給我；不過，說句公道話，我和他們談不到五分鐘，就看出他們根本拿不出什麼我早已不熟悉的東西。《奧德賽》太平易、太單純、太直截了當，學者們理解不了──正如我在《塞繆爾．巴特勒博士生平與書信》裡說過的，它要是難懂一點，早就被人懂了──不過，話又說回來，我也說不準；《伊里亞德》確實難懂得多，可是學者們誤解它的本事，似乎也綽綽有餘。
> I wish I could find some one who would give me any serious reason why Nausicaa should not have written the *Odyssey*. For the last five years I have pestered every scholar with whom I have been able to scrape acquaintance, by asking him to explain why the *Odyssey* should not have been written by a young woman. One or two have said that they could see none whatever, but should not like to commit themselves to a definite opinion without looking at the work again. One well-known and very able writer said that when he had first heard of the question as being mooted, he had supposed it to be some paradox of my own, but on taking up the *Odyssey* he had hardly read a hundred lines before he found himself saying "Why of course it is." The greater number, however, gave me to understand that they should not find it a difficult matter to expose the absurdity of my contention if they were not otherwise employed, but that for the present they must wish me a very good morning. They gave me nothing, but to do them justice before I had talked with them for five minutes I saw that they had nothing to give with which I was not already familiar. The *Odyssey* is far too easy, simple, and straight-forward for the understanding of scholars—as I said in my *Life of Dr. Butler of Shrewsbury*, if it had been harder to understand, it would have been sooner understood—and yet I do not know; the *Iliad* is indeed much harder to understand, but scholars seem to have been very sufficiently able to misunderstand it.

〔U1086 para〕
每一位學者都東一處西一處地讀過《奧德賽》的一兩卷；有些人讀過全書；少數人通讀過不只一遍；但我問過的人裡，沒有一個能告訴我奧德修斯有沒有姊妹──更別說她叫什麼名字了。到目前為止，我有幸在英格蘭遇見的人裡──在西西里我倒是遇見過這樣的人──沒有一個讓自己浸透在這部詩裡，而且是在不受任何一個與它有關的成見牽絆的情況下浸透其中。不做到這一步，就毫無用處。
> Every scholar has read a Book or two of the *Odyssey* here and there; some have read the whole; a few have read it through more than once; but none that I have asked have so much as been able to tell me whether Ulysses had a sister or no—much less what her name was. Not one of those whom I have as yet had the good fortune to meet in England—for I have met with such in Sicily—have saturated themselves with the poem, and that, too, unhampered by a single preconceived idea in connection with it. Nothing short of this is of the smallest use.

〔U1087 heading〕
第十二章
> CHAPTER XII.

〔U1088 para〕
這部詩的年代，並比較《奧德賽》所呈現的西西里西北部狀況，與修昔底德對同一地區已知最早時代的記述
> THE DATE OF THE POEM, AND A COMPARISON OF THE STATE OF THE NORTH WESTERN PART OF SICILY AS REVEALED TO US IN THE ODYSSEY, WITH THE ACCOUNT GIVEN BY THUCYDIDES OF THE SAME TERRITORY IN THE EARLIEST KNOWN TIMES.

〔U1089 para〕
認定《奧德賽》寫於特拉帕尼，將為這部詩的年代問題投下意想不到的光。我們永遠無法把它的年代確定在一百年左右的範圍之內，但我將試著說明：我們必須把它定在不晚於西元前 1050 年──即使晚，也晚不了多少──而且不早於西元前 1150 年。
> The view that the *Odyssey* was written at Trapani will throw unexpected light upon the date of the poem. We can never date it within a hundred years or so, but I shall attempt to show that we must place it very little, if at all, later than 1050, and not earlier than 1150 B.C.

〔U1090 para〕
我發現，我可以援引傑布教授的權威，至少在某種程度上支持這兩個年代中較晚的那一個。他寫道：
> I see that I may claim Professor Jebb's authority as to some extent, at any rate, supporting the later of these two dates. He writes:—

〔U1091 quote〕
至於《奧德賽》的年代，我們可以假定，最初的「歸返」早在西元前十一世紀便已在希臘本土寫成，而第一次擴充則在西元前 850 年以前完成。[註1]
> With regard to the age of the *Odyssey*, we may suppose that the original "Return" was composed in Greece Proper as early as the Eleventh Century B.C., and that the first enlargement had been made before 850 B.C.[註1]

〔U1092 para〕
我已經說明過，我為什麼不能承認《奧德賽》有任何部分寫於希臘本土；而且，儘管我承認這部詩顯然經過擴充，加上了卷一至卷四，以及卷十三 187 行至卷二十四──這一點我將在後面一章詳細討論──我卻不認為擴充出自別人之手，而認為出自這部詩原始形態的那位女作者本人。儘管如此，傑布教授的支持就其所及而言，我仍樂於援引，用來把《奧德賽》的起始定在西元前十一世紀。
> I have shown why I cannot admit that any part of the *Odyssey* was written in Greece Proper, and while admitting that the poem has been obviously enlarged by the addition of Books i.-iv. and line 187 of Book xiii.-xxiv., with which I will deal fully in a later Chapter—I cannot think that the enlargement was by another hand than that of the authoress of the poem in its original form. Nevertheless I am glad to claim Professor Jebb's support as far as it goes, for dating the inception of the *Odyssey* as in the eleventh century B.C.

〔U1093 para〕
我先說明，為什麼我認為《奧德賽》無論如何必定早於西元前 734 年。
> I will begin by giving my reasons for thinking that the *Odyssey* must at any rate be earlier than 734 B.C.

〔U1094 para〕
歐邁俄斯向奧德修斯講述自己的童年時（卷十五 403 行等處），說他出生在俄爾提基亞對面的「敘里埃的島」（the Syrian island），而我把它譯成「敘里埃島」（the island of Syra），依據的是「普緒里埃的島」（卷三 171 行）的類比──那無疑是指普緒里埃島。
> When Eumæus is telling the story of his childhood to Ulysses (xv. 403 &c.), he says that he was born in the Syrian island over against Ortygia, and I have rendered "the Syrian island" "the island of Syra," guided by the analogy of the "Psyrian island" (iii. 171), which unquestionably means the island of Psyra.

〔U1095 para〕
一座敘里埃島與一塊陸地俄爾提基亞連在一起，令人聯想到敘拉古；儘管事實上俄爾提基亞是一座島，而敘拉古既在島上，也在毗鄰的大陸上──因為，正如我已經說過太多次的，《奧德賽》中所有的西西里地方都經過喬裝，不管喬裝得多麼單薄。
> The connection of an island Syra with a land Ortygia, suggests Syracuse, in spite of the fact that in reality Ortygia was an island, and Syracuse both on the island and on the adjacent mainland—for as I have already too often said all Sicilian places in the *Odyssey* are travestied, however thinly.

〔U1096 para〕
我們接著讀到，「太陽的轉向」就在「那裡」──我想這可以引申為「那一帶」──於是，詩中暗指的是敘拉古[註2]這個印象就更深了。那麼，什麼是「太陽的轉向」？我查了利德爾與斯科特的辭典──對於他們的成果，沒有人能比我懷有更衷心的敬佩、更深切的感激──發現太陽的轉向是「至日，或回歸點，*i.e*.（即）仲夏與仲冬的轉折點」。就時間而言，這也許說得通，就地點而言卻不然。關於《奧德賽》這一段，我讀到的是：「太陽的轉向表示天空中的某一點，大概在西方。」
> The impression that Syracuse[註2] is being alluded to is deepened by our going on to read that "the turnings of the sun" are "there"—which I presume may be extended so as to mean "thereabouts." Now what are "the turnings of the sun"? I looked in Liddell and Scott, for whose work no one can feel a more cordial admiration, nor deeper sense of gratitude, and found that the turnings of the sun are "the solstices, or tropics, *i.e*., the turning points of midsummer and midwinter." This may do very well as regards time, but not as regards place. In reference to the Odyssean passage, I read that "the turning of the sun denotes a point in the heavens probably to the Westward."

〔U1097 para〕
可是，我們要的不是太陽在天空中的某一點轉向，而是在敘里埃和俄爾提基亞附近轉向，而且是以一種在別處不會出現的方式轉向。要達到這個目的，最簡單的辦法是假定：《奧德賽》的作者採用了一種我們搭火車旅行時常用的說法──我們說太陽轉過去了，現在從另一邊的窗子照進來──意思是鐵路轉了一個急彎，我們正朝新的方向前進。作者的意思無非是：在這兩個地方附近，陸地急轉了一個彎，因此沿著海岸航行的水手會發現，太陽換到了船的另一側，不再是先前那一側；我這樣想，想必沒有錯吧。
> But we want the sun to turn not at a point in the heavens, but in the neighbourhood of Syra and Ortygia, and to do so here in a way that he does not do elsewhere. The simplest way of attaining this end will be to suppose that the writer of the *Odyssey* was adopting a form of speech which we often use on a railway journey, when we say that the sun has turned and is coming in at the other window—meaning that the line has taken a sharp turn, and that we are going in a new direction. Surely I am not wrong in thinking that the author meant nothing more recondite than that near the two places named the land turns sharply round, so that sailors who follow it will find the sun on the other side of their ship from what it has hitherto been.

〔U1098 para〕
看一眼地圖就會明白：敘里埃與俄爾提基亞的組合所暗示的地點，可以由一個事實得到證實──就在它南邊不遠處，西西里的海岸陡然轉向，此後便朝新的方向延伸。其實，從普勒繆里翁岬角本身開始，海岸就已經急遽轉彎了。因此，應當把歐邁俄斯的話理解為：他出生在我們所知的敘拉古；而據他說，那裡當時是兩座小城的集合，居民不多。那似乎是個安靜悠閒的小地方，人人有足夠的吃喝，除了壽終正寢，沒有人會死，各種疾病一概不知。那裡做生意想必非常從容不迫，因為腓尼基人花了整整一年才把船裝滿貨，而那個時代最大的船，也大不到哪裡去。
> A glance at the map will show that the site which the combination of Syra and Ortygia has suggested is confirmed by the fact that shortly South of it the coast of Sicily turns abruptly round, and continues thenceforward in a new direction. Indeed it begins to turn sharply with the promontory of Plemmyrium itself. Eumæus, therefore, should be taken as indicating that he was born at the place which we know as Syracuse, and which was then, so he says, an aggregate of two small towns, without many inhabitants. It seems to have been a quiet easy-going little place, where every one had enough to eat and drink, and nobody died except of sheer old age, diseases of all kinds being unknown. Business must have been carried on in a very leisurely fashion, for it took the Phœnicians a twelvemonth to freight their vessel, and the largest ship of those times cannot have been very large.

〔U1099 para〕
這不是一個繁忙的新建聚落的寫照，而西元前 734 年的敘拉古正會是那樣的聚落。這番描寫更不適用於敘拉古後來的任何時代。作者處理更早的時代時，像莎士比亞一樣坦然地把它現代化：我從未在她身上察覺任何一絲考古的本能。因此我相信，她說的是她對自己那個時代的敘拉古所知的一點點，而那個時代早於科林斯殖民者的到來。我也認為很可能，她之所以讓歐邁俄斯來自敘拉古，是因為她覺得在奧德修斯的航程中，自己多少應該在敘拉古安排點什麼，可是在那種情形下，又不好在卡律布狄斯與卡呂普索的島之間打斷他的旅程。於是，她用了另一種方式把敘拉古帶進她的故事。
> This is not the description of a busy newly founded settlement, as Syracuse would be in 734 B.C. Still less will it apply to any later Syracusan age. The writer modernises when dealing with an earlier age as frankly as Shakspeare: I have never detected a trace in her of any archæological instinct. I believe, therefore, that she was telling what little she knew of the Syracuse of her own day, and that that day was one prior to the arrival of the Corinthian Colony. I think it likely also that she made Eumæus come from Syracuse because she felt that she rather ought to have done something at Syracuse during the voyage of Ulysses, but could not well, under the circumstances, break his journey between Charybdis and Calypso's Island. She, therefore, took some other way of bringing Syracuse into her story.

〔U1100 para〕
也許有人會主張：除了科林斯人建立的那個文明之外，我們沒有其他證據顯示敘拉古曾存在過任何相當程度的文明；就文字證據而言，這話沒錯，至少就我所知是如此；但我們有一種更具決定性的非文字證據。在敘拉古或其近郊發現的陶器與器具遺物，從羅馬時代以後一路不間斷地上溯到石器時代；而無數墓葬中出土器物的形制與材質，顯示至少從邁錫尼時代起，這裡就和伯羅奔尼撒有貿易往來。我有幸由奧爾西博士陪同參觀敘拉古博物館；在與西西里最早期歷史有關的一切問題上，沒有比他更審慎、更勝任的嚮導了；他一再強調，西西里東南岸──其實是整個東岸──與伯羅奔尼撒之間必定早已存在貿易，而那個年代是何等久遠。因此，那種人們罔顧修昔底德本人的說法、卻仍過於普遍地為人抱持的看法，認為在科林斯人到來以前，敘拉古或其附近沒有人居住，必須放棄；而我相信，我們可以確信，在歐邁俄斯的故事裡，我們得以一窺它在科林斯人以前的時代的情形。
> It may be urged that we have no other evidence of any considerable civilisation as having existed at Syracuse before the one founded by the Corinthians, and as regards written evidence this is true, so far at least as I know; but we have unwritten evidence of an even more conclusive kind. The remains of pottery and implements found at, or in the near neighbourhood of, Syracuse go back in an unbroken line from post-Roman times to the age of stone, while commerce with the Peloponese, at any rate from the Mycenæan age, is shown by the forms and materials of the objects discovered in countless tombs. I had the advantage of being shown over the Museum at Syracuse by Dr. Orsi, than whom there can be no more cautious and capable guide on all matters connected with the earliest history of Sicily, and he repeatedly insisted on the remoteness of the age at which commerce must have existed between the South East, and indeed all the East, coast of Sicily, and the Peloponese. The notion, therefore, too generally held in the very face of Thucydides himself, that there were no people living at or near Syracuse till the arrival of the Corinthians must be abandoned, and I believe we may feel confident that in the story of Eumæus we have a peep into its condition in pre-Corinthian times.

〔U1101 para〕
歐邁俄斯告訴我們的那兩個聚落，大概一個在普勒繆里翁岬角上，另一個在相距三、四英里的地方，今名科佐潘塔諾；奧爾西博士在這兩處遺址各發現了一個大型村落或城鎮──*borgo*（村鎮）──的墓地，並且在他注意到《奧德賽》中提到史前敘拉古之前，就已經把它們定年為西元前十二至十一世紀。在這兩處遺址出土的器具，有許多樣本可以在敘拉古博物館看到。據我所知，在敘拉古或緊鄰敘拉古的地方，並沒有發現其他史前墓地。
> The two communities of which Eumæus tells us were probably, one, on the promontory of Plemmyrium, and the other, at a place between three and four miles distant, now called Cozzo Pantano, on each of which sites Dr. Orsi has discovered the burying ground of an extensive village or town (*borgo*) to which he had assigned the date xii.-xi. centuries B.C. before his attention had been called to the existence of a reference to prehistoric Syracuse in the *Odyssey*. Many examples of implements found on these two sites may be seen in the museum at Syracuse. I did not gather that any other prehistoric burying grounds had been found at or in close proximity to Syracuse.

〔U1102 para〕
在上述地點留下墓地的人，究竟是後來被西克洛伊人取代的希臘人──正如西克洛伊人後來又被科林斯人取代──還是某一波更早、未見記載的移民潮中的西克洛伊人，這得留給奧爾西博士和其他人去判定；不過，沖刷西西里東岸的那片海的名稱（愛奧尼亞海），顯示東西西里沿岸曾經有過廣大的愛奧尼亞人聚落。再者，Aci 這個名稱──見於 *Aci reale*（阿奇雷亞萊）、*Aci Castello*（阿奇卡斯泰洛）和 *Aci trezza*（阿奇特雷扎），而且在平民口中如今總是念作 Iaci（伊阿奇）──暗示它有一個遙遠的愛奧尼亞源頭；因為我們可以假定，西元前 734 年以後，並沒有哪一次愛奧尼亞移民重要到足以讓西西里的海域、城鎮和島嶼以「愛奧尼亞」為名。讀者在下一章會再次想起：Ἰακός 的意思是「愛奧尼亞的」。
> Whether the people whose burying grounds have been found at the above named places were Greeks, who were displaced later by Sicels, as the Sicels in their turn were displaced by the Corinthians, or whether they were Sicels of an earlier unrecorded immigration, I must leave Dr. Orsi and others to determine, but the name of the sea which washes the East coast of Sicily points to the existence at one time of extensive Ionian settlements on East Sicilian shores. The name, again, Aci, which is found in *Aci reale, Aci Castello*, and *Aci trezza*, and which among the common people is now always sounded Iaci, suggests a remote Ionian origin—for we may assume that there was no Ionian migration later than 734 B.C. of sufficient importance to give the name Ionian to Sicilian waters, towns, and islands. The reader will be reminded in the following Chapter that Ἰακός means Ionian.

〔U1103 para〕
歐邁俄斯被拐走時年紀還很小，所以即使希臘語不是他的母語，幾年之內他也會變成希臘人了；不過我傾向於認為，假如《奧德賽》的作者心裡把他設想成西克洛伊人，她總會說點什麼。她似乎認為他生來就是希臘人。
> Eumæus was so young when he was carried off that even though Greek was not his native language, he would have become Grecised in a few years; I incline to think, however, that the writer of the *Odyssey* would have said something about his being a Sicel if she had so conceived of him in her own mind. She seems to think of him as a Greek by birth.

〔U1104 para〕
不過，西克洛伊人大概也說希臘語。位於義大利腳尖的忒墨塞，那裡的居民似乎確實不說希臘語（《奧德賽》卷一 183 行）；但我們並不知道他們是西克洛伊人。在普勒繆里翁和科佐潘塔諾都沒有發現任何文字；因此，我們可以依據的東西非常少。
> The Sicels, however, also probably spoke Greek. The inhabitants of Temesa, on the toe of Italy, do not indeed seem to have done so (*Od*. i. 183); but we do not know that they were Sicels. No writing has been found at Plemmirio nor yet at Cozzo Pantano; we have therefore very little to go upon.

〔U1105 para〕
不過，修昔底德對敘拉古細節的準確，證明他即使不曾親身到過敘拉古，至少也有辦法掌握西西里歷史的資訊；他顯然下過功夫，而他的聲譽也沒有任何一位史家能夠超越──假如我們可以接受他的說法（卷六第 2 章），即那場使這個國家從西卡尼亞（Sicania）改名為西西里（Sicelia）的西克洛伊人大舉入侵，發生在西元前 734 年之前約 300 年，那麼，我想我們可以放心地把《奧德賽》的年代往前推到西元前 1000 年以前。
> But postulating that we may accept Thucydides—whose accuracy as regards Syracusan details proves that even though he had not been at Syracuse himself, he had at any rate means of informing himself on Sicilian history—who is evidently taking pains, and whose reputation is surpassed by that of no other historian—postulating that we may accept his statement (vi. 2) that the great irruption of Sicels which changed the name of the country from Sicania to Sicelia took place about 300 years before B.C. 734, I think we may safely put back the date of the *Odyssey* to a time before B.C. 1000.

〔U1106 para〕
因為《奧德賽》完全沒有給人一種印象，彷彿整個西西里不久前才被西克洛伊人征服、蹂躪。就局部而言，埃律克斯山頂的那座城市確實曾被征服、推翻，這我們已經看過（《奧德賽》卷七 60 行）；但我將援引修昔底德以及其他證據來說明，在這件事上，勝利者比較可能是亞洲的希臘人，而不是西克洛伊人。這部詩顯示的是一個當下深沉太平、無所憂懼的時代；而作者唯一一次用西西里自己的名字稱呼它時，用的是它在西克洛伊人以前的名稱：西卡尼亞。[註3]那位服侍拉厄爾特斯的西克洛伊老婦人（卷二十四 211 行及他處），詩中提到她時，既看不出作者對西克洛伊人有任何惡感，也看不出他們是統治民族。最後，求婚者之一（卷二十 382 行）勸忒勒馬科斯把忒俄克呂墨諾斯和奧德修斯裝上船，送到西克洛伊人那裡去。如果作者心裡想的是真正的伊薩卡，那麼不論西克洛伊人在義大利還是在西西里，都只能走海路才到得了；可是我已經說明過，她除了她在特拉帕尼創造出來的那個伊薩卡之外，從未設想過任何別的伊薩卡；因此，忒俄克呂墨諾斯和奧德修斯得先上船才能到達西克洛伊人那裡，這個事實顯示，她設想中的西克洛伊人（偶爾移入的人除外）是在她自己這座島的範圍之外。
> For the *Odyssey* conveys no impression as though Sicily at large had been lately subdued and overrun by Sicels. Locally, indeed, the city at the top of Mt. Eryx had, as we have seen (*Od*. vii. 60), been conquered and overthrown; but I shall bring Thucydides, as well as other evidence, to show that in this case the victors are more likely to have been Asiatic Greeks than Sicels. The poem indicates a time of profound present peace and freedom from apprehension, and on the one occasion in which the writer speaks of Sicily under its own name, she calls it by its pre-Sicelian name of Sicania.[註3] The old Sicel woman who waited on Laertes (xxiv. 211 and elsewhere) is not spoken of as though there were any ill-will on the part of the writer towards the Sicels, or as though they were a dominant race. Lastly, one of the suitors (xx. 382) advises Telemachus to ship Theoclymenus and Ulysses off to the Sicels. Now if the writer had the real Ithaca in her mind, the Sicels could only have been reached by sea, whether they were in Italy or Sicily; but I have already shown that she never pictured to herself any other Ithaca than the one she had created at Trapani; the fact, therefore, that Theoclymenus and Ulysses were to be put on board ship before they could reach the Sicels, shows that she imagined these last as (except for an occasional emigrant) outside the limits of her own island.

〔U1107 para〕
前面的推理若被接受，那麼西元前 1050 年，大約就是《奧德賽》年代最晚可以穩妥設定的時間；再晚幾年也有可能，但我認為不大可能。不幸的是，這個年代將迫使我們把特洛伊的陷落，移到比公認年代早得多的時候。因為，人們會以為，《奧德賽》與《伊里亞德》之間，至少得隔上一百年。神話與史詩系列的發展，在《奧德賽》中留下了大量痕跡，其規模之大，使任何更短的間隔都不大可能。因此我得出結論：西元前 1150 年是我們應該為《伊里亞德》設定的最晚年代。
> If the foregoing reasoning is admitted, 1050 B.C. will be about as late as it is safe to place the date of the *Odyssey*; but a few years later is possible, though hardly, I think, probable. Unfortunately this date will compel us to remove the fall of Troy to a time very considerably earlier than the received date. For a hundred years is, one would think, the shortest interval that can be allowed between the *Odyssey* and the *Iliad*. The development of myth and of the Epic cycle, of which we find abundant traces in the *Odyssey*, is too considerable to render any shorter period probable. I therefore conclude that 1150 B.C. is the latest date to which we should assign the *Iliad*.

〔U1108 para〕
通常公認的特洛伊陷落年代是西元前 1184 年。這是根據修昔底德的一段話（卷一第 12 章）推算出來的：他說特洛伊陷落六十年後，皮奧提亞人被逐出阿爾涅，定居在原名卡德墨伊斯、後來稱為皮奧提亞的地方。他告訴我們，再過二十年，多利安人和赫拉克勒斯後裔成了伯羅奔尼撒的主人；可是，他既沒有確定這後一個年代──大概是因為他無法確定──所以也沒有確定特洛伊陷落的年代。
> The usually received date for the fall of Troy is 1184 B.C. This is arrived at from a passage in Thucydides (i. 12) which says that sixty years after the fall of Troy, the Bœotians were driven from Arne and settled in what was originally called Cadmeis, but subsequently Bœotia. Twenty years later, he tells us, the Dorians and the Heraclidæ became masters of the Peloponese; but as he does not fix this last date, probably because he could not, so neither does he fix that of the fall of Troy.

〔U1109 para〕
赫拉克勒斯後裔回歸並征服伯羅奔尼撒，一般接受的年代是 1104 年，[註4]但翻閱繆勒《多利安族史》[註5]第一卷第 53 頁的人就會看到，這個年代沒有任何值得考慮片刻的根據；而這裡的根據一旦落空，以西元前 1184 年為特洛伊陷落的年代，就完全沒有根據了。
> The date commonly accepted for the return of the Heraclidæ and their conquest of the Peloponese is 1104,[註4] but those who turn to Müller's *History of the Doric Race*,[註5] Vol. I., p. 53, will see that there is no authority for this date which is worth a moment's consideration; and with the failure of authority here, we are left absolutely without authority for 1184 B.C. as the date of the fall of Troy.

〔U1110 para〕
姑且承認西元前 1150 年是我們應該為《伊里亞德》設定的最晚年代，問題就來了：荷馬寫作的時間比特洛伊陷落晚了多久？格萊斯頓先生曾非常有力地論證一種看法：荷馬寫作時，距他所記述的事件只有四、五十年；照這樣看來，格萊斯頓先生為《伊里亞德》所定的年代，似乎不會比我所定的最晚年代晚多少，因為他看來並不質疑公認的特洛伊陷落年代，雖然他也沒說他接受這個年代。我若能在這一點上援引格萊斯頓先生的支持，那是再高興不過了，但再進一步，我就不敢指望了；因為《伊里亞德》給我的印象是：荷馬所寫的那個時代，對他來說，大約就像中世紀對我們一樣。
> Admitting for the moment 1150 B.C. as the latest date to which we should assign the *Iliad*, the question arises: How much later than the fall of Troy did Homer write? Mr. Gladstone has argued very ably in support of the view that he wrote only some forty or fifty years after the events he is recording, in which case it would seem that he must date the *Iliad* hardly at all later than the latest date to which I would assign it, for he does not appear to dispute the received date for the fall of Troy, though he does not say that he accepts it. I should be only too glad to find that I can claim Mr. Gladstone's support so far, but farther I cannot expect to do so; for the impression left upon me by the *Iliad* is that Homer was writing of a time that was to him much what the middle ages are to ourselves.

〔U1111 para〕
假如他真像格萊斯頓先生所設想的那樣，生活在離特洛伊戰爭那麼近的時代，他總該給我們一點暗示，透露特洛伊是怎樣陷落的；然而，除了城已陷落這個光禿禿的事實之外，看不出他知道得更多。他一再告訴我們這一點，但總是比我們預期的更簡短、更乾巴巴；而他對攻陷這座城的經過絕口不提，這就大大證明了：要不是關於 *modus in quo*（陷落方式）的一切記載都已湮滅──這意味著相隔了相當長的時間──就是暗示了一個事實；這個事實，我雖然常常認為有可能，卻幾乎不敢寫出來──我的意思是：特洛伊根本從未陷落，或者至少不是在特洛伊戰爭結束時陷落，而荷馬對此知道得一清二楚。
> If he had lived as near the Trojan War as Mr. Gladstone supposes, he would surely have given us some hint of the manner in which Troy fell, whereas he shows no signs of knowing more than the bare fact that the city had fallen. He repeatedly tells us this much, but always more curtly and drily than we should expect him to do, and his absolute silence as to the way in which the capture of the city was effected, goes far to prove either that all record of the *modus in quo* had perished—which would point to a very considerable lapse of time—or else to suggest a fact which, though I have often thought it possible, I hardly dare to write—I mean that Troy never fell at all, or at any rate that it did not fall with the close of the Trojan War, and that Homer knew this perfectly well.

〔U1112 para〕
《伊里亞德》的無窮精妙，幾乎和《奧德賽》的單純一樣深不可測──至少《奧德賽》的單純到目前為止已證明是如此。它的作者寫給一群他顯然瞧不起的希臘聽眾，順著他們的心意盡情愚弄他們，卻始終與風向保持足夠的距離，以免翻船；這樣一位作者，會非常小心地告訴他們──請容我用個時代錯置的比喻──拿破崙打贏了滑鐵盧之役，儘管他心裡很清楚，打贏的是威靈頓。可以確定的是，希臘人自己當中，連一個勉強說得過去的特洛伊陷落故事都沒有；一切說得過去的情節，都在他們燒掉營帳、鎩羽揚帆而去時結束了（《奧德賽》卷八 500、501 行）──另見普羅克洛斯殘篇中所載的《小伊里亞德》提要。木馬那個荒誕的故事，只是更突顯了一個事實：人們不知道什麼更合理的說法。
> The infinite subtlety of the *Iliad* is almost as unfathomable as the simplicity of the *Odyssey* has so far proved itself to be, and its author, writing for a Greek audience whom he obviously despised, and whom he was fooling to the top of their bent though always sailing far enough off the wind to avoid disaster, would take very good care to tell them that—if I may be allowed the anachronism—Napoleon won the battle of Waterloo, though he very well knew that it was won by Wellington. It is certain that no even tolerably plausible account of the fall of Troy existed among the Greeks themselves; all plausibility ends with their burning their tents and sailing away baffled (*Od*. viii. 500, 501)—see also the epitome of the *Little Iliad*, given in the fragment of Proclus. The wild story of the wooden horse only emphasises the fact that nothing more reasonable was known.

〔U1113 figure〕
〔圖版 17：希沙利克的城牆，顯示風化的石層與受到保護的石層之間的對比〕
> WALL AT HISSARLIK, SHOWING CONTRAST BETWEEN WEATHERED AND PROTECTED COURSES.

〔U1114 figure〕
〔圖版 18：《伊里亞德》中的城牆〕
> THE ILIADIC WALL.

〔U1115 para〕
不過，讓我們假定特洛伊確實陷落了，而荷馬的沉默，是因為關於它如何陷落的記載已全部失傳。這樣的話，人們會以為，特洛伊陷落與《伊里亞德》寫成之間，必定隔了兩百年，甚至三百年。我們就把這段時間定為和英國議會戰爭到今天的距離一樣長吧。這會把特洛伊戰爭推回到西元前 1400 年左右；而如果我們接受荷馬的說法，即特洛伊的城牆（*i.e*.（即）德普菲爾德博士在 1893 年發掘的那道城牆──它就是《伊里亞德》中的城牆，這一點可以視為確定）是在普里阿摩斯的父親拉俄墨東時代建造的，那麼這道城牆大約應定在西元前 1450 年。我還可以補充一點：在我看來，它的年代比邁錫尼那座所謂的阿特柔斯寶庫略早一些，因此又比冠上克呂泰涅斯特拉之名的那座更早。
> But let us suppose that Troy fell, and that Homer's silence was dictated by the loss of all record as to the manner of its falling. In this case one would think that two, or even three, hundred years must have passed between the fall of Troy and the writing of the *Iliad*. Let us make it the same distance of time as that between the Parliamentary Wars and the present day. This would throw back the Trojan War to about 1400 B.C., and if we accept Homer's statement that the wall of Troy (*i.e*. that which Dr. Dörpfeld excavated in 1893—for that this is the Iliadic wall may be taken as certain) was built in the time of Priam's father Laomedon, we should date the wall roughly as 1450 B.C. I may add, that it seems to me to be of somewhat earlier date than the so-called Treasury of Atreus at Mycene, and hence still earlier than that which bears the name of Clytemnestra.

〔U1116 para〕
我從這方面的最新著作[註6]得知，德普菲爾德博士把它定在西元前 1500 年到 1000 年之間。我知道，拿遙遠國度的其他城牆來類比，為一道城牆定年，是多麼危險的事，因為那些城牆本身的年代就無法精確確定；不過，我既看過《伊里亞德》中的城牆，也看過提任斯和邁錫尼的城牆，以及拉丁人與沃爾西人城市現存的大部分城牆，我會說，特洛伊的城牆比巨石時代的城牆晚得多，但仍絕未擺脫巨石建築者的傳統。我大致會把它定在不晚於西元前 1300 年、也不大可能早於西元前 1500 年。[註7]
> I see by the latest work on the subject[註6] that Dr. Dörpfeld dates it as between 1500 and 1000 B.C. I know how perilous it is to date a wall by the analogy of other walls in distant countries, which walls are themselves undateable with anything like precision, but having seen the Iliadic wall as also those of Tiryns and Mycene, as well as most of those that remain in the Latin and Volscian cities, I should say that the wall of Troy was much later than those of the megalithic ages, but still not by any means free from the traditions of megalithic builders. I should date it roughly at not later than 1300 B.C. and hardly earlier than 1500 B.C.[註7]

〔U1117 para〕
不過，我將把《伊里亞德》中的城牆定在西元前 1400 年。這樣一來，特洛伊戰爭便應假定發生於西元前 1360 至 1350 年；《伊里亞德》寫於 1150 年左右；《奧德賽》則寫於西元前 1050 年左右。這很勉強，我倒樂意把《伊里亞德》中的城牆推回到德普菲爾德博士所定兩個年代中較早的那一個，可是精確是談不上的；西元前 1400 年大概和我們可能得到的任何年代一樣接近真相，而且能讓下列幾項彼此吻合：由特洛伊城牆可推得的考古證據、《伊里亞德》和《奧德賽》的內證、修昔底德所說西克洛伊人最後也最大的一次入侵發生在西元前 1030 年左右，以及我們關於《奧德賽》寫於這個年代之前的結論。
> I will, however, date the Iliadic wall as 1400 B.C. The Trojan war will then be supposed to have taken place from 1360-1350 B.C.; the writing of the *Iliad* will be about 1150; and that of the *Odyssey* about 1050 B.C.
