This is a tight fit, and I should be glad to throw the Iliadic wall back to the earlier of the two dates between which Dr. Dörpfeld has placed it, but precision is out of the question; 1400 B.C. will be as near the truth as anything that we are likely to get, and will bring the archæological evidence as derivable from the wall of Troy, the internal evidence of the *Iliad* and *Odyssey*, the statement of Thucydides that the last and greatest inroad of the Sicels occurred about 1030 B.C., and our conclusion that the *Odyssey* was written before that date, into line with one another.

〔U1118 para〕
西元前 1050 年這個年代，可以解釋為什麼《奧德賽》完全沒有提到烏提卡──在少數幾個特別的日子裡，從埃律克斯山可以望見烏提卡附近的陸地。《奧德賽》知道腓尼基人，對他們既厭惡又不信任，但看來並不畏懼他們；假如如此強大的航海民族已經在作者住處附近落腳，她一定會畏懼的。說到底，她對腓尼基人似乎所知不多，因為她在卷四 83 行讓墨涅拉俄斯說，他去過塞浦路斯、腓尼基和埃及人那裡，接著在下一行又加上他也去過埃塞俄比亞人和西頓人那裡，彷彿她不知道西頓是腓尼基的城市。
> The date 1050 B.C. will explain the absence of all allusion in the *Odyssey* to Utica, the land near which, on certain rare days, can be seen from Mt. Eryx. The Phœnicians are known in the *Odyssey*, disliked and distrusted, but they do not seem to be feared as they would surely be if so powerful a maritime nation were already established so near the writer's own abode. She does not seem to know much about the Phœnicians after all, for in iv. 83 she makes Menelaus say that he had gone to Cyprus, Phœnicia, and the Egyptians, and in the next line she adds that he had also been to the Ethiopians and the Sidonians, as though she was not aware that Sidon was a Phœnician city.

〔U1119 para〕
忒勒馬科斯從皮洛斯回程時，詩中完全沒有提到奧林匹亞；最自然的解釋是假定奧林匹亞當時還沒有出名。雅典的主要英雄，看來是已知最早的全民崇拜對象，我指的是厄瑞克透斯（卷七 81 行）；較晚、但仍然很早的忒修斯崇拜，則沒有提及。凡是已知發生在西元前 1100 年以後的希臘歷史事件，詩中一件也沒有提到，哪怕是隱約的影射；某一件特定事件沒有提到，固然可以用漠不關心或遺忘來解釋，但完全沒有提到任何事件，我得說，這強烈暗示：那些本可望提到其中某一件的事件，當時都還沒有發生。
> The absence of all allusion to Olympia when Telemachus was on his return from Pylos is most naturally explained by supposing that Olympia was not yet famous. The principal hero at Athens appears to be the earliest known object of the national cult, I mean Erechtheus (vii, 81); the later, though still very early, cult of Theseus is not alluded to. There is no allusion, however vague, to any event known as having happened in Greek history later than 1100 B.C., and though the absence of reference to any particular event may be explained by indifference or forgetfulness, the absence of all reference to any event whatever suggests, I should say strongly, that none of the events to one or other of which reference might be expected had as yet happened.

〔U1120 para〕
然而，我雖把西元前 1050 年定為《奧德賽》的最晚界限，卻看不出我們怎能把它定在 1150 年以前，除非把《伊里亞德》城牆的年代推得比我們敢推的更早；因為我們很難把那道城牆定在西元前 1500 年以前，而從那道城牆的建造到《奧德賽》的寫成，350 年已經是我們所能容許的最短間隔了。
> While, however, placing 1050 B.C. as the latest limit for the *Odyssey* I do not see how we can place it earlier than 1150 without throwing the date of the Iliadic wall farther back than we can venture to do, for we can hardly date it earlier than 1500 B.C., and 350 years is as short an interval as we can well allow between the building of that wall and the writing of the *Odyssey*.

〔U1121 para〕
現在，讓我們把《奧德賽》向我們揭示的西西里西北角的歷史──始終假定卷七中阿爾基諾俄斯和阿瑞忒的家世在主要事實上是史實──與修昔底德關於同一地區最早歷史的記述加以比較。
> Let us now compare the history of the N.W. corner of Sicily as revealed to us in the *Odyssey*—always assuming that the pedigree of Alcinous and Arete in Book vii. is in its main facts historic—with the account given by Thucydides concerning the earliest history of the same district.

〔U1122 para〕
在《奧德賽》中，我們已經看到，西卡尼人（我想我已經充分辨認出他們的身分）原本占有埃律克斯山，由一位在《奧德賽》中名叫歐律墨東的國王統治。看來，大約在《奧德賽》寫成前一百年──這是根據他是瑙西卡的高祖父這一事實所能推得最接近的估計──他被不知名的敵人推翻，他的人民的勢力也被摧毀。
> In the *Odyssey* we have seen the Sicans (whom I think that I have sufficiently identified) as originally in possession of Mt. Eryx under a king whose Odyssean name is Eurymedon. He, it seems, was overthrown, and the power of his people was broken, by enemies whose name is not given, about a hundred years before the writing of the *Odyssey*, as nearly as we can gather from the fact of his having been Nausicaa's great great grandfather.

〔U1123 para〕
《奧德賽》的作者所用的語言以愛奧尼亞方言為主，但含有相當多的埃俄利斯成分。因此必須推斷，她的家人與聽眾──也就是費埃克斯人──說的是一種具有這些特徵的方言。最可能找到這種方言的地方，莫過於特洛阿斯稍南的福凱亞；因為福凱亞是一座愛奧尼亞城市，陸地的各面都被埃俄利斯人的領土完全包圍。我從傑布教授的《荷馬導論》[註8]得知，阿里斯塔克斯在編訂《伊里亞德》和《奧德賽》、藉著比對已知最好的抄本，把文本大致確定成我們今天所見的樣子時，最常使用的是馬賽的城邦版，其中同時收有《伊里亞德》和《奧德賽》。大家應當記得，馬賽是福凱亞人的殖民地。
> The writer of the *Odyssey* wrote in a language mainly Ionian, but containing a considerable Æolian element. It must be inferred, therefore, that her family and audience—that is to say the Phæacians—spoke a dialect in which these characteristics are to be found. The place of all others where such a dialect might be looked for is Phocæa, a little South of the Troad; for Phocæa was an Ionian city entirely surrounded on its land sides by Æolian territory. I see from Professor Jebb's *Introduction to Homer*[註8] that Aristarchus when editing the *Iliad* and *Odyssey*, and settling the text to all intents and purposes as we now have it, by comparison of the best copies known, made most frequent use of the civic edition of Marseilles which contained both *Iliad* and *Odyssey*. It will be remembered that Marseilles was a Phocæan Colony.

〔U1124 para〕
「費埃克斯人」這個名稱，作為「福凱亞人」的薄薄偽裝，並非毫無暗示；此外，卷四 441–443 行，如果我們設想那些氣味難聞的海豹（Phocæ）指的是作者顯然厭惡的同胞，而「誰會願意和海怪同床，要是避得開的話？」這句話，是她對費埃克斯年輕人據稱抱怨她誰也不肯嫁（卷六 276 行等處）的反唇相譏，那麼這幾行就會大大增添鋒芒。因此，撇開任何外部證據不談，我會懷疑費埃克斯人就是在島上這一帶定居下來的福凱亞人。[註9]從《奧德賽》時代的費埃克斯人顯然不但在特拉帕尼、也在埃律克斯山上居於統治地位這個事實，我推斷：歐律墨東以及西卡尼人在島上這一帶的勢力之所以被推翻，他們至少出了相當大的力。假如他們還有盟友同行，這些盟友似乎已轉往已知有埃利米城市存在的其他地點，因為《奧德賽》中沒有提到有任何其他民族與費埃克斯人共享許珀瑞亞和斯刻里亞。
> The name Phæacians is not unsuggestive of a thin disguise for Phocæans; lines iv. 441-443, moreover, will gain greatly in point, if we imagine that the seals, or Phocæ, with their disgusting smell, are meant for the writer's countrymen whom she evidently dislikes, and that the words, "who, indeed, would go to bed with a sea monster if he could help it?" are her rejoinder to the alleged complaint of the young Phæacians that she would marry none of them (vi. 276 &c.). Apart, therefore, from any external evidence, I should suspect the Phæacians to have been Phocæans, who had settled on this part of the island.[註9] From the fact that the Phæacians in the time of the *Odyssey* were evidently dominant on Mt. Eryx as well as at Trapani, I conclude that they must have had, to say the least of it, a considerable share in the overthrow of Eurymedon and of the Sican power in that part of the island. If they had allies with them, these allies seem to have gone on to other sites on which Elymite cities are known to have existed, for we find no reference in the *Odyssey* to any other people as sharing Hypereia and Scheria with the Phæacians.

〔U1125 para〕
埃律克斯的西卡尼人勢力雖已瓦解，費埃克斯人也在許珀瑞亞立足──同樣在埃律克斯山頂，距西卡尼人的城市不到一英里──但西卡尼人仍是麻煩的鄰居；不過，費埃克斯人中某位首領似乎與老國王歐律墨東的幼女佩里波亞結了婚，這無疑會使兩個民族在某種程度上趨於融合。這樁婚姻的後代瑙西托俄斯，詩中說他是波賽頓所生，我由此推斷，這樁婚姻多少有些不合常規；但毫無疑問，瑙西托俄斯有一位費埃克斯人父親，會說費埃克斯方言，而西卡尼人雖然十之八九是說希臘語的民族，卻不能認為他們也說這種方言。瑙西托俄斯似乎是個能幹的人；他發現那些半淪為亡命之徒的西卡尼人不斷劫掠，仍舊騷擾不休，或許也因為特拉帕尼所在的岬角，比高聳而荒涼的埃律克斯山頂更適合一個航海民族，於是他把族人遷到海邊，建立了如今名為特拉帕尼的城市──不過，他保留了許珀瑞亞舊址作為自己的產業，讓他的豬和山羊在那裡覓食，他的家人也會在盛夏酷熱時上山避暑，正如特拉帕尼人至今仍然這麼做。
> Though the power of the Sicans at Eryx was broken, and the Phæacians were established at Hypereia, also on the top of Mt. Eryx and less than a mile from the Sican city, the Sicans were still troublesome neighbours; there seems, however, to have been a marriage between some chief man among the Phæacians and Peribœa, youngest daughter of the old king Eurymedon, and this no doubt would lead to some approach to fusion between the two peoples. The offspring of this marriage, Nausithous, is said in the poem to have been by Neptune, from which I infer that the marriage may have been of a more or less irregular kind, but there can be no doubt that Nausithous came of a Phæacian father and would speak the Phæacian dialect, which the Sicans, though in all probability a Greek-speaking race, cannot be supposed to have done. Nausithous seems to have been a capable man; finding the continued raids of semi-outlawed Sicans still harassing, perhaps, also, induced by the fact that the promontory on which Trapani stands was better suited to a race of mariners than the lofty and inhospitable top of Mt. Eryx, he moved his people down to the seaside and founded the city that now bears the name of Trapani—retaining, however, the site of Hypereia as his own property on which his pigs and goats would feed, and to which also his family would resort, as the people of Trapani still do, during the excessive heat of summer.

〔U1126 para〕
讀者應該已經注意到，歐邁俄斯──我們絕不可忘記，他取材自埃律克斯山，而不是伊薩卡──夜裡看守豬群時，認為必須全副武裝（卷十四 526 行）。從卷十六 9 行看來，他似乎也有鄰居，由此我們可以推斷，古老的西卡尼城市埃律克斯當時還沒有完全荒廢；不過，要不是西卡尼人和福凱亞人已相當充分地融合，歐邁俄斯根本不會在那裡。西卡尼人在《奧德賽》中以庫克羅普斯人和萊斯特律戈涅斯人的名義出現，而西克洛伊人還沒有來。這就是我們能從《奧德賽》蒐集到的全部資訊。
> The reader will have noted that Eumæus, who we must never forget is drawn not from Ithaca but from Mt. Eryx, when watching over his pigs by night thought it necessary to be fully armed (xiv. 526). He seems also from xvi. 9, to have had neighbours, from which we may infer that the old Sican city of Eryx was not yet entirely abandoned; nevertheless, Eumæus would not be there at all unless the fusion between the Sicans and the Phocæans had been fairly complete. The Sicans appear in the *Odyssey* under the names of Cyclopes and Læstrygonians, and the Sicels are not yet come. This is all that we can collect from the *Odyssey*.

〔U1127 para〕
現在我們來看看，上面勾勒的輪廓能從修昔底德（卷六第 2 章）那裡得到什麼支持。據他說，被提到是西西里最早居民的萊斯特律戈涅斯人和庫克羅普斯人，只是詩人的虛構。不過，這並不排除他們在某個名稱不同的真實西西里民族身上有其原型；而且無論如何，不管他們多麼虛構，他總是把他們安置在西西里。
> We will now see what support the sketch given above will derive from Thucydides (vi. 2). According to him the Læstrygonians and the Cyclopes, mentioned as the earliest inhabitants of Sicily, are mere poetical fictions. This, however, does not preclude their having had their prototype in some real Sicilian people who bore another name; and at any rate, however fictitious they may be, he locates them in Sicily.

〔U1128 para〕
他接著說，島上有史可考的最古老居民是西卡尼人，據他們自己說，他們自古以來就住在那裡。修昔底德否認這一點，因為他說他們是伊比利亞人；他說這話的口氣，彷彿經過適當的查考，已讓自己確信無疑；但由於他沒有暗示他們何時到來，他並沒有否定他們所說的在島上定居始於遙遠的古代。他說西卡尼人來自西班牙，這很可能是對的；而且確實，在伊貝魯斯河口以北約五、六十英里的塔拉戈納，有一些巨石城牆，就我從照片所能判斷的，和埃律克斯的城牆極為相似。在修昔底德自己的時代，西西里西部仍有西卡尼人。
> He continues that the oldest historic inhabitants of the island were the Sicans, who by their own account had been there from time immemorial. This he denies, for he says they were Iberians, and he says it as though he had satisfied himself after due inquiry, but since he gives no hint as to the date of their arrival, he does not impugn their statement that their settlement in the island dated from a remote time. It is most likely that he is right about the Sicans having come from Spain; and indeed at Tarragona, some fifty or sixty miles North of the mouth of the river Iberus, there are megalithic walls that bear, so far as I can judge from photographs, a very considerable analogy with those of Eryx. In Thucydides' own times there were still Sicans in the Western part of Sicily.

〔U1129 para〕
他接著又說，特洛伊陷落之後──但他沒有說之後多久──一些逃過希臘人之手的特洛伊人移居西西里。他們定居在西卡尼人附近，合稱埃利米人，他們的城市是埃律克斯和塞傑斯塔。和他們一起定居的還有──但究竟是同時，還是更早或更晚，如果是，又早晚多少，修昔底德都沒說──某些特洛伊一支的福基斯人，*i.e*.（即）福凱亞人；福凱亞是由來自科林斯灣的福基斯人，在雅典首領菲洛革涅斯和達蒙的率領下建立的（*Strab*.（斯特拉波）卷十四 § 633；*Pausan*.（保薩尼阿斯）卷七第 3 章 § 5；參見 *Herod*（希羅多德）卷一第 146 節）。這些福凱亞人先被一場暴風吹到利比亞，[註10]再從那裡到了西西里。
> He then goes on to say that after the fall of Troy, but he does not say how much after, some of the Trojans who had escaped the Greeks migrated to Sicily. They settled in the neighbourhood of the Sicans and were all together called Elymi, their cities being Eryx and Segesta. There were also settled with them—but whether at the same date, or earlier or later, and if so, how much, Thucydides does not say—certain Phocians of the Trojan branch, *i.e*., Phocæans—Phocæa having been founded by Phocians from the gulf of Corinth under the leadership of the Athenian chiefs Philogenes and Damon (*Strab*. xiv. § 633; *Pausan*. VII. 3, §5; cf. *Herod*, I. 146). These Phocæans had been carried first by a tempest to Libya,[註10] and thence to Sicily.

〔U1130 para〕
我們不必跟著他講到西克洛伊人的到來，因為我希望我已經讓讀者相信，《奧德賽》屬於西克洛伊人以前的時代，而我處理的只是《奧德賽》與修昔底德共同涵蓋的那段時期。
> We need not follow him to the arrival of the Sicels, for I have already, I hope, satisfied the reader that the *Odyssey* belongs to a pre-Sicelian age, and I am only dealing with the period which the *Odyssey* and Thucydides cover in common.

〔U1131 para〕
我或許應該把一件事說得毫無疑義：修昔底德指的是福凱亞人，而不是福基斯人。首先，福基斯人站在亞該亞人一方（《伊里亞德》卷二 518 行），很難理解他們怎麼會和特洛伊人混居融合；其次，修昔底德的話，無法解釋成一般人所加給它的那種意思，彷彿這裡說的福基斯人正從特洛伊返回福基斯途中。他的原話是 Φωκέων τινες τῶν ἀπὸ Τροίας（來自特洛伊的那些福基斯人中的一些），這不能解釋成他說的是 Φωκέων τινες τῶν ἀνερχομένων ἐν νόστῳ ἀπὸ Τροίας（正從特洛伊歸返途中的一些福基斯人）。如果 ἀπό（從）要表示「從某處移動而來」，它前面就應該有一個含有移動之意的動詞或分詞；沒有的話，它就是表示居住於某地的常見說法。例如，Ὀρέστης ἤλυθεν ... ἀπ᾽ Ἀθηνάων（卷三 307 行）的意思是「俄瑞斯忒斯從雅典來」，而 Ὀρέστης ὁ ἀπ᾽ Ἀθηνάων 的意思則會是「雅典人俄瑞斯忒斯，或準雅典人俄瑞斯忒斯」，正如 Λακεδαιμόνιοι οἱ ἀπὸ Σπάρτης 的意思是「住在斯巴達的拉刻代蒙人」。後面這兩個例句，都無法解釋成「正從雅典前來途中的俄瑞斯忒斯」或「正從斯巴達前來途中的拉刻代蒙人」。讀者在利德爾與斯科特的辭典裡查一查 ἀπὸ，就會找到大量例子。對修昔底德來說，小亞細亞的福凱亞人和科林斯灣的福基斯人，由於同出一源，同樣都是福基斯人；但為了避免誤會，他把福凱亞人稱為「特洛伊一支的福基斯人」，所謂「特洛伊」，意思是離特洛伊不遠。應當注意，在《伊里亞德》卷九 517 行、卷十五 516 行、卷十七 307 行，科林斯灣的福基斯人被稱為 Φωκῆες，而不是 Φωκέες。我發現，多布里（《修昔底德札記》*Adversaria in Thucyd*.）對我們現在討論的這段修昔底德文字中 Φωκέων（福基斯人的）這個讀法存疑。他顯然認為 Φωκέων 必定是指科林斯灣的福基斯人；如果沒有 τῶν ἀπὸ Τροίας（來自特洛伊的那些）這幾個字加以限定，確實如此；但有了這幾個字，就排除了指歐洲福基斯人的可能。
> I should perhaps put it beyond doubt that Thucydides means Phocæans and not Phocians. In the first place it is difficult to understand how Phocians, who were on the Achæan side (*Il*. II. 518), should amalgamate with Trojans; and in the next Thucydides' words cannot be made to bear the meaning that is generally put upon them, as though the Phocians in question were on their way back from Troy to Phocis. His words are Φωκέων τινες τῶν ἀπὸ Τροίας, and this cannot be construed as though he had said Φωκέων τινες τῶν ἀνερχομένων ἐν νόστῳ ἀπὸ Τροίας. If ἀπό is to imply motion from, it should have a verb or participle involving motion before it; without this it is a common way of expressing residence in a place. For example, Ὀρέστης ἤλυθεν ... ἀπ᾽ Ἀθηνάων (iii. 307) means Orestes came from Athens, whereas Ὀρέστης ὁ ἀπ᾽ Ἀθηνάων would mean "Orestes the Athenian, or quasi-Athenian," as Λακεδαιμόνιοι οἱ ἀπὸ Σπάρτης means "the Lacedæmonians who live at Sparta." Neither of these last two passages can be made to bear the meaning "Orestes, who was on his way from Athens," or "the Lacedæmonians, who were on their way from Sparta." The reader who looks out ἀπὸ in Liddell & Scott will find plenty of examples. To Thucydides, Phocæans in Asia Minor and Phocians on the gulf of Corinth would be alike Phocians in virtue of common descent, but to avoid misapprehension he calls the Phocæans "Phocians of the Trojan stock," by "Trojan" meaning not very far from Troy. It should be noted that the Phocians of the gulf of Corinth are called Φωκῆες, not Φωκέες in *Il*. IX. 517, XV. 516, XVII. 307. I see that Dobree (*Adversaria in Thucyd*.) is suspicious of the reading Φωκέων in the passage of Thucydides which we are now considering. He evidently considers that Φωκέων must mean Phocians from the gulf of Corinth, and so it would, if it were not qualified by the words τῶν ἀπὸ Τροίας which negative the possibility of European Phocians being intended.

〔U1132 para〕
修昔底德完全沒有提到有一個叫埃利米人的民族入侵西西里。在他看來，埃利米人不過是亞洲人與西卡尼人合而為一、後來被稱為埃利米人的民族罷了。假如他相信埃利米人是一批獨立的移民，他總會多寫一兩行介紹他們。
> Thucydides says nothing about any invasion of Sicily by a people called Elymi. He does not see the Elymi as anything more than the combined Asiatic and Sican peoples, who came to be called Elymi. If he had believed in the Elymi as a distinct batch of immigrants he would have given us a line or two more about them.

〔U1133 para〕
福基斯人與福凱亞人之間眾所周知的關聯，或許正可以解釋，為什麼奧德修斯的外祖父被安排住在帕爾那索斯山[註11]──那座山在福基斯。對《奧德賽》的作者來說，奧德修斯是入了籍的費埃克斯人，因為在她眼中，她的家鄉已經既成了斯刻里亞，也成了伊薩卡。因此，她想把他的祖先和福基斯──福凱亞人的祖居之地──連在一起，也不是不自然的事。
> It is just possible that the known connection between Phocians and Phocæans may explain why Ulysses' maternal grandfather should have been made to live on Mt. Parnassus,[註11] which is in Phocis. Ulysses, to the writer of the *Odyssey*, was a naturalised Phæacian, for her native town had become in her eyes both Scheria and Ithaca. It would not be unnatural, therefore, that she should wish to connect his ancestry with Phocis, the ancestral seat of the Phocæans.

〔U1134 para〕
回到修昔底德：他與《奧德賽》版本唯一不同的地方，是他讓福凱亞人以外的其他特洛伊人也移居埃律克斯；而《奧德賽》的作者只提到費埃克斯人，對他們出自福凱亞人一事隻字未提。不過，她已經把自己的底細洩露得相當充分了。再者，修昔底德並沒有告訴我們福凱亞人又在德列帕農重新定居；可是，一個人只是概述他寫作之前約七百年發生的事件，很難指望他交代這麼細小的細節。奇妙的是，《奧德賽》竟能如此驚人地印證他的說法、證實他的準確。因此，我們現在明白了：在埃律克斯山附近發現一部愛奧尼亞－埃俄利斯方言的詩，非但沒有任何值得驚訝之處，這一帶反而正是我們預期會找到這樣一部詩的地方。
> Returning to Thucydides, the only point in which he varies the Odyssean version is that he makes other Trojans migrate to Eryx as well as the Phocæans, whereas the writer of the *Odyssey* mentions only the Phæacians without saying anything about their having been of Phocæan descent. She has, however, betrayed herself very sufficiently. Thucydides again does not tell us that the Phocæans re-settled themselves at Drepanum, but a man who is giving a mere outline of events which happened some seven hundred years before he was writing, can hardly be expected to give so small a detail as this. The wonder is that the *Odyssey* should bear him out and confirm his accuracy in so striking a way as it does. We now, therefore, see that instead of there being any cause for surprise at finding an Ionic-Æolian poem written near Mt. Eryx, this is the very neighbourhood in which we might expect to find one.

〔U1135 para〕
最後，讓我們看看維吉爾。他作為史家的權威一文不值；但我們不能設想，假如他認為早在荷馬時代以前德列帕農就有說希臘語的民族是件荒謬的事──荒謬得像我們那些傑出的荷馬學者所以為的那樣──他還會讓埃涅阿斯看似建立了德列帕農。我說「看似建立了德列帕農」，是因為埃涅阿斯所建的那座城市（《埃涅阿斯紀》卷五 755–761 行），其位置不太容易確定；因為在《埃涅阿斯紀》卷三結尾，安基塞斯死於德列帕農，彷彿這座城市早已存在。不過，不論埃涅阿斯所建的城市真的就是德列帕農，還是緊鄰它的另一座城市，顯然維吉爾都把說希臘語的人安置在德列帕農或其附近，而且就在特洛伊陷落之後。除非在他那個時代，人們相信德列帕農是一座極為古老、由說希臘語的人所建立的城市，否則他不大會這麼做。至於特洛伊人的語言是希臘語，這一點不會有人爭議。
> Finally, let us turn to Virgil. His authority as a historian is worthless, but we cannot suppose that he would make Æneas apparently found Drepanum, if he held the presence of a Greek-speaking people at Drepanum even before the age of Homer to be so absurd as it appears to our eminent Homeric scholars. I say "apparently found Drepanum," for it is not quite easy to fix the site of the city founded by Æneas (*Æn* v. 755-761), for at the close of *Æn*. III. Anchises dies at Drepanum, as though this city was already in existence. But whether the city founded by Æneas was actually Drepanum, or another city hard by it, it is clear that Virgil places Greek-speaking people at Drepanum, or close to it, immediately after the fall of Troy. He would hardly do this unless Drepanum was believed in his time to be a city of very great antiquity, and founded by Greek-speaking people. That the Trojan language was Greek will not be disputed.

〔U1136 footnote〕
《荷馬導論》，1888 年版，第 172、173 頁。
> *Introduction to Homer*, Ed. 1888, pp. 172, 173.

〔U1137 footnote〕
在早期的錢幣上，敘拉古不時以 Syra（敘里埃）之名出現。
> On its earlier coins Syracuse not unfrequently appears as Syra.

〔U1138 footnote〕
在這座島被稱為 Σικελία（西西里）的許多世紀裡，Σικανίης（西卡尼亞，卷二十四 397 行）竟沒有訛變成 Σικελίης（西西里）──後者在格律上同樣說得通──這個事實暗示有一個書面的原本；不過，不用我說也知道，這麼小的一件事若是單獨存在，我是不會倚靠它的。
> The fact that Σικανίης (xxiv. 397) should not have got corrupted into Σικελίης—which would scan just as well—during the many centuries that the island was called Σικελία, suggests a written original, though I need hardly say that I should not rely on so small a matter if it rested by itself.

見傑布教授《荷馬導論》，1888 年版，第 43 頁註一。
> See Prof. Jebb's *Introduction to Homer*, ed. 1888, Note I on p. 43.

默里出版，1830 年。

> *The Mycenæan Age*, by Dr. Chrestos Tsountas and Dr. J. Irving Manatt, Macmillan, 1897, p. 369.

〔U1142 footnote〕
我那張插圖上橫過的一道黑線，只是我的底片出了意外所致。我相信（但不十分確定，因為我關於這件事的筆記不是在現場寫的），我第二張插圖所示的那一段城牆，和《伊里亞德》中的城牆毫無關係，年代要晚得多。我刊出它，是為了顯示要判斷一道飽經風化的城牆，需要多少想像力。
> The dark line across my illustration is only due to an accident that happened to my negative. I believe (but am not quite sure, for my note about it was not written on the spot) that the bit of wall given in my second illustration has nothing to do with the Iliadic wall, and is of greatly later date. I give it to show how much imagination is necessary in judging of any wall that has been much weathered.

〔U1143 footnote〕
1888 年版，第 91 頁註。
> Ed. 1888, note on p. 91.

〔U1144 footnote〕
希羅多德告訴我們（卷一第 163 節），福凱亞人是最早從事遠航的民族，他們探索了第勒尼安海，最遠到達加的斯。他說，他們的船不是一般用於貿易的圓船，而是有五十名槳手的長船。讀者會記得，福凱亞人航海的這個特點，也見於費埃克斯人身上：他們用一艘有五十名槳手的船，把奧德修斯送到我們應視為伊薩卡的地方。
> Herodotus tells us (I. 163) that the Phocæans were the first people to undertake long voyages, exploring the Tuscan sea, and going as far as Cadiz. He says that their ships were not the round ones commonly used for commerce, but long vessels with fifty oarsmen. The reader will recollect that this feature of Phocæan navigation is found also among the Phæacians, who sent Ulysses to the place that we are to take as Ithaca, in a vessel that had fifty oarsmen.

〔U1145 footnote〕
讓人不禁想到，食蓮人的插曲，會不會是因為費埃克斯人之間流傳著一些傳說，說他們的祖先在抵達西西里之前，曾在利比亞逗留過一段時間。
> One cannot help wondering whether the episode of the Lotus-eaters may not be due to the existence of traditions among the Phæacians that their ancestors had made some stay in Libya before reaching Sicily.

〔U1146 footnote〕
《奧德賽》卷十九 410、432 行。
> *Od*. xix. 410, 432.

〔U1147 heading〕
第十三章
> CHAPTER XIII.

〔U1148 para〕
特拉帕尼或其近旁早有愛奧尼亞人定居的進一步證據
> FURTHER EVIDENCE IN SUPPORT OF AN EARLY IONIAN SETTLEMENT AT OR CLOSE TO TRAPANI.

〔U1149 para〕
常有人問我：古代作家筆下，從來找不到任何傳說提到《奧德賽》寫於德列帕農，或者作者是個女人，這一點我要怎麼解釋？他們把這個難題擺到我面前，彷彿它幾乎足以致命。不過我得承認，比起另一個難題──這兩件事實怎麼會這麼久都沒有被人重新發現──它在我看來實在微不足道。波賽頓固然沒有把斯刻里亞壓在埃律克斯山底下，但他，或者某位同樣心懷怨恨的神，似乎把斯刻里亞連同它偉大的女詩人一起埋進了另一座山底下；那座山，我只怕會比埃律克斯山還要難以搬動──我說的是一大片近乎地質構造的學院博學。
> I am often asked how I explain the fact that we find no trace in ancient authors of any tradition to the effect that the *Odyssey* was written at Drepanum or that the writer was a woman. This difficulty is laid before me as one that is almost fatal. I confess, however, that I find it small in comparison with that of explaining how both these facts should have failed of being long since rediscovered. Neptune indeed did not overwhelm Scheria under Mt. Eryx, but he, or some not less spiteful god, seems to have buried both it and its great poetess under another mountain which I fear may be found even more irremoveable—I mean a huge quasi-geological formation of academic erudition.

〔U1150 para〕
這項反對意見所暗含的事實，本身就沒有足夠的根據。費埃克斯人是真實存在的民族，住在一個名叫德瑞帕涅的地方（就一切實際用途而言，這個名字和德列帕農已經夠接近了）[註1]，這件事從來不曾被人遺忘──只有覺得遺忘比較方便的人才會忘記。修昔底德（卷一 25 節）告訴我們，科孚的居民是費埃克斯人的後裔；而費埃克斯人的船護送奧德修斯到伊薩卡之後，在駛進港口時被變成的那塊岩石，至今仍在科孚供人指認──那是一座 58 英尺高的小島，頂上有一座修道院。可是科孚的舊名正是德瑞帕涅[註2]；迦太基人在西西里的德列帕農站穩腳跟之後，科孚德瑞帕涅的居民要自稱費埃克斯人的後裔，自然輕而易舉；他們接著果然也這麼做了，還把自己的島叫做斯刻里亞，儘管這座島和《奧德賽》的描述沒有一處對得上。
> The objection is without sufficient foundation in its implied facts; for that the Phæacians were a real people who lived at a place bearing the name of Drepane (which is near enough to Drepanum for all practical purposes),[註1] has never been lost sight of at all—except by those who find it convenient to lose sight of it. Thucydides (i. 25) tells us that the inhabitants of Corfu were the descendants of the Phæacians, and the rock into which their ship was turned as it was entering the harbour after having escorted Ulysses to Ithaca is still shown at Corfu—as an island 58 feet high with a monastery on the top of it. But the older name of Corfu was Drepane,[註2] and when the Carthaginians had established themselves at the Sicilian Drepanum, it would be an easy matter for the inhabitants of the Corfu Drepane to claim Phæacian descent, and—as they proceeded to do—to call their island Scheria, in spite of its offering no single point of correspondence with the description given in the *Odyssey*.

〔U1151 para〕
我承認，並沒有明確的傳說說整部《奧德賽》是在科孚寫成的；但費埃克斯插曲是全詩的眼睛。因此我認為，傳統長久以來、而且至今仍然，透過暗示把這段插曲和一個地方連在一起，而那個地方已知最早的名字，實際上和我主張它寫成的那座城鎮的名字並無二致。
> I grant that no explicit tradition exists to the effect that the *Odyssey* as a whole was written at or in Corfu, but the Phæacian episode is the eye of the poem. I submit, then, that tradition both long has, and still does, by implication connect it with a place of which the earliest known name was to all intents and purposes the same as that of the town where I contend that it was written.

〔U1152 para〕
雅典的作家，包括修昔底德在內，對任何能把荷馬──他們無知地這樣稱呼《奧德賽》的作者──拉得離自家門口更近的地點，都會心存偏袒。再說，埃律克斯和塞傑斯塔的人，連帶德列帕農的人，都被視為蠻族，修昔底德本人就這樣稱呼他們（卷六 2 節）；在他眼裡，只要有任何傳說支持科孚，不管多麼含糊，若還在科孚的德瑞帕涅和西西里的德列帕農之間猶豫不決，簡直就和褻瀆神明差不多。然而修昔底德不太可能不知道西西里方面的主張──不只主張費埃克斯插曲，而是主張整部詩；因為晚至西元前 430 年，也就是他自己著書前不久，埃律克斯山上或其附近仍然有人看來分明提出過這樣的主張，這一點我馬上就要說明。
> The Athenian writers, Thucydides included, would be biassed in favour of any site which brought Homer, as they ignorantly called the writer of the *Odyssey*, nearer their own doors. The people, moreover, of Eryx and Segesta, and hence also of Drepanum, were held to be barbarians, and are so called by Thucydides himself (vi. 2); in his eyes it would be little less than sacrilege to hesitate between the Corfu Drepane and the Sicilian Drepanum, did any tradition, however vague, support Corfu. But it is not likely that Thucydides was unaware of the Sicilian claim not only to the Phæacian episode, but to the entire poem, for as late as 430 B.C., only a little before the date of his own work, there were still people on or near Mt. Eryx who present every appearance of having claimed it, as I will almost immediately show.

〔U1153 para〕
至於忘記了這部詩出自女人之手：連「荷馬不可能寫出它」這件事都能忘記的人，還有什麼不能忘記？一個民族不但能做到這一點，還能把指出他們錯誤的人有效地掐熄，那麼對於兩部詩背後的差異，他們所知道的，大概不會比一般英國平信徒對三部對觀福音書與〈約翰福音〉之間的差異知道得更多。
> As for losing sight of its having been written by a woman, the people who could lose sight of the impossibility of its having been written by Homer could lose sight of anything. A people who could not only do this, but who could effectually snuff out those who pointed out their error, were not likely to know more about the difference underlying the two poems than the average English layman does about those between the synoptic gospels and that of St. John.

〔U1154 para〕
現在我要回到我的論斷：在修昔底德的時代，似乎有不少人知道德列帕農自稱是《奧德賽》的誕生地，而且自己也參與了這項主張。
> I will now return to my assertion that in the time of Thucydides there seem to have been not a few who knew of, and shared in, the claim of Drepanum to the authorship of the *Odyssey*.

〔U1155 para〕
大英博物館藏有一枚獨一無二的小銅幣，被十足有把握地歸入埃律克斯與塞傑斯塔的錢幣之列。它出自錢幣藝術最巔峰的時期，博物館當局把它的年代定在約西元前 430 年。
> The British Museum possesses a unique example of a small bronze coin which is classed with full confidence among those of Eryx and Segesta. It is of the very finest period of the numismatic art, and is dated by the museum authorities as about 430 B.C.

〔U1156 figure〕
〔圖版 19：（原書無圖說）〕
> 

〔U1157 para〕
讀者會看到，錢幣正面鑄有銘文 IAKIN，背面則是奧德修斯所描述的那枚胸針（《奧德賽》卷十九 225–231 行）。這段文字的譯文見第 80 頁。
> The reader will see that the obverse bears the legend IAKIN, and the reverse a representation of the brooch described by Ulysses (*Od*. xix. 225-231). A translation of this passage is given on page 80.

〔U1158 para〕
原物上看不見 A 的那一橫，但博物館方面毫不懷疑它原本存在。
> The cross line of the A is not visible in the original, but no doubt is felt at the Museum about its having existed.

〔U1159 para〕
然而，銘文究竟該讀作 IAKIN 還是 ΓIAKIN（Γ 是 Π 的古體），似乎比較有疑問。也許是想兩種情形都不落錯，博物館的目錄在插圖裡作 IAKIN，在說明文字裡卻作 ΓIAKIN。就我的論證而言，兩種讀法幾乎一樣管用，因為我的論證只需要這枚錢幣屬於埃律克斯與塞傑斯塔一系、年代約在西元前 430 年──這兩點都沒有人懷疑。不過，我還是要把讓我確信 IAKIN 才是正確讀法的理由交代出來。
> There seems, however, to be more doubt whether the legend should be IAKIN, or ΓIAKIN—Γ being the older form of Π. Possibly from a desire to be right in either case, the Museum catalogue gives it as IAKIN in the illustration, and ΓIAKIN in the descriptive letterpress. The one reading will do nearly as well as the other for my argument, which only requires that the coin should belong to the Eryx and Segesta group and be dated about 430 B.C.—neither of which points are doubted. I will, however, give the reasons that convince me that IAKIN is the true reading.

〔U1160 para〕
第一，在最下方那個凸飾與頸部之間，我自己找不到任何 Γ 的痕跡，我幾位藝術家朋友──他們的意見比我的寶貴不知多少倍──也找不到。我知道有些能力一流的專家聲稱看得出這種痕跡，但在博物館目錄裡描繪這枚錢幣的畫師顯然看不出來，而那些專家對自己的意見似乎也沒有自信到要他改畫。
> Firstly, neither I nor some artist friends of mine whose opinion is infinitely better worth having than my own, can find any traces of a Γ between the lowermost boss and the neck. I am aware that some experts of the highest competence profess to be able to detect such traces, but the artist who figured the coin in the Museum Catalogue evidently could not do so, and the experts do not seem to have had such confidence in their own opinion as to make him alter his drawing.

〔U1161 para〕
第二，這幅構圖顯然是有意對稱的。能設計出如此精美錢幣的人，若要把一個 Γ 硬擠進它（假如真有的話）非放不可的位置，從而破壞整體的平衡，那是違背他本能、令他深惡痛絕的事。
> Secondly, the composition is obviously and intentionally symmetrical. It would be abhorrent to the instincts of the man who could design so exquisite a coin to destroy its balance by crowding a Γ into the place which must be assigned to it if it exists at all.

〔U1162 para〕
第三，把這枚錢幣賣給博物館的商人，原本把它歸於皮亞庫斯城。拜占庭的斯特凡努斯曾極簡略地提到皮亞庫斯是一座西西里城市，此外再沒有別的作家提過；而且他明白指出，那裡的市民叫做 ΠΙΑΚΗΝΟΙ；所以這枚錢幣若是他們的，銘文應該是 ΓΙΑΚΗΝ，而不是所謂的 ΓIAKIN。拜占庭的斯特凡努斯要到西元 500 年左右才著書，既然他沒有說皮亞庫斯是一座古城，就斷定它在他提到時已經存在了至少一千年，未免有點魯莽；任何方面都沒有證據支持這樣的結論。比較穩當的結論是：上面說的那位商人不知道這枚錢幣出自何處，便到拜占庭的斯特凡努斯書裡找一座城市，發現最接近的只有皮亞庫斯──於是他在錢幣裡「看出」了一個 Γ（在 Π 字上動手腳，這已是他所能做的最小的一筆），然後把它賣給了大英博物館，售價比起它如今證明具有的價值，大概只是區區幾文。就這樣，博物館當局把「銘文是 ΓΙΑΚΗΝ」寫進了他們筆記的一部分（因為他們似乎又把 IAKIN 寫進了另一部分），於是很自然地，他們在錢幣上看到的東西，比沒有筆記的人所能完全認可的還要多。不過我不再多說了。銘文顯然是 IAKIN。
> Thirdly, Piacus, to which town the coin had been ascribed by the dealer from whom the Museum bought it, is mentioned very briefly by Stephanus Byzantinus, but by no other writer, as a Sicilian city, and he expressly states that its citizens were called ΠΙΑΚΗΝΟΙ; so that the coin, if it was one of theirs, should bear the legend ΓΙΑΚΗΝ instead of the alleged ΓIAKIN. Stephanus Byzantinus did not write till about 500 A.D., and in the absence of any statement from him to the effect that Piacus was an old city, it argues some recklessness to conclude that it had existed for at least a thousand years when he mentioned it; there is no evidence from any quarter to support such a conclusion, and a safer one will be that the dealer above referred to, not knowing where the coin came from, and looking for a city in Stephanus Byzantinus, found he could get nothing nearer than Piacus—whereon he saw a Γ as the smallest thing he could do in Πs, into his coin, and sold it to the British Museum probably for a song as compared with the value which it now proves to have. Thus the Museum authorities having got it into part of their notes (for they seem to have got IAKIN into another part) that the legend was ΓΙΑΚΗΝ, have very naturally been led to see more on the coin than those who have no notes will quite bear them out in seeing. But I will add no more. The legend is obviously IAKIN.

〔U1163 para〕
這是 ΙΑΚΙΝΩΝ（伊阿克斯人的）的縮寫，就像 ΕΡΥΚΙΝ 與 ΚΕΝΤΟΡΙΠΙΝ 分別是 ΕΡΥΚΙΝΩΝ（埃律克斯人的）與 ΚΕΝΤΟΡΙΠΙΝΩ（肯托里帕人的）的縮寫一樣，其他例子就不必再舉了。它表示鑄幣的人叫做 ΙΑΚΙΝΕΣ；雖然我們無法確定他們城市的確切名稱，卻可以有把握地推斷，那是 ΙΑΚΟΣ 的某個派生詞──利德爾與斯科特的辭典把 ΙΑΚΟΣ 解作「愛奧尼亞的」。城名很可能是 ΙΑΞ（伊阿克斯），雖然我找不到任何根據證明有這樣一座城鎮存在。
> This is an abbreviation for ΙΑΚΙΝΩΝ, as ΕΡΥΚΙΝ and ΚΕΝΤΟΡΙΠΙΝ are for ΕΡΥΚΙΝΩΝ and ΚΕΝΤΟΡΙΠΙΝΩ, not to quote further examples. It means that the people who struck it were called ΙΑΚΙΝΕΣ, and though we cannot determine the precise name of their city we may infer with confidence that it was some derivative of ΙΑΚΟΣ, which is given in Liddell and Scott as meaning Ionian. The name may very likely have been ΙΑΞ though I cannot find any authority for the existence of such a town.

〔U1164 para〕
因此我認為，晚至西元前 430 年，特拉帕尼附近仍有一座或多或少自治的城鎮，自稱是愛奧尼亞人的後裔，也自稱與《奧德賽》有某種特殊的關聯；因為我得到保證，錢幣上只容許出現對鑄幣者過去歷史有重大意義的東西。既然承認這枚錢幣的背面必須看作奧德修斯那枚胸針的再現──在這一點上，我發現博物館的錢幣學家之間沒有任何異議──那麼要在錢幣上說出「奧德賽」三個字，還有什麼辦法比鑄上全詩中最適合以錢幣表現的那個題材更貼切，實在很難想像。所以在我看來，「埃律克斯附近有一座能自稱與《奧德賽》有關的愛奧尼亞城市」這個理論，沒有這枚錢幣也完全站得住；這枚錢幣卻非得牽涉到「埃律克斯附近有一座自稱與《奧德賽》有關的愛奧尼亞城市」才站得住。可喜的是，這枚錢幣雖然獨一無二，其真偽卻毫無疑問。
> I hold, therefore, that as late as B.C. 430 there was near Trapani a town still more or less autonomous, which claimed Ionian descent and which also claimed to be in some special way connected with the *Odyssey*; for I am assured that nothing would be allowed on a coin except what had an important bearing on the anterior history of those who struck it. Admitting that the reverse of the coin in question must be taken as a reproduction of Ulysses' brooch—and I found no difference of opinion among the numismatists at the Museum on this head—it is hard to see what more apposite means of saying "Odyssey" upon a coin can be suggested than to stamp it with the subject which invites numismatic treatment more than any other in the whole poem. It seems to me, then, that though the theory that there was an Ionian city in the neighbourhood of Eryx which could claim connection with the *Odyssey* will stand perfectly well without the coin, the coin cannot stand without involving the existence of an Ionian city near Eryx which claimed connection with the *Odyssey*. Happily, though the coin is unique, there is no question as to its genuineness.

〔U1165 para〕
因此，有人向我要紀念碑、建築遺跡、歷史文獻，來證明埃律克斯附近在迄今被視為史前的時代有過西卡尼－愛奧尼亞文明，我的回答是：晚至西元前 430 年，這一切似乎都還存在。且不說修昔底德的證詞，一枚愛奧尼亞錢幣，對一座愛奧尼亞城市而言，總不能算是微不足道的歷史文獻吧。一枚錢幣說的話比什麼都多，用字比什麼都少，也比什麼都更有權威。這枚錢幣不可能屬於西元前 430 年才在埃律克斯山上或其附近新建的愛奧尼亞殖民地。若有這樣一個殖民地，我們應該會聽說；再者，照這個假設，把《奧德賽》扯進來又是何等不可思議。這座城市如果真的存在，就只能是修昔底德告訴我們的那個福凱亞人定居點的遺存。
> To those, therefore, who ask me for monuments, ruins of buildings, historical documents to support a Sicano-Ionian civilisation near Eryx in times heretofore prehistoric, I reply that as late as 430 B.C. all these things appear to have existed. Letting alone the testimony of Thucydides, surely an Ionian coin is no small historical document in support of an Ionian city. A coin will say more in fewer words and more authoritatively than anything else will. The coin in question cannot belong to an Ionian colony on Mt. Eryx or thereabouts recently established in 430 B.C. We should have heard of such a colony; how inconceivable again is the bringing in of the *Odyssey* on this supposition. If the city existed at all it can only have done so as a survival of the Phocæan settlement of which Thucydides tells us.

〔U1166 para〕
至於這樣一個定居點在後世是否存續，我不需要任何證據；證明它存續與否，並不是我的責任。《奧德賽》裡所有斯刻里亞與伊薩卡的場景，同特拉帕尼及其近郊之間那些豐富──我幾乎要說過於豐富──的吻合之處，已經足以證明這部詩源出特拉帕尼。詩中那些早於敘拉古、早於西克洛伊人的跡象，把它的年代定在不晚於約西元前 1050 年；它的方言是愛奧尼亞－埃俄利斯方言，把它和上面提到的福凱亞人連在一起。《奧德賽》寫成之後這些福凱亞人的下落如何，與我無關；我對 IAKIN 錢幣所說的話，與其說是為了《奧德賽》，不如說是為了這枚錢幣；因為《奧德賽》本身對自己的*出處*與年代所提供的證明，比任何晚了約六百年才鑄造的錢幣所能提供的，都更有力、更無可辯駁。不過，錢幣既然擺在那裡，我就拿它來回答那些要求《奧德賽》本身以外證據的人。他們問我的紀念碑在哪裡，我就回答：都在這枚錢幣裡，圈在至多一英寸半的小小圓周之內。因為一枚錢幣就是一座具體而微的城市；看著它的人，看到的是一個民族、一份產權憑證、一整個連同各式建築的文明。把這些都毀掉吧，只要它的錢幣還留下一枚，這座城市便雖死猶生，而它曾有過會淪為廢墟的建築這件事，就像廢墟本身流傳到我們手上一樣確鑿可觸。
> I want no evidence for the survival of such a settlement in later times; it is not incumbent upon me to show whether it survived or no; the abundant, I might almost say super-abundant, coincidences between all both Scherian and Ithacan scenes in the *Odyssey*, and Trapani with its immediate neighbourhood, is enough to demonstrate the Trapanese origin of the poem. Its pre-Syracusan and pre-Sicelian indications fix it as not later than about 1050 B.C., its dialect, Ionic-Æolian, connects it with the Phocæans above referred to. It does not concern me to show what became of these Phocæans after the *Odyssey* had been written; what I have said about the coin IAKIN is said more in the interests of the coin than of the *Odyssey*, which is a more potent and irrefragable proof of its own *provenance* and date than any coin struck some 600 years later can conceivably be. Still, the coin being there, I use it to answer those who demand some evidence external to the *Odyssey* itself. When they ask me where are my monuments, I answer that they are within the coin, circumscribed by the small cincture of an inch and a half at most. For a coin is a city in little; he who looks on one beholds a people, an evidence of title, a whole civilisation with its buildings of every kind. Destroy these, but so long as a single one of its coins remains, the city though dead is yet alive, and the fact of its having had buildings that could become ruinous is as palpable as though the ruins themselves had come down to us.

〔U1167 para〕
這座名叫伊阿克斯（Iax、Iacus 或 Iace）的城市確切位置已無法確定，但我傾向把它定在特拉帕尼以東約一英里或一英里半處，在一個叫阿爾真泰里亞的地方或其附近。據說此地出過銀，但沒有人相信真有其事。那是一座採石場，規模一點也不大，就在登上埃律克斯山的坡道起點。有人說阿爾真泰里亞是切塔里亞的訛變，指的是在這裡被殺的一條巨魚──至於這條魚怎麼會跑到離海這麼遠的地方，就不得而知了；不過我認為，它更可能是伊阿琴泰里亞的訛變，而伊阿克斯是德列帕農一個半自治的郊區；迦太基人佔據城中臨港的地帶時，希臘居民獲准退居到那裡。
> The exact situation of this city Iax, Iacus, or Iace, cannot be determined, but I incline to place it about a mile or a mile and a half East of Trapani at or near a place called Argenteria. This place is said to have yielded silver, but no one believes that it ever did so. It is a quarry and by no means a large one, just at the beginning of the rise to Mt. Eryx. Some say that Argenteria is a corruption of Cetaria and refers to a monster fish that was killed here, though how it got so far from the sea is not apparent; I think it much more likely, however, that it is a corruption of Iacinteria and that Iax, or Iace, was a quasi-autonomous suburb of Drepanum to which the Greek inhabitants were permitted to retire when the Carthaginians took possession of the parts of the town bordering on the harbour.

〔U1168 para〕
我的朋友、特拉帕尼的蘇加梅利先生，在這件事上的熱心遠遠超過我──我若知道怎麼節制它，倒樂於替他節制一下──他向我保證，他年輕時蓋房子常用一種石頭，石匠告訴他，那石頭出自埃律克斯山腳一座叫 Dacinoi 或 D'Acinoi（達奇諾伊）的採石場。這是在任何人想到要把愛奧尼亞人帶到特拉帕尼來的好多年以前。蘇加梅利先生提出，這個名字也許是 D'Alcinoo（「阿爾基諾俄斯的」）的訛變──但我們可以肯定，阿爾基諾俄斯的名字無論是什麼，總之不是阿爾基諾俄斯。我請蘇加梅利先生把那位石匠找來，但他既找不到人，也打聽不到達奇諾伊這座採石場。儘管如此，我確信他確實聽人這麼說過；而這座採石場既然不可能離阿爾真泰里亞太遠，我認為它的名字很可能是 *degli Iacinoi*（伊阿奇諾伊人的）的訛變。
> My friend Signor Sugameli of Trapani, whose zeal in this matter so far outstrips even my own, that I would gladly moderate it if I knew how to do so, assures me that in his younger days he used to employ a stone in building that the mason told him came from a quarry at the foot of Mt. Eryx called Dacinoi or D'Acinoi. This was years before any one thought of bringing Ionians to Trapani. Signor Sugameli suggested that possibly the name might be a corruption of D'Alcinoo—but we may be sure that whatever else Alcinous's name may have been it was not Alcinous. I asked Signor Sugameli to produce the mason, but he could neither find him nor hear of the quarry Dacinoi. Nevertheless I feel sure that he was told what he said he was, and as the quarry cannot have been far from the Argenteria, I think it probable that its name was a corruption of *degli Iacinoi*.

〔U1169 para〕
不論這個說法是否站得住，我都毫不懷疑，在我們紀元十一世紀的西西里歷史上舉足輕重的伊阿森塞人，應該和產生《奧德賽》的那個愛奧尼亞定居點有關。當時伊阿森塞人主要定居在特拉帕尼以東約四十英里處；不過從 IAKIN 錢幣的年代到諾曼人征服西西里，中間隔了大約一千四百多年，足夠他們散布或遷徙。
> Whether this is sound or not, I do not doubt that the Iacenses who figure so largely in Sicilian history during the Eleventh Century of our own era are to be connected with the Ionian settlement that produced the *Odyssey*. The Iacenses were then settled chiefly about forty miles East of Trapani, but the interval of some 1400 years and more between the date of the coin Iakin and the conquest of Sicily by the Normans will leave plenty of time for them to have spread or migrated.

〔U1170 footnote〕
德列帕農（Drepanum）的意思是彎刀或短彎刀；德瑞帕涅（Drepane）則是鐮刀。
> Drepanum means a curved sword or scymitar. Drepane is a sickle.

〔U1171 footnote〕
見史密斯《古典地理辭典》「科西拉」條，那裡有完整的參考資料。
> See Smith's *Dictionary of Classical Geography*, under Corcyra, where full references will be found.

〔U1172 heading〕
第十四章
> CHAPTER XIV.

〔U1173 para〕
《奧德賽》作者所知的《伊里亞德》與我們今天所見的相同
> THAT THE ILIAD WHICH THE WRITER OF THE ODYSSEY KNEW WAS THE SAME AS WHAT WE NOW HAVE.

〔U1174 para〕
剩下要我說明的，是《奧德賽》的作者手邊那部《伊里亞德》，實際上就是我們今天所見的這部；並且要談一談這部詩在她手下是怎樣逐漸成形的。
> It remains for me to show that the writer of the *Odyssey* had the *Iliad* before her to all intents and purposes as we now have it, and to deal with the manner in which the poem grew under her hands.

〔U1175 para〕
在我自己的《伊里亞德》和《奧德賽》上，我把兩部詩共有的段落都劃了線，並註明出處。真希望我們哪一所大學出版社能給我們出一部《奧德賽》，把所有出自《伊里亞德》的段落用略為不同的字體印出，並附上出處，體例大致像本章所收《伊里亞德》卷一與卷二十四的摘錄那樣。這些段落可在鄧巴《奧德賽詞語索引》的書末找到，但在詩中它們出現之處一一標出，會更有啟發性。在我（現已完成）的兩部詩的譯本中，相同的段落我都盡可能用相同的字句譯出。在《奧德賽》譯本裡，我打算把它們用另一種字體印出，並註明在《伊里亞德》中的出處。在《伊里亞德》的譯本裡這樣做就沒有用處了，因為沒有人認為荷馬從《奧德賽》拿過什麼。不過，這兩部譯本的出版，我只怕得延後了；但我會在本章舉出足夠多的例子，我想足以讓讀者滿意：《奧德賽》作者的《伊里亞德》，就是我們自己的《伊里亞德》。
> In my own copies of the *Iliad* and *Odyssey* I have underlined all the passages that are common to both poems, giving the references. It is greatly to be wished that one or other of our University presses would furnish us with an *Odyssey* in which all the Iliadic passages are printed in a slightly different type and with a reference, somewhat in the style of the extracts from *Il*. I. and XXIV. here given. The passages are to be found at the end of Dunbar's *Concordance to the Odyssey*, but the marking of them as they occur in the course of the poem will be more instructive. In my translations of the poems (now finished) I have translated identical passages as nearly as possible in identical words. In the *Odyssey* I propose to print them in another type and give the references to the *Iliad*. In the translation of the *Iliad* there is no use in doing this, for no one supposes that Homer took anything from the *Odyssey*. The publication, however, of these translations must, I fear, be postponed, but I will give in this Chapter as many instances as I think will be sufficient to satisfy the reader that the *Iliad* of the writer of the *Odyssey* was our own *Iliad*.

〔U1176 para〕
我先舉《伊里亞德》的兩段，一段出自卷一，另一段出自卷二十四，所附出處一律指《奧德賽》。這兩段被《奧德賽》作者採用的詩行，或許比《伊里亞德》其他任何段落都多，雖然也有幾段與它們不相上下。以較小字體印出的詩行或半行，不見於《奧德賽》。
> I will begin by giving two passages from the *Iliad*, one from Book I., and the other from Book XXIV., the references in all cases being to the *Odyssey*. These are perhaps fuller of lines adopted by the writer of the *Odyssey* than any others in the *Iliad*, though there are some that run them closely. Lines or parts of lines in the smaller type do not occur in the *Odyssey*.

〔U1177 para〕
我要請讀者注意的第一段，是《伊里亞德》卷一 455–485 行，原文如下：──
> The first passage that I will call attention to is *Iliad* i. 455-485, which is as follows:—

〔U1178 figure〕
〔圖版 20：（原書無圖說）〕
> 

〔U1179 para〕
或許我該告訴讀者，《伊里亞德》卷一是現代批評仍允許保留在原詩人名下的少數幾卷之一──這位詩人寫的，就是傑布教授所謂「原始的」《伊里亞德》。
> I should perhaps tell the reader that the first Book of the *Iliad* is one of the few which modern criticism allows to remain in the possession of the poet who wrote what Professor Jebb calls the "primary" *Iliad*.

〔U1180 para〕
上面提到的兩段中，第二段是《伊里亞德》卷二十四 621–651 行，原文是：──
> The second of the two passages above referred to is *Iliad* XXIV. 621-651, which runs:—

〔U1181 figure〕
〔圖版 21：（原書無圖說）〕
> 

〔U1182 para〕
傑布教授傾向於把《伊里亞德》卷二十四歸給卷九的作者──他並不認為卷九出自荷馬──並把它定年在*約*西元前 750–600 年。遺憾的是，我只能跟他走到「卷二十四與卷九出自同一人之手」這一步為止。
> Professor Jebb is disposed to attribute *Il*. XXIV. to the writer of *Il*. IX., which he does not ascribe to Homer, and would date *circ*. B.C. 750-600. I regret that I can go no further with him than that *Il*. XXIV. and *Il*. IX. are by the same hand.

〔U1183 para〕
我在若干《奧德賽》詩行後註明了出處，這些詩行與《伊里亞德》之間有些細微而完全無關緊要的差異；逐一指出它們，超出了本書的範圍。這些差異幾乎無一例外，都是上下文不同所致。它們雖然無關緊要，卻不是沒有意思；但我只好留給有興趣的讀者自己去查考。
> It is beyond my scope to point out the slight and perfectly unimportant variations from the *Iliad* which are found in some of the Odyssean lines to which I have given a reference; they are with hardly an exception such as are occasioned by difference of context. Though unimportant they are not uninteresting, but I must leave them for the reader to examine if he feels inclined to do so.

〔U1184 para〕
讀者會注意到，上面兩段摘錄之中，有些詩行彼此幾乎相同，有些完全相同；我還應補充一句：另有不少詩行也在《伊里亞德》別處重複出現。但兩段摘錄各自獨有的詩行仍剩下夠多，足以讓我確信《奧德賽》的作者兩段都熟悉。不僅如此，這些詩句湧上她心頭時，似乎就像《聖經》、《公禱書》和莎士比亞的句子湧上我們心頭一樣自然，而且無疑往往一樣不自覺。
> He will observe that some lines are nearly and some quite common to the two extracts above given, and I should add that not a few other lines are repeated elsewhere in the *Iliad*, but enough remains that is peculiar to either of the two extracts to convince me that the writer of the *Odyssey* knew them both. And not only this, but they seem to have risen in her mind as spontaneously, and often no doubt as unconsciously, as passages from the Bible, Prayer-book, and Shakspeare do to ourselves.

〔U1185 para〕
那麼，如果我們發現這位作者對《伊里亞德》首卷與末卷中如此可觀的兩段這樣熟悉──因為我相信讀者會和我一樣毫不懷疑，她熟悉它們，也在借用它們──我們還能不認為，她至少很可能也熟悉中間各卷嗎？這無疑應是最自然、最不牽強的結論；但我還要進一步證明，她對中間各卷熟悉得很。
> If, then, we find the writer so familiar with two such considerable extracts from the first and last Books of the *Iliad*—for I believe the reader will feel no more doubt than I do, that she knew them, and was borrowing from them—can we avoid thinking it probable that she was acquainted, to say the least of it, with the intermediate Books? Such surely should be the most natural and least strained conclusion to arrive at, but I will proceed to shew that she knew the intermediate Books exceedingly well.

〔U1186 para〕
門托爾的名字是怎樣從涅斯托爾的名字變造出來的（參見《伊里亞德》卷二 76–77 行與《奧德賽》卷二 224、225、228 行），我就略過不談，直接進入奧德修斯的僕人歐律巴特斯這個引人注目的例子。在《奧德賽》卷十九 218、219 行，潘妮洛碧向奧德修斯（他喬裝改扮，她認不出他來）打聽奧德修斯前往特洛伊途中帶在身邊的隨從的細節，奧德修斯回答說，他有個名叫歐律巴特斯的僕人，肩膀是駝的（卷十九 247 行）。翻到《伊里亞德》卷二 184 行，我們發現奧德修斯確實有個來自伊薩卡、名叫歐律巴特斯的僕人，但他似乎並不駝背；然而再往下讀，我們馬上就碰到忒耳西忒斯，「他的雙肩向前拱起，蓋住了胸膛」（《伊里亞德》卷二 217、218 行）。《奧德賽》的作者想多添一個細節好讓潘妮洛碧更加放心，在《伊里亞德》裡又找不到，於是把駝背從緊挨著的下一個人背上取下來，安到歐律巴特斯背上──我這樣下結論，是否太倉促了？我並不是說，為了支持別的結論就無法構想出其他假說；但我認為上述這個最合乎常識；而由此得出的最自然的推論是：《奧德賽》的作者至少熟悉《伊里亞德》卷二的一部分，大致就像我們今天所見的那樣。
> I pass over the way in which Mentor's name is coined from Nestor's (*cf*. *Il*. II. 76-77 and *Od*. ii. 224, 225, and 228), and will go on to the striking case of Ulysses' servant Eurybates. In *Od*. xix. 218, 219 Penelope has asked Ulysses (who is disguised so that she does not recognise him) for details as to the followers Ulysses had with him on his way to Troy, and Ulysses answers that he had a servant named Eurybates who was hunched in the shoulders (xix. 247). Turning to *Il*. II. 184 we find that Ulysses had a servant from Ithaca named Eurybates, but he does not seem to have been hunched in the shoulders; on reading further, however, we immediately come to Thersites, "whose shoulders were hunched over his chest" (*Il*. II. 217, 218). Am I too hasty in concluding that the writer of the *Odyssey*, wanting an additional detail for Penelope's greater assurance, and not finding one in the *Iliad*, took the hunchiness off the back of the next man to him and set it on to the back of Eurybates? I do not say that no other hypothesis can be framed in order to support a different conclusion, but I think the one given above will best commend itself to common sense; and the most natural inference from it is that the writer of the *Odyssey* knew at any rate part of *Il*. II. much as we have it now.

〔U1187 para〕
我常納悶，為什麼要讓墨涅拉俄斯恰好在俄瑞斯忒斯為埃癸斯托斯和克呂泰涅斯特拉舉行喪宴的同一天回來；告訴我們這件事的希臘原文是：──
> I often wondered why Menelaus should have been made to return on the self-same day as that on which Orestes was holding the funeral feast of Ægisthus and Clytemnestra; the Greek which tells us that he did so runs:—

〔U1188 para〕
αὐτῆμαρ δέ οἱ ἦλθε βοὴν ἀγαθὸς Μενέλαος（就在同一天，擅長吶喊的墨涅拉俄斯來到他那裡）（《奧德賽》卷三 311 行）。
> αὐτῆμαρ δέ οἱ ἦλθε βοὴν ἀγαθὸς Μενέλαος (*Od*. iii. 311).

〔U1189 para〕
直到我想起《伊里亞德》卷二 408 行，才找到解答：阿伽門農邀請亞該亞眾首領赴宴時，並沒有請墨涅拉俄斯，因為墨涅拉俄斯是自己來的：──
> I did not find the explanation till I remembered that in *Iliad* II. 408, when Agamemnon has been inviting the Achæan chieftains to a banquet, he did not ask Menelaus, for Menelaus came of his own accord:—

〔U1190 para〕
αὐτόματος δέ οἱ ἦλθε βοὴν ἀγαθὸς Μενέλαος（擅長吶喊的墨涅拉俄斯不請自來，到了他那裡）；
> αὐτόματος δέ οἱ ἦλθε βοὴν ἀγαθὸς Μενέλαος,

〔U1191 para〕
想起這一點，我便看出：要保住這行詩其餘部分還能用，與其用別的辦法改動 αὐτόματος（自動、不請自來），不如讓墨涅拉俄斯恰好在埃癸斯托斯喪宴那天回家，來得省事。如果我不相信這純粹是無意識腦力運作的結果，我會替《奧德賽》的作者感到羞愧。凡是稍有處理文學問題經驗的人，憑直覺都會認定：《奧德賽》這行和《伊里亞德》那行，必有一行取自另一行；而若說在《伊里亞德》裡，墨涅拉俄斯之所以被寫成不請自來，是因為在《奧德賽》裡他碰巧在俄瑞斯忒斯為埃癸斯托斯舉行喪宴的那一天回來，那是不可想像的。《伊里亞德》的上下文說明了墨涅拉俄斯為什麼不請自來──因為他知道阿伽門農忙得無暇邀請他。因此我推斷，《奧德賽》的作者又一次顯示出她熟悉《伊里亞德》卷二的某個部分。
> on remembering this I observed that it would be less trouble to make Menelaus come home on the very day of Ægisthus' funeral feast than to alter αὐτόματος in any other way which would leave the rest of the line available. I should be ashamed of the writer of the *Odyssey* for having done this, unless I believed it to be merely due to unconscious cerebration. That the Odyssean and Iliadic lines are taken the one from the other will approve itself to the instincts of any one who is accustomed to deal with literary questions at all, and it is not conceivable that Menelaus should, in the *Iliad*, have been made to come uninvited because in the *Odyssey* he happened to come back on the very day when Orestes was holding Ægisthus' funeral feast; the Iliadic context explains why Menelaus came uninvited—it was because he knew that Agamemnon was too busy to invite him. I infer, therefore, that the writer of the *Odyssey* again shows herself familiar with a part of *Il*. II.

〔U1192 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷二中亞該亞與特洛伊兩軍的名錄，原本就是荷馬脫手時《伊里亞德》的一部分；對此我看不出有什麼充分的理由去懷疑，哪怕只是提出疑問。需要這些名錄，才能交代我們在詩的主體中將會聽到的那些人是誰；它們的位置十分自然；亞該亞名錄在涅斯托爾的發言中已有鋪墊（卷二 360–368 行）；荷馬幾乎等於告訴我們，他編這份名錄時得過協助，因為他呼求繆思──後面幾卷他也不只一次這樣做──並聲明他本人一無所知，完全仰賴別人告訴他的話[註1]；《奧德賽》中引用或暗指的詩行太過顯眼，容不得我們懷疑作者對兩份名錄都爛熟於心。限於篇幅，我無法一一列出，但要請讀者注意以下各處：《伊里亞德》卷二 488 行，參見《奧德賽》卷四 240 行；把斯巴達和拉刻代蒙當成兩個地方（《伊里亞德》卷二 580、581 行），《奧德賽》的作者也這樣做（卷四 10 行），儘管她已充分表明，她知道兩者其實是同一處；《伊里亞德》卷二 600 行，參見《奧德賽》卷三 386 行；614 行的行尾 θαλάσσια ἔργα μεμήλειν（關心海上的營生），參見《奧德賽》卷五 67 行 θαλάσσια ἔργα μέμηλειν；670 行，參見《奧德賽》卷二 12 行；673、674 行，參見《奧德賽》卷十一 469、470 行；《伊里亞德》卷二 706 行 αὐτοκασγνητος μεγαθύμον Πρωτεσιλάου（心志高昂的普洛忒西拉俄斯的親兄弟），這必定就是《奧德賽》卷十 137 行 αὐτοκασιγνήτου ὀλοόφρονος Αἰήταο（心懷惡意的埃厄忒斯的親姊妹）這一行的母本；《伊里亞德》卷二 707 行 ὁπλότερος γένεῇ ὁ δ᾿ ἅμα πρότερος καὶ ἀρείων（他出生較晚，另一人卻既年長又更勇武），參見《奧德賽》卷十九 184 行，同一行詩在那裡出現；《伊里亞德》卷二 721 行 ἀλλ᾿ ὁ μὲν ἐν νήσῳ κεῖτο κρατέρ᾿ ἄλγεα πάσχων（但他躺在島上，忍受著劇烈的痛苦），參見《奧德賽》卷五 13 行，同一行詩在那裡出現，只是為了配合上下文，κεȋτο 改成了 κεῖται；另參見《奧德賽》卷五 395 行，那裡有 πατρός, ὃς ἐν νούσῳ κῆται, κρατέρ᾿ ἄλγεα πάσχων（父親，他臥病在床，忍受著劇烈的痛苦），這行詩顯示《奧德賽》的作者被《伊里亞德》浸透到了何等程度；《伊里亞德》卷二 755 行 Στυγὸς ὕδατός ἐστιν ἀπορρώξ（是斯堤克斯河之水的支流），參見《奧德賽》卷十 514 行，同樣的字眼在那裡收束詩行；《伊里亞德》卷二 774 行 δίσκοισιν τέρποντο καὶ αἰγανέῃσιν ἱέντες（擲鐵餅、投標槍取樂），參見《奧德賽》卷四 626 行與卷十七 168 行，同一行詩在那裡出現；《伊里亞德》卷二 776 行，說密爾米頓人的馬 λωτὸν ἐρεπτόμενοι（嚼食蓮草），參見《奧德賽》卷九 97 行，同樣的字眼用在奧德修斯那些與食蓮人在一起的手下身上；《伊里亞德》卷二 873 行 νήπιος, οὐδέ τί οἱ τό γ' ἐπήρκεσε λυγρὸν ὄλεθρον（愚蠢的人，這也沒能替他擋住悲慘的毀滅），參見《奧德賽》卷四 292 行 ἄλγιον, οὐ γάρ οἵ τι τά γ' ἤρκεσε λυγρὸν ὄλεθρον（更令人痛心，因為這些都沒能替他擋住悲慘的毀滅）。
> I can see no sufficient reason for even questioning that the catalogues of the Achæan and Trojan forces in the second Book of the *Iliad* were part of the *Iliad* as it left Homer's hands. They are wanted so as to explain who the people are of whom we are to hear in the body of the poem; their position, is perfectly natural; the Achæan catalogue is prepared in Nestor's speech (II. 360-368); Homer almost tells us that he has had assistance in compiling it, for he invokes the Muse, as he does more than once in later Books, and declares that he knows nothing of his own knowledge, but depends entirely upon what has been told him[註1]; the lines quoted or alluded to in the *Odyssey* are far too marked to allow of our doubting that the writer knew both catalogues familiarly; I cannot within my limits give them, but would call the reader's attention to *Il*. II. 488, *cf*. *Od*. iv. 240; to the considering Sparta and Lacedæmon as two places (*Il*. II. 580, 581) which the writer of the *Odyssey* does (iv. 10), though she has abundantly shown that she knew them to be but one; to *Il*. II. 600, *cf*. *Od*. iii. 386; to the end of line 614, θαλάσσια ἔργα μεμήλειν, *cf*. *Od*. v. 67, θαλάσσια ἔργα μέμηλειν; to 670, *cf*. *Od*. ii. 12; to 673, 674, *cf*. *Od*. xi. 469, 470; to *Il*. II. 706, αὐτοκασγνητος μεγαθύμον Πρωτεσιλάου, which must surely be parent of the line αὐτοκασιγνήτου ὀλοόφρονος Αἰήταο, *Od*. X. 137; to *Il*. II. 707, ὁπλότερος γένεῇ ὁ δ᾿ ἅμα πρότερος καὶ ἀρείων, *cf*. *Od*. xix. 184, where the same line occurs; to *Il*. II. 721, ἀλλ᾿ ὁ μὲν ἐν νήσῳ κεῖτο κρατέρ᾿ ἄλγεα πάσχων, *cf*. *Od*. v. 13, where the same line occurs, but with κεῖται instead of κεȋτο to suit the context; *cf*. also *Od*. v. 395, where we find πατρός, ὃς ἐν νούσῳ κῆται, κρατέρ᾿ ἄλγεα πάσχων, a line which shows how completely the writer of the *Odyssey* was saturated with the *Iliad*; to *Il*. II. 755, Στυγὸς ὕδατός ἐστιν ἀπορρώξ, *cf*. *Od*. x. 514, where the same words end the line; to *Il*. II. 774, δίσκοισιν τέρποντο καὶ αἰγανέῃσιν ἱέντες, *cf*. *Od*. iv. 626, and xvii. 168, where the same line occurs; to *Il*. II. 776, where the horses of the Myrmidons are spoken of as λωτὸν ἐρεπτόμενοι, *cf*. *Od*. ix. 97, where the same words are used for Ulysses' men when with the Lotus-eaters; to *Il*. II. 873, νήπιος, οὐδέ τί οἱ τό γ' ἐπήρκεσε λυγρὸν ὄλεθρον, *cf*. *Od*. iv. 292, ἄλγιον, οὐ γάρ οἵ τι τά γ' ἤρκεσε λυγρὸν ὄλεθρον.

〔U1193 para〕
以上引用或提到的段落，沒有一處見於《伊里亞德》其他地方；所以它們如果出自《伊里亞德》，就是出自這兩份名錄。不過，既然我相信已經證明了《奧德賽》的作者熟悉卷二第 76、77、78、184、216、217 與 408 行，又既然我承認卷二其餘部分出自荷馬之手（不論有沒有人協助），對於我的論點──整個卷二都為《奧德賽》的作者所熟知，而且偶爾被她借用──我就不再多加論證了。
> None of the passages above quoted or referred to are to be found anywhere else in the *Iliad*, so that if from the *Iliad* at all, they are from the catalogues. But having already shown, as I believe, that the writer of the *Odyssey* knew lines 76, 77, 78, 184, 216, 217, and 408 of Book II., and accepting the rest of the Book as written by Homer, with or without assistance, I shall not argue further in support of my contention that the whole of Book II. was known to, and occasionally borrowed from, by the writer of the *Odyssey*.

〔U1194 para〕
《奧德賽》中無意識腦力運作最漂亮的例子，或許要數卷三的開篇一行：ἠέλιος δ' ἀνόρουσε λιπὼν περικαλλέα λίμνην（太陽離開美麗的湖水，一躍而起），它取自《伊里亞德》卷五 20 行 Ιδαῖος δ' ἀνόρουσε λιπὼν περικαλλέα δίφρον·（伊代俄斯離開華美的戰車，一躍而下）。一位顯然下筆如此輕鬆的作者，竟會這樣漂到一行意思截然不同的《伊里亞德》詩句上，除了浸淫太深所致之外，實在令人想不出還有什麼別的解釋。說她四處搜尋一行詩來表達太陽升起，結果認為伊代俄斯跳下戰車那一行正好合用，那是不可想像的。她在卷二十二 95 行寫下 Τηλέμαχος δ' ἀπόρουσε λιπὼν δολιχόσκιον ἔγχος（忒勒馬科斯丟下影子長長的長矛，跳開了）時，心裡又想著這一行。
> Perhaps the prettiest example of unconscious cerebration in the *Odyssey* is to be found in the opening line of *Od*. iii, which runs ἠέλιος δ' ἀνόρουσε λιπὼν περικαλλέα λίμνην, which is taken from *Il*. v. 20, Ιδαῖος δ' ἀνόρουσε λιπὼν περικαλλέα δίφρον· One is at a loss to conceive how a writer so apparently facile should drift thus on to an Iliadic line of such different signification except as the result of saturation. It is inconceivable that she should have cast about for a line to say that the sun was rising, and thought that Idæus jumping off his chariot would do. She again has this line in her mind when in Book xxii. 95 she writes Τηλέμαχος δ' ἀπόρουσε λιπὼν δολιχόσκιον ἔγχος.

〔U1195 para〕
上面最後提到的那幾行所顯示的同一類無意識腦力運作，有時也使她以奇特的方式重複自己的詩句，而她多半全然不自覺。例如：──
> The same kind of unconscious celebration evidenced by the lines last referred to leads her sometimes to repeat lines of her own in a strange way, without probably being at all aware of it. As for example:—

βασιλῆες.....εἰσὶ καὶ ἄλλοι πολλοὶ ἐν ἀμφιάλῳ Ἰθάκῃ νέοι ἠδὲ παλαιοί,（王公們……在四面環海的伊薩卡還有許多別的，有年輕的，也有年老的）（卷一 394、395 行）。
> βασιλῆες.....εἰσὶ καὶ ἄλλοι πολλοὶ ἐν ἀμφιάλῳ Ἰθάκῃ νέοι ἠδὲ παλαιοί, (i. 394, 395).

〔U1197 para〕
這段文字到了下一卷就變成：──
> This passage in the following Book becomes:—

εἰσὶ δὲ νῆες πολλαὶ ἐν ἀμφιάλῳ Ἰθάκῃ νέαι ἠδὲ παλαιαί·（在四面環海的伊薩卡有許多船，有新的，也有舊的）（卷二 292、293 行）。
> εἰσὶ δὲ νῆες πολλαὶ ἐν ἀμφιάλῳ Ἰθάκῃ νέαι ἠδὲ παλαιαί· (ii. 292, 293).

〔U1199 para〕
另一個類似的例子，是那行寫西西弗斯的石頭在一串長短短格中蹦跳下山的著名詩句，見《奧德賽》卷十一 598 行，原文是：──
> Another similar case is that of the famous line about Sisyphus' stone bounding down hill in a string of dactyls, *Od*. xi. 598, it runs:—

〔U1200 para〕
αὖτις ἔπειτα πέδονδε κυλίνδετο λᾶας ἀναιδής.
> αὖτις ἔπειτα πέδονδε κυλίνδετο λᾶας ἀναιδής.

〔U1201 para〕
「那塊無情的石頭又蹦跳著滾回平地。」我相信這不過是無意識地改編自《伊里亞德》中我記得的唯一一行長短短格詩句，我指的是：──
> "The cruel stone came bounding down again on to the plain." I believe this to be nothing but an unconscious adaptation from the one dactylic line that I can remember in the *Iliad*, I mean:—

〔U1202 para〕
ἀμφοτέρω δὲ τένοντε καὶ ἴστέα λᾶας ἀναιδὴς ἄχρις ἀπηλοίησεν.《伊里亞德》卷四 521、522 行。
> ἀμφοτέρω δὲ τένοντε καὶ ἴστέα λᾶας ἀναιδὴς ἄχρις ἀπηλοίησεν. *Il*. IV 521, 522.

〔U1203 para〕
「那塊無情的石頭把頸骨連同筋腱一起砸得粉碎。」就算《奧德賽》那行詩可能有以音配義之妙（這一點十分可疑），我也認為那靈感來自《伊里亞德》那一行。
> "The cruel stone shattered the bones of the neck, tendons and all." Granted (which is very doubtful) that there may be an accommodation of sound to sense in the Odyssean line, I contend that the suggestion came from the Iliadic line.

〔U1204 para〕
我很樂意把整部《伊里亞德》從頭到尾走一遍，一一指出《奧德賽》的作者如何利用它，但這樣做幾乎需要專門寫一本書。因此我要請讀者接受我的說法：就被利用的情形而言，《伊里亞德》沒有哪一卷和其他各卷有明顯差別；我只談最普遍被宣稱為後來增補的那幾卷──我指的是卷十與卷十八──因為我認為，我已經充分證明《奧德賽》的作者熟悉卷一、卷二十四，以及卷二中的名錄。不過，把卷十一也納入檢視或許不錯，因為這是最無可置疑的幾卷之一；而且值得一提的是，最受懷疑的幾卷和最無可置疑的幾卷，《奧德賽》的作者同樣瞭如指掌。我只會指出在《伊里亞德》中出現不超過一次的段落，至於為數極多、可視為套語的那些，一律略去。
> I would gladly go through the whole *Iliad* calling attention to the use the writer of the *Odyssey* has made of it, but to do this would require hardly less than a book to itself. I will therefore ask the reader to accept my statement that no one Book in the *Iliad* shows any marked difference from the others as regards the use that has been made of it, and will limit myself to those Books that have been most generally declared to be later additions—I mean Book X. and Book XVIII.—for I consider that I have already sufficiently shown the writer of the *Odyssey* to have known Books I., XXIV., and the Catalogues in Book II. It may be well, however, to include Book XI. in my examination, for this is one of the most undoubted, and it will be interesting to note that the writer of the *Odyssey* has both the most doubted and undoubted Books equally at her fingers' ends. I shall only call attention to passages that do not occur more than once in the *Iliad*, and will omit the very numerous ones that may be considered as common form.

在《伊里亞德》卷十 141、142 行，我們讀到：──

> τίφθ' οὔτω;..... Νύκτα δι' ἀμβροσίην, and in *Od*. ix. 403, 404. τίπτε τόσον..... Νύκτα δι' ἀμβροσίην.

> In *Il*. X. 142, ὅτι δὴ χρείω τόσον ἴκει; *Il*. *Od*. ii. 28, τίνα χρειὼ τόσον ἵκει.

《伊里亞德》卷十 214 行有 ὅσσοι γὰρ νήεσσιν ἐπικρατέουσιν ἄριστοι（因為凡在船上掌權的首領們），這一行見於《奧德賽》卷一 245 行、卷十六 122 行、卷十九 130 行，只是把 νήεσσιν（船上）換成了 νήσοισιν（島上）。

> *Il*. X. 243, 244 have, πῶς ἂν ἔπειτ' Ὀδυσῆος ἐγὼ θείοιο λαθοίμην, οὗ περὶ μὲν.....

〔U1213 para〕
在《奧德賽》卷一 65、66 行，我們看到同樣的字句，只是把 οὗ 換成了 ὅς。這是非常有說服力的例子，因為 ἔπειτα（那麼）在《伊里亞德》那行裡十分自然，放在《奧德賽》這行裡卻讓人覺得有些不得其所，這就清楚表明《奧德賽》的段落取自《伊里亞德》的段落，而不是*反過來*。
> In *Od*. i. 65, 66 we find the same words only with ὅς instead of οὗ. This is a very convincing case, for the ἔπειτα, which is quite natural in the Iliadic line, is felt to be rather out of place in the Odyssean one, and makes it plain that the Odyssean passage was taken from the Iliadic, not *vice versâ*.

> *Il*. X. 255 ends with μενοπτόλεμος Θρασυμήδης, so also does *Od*. iii. 442.

《伊里亞德》卷十 278、279 行：……ἥ τέ μοι αἰεὶ ἐν πάντεσσι πόνοισι παρίστασαι.....（你在一切艱難之中，總是站在我身旁……），參見《奧德賽》卷十三 300、301 行：……ἥ τέ τοι αἰεὶ ἐν πάντεσσι πόνοισι παρίσταμαι.....（我在一切艱難之中，總是站在你身旁……）。《伊里亞德》卷十 292–295 行：σοὶ δ᾿ αὖ ἐγὼ ῥέξω βοȗν ἦνιν εὐρυμέτωπον ἀδμήτην, ἣν οὐ πω ὑπὸ ζυγὸν ἤγαγεν ἀνήρ. τήν τοι ἐγὼ ῥέξω χρυσὸν κέρασιν περιχεύας. ὧς ἔφαν εὐχόμενοι, τῶν δ᾿ ἔκλυε Παλλὰς Ἀθήνη.（我要向你獻上一頭一歲、寬額、未經馴服的小母牛，從沒有人把牠套上軛；我要給牠的角包上黃金，獻祭給你。他們這樣祈禱，帕拉斯．雅典娜聽見了他們。）
> *Il*. X. 278, 279,.....ἥ τέ μοι αἰεὶ ἐν πάντεσσι πόνοισι παρίστασαι..... *cf*. *Od*. xiii. 300, 301,.....ἥ τέ τοι αἰεὶ ἐν πάντεσσι πόνοισι παρίσταμαι..... *Il*. X 292-295, σοὶ δ᾿ αὖ ἐγὼ ῥέξω βοȗν ἦνιν εὐρυμέτωπον ἀδμήτην, ἣν οὐ πω ὑπὸ ζυγὸν ἤγαγεν ἀνήρ. τήν τοι ἐγὼ ῥέξω χρυσὸν κέρασιν περιχεύας. ὧς ἔφαν εὐχόμενοι, τῶν δ᾿ ἔκλυε Παλλὰς Ἀθήνη.

〔U1216 para〕
這四行的前三行，在《奧德賽》卷三 382–384 行中逐字重複。在《奧德賽》385 行，第四行變成 ὧς ἔφατ᾿ εὐχόμενος τοȗ δ' ἔκλυε Παλλὰς Ἀθήνη（他這樣祈禱，帕拉斯．雅典娜聽見了他）。
> The first three of these four lines is repeated verbatim in *Od*. iii. 382-384. In *Od*. 385 the fourth line becomes ὧς ἔφατ᾿ εὐχόμενος τοȗ δ' ἔκλυε Παλλὰς Ἀθήνη.

〔U1217 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十 351 行：……ὅσσον τ' ἐπὶ οὖρα πέλονται ἡμιόνων（有騾子犁一程那麼遠），參見《奧德賽》卷八 124 行 ἕσσον τ' ἐν νείῳ οὖρον πέλει ἡμιόνοιιν（就像一對騾子在休耕地上犁一程那麼遠）。
> *Il*. X. 351.....ὅσσον τ' ἐπὶ οὖρα πέλονται ἡμιόνων, *cf*. *Od*. viii. 124 ἕσσον τ' ἐν νείῳ οὖρον πέλει ἡμιόνοιιν.

〔U1218 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十 400 行 τὸν δ' ἐπιμειδήσας προσέφη πολύμητις Ὀδύσσευς（足智多謀的奧德修斯微微一笑，對他說），這一行見於《奧德賽》卷二十二 371 行。
> *Il*. X. 400, τὸν δ' ἐπιμειδήσας προσέφη πολύμητις Ὀδύσσευς, this line occurs *Od*. xxii. 371.

〔U1219 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十 429 行以 δῖοί τε Πελασγοί（以及神聖的佩拉斯吉人）結尾，《奧德賽》卷十九 177 行也是。
> *Il*. X. 429 ends with δῖοί τε Πελασγοί, so also does *Od*. xix. 177.

〔U1220 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十 457 行 φθεγγομένου δ' ἄρα τοῦ γε κάρη κονίῃσιν ἐμίχθη（他話還在嘴邊，頭顱就已滾落塵土），這一行見於《奧德賽》卷二十二 329 行。
> *Il*. X. 457, φθεγγομένου δ' ἄρα τοῦ γε κάρη κονίῃσιν ἐμίχθη, this line is found *Od*. xxii. 329.

〔U1221 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十 534 行 ψεύσομαι ἦ ἒτυμον ἐρέω κέλεται δέ με θυμός（我是說錯了，還是說對了？可我的心催我說出來）。這一行見於《奧德賽》卷四 140 行。
> *Il*. X. 534, ψεύσομαι ἦ ἒτυμον ἐρέω κέλεται δέ με θυμός. In *Od*. iv. 140 this line is found.

〔U1222 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十 556 行 ῥεῖα θεός γ' ἐθέλων καί κ.τ.λ.（神只要願意，便能輕易……云云）。參見《奧德賽》卷三 231 行。
> *Il*. X. 556, ῥεῖα θεός γ' ἐθέλων καί κ.τ.λ. *Cf*. *Od*. iii. 231.

〔U1223 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十 576 行 ἔς ῥ ἀσαμίνθους βάντες εὐξέστας λούσαντο（他們步入光潔的浴盆沐浴）。見《奧德賽》卷四 48 行、卷十七 87 行。
> *Il*. X. 576 ἔς ῥ ἀσαμίνθους βάντες εὐξέστας λούσαντο. See *Od*. iv. 48, xvii. 87.

〔U1224 para〕
這樣，卷十就有十七處明顯的引用，還不算那些雖見於《奧德賽》、卻也見於《伊里亞德》其他各卷的詩行──有人可以聲稱，《奧德賽》的作者是從那些卷而不是從卷十取來的。這等於說，卷十共 579 行，《奧德賽》的作者大約每 33 行就取了一行。沃爾夫的門徒──不過他們之中沒有哪兩個人意見相同，所以很難說究竟誰算是──若要反駁我的理論，即《奧德賽》全部寫於一地、出自一手、成於西元前十一世紀，那麼要嘛就得拿出比過去六年來他們顯示過的更強的武器，要嘛就只好退回某種勒皮他式的造書理論──這種理論他們會比我更擅長設計。
> Here, then, are seventeen apparent quotations from Book X., omitting any claim on lines which, though they are found in the *Odyssey*, are also found in other Books of the *Iliad*, from which, and not from Book X., it may be alleged that the writer of the *Odyssey* took them. This makes the writer of the *Odyssey* to have taken about one line in every 33 of the 579 lines of which Book X. consists. Disciples of Wolf—no two of whom, however, are of the same opinion, so it is hard to say who they are—must either meet my theory that the *Odyssey* is all written at one place, by one hand, and in the eleventh century B.C., with stronger weapons than during the last six years they have shown any signs of possessing, or they must fall back on some Laputan-manner-of-making-books theory, which they will be able to devise better than I can.

〔U1225 para〕
我沒有忘記，反對《伊里亞德》卷十為真作的人，可能會主張上面所舉的段落是取自《奧德賽》；但這種主張不應只針對卷十而不針對其他各卷，因為其他各卷全都同樣充滿見於《奧德賽》的段落；而且就上面所舉《伊里亞德》卷十 243、244 行與《奧德賽》卷一 65、66 行的例子而言，很難懷疑《伊里亞德》的段落是原作、《奧德賽》的是抄本。
> I do not forget that the opponents of the genuineness of *Il*. X. may contend that the passages above given were taken from the *Odyssey*, but this contention should not be urged in respect of Book X. more than in respect of the other Books, which are all of them equally replete with passages that are found in the *Odyssey*, and in the case given above of *Il*. X. 243, 244 and *Od*. i. 65, 66, it is not easy to doubt that the Iliadic passage is the original, and the Odyssean the copy.

〔U1226 para〕
現在我要處理無可置疑的卷十一；和處理卷十時一樣，凡是見於其他各卷的詩行一律略去，除非我特別提請注意。
> I will now deal with the undoubted Book XI., omitting as in the case of Book X. all lines that occur in other Books, unless I call special attention to them.

〔U1227 para〕
卷十一的頭兩行，和《奧德賽》卷五的頭兩行完全相同，但《伊里亞德》卷十一 2 行也見於《伊里亞德》卷十九 2 行。
> The first two lines of Book XI. are identical with the first two of Book V. of the *Odyssey*, but *Il*. XI. 2 occurs also in *Il*. XIX. 2.

〔U1228 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十一 42、43 行：ἵππουριν· δεινὸν δὲ λόφος καθύπερθεν ἔνευεν, εἵλετο δ' ἄλκιμα δοῦρε δύω, κεκορυθμένα χαλκῷ.（……飾有馬鬃，那盔飾在上方可怕地搖晃；他抓起兩支結實的長矛，矛頭包著青銅。）
> *Il*. XI. 42, 43, ἵππουριν· δεινὸν δὲ λόφος καθύπερθεν ἔνευεν, εἵλετο δ' ἄλκιμα δοῦρε δύω, κεκορυθμένα χαλκῷ.

〔U1229 para〕
這兩行見於《奧德賽》卷二十二 124、125 行，但其中第一行在《伊里亞德》別處也出現過三四次。
> These two lines are found *Od*. xxii. 124, 125, but the first of them occurs three or four times elsewhere in the *Iliad*.

〔U1230 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十一 181 行：ἀλλ' ὅτε δὴ τάχ' ἔμελλεν ὑπὸ πτόλιν αἰπύ τε τεῖχος ἵξεσθαι τότε δὴ.....（但當他眼看就要抵達城下與高牆時，就在那時……），參見《奧德賽》卷四 514、515 行：ἀλλ' ὅτε δὴ τάχ' ἔμελλεν Μαλείαων ὄρος αἰπύ ἵξεσθαι τότε δὴ.....（但當他眼看就要抵達馬勒亞的陡峭山岬時，就在那時……）。
> *Il*. XI. 181, ἀλλ' ὅτε δὴ τάχ' ἔμελλεν ὑπὸ πτόλιν αἰπύ τε τεῖχος ἵξεσθαι τότε δὴ..... *cf*. *Od*. iv. 514, 515, ἀλλ' ὅτε δὴ τάχ' ἔμελλεν Μαλείαων ὄρος αἰπύ ἵξεσθαι τότε δὴ.....

〔U1231 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十一 201 行 προέηκε τεῒν τάδε μυθήσασθαι（派我來把這些話告訴你），參見《奧德賽》卷四 829 行，同樣的字句在那裡出現。
> *Il*. xi. 201, προέηκε τεῒν τάδε μυθήσασθαι, *cf*. *Od*. iv. 829, where the same words occur.

〔U1232 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十一 253 行 ἀντικρὺς δὲ δίεδχε φαεκνοῦ δουρὸς ἀκωκή.（閃亮的矛尖直直穿了過去。）參見《奧德賽》卷十九 453 行，同一行詩在那裡出現，只是把 δίεσχε 換成了 διῆλθε。
> *Il*. XI. 253, ἀντικρὺς δὲ δίεδχε φαεκνοῦ δουρὸς ἀκωκή. *cf*. *Od*. xix. 453, where the same line occurs but with διῆλθε for δίεσχε.

〔U1233 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十一 531 行 ὧς ἄρα φωνήσας ἵμασεν καλλίτριχας ἵππους（他說罷，便揮鞭催動鬃毛美麗的馬），參見《奧德賽》卷十五 215 行，同一行詩在那裡出現，只是把 ἵμασεν（揮鞭）換成了 ἔλασεν（驅趕）。
> *Il*. XI. 531, ὧς ἄρα φωνήσας ἵμασεν καλλίτριχας ἵππους *cf*. *Od*. xv. 215, where the same line occurs but with ἔλασεν instead of ἵμασεν.

〔U1234 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十一 624–639 行。赫卡墨得為帕特羅克洛斯和馬卡翁調製的那份羹湯，《奧德賽》的作者在講喀耳刻為奧德修斯手下調製的那份羹湯時（《奧德賽》卷十 234、235 行），心裡想必想著它。
> *Il*. XI. 624-639. The mess which Hecamedé cooked for Patroclus and Machaon was surely present to the mind of the writer of the *Odyssey* when she was telling about the mess which Circe cooked for Ulysses' men, *Od*. X. 234, 235.

〔U1235 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十一 668、669 行：……οὐ γὰρ ἐμὴ ἲς ἔσθ', οἵη πάρος ἔσκεν ἐνὶ γναμπτοῖσι μέλεσσιν（因為我柔韌的四肢裡，已沒有往日那股力氣），參見《奧德賽》卷十一 393、394 行 ἀλλ᾿ οὐγάρ οἱ ἔτ᾿ ἦν ἲς ἔμπεδος οὐδέ τι κȋκυς οἵη περ πάρος ἔσκεν ἐνὶ γναμπτοȋσι μέλεσσιν（但他柔韌的四肢裡，已不再有往日那股堅實的力量與活力）。《伊里亞德》卷十一 678、679 行：……ἀγέλας, τόσα πώεα οἰῶν τόσσα συῶν συβόσια, τόσ' αἰπόλια πλατέ' αἰγῶν（……牛群，同樣多的綿羊群，同樣多的豬群，同樣多四處散布的山羊群）。
> *Il*. XI. 668, 669.....οὐ γὰρ ἐμὴ ἲς ἔσθ', οἵη πάρος ἔσκεν ἐνὶ γναμπτοῖσι μέλεσσιν *cf*. *Od*. xi. 393, 394, ἀλλ᾿ οὐγάρ οἱ ἔτ᾿ ἦν ἲς ἔμπεδος οὐδέ τι κȋκυς οἵη περ πάρος ἔσκεν ἐνὶ γναμπτοȋσι μέλεσσιν. *Il*. XI. 678, 679.....ἀγέλας, τόσα πώεα οἰῶν τόσσα συῶν συβόσια, τόσ' αἰπόλια πλατέ' αἰγῶν.

〔U1236 para〕
這兩行見於《奧德賽》卷十四 100、101 行，只是把 ἀγέλας 換成了 ἀγέλαι。
> These lines occur *Od*. xiv. 100, 101 but with ἀγέλαι instead of ἀγέλας.

〔U1237 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十一 742 行 τὸν μὲν ἐγὼ προσιόντα βάλον χαλκήρεϊ δουρί（他衝上前來，我用包青銅的長矛擊中了他）。這一行見於《奧德賽》卷十三 267 行，只是把 προσιόντα（衝上前來）換成了 κατιόντα（走下來）。
> *Il*. XI. 742, τὸν μὲν ἐγὼ προσιόντα βάλον χαλκήρεϊ δουρί. This line is found *Od*. xiii. 267 but with κατιόντα for προσιόντα.

〔U1238 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十一 777 行 στῆμεν ἐνὶπροθύροιστι ταφὼν δ' ἀvόρουσεν Ἀχιλλεύς（我們站在門廊前，阿基里斯吃驚地跳起身來），參見《奧德賽》卷十六 12 行 ἔστη ἐνὶ προθύροιστι ταφὼν δ' ἀvόρουσε συβώτης（他站在門廊前，牧豬人吃驚地跳起身來）。
> *Il*. XI. 777, στῆμεν ἐνὶπροθύροιστι ταφὼν δ' ἀvόρουσεν Ἀχιλλεύς, *cf*. *Od*. xvi. 12, ἔστη ἐνὶ προθύροιστι ταφὼν δ' ἀvόρουσε συβώτης.

〔U1239 para〕
這裡兩部詩共有的明顯段落只有十一處，儘管卷十一比卷十長了將近三百行；但我已自我設限，凡是也見於《伊里亞德》其他任何一卷的段落都不能引用。瀏覽我那部《伊里亞德》裡劃了線的詩行，我並不覺得卷十一和其他各卷在這方面的頻率有多大差別，雖然我承認確有一些。此外，我承認我覺得卷十一或許是整部《伊里亞德》中最乏味、寫得最敷衍的一卷，也很能相信《奧德賽》的作者之所以從中借得較少，正是因為她也有同感；但無論如何，上面蒐集到的共有段落已足以確立：《奧德賽》的作者心中既有卷十，也有卷十一。
> Here we have only eleven well-marked passages common to both poems, in spite of the fact that Book XI. is nearly 300 lines longer than Book X., but I am precluded from referring to any passages that occur also in any other Book of the *Iliad*. Running my eye over the underlined lines in my copy of the *Iliad*, I do not find much, though I admit that there is some, difference between their frequency in Book XI., and in the other Books. Furthermore I own to finding Book XI. perhaps the least interesting and the most perfunctorily written in all the *Iliad*, and can well believe that the writer of the *Odyssey* borrowed from it less because she was of the same opinion, but however this may be, the number of common passages above collected is ample to establish the fact that the writer of the *Odyssey* had Book XI. in her mind as well as Book X.

〔U1240 para〕
現在我要接著檢視《伊里亞德》卷十八中被《奧德賽》的作者全部或部分採用的段落。它們是：──
> I will now go on to examine the passages in *Il*. XVIII. which the writer of the *Odyssey* has wholly or in part adopted. They are:—

〔U1241 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十八 22–24 行：ὦς φάτο τὸν δ' ἄχεος νεφέλη ἐκάλυψε μέλαινα ἀμφοτέρῃσι δὲ χερσὶν ἑλὼν κόνιν αἰθαλόεσσαν χεύατο κὰκ κεφαλῆς χαρίεν δ' ᾔσχυνε πρόσωπον.（他這樣說，一團黑色的悲雲籠罩了他；他雙手抓起焦黑的塵土，撒在頭上，玷污了俊美的面容。）
> *Il*. XVIII. 22-24, ὦς φάτο τὸν δ' ἄχεος νεφέλη ἐκάλυψε μέλαινα ἀμφοτέρῃσι δὲ χερσὶν ἑλὼν κόνιν αἰθαλόεσσαν χεύατο κὰκ κεφαλῆς χαρίεν δ' ᾔσχυνε πρόσωπον.

〔U1242 para〕
這幾行見於《奧德賽》卷二十四 315–317 行，只是因為說的是一位老人，而不像《伊里亞德》說的是一個年輕人，χαρίεν δ' ᾔσχυνε πρόσωπον（玷污了俊美的面容）就變成了 πολιῆς ἀδινὰ στεναχίζων（〔撒在〕灰白的〔頭上〕，不住地呻吟）。三行中的第一行也見於《伊里亞德》卷十七 591 行。
> These lines are found *Od*. xxiv. 315-317 except that as they refer to an old man, instead of, as in the *Iliad*, to a young one, χαρίεν δ' ᾔσχυνε πρόσωπον has become πολιῆς ἀδινὰ στεναχίζων. The first of the three lines occurs also in *Il*. XVII. 591.

〔U1243 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十八 108 行 καὶ χόλος ὅς τ' ἐφέηκε πολύφρονά περ χαλεπῆναι（還有那怒氣，它使人即使再明智也會暴躁起來），參見《奧德賽》卷十四 464 行 ἠλεός, ὅς τ' ἐφέηκε πολύφρονά περ μάλ' ἀεῖσαι（〔酒〕令人昏亂，它使人即使再明智也會引吭高歌）。
> *Il*. XVIII. 108, καὶ χόλος ὅς τ' ἐφέηκε πολύφρονά περ χαλεπῆναι, *cf*. *Od*. xiv. 464, ἠλεός, ὅς τ' ἐφέηκε πολύφρονά περ μάλ' ἀεῖσαι.

〔U1244 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十八 250 行 Πανθοΐδης· ὁ γὰρ οἶος ὅρα πρόσσω καὶ ὀπίσσω（潘托俄斯之子；因為唯有他能瞻前顧後），參見《奧德賽》卷二十四 452 行，不過那裡 Πανθοΐδης（潘托俄斯之子）變成了 Μαστορίδης（馬斯托爾之子）。
> *Il*. XVIII. 250, Πανθοΐδης· ὁ γὰρ οἶος ὅρα πρόσσω καὶ ὀπίσσω, *cfOd*. xxiv. 452, where however Πανθοΐδης becomes Μαστορίδης.

〔U1245 para〕
《伊里亞德》卷十八 344–349 行：
> *Il*. XVIII. 344-349,

〔U1246 para〕
ἀμφί πυρί στῆσαι τρίποδα μέγαν ὄφρα τάχιστα Πάτροκλον λούσειαν ἄπο βρότον αἱματόεντα. οἱ δὲ λοετροχόον τρίποδ' ἵστασαν ἐν πυρὶ κηλέῳ, ἐν δ' ἄρ' ὔδωρ ἐχέαν, ὑπὸ δὲ ξύλα δαῖον ἑλόντες· γάστρην μὲν τρίποδος πῦρ ἄμφεπε, θέρμετο δ' ὔδωρ αὖτὰρ ἐπεὶ δὴ ζέσσεν ὕδωρ ἐνὶ ἤνοπι χαλκῷ,（在火上架起一只大三腳鼎，好儘快替帕特羅克洛斯洗去凝結的血污。他們便把沐浴用的三腳鼎架在熊熊的火上，倒進水，又拿來木柴在底下燒起；火舌舔著鼎腹，水漸漸熱了；等到水在發亮的青銅鼎裡沸騰起來，）
